## Sadhana-bhakti

## Purpose of Sadhana-bhakti

We have needs other than simply maintaining our physical existence. Human existence is meant to facilitate our understanding of the purpose of life. Govinda dasa writes that just as a drop of water appears on a lotus leaf and can roll off at any moment, so life in the body is temporary. But let me worship Sri Hari. This is the only true necessity in life.

*etad isanam isasya*  
*prakrti-stho ’pi tad-gunaih*  
*na yujyate sadatma-sthair*  
*yatha buddhis tad-asraya*

“This is the divinity of the Personality of Godhead: He is not affected by the qualities of material nature, even though He is in contact with them. Similarly, the devotees who have taken shelter of the Lord do not become influenced by the material qualities. ” *(Bhag.* 1. 11. 38)

Krsna explains in *Bhagavad-gita* that the qualities of goodness, passion, and ignorance are manifested by His energies and are in Him, but He is above these three modes, never coming under their influence. Thus although these three modes are difficult to overcome, if we surrender to Krsna, we can easily cross beyond them.

In his commentary on *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* Srila Jiva Gosvami gives the example of how the sun creates clouds, rain, darkness, and snowfall, yet the sun is never disturbed by them. Clouds are formed when the sun evaporates ocean and other water, and they manifest over particular places without covering the entire sky. Although clouds are created by the sun, the sun is not disturbed or truly covered by them because it is always above the clouds. Similarly, the Supreme Lord creates the three modes of material nature, which act according to His direction but never disturb or cover Him. Krsna says:

*janma karma ca me divyam*  
*evam yo vetti tattvatah*  
*tyaktva deham punar janma*  
*naiti mam eti so ’rjuna*

“One who knows the transcendental nature of My appearance and activities does not, upon leaving the body, take his birth again in this material world, but attains My eternal abode, O Arjuna. ” (Bg. 4. 9) By taking shelter of Krsna we come under the protection of the Lord’s internal potency and are therefore ourselves protected from the influence of the three modes. How do we take shelter of Krsna? We simply have to transform our consciousness from material consciousness to our natural spiritual consciousness. It is natural for the tiny spirit soul to seek shelter under the Supreme.

Prabhupada gives the example that we do not say “shine” but “sunshine. ” When we speak of consciousness, we may think of self-consciousness, but real self-consciousness means “Krsna consciousness. ” The relationship between the *jiva* and Krsna is a natural one, and it is not possible to see ourselves separately from that relationship. Unfortunately, we have come to see ourselves through the reflection of material nature rather than our true spiritual nature. Just as pure rainwater when falling from the sky contacts the earth and is muddied, so the pure spirit soul, when coming in contact with the three modes of material nature, becomes “muddied” by material nature and absorbs itself in various material designations that have nothing to do with the real self.

Devotional service, however, clears the mud—and the consciousness—and allows us to see ourselves in our natural relationship with Krsna. When we engage in devotional service to the Supreme Lord, there are two automatic byproducts: we become free from material designations *(sarva upadhi),* and Hrsikesa, the master of all senses, controls our senses. We then attain the transcendental platform, free of the modes. Krsna is always transcendental to matter; because devotional service to Krsna is nondifferent from Krsna, devotional service is also transcendental to matter. It is therefore natural to conclude that those who perform devotional service are also transcendental to the modes of material nature.

Is this true of *all* devotees? It is necessary to distinguish between unalloyed devotees and mixed devotees, who approach the Lord to have their miseries or ignorance mitigated. The *Bhagavad-gita* lists four types of mixed devotees: “the distressed, the desirer of wealth, the inquisitive, and he who is searching for knowledge of the Absolute. ” (Bg. 7. 16) These four types of pious people are mixed devotees; they want something material from the Lord. The unalloyed devotees, however, are purely attached to the Lord in love, and the Lord is transcendentally attached to them. Whereas for others the Lord has nothing to reciprocate and is therefore called *atma rama* (self-satisfied), for the unalloyed devotees He is known as *bhakta-vatsala,* the lover of His devotees.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura comments that of the four types of mixed devotees, the first three are called *karma-misra-bhaktas,* because although they turn to the Lord, their devotion is mixed with a desire for material gain, freedom from distress or suffering, or the satisfaction of their inquisitive nature. The last type is also mixed, but with knowledge, or *jnana,* and is known as a *jnana-misra-bhakta.* Mixed devotion is not the same as pure devotion, or *kevala-bhakti.*

We should strive to practice *kevala-bhakti,* and therefore to help us purify our desires, the *sastras* have recommended that we follow the regulative principles of *bhakti-yoga.* Krsna tells Arjuna in *Bhagavad-gita* (12. 9):

*atha cittam samadhatum*  
*na saknosi mayi sthiram*  
*abhyasa-yogena tato*  
*mam icchaptum dhananjaya*

“If you cannot fix your mind upon Me without deviation, then follow the regulative principles of *bhakti-yoga.* In this way develop a desire to attain Me. ”

Devotional service has three stages, *sadhana-bhakti* (devotional service in practice), *bhava-bhakti* (devotional service in ecstasy), and *prema-bhakti* (devotional service in pure love of Godhead). One only begins to approach *kevala-bhakti* at the stage of *bhava-bhakti.*

*The Nectar of Devotion* describes the six characteristics of *bhakti,* and the *acaryas* have grouped them into pairs according to which stage of *bhakti* they refer. The two qualities of *sadhana-bhakti* are *klesaghni* (relief from distress) and *subhada* (auspiciousness). That *klesaghni* is a characteristic of *sadhana-bhakti* means that one does not need to have achieved *bhava-bhakti to* begin to experience the upliftment *bhakti* brings into one’s life. The *sastras* also explain that the reactions to past sinful activities are cleared at *nistha,* when one has developed firm faith in chanting the Lord’s holy name.

Similarly, *subhada,* all-auspiciousness, appears when one practices *sadhana-bhakti.* Something is considered all-auspicious when it has the following four qualities: it is attractive to everyone, it benefits everyone, it produces good qualities, and it bestows upon one a superior form of happiness.

*Bhava-bhakti* is characterized by the two qualities of *sadhana-bhakti* and two more qualities: *moksa-laghuta-krt* (practitioners of *bhava-bhakti* are not attracted to liberation) and *sudurlabha (bhava-bhakti* is rarely achieved).

Finally, *prema-bhakti* includes all the qualities of *sadhana-bhakti* and *bhava-bhakti,* but has two more characteristics: *sandrananda-visesatma* (it has the power to fill the *bhakta* with incalculable bliss) and sri-*krsnakarsini* (it alone attracts Sri Krsna, the Absolute Personality of Godhead).

Prahlada Maharaja explains that Krsna is not attracted by one’s brahminical expertise, etiquette, advanced learning, or by one’s austerities, sacrifices, or vows. Nothing can awaken the Lord’s pleasure except the devotee’s unflinching love for Him. Since unflinching love is a characteristic of the purest devotion, *sri-krsnakarsini* is a characteristic of *prema.*

Until we reach *bhava-* or *prema-bhakti,* we must perform *sadhana-bhakti,* in which we steadily practice engaging our senses in the Lord’s service. If we wish to actually engage our senses in the Lord’s service we must remember Him constantly. The *Padma Purana* states:

*smartavyah satatam visnur*  
*vismartavyo na jatucit*  
*sarve vidhi-nisedhah syur*  
*etayor eva kinkarah*

“Krsna is the origin of Lord Visnu. He should always be remembered and never be forgotten at any time. All the rules and prohibitions mentioned in the *sastras* should be the servants of these two principles. ”

To facilitate constant devotional remembrance, Krsna consciousness, the *sastra* lists both prescriptions *(vidhi)* and prohibitions *(nisedhah*). Examples of prohibitions are the regulative principles: no eating meat, no gambling, no illicit sex, and no intoxication. However, following these regulative principles of human life does not awaken *bhakti.* We must also practice the prescriptive activities, because they are conducive for awakening devotion in the heart.

The nine prescriptive regulative principles of *bhakti* are stated in *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (7. 5. 23):

*sravanam kirtanam visnoh*  
*smaranam pada-sevanam*  
*arcanam vandanam dasyam*  
*sakhyam atma-nivedanam*

“Hearing and chanting about the transcendental holy name, form, qualities, paraphernalia and pastimes of Lord Visnu, remembering them, serving the lotus feet of the Lord, offering the Lord respectful worship with sixteen types of paraphernalia, offering prayers to the Lord, becoming His servant, considering the Lord one’s best friend, and surrendering everything unto Him. ”

When we become serious about spiritual life, we make our initiation vows to accept *vidhi* and *nisedhah.* Restricting ourselves from prohibited activities will help us to chant Hare Krsna. This is important, because it is the chanting of the holy name that helps us become Krsna conscious.

Srila Prabhupada explains that the scriptures provide a license for animal slaughter, but the license is given to create a restriction against unnecessary animal slaughter—animal slaughter meant simply for one’s unrestricted sense enjoyment. The license appears in the form of a sacrifice: a person cannot simply slaughter an animal before eating it; he or she must first offer the animal in sacrifice. During the sacrifice the person is supposed to chant a mantra into the animal’s ear, saying, “In the next life, you can kill me as I am now killing you. ” It is assumed that if a person chants this mantra enough times, he or she will come to the realization that it is not in his or her selfinterest to kill animals. Thus the license given in scripture is meant to restrict sense gratification and gradually uplift the practitioner.

Similarly, the regulated activities of *bhakti-yoga* are meant to help us restrict our tendencies for sense gratification. As we practice them, we gradually learn what is favorable for devotional service and what is unfavorable.

Regulated devotional service is meant to eventually lead to spontaneous devotional service. When persons reach the spontaneous platform of *bhakti,* they no longer need to be conscious of all the prohibitions. This does not mean that they are free to engage in sinful activity; rather, persons on the spontaneous platform are no longer inclined to commit sinful activity and therefore spontaneously follow rules and regulations. Their consciousness is always absorbed in thoughts of Krsna and they are always engaged in the nine prescribed activities of *bhakti*—hearing, chanting, remembering, etc. In that sense, these devotees are above the rules and regulations.

If we find that we are not spontaneously engaged in hearing and chanting and the other prescribed devotional activities, we should engage in one or more of them according to regulation. Thus every day without fail we chant Hare Krsna, worship the Deities, and hear or read *Srimad-Bhagavatam.* We actually regulate our lives around these prescribed activities in order to provoke thoughts and remembrances of Krsna, which is our goal.

To think of Krsna is the essence of *sadhana-bhakti,* and to serve the guru by evoking such thoughts is the disciple’s essential duty. Narada Muni mentions remembrance as key to *sadhana-bhakti* in *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (7. 1. 31-32):

My dear King Yudhisthira, the *gopis* by their lusty desires, Kamsa by his fear, Sisupala and other kings by envy, the Yadus by their familial relationship with Krsna, you Pandavas by your great affection for Krsna, and we, the general devotees, by our devotional service, have obtained the mercy of Krsna.

Somehow or other, one must consider the form of Krsna very seriously. Then, by one of the five different processes mentioned above, one can return home, back to Godhead. Atheists like King Vena, however, being unable to think of Krsna’s form in any of these five ways, cannot attain salvation. Therefore, one must somehow think of Krsna, whether in a friendly way or inimically.

Narada Muni further explains that Sisupala and Kamsa became liberated by fixing their minds on Krsna even though they did so out of hatred and fear. Fixing the mind on Krsna is so powerful that it can purify us. Lord Krsna says in the *Bhagavad-gita* (8. 5):

*anta-kale ca mam eva*  
*smaran muktva kalevaram*  
*yah prayati sa mad-bhavam*  
*yati nasty atra samsayah*

“Whoever, at the end of his life, quits his body remembering Me alone at once attains My nature. Of this there is no doubt. ”

The great saint Ambarisa Maharaja, although a responsible king, fixed his mind on Krsna, and similarly anyone who tries to fix his mind in this way will rapidly progress in successfully reviving his original Krsna consciousness.

Because *sadhana-bhakti* is so important, let us study it in more depth. *Sadhana-bhakti* can be divided into two parts, *vaidhi* and *raganuga.* When one practices devotional service according to regulative principles *(vaidhi),* one must follow the regulative principles on the spiritual master’s order or on the strength of the directives of authoritative scripture. There can be no question of refusing the order. One *has* to follow without argument.

In *raganuga-sadhana-bhakti,* however, one has already followed the regulative principles to the point of developing a little attachment to Krsna. From that attachment we execute devotional service out of our natural love. For example, a person engaged in devotional service may be ordered to rise early in the morning and offer *arati.* In the beginning, by the spiritual master’s order, one rises early in the morning and offers *arati.* But over time one develops real attachment both for the service and for

Krsna. One then *wants* to decorate the deity with different dresses and automatically thinks of various plans for how to execute one’s devotional service nicely. Although these plans still fall within the category of practice, the offering of loving service is spontaneous. This is what it means to practice spontaneous devotion in *sadhana-bhakti.*

The difference between *vaidhi* and raganuga *sadhana-bhakti* is the inspiration that drives them. In *vaidhi bhakti* the inspiration comes from the sense of obligation one feels to follow the instructions of the spiritual master and the scripture. That obligation may be based on fear of what will happen if one does not follow, one’s sense of duty, which is usually based on scriptural injunction, or habit. The practice of *raganuga bhakti* however, is inspired by one’s natural attraction to the devotional service itself.

Therefore, it is important for us to develop and then maintain our inspiration to serve the Lord. It helps if we understand first why we are obliged to perform devotional service at all. If we have forgotten why we are making a particular offering, we will not be able to maintain our inspiration, but will gradually begin to think, “I have vowed to perform this activity, but I would really rather be doing something else. ”

This mentality is called *niyamagraha,* or following the rules and regulations without being aware of the purpose behind them. We should be very careful not to follow rules and regulations simply for their own sake, forgetting the purpose behind them. For example, we may chant a particular number of rounds per day thinking that we are “safe” because we are meeting our quota; but if we do not have a sense of what chanting the holy name is meant to be about, we may think it doesn’t matter what else we do while we chant, and thus commit *namaparadha.*

It is all right to feel commitment to our *sadhana* activities, but don’t perform them mechanically. Mechanical *sadhana-bhakti* does not produce *bhava* (pure spiritual emotion).

Adhering to our *sadhana* practices purifies our heart, and gradually allows us to develop a genuine longing to serve the Lord. When *sadhana* is executed with genuine longing, it has the potential to awaken *bhava-bhakti.* Thus if we wish to make tangible advancement, we must not allow our *sadhana to* become mechanical or devoid of spiritual aspiration. We must execute our daily practices with the yearning to achieve Krsna’s service. The *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* (1. 2. 2) states:

krti-sadhya bhavet sadhya-bhava sa sadhanabhidha

nitya-siddhasya bhavasya prakatyam hrdi sadhyata

“When the senses are engaged to attain the ultimate goal of spontaneous devotional surrender, this is known as *sadhana-bhakti.* The pure devotional tendency of the *jiva* is eternal and selfperfected. However, the process by which this latent attitude is awakened is *sadhana,* practical devotional service guided by scriptural injunctions. ”

## The Process for Awakening Bhakti

Only *bhakti* causes *bhakti.* If a Vaisnava is preaching Krsna consciousness to us, it is the *bhakti* in his or her heart that awakens the *bhakti* in ours. The Vaisnava is a receptacle of *bhakti.*

Prabhupada often gave the example of how a cloud does not carry its own water but water from the ocean. Similarly, a Vaisnava does not claim to bestow his or her own mercy but carries Krsna’s mercy. The *bhakti* in the Vaisnava’s heart is what causes *bhakti to* awaken in the hearts of others. The *Narada-bhakti-sutra* (3. 37) states, *loke ’pi bhagavad-guna-sravana-kirtanat:* “One achieves *bhakti* by hearing and chanting about the Supreme Lord’s special qualities, even while engaged in the ordinary activities of life in this world. ”

This verse is relevant to all who aspire to achieve *bhakti,* and especially for those engaged in ordinary mundane activities. In this world there are both *sadhakas* (practitioners) and *Siddhas* (perfected souls). While *Siddhas* do not need to practice *bhakti* since they embody *bhakti,* the *sadhakas* practice *sadhana* in order to achieve their goal, *krsna-prema.* Narada Muni is specifically addressing *sadhakas* in his verse.

*Bhakti* is inherent in every living entity’s heart, but that *bhakti* has to be awoken by practicing *sadhana-bhakti.*

Practice means employing our senses in some particular type of work. Therefore devotional service in practice means utilizing our different sensory organs in service to Krsna. Some of the senses are meant for acquiring knowledge, and some are meant for executing the conclusions of our thinking, feeling and willing. So practice means employing both the mind and the senses in practical devotional service. This practice is not for developing something artificial. *(Nectar of Devotion,* p. 20)

For example, it is not artificial for a young child to practice walking; there is a natural propensity within the child to walk. But in a young child, the *ability* to walk has not yet developed. Therefore, the child practices. Similarly, it is our natural propensity to love God, but until the ability to love God has developed, it is also natural for us to *practice* loving God by performing *sadhana-bhakti.*

When we hear about Krsna with the desire to remember Him, and to become more engaged in His service, a hankering awakens within our hearts for His association and the association of devotees who help us remember Him, Hankering for the association of Krsna and His devotees purifies our heart. It is this kind of hankering that *sadhana-bhakti* is meant to generate. Hankering for Krsna has nothing to do with the senses. Rather, devotional hankering is spiritual—that is, it is experienced by the soul. The mundane senses cannot touch it.

Still, we have contact with the physical body, so to execute prescribed activities and to help us experience a higher taste, we have to know how to dovetail our senses in the Lord’s service. Srila Rupa Gosvami defines *sadhana-bhakti* as devotional service rendered with the senses. Thus a *sadhaka* uses the ears to hear about Krsna, the tongue to chant about Krsna, the eyes to look at Krsna’s deity form, and the nose to smell the flowers and *tulasi* leaves offered to Krsna.

Engaging the mind, body, and senses in *sadhana-bhakti* is meant to awaken our attachment to the Lord, by which we can fully transcend the material body and gradually awaken pure love of God. Such pure love goes completely beyond the physical body when it reaches the stages of *bhava-bhakti* and *prema-bhakti.*

*visaya vinivartante*  
*niraharasya dehinah*  
*rasa-varjam raso ’py asya*  
*param drstva nivartate*

The embodied soul may be restricted from sense enjoyment, though the taste for sense objects remains. But, ceasing such engagements by experiencing a higher taste, he is fixed in consciousness.

What is the higher taste? It is different from the taste we get when our senses engage with their objects. When the senses come in contact with sense objects, they taste something, but it is temporary; the taste has a beginning and end. The taste derived from practicing *bhakti* has no such beginning and end, because *bhakti* is eternal. The mundane senses are unable to taste anything eternal; they are grounded in matter. Thus it is the self and not the material senses that tastes *bhakti.* Hankering to achieve this higher taste is what elevates our higher consciousness.

Sometimes before we can develop a hankering for a higher taste, however, we need a push. When Srila Prabhupada was asked to define *guru-krpa*, he pushed his disciple’s chest with his finger and said, *“This* is *guru-krpa. ”* That push gives us inspiration to aspire for something higher than what we already know. The spiritual master’s mercy is his application of force through his instructions.

Faith and the desire to please the spiritual master are essential ingredients in the practice of *vaidhi-sadhana-bhakti.* Disciples should follow their spiritual master’s instructions not only out of obligation but to please the spiritual master. It is natural to the heart of every living being to want to serve and please someone. Devotees should mold their lives in such a way as to please their spiritual master. The spiritual master repeatedly instructs his disciples to execute regulated *sadhana-bhakti.* We may still have other duties to perform, but we must not neglect our daily *sadhana* practices, and we must perform it with the desire to achieve Krsna’s service. *Sadhana* practiced in this way will create a yearning for Krsna in the heart, and gradually, Bhaktidevi will be attracted to manifest her potency.

**Question: If one has not had previous contact with** *bhakti,* **how can one become attracted to devotional service?**

Attraction to *bhakti* is a circular process: We require attachment to begin devotional service, but we require previous practice in devotional service to achieve attraction. Devotional service begins when we receive the causeless mercy of a devotee. By such mercy we may unknowingly execute devotional service (*ajnata-sukrti*), and thus develop a slight taste or attraction to *bhakti.* Perhaps because of a devotee’s mercy, we listened to a lecture and, without planning it, heard devotional service described. Or perhaps we heard the holy name from a Vaisnava’s lips. Even walking by a *harinama* party and hearing the Lord’s holy name gives *ajnata-sukrti.* One requires no qualification for performing *ajnata-sukrti.*

Then by that *sukrti,* we associate with a Vaisnava, and by our own free will begin to execute *bhajana, or* devotional service. That is, we are no longer accidentally contacting *bhakti;* we are approaching devotional service voluntarily by our own attraction. This attraction grows as we engage ourselves more and more in devotional service. Gradually we qualify ourselves to make a serious commitment to *sadhana-bhakti.*

Srila Rupa Gosvami gives the example that in order for a seed to grow, it has to have contact with heat and light. Similarly, the seed of *bhakti* has to have contact with *suddha-sattva* (pure goodness). *Suddha-sattva* consists of the Lord’s *samvit* and hladini potencies. The *samvit* potency enables us to understand or know Krsna, and when it mixes with His *hladini-sakti*—His internal pleasure potency—in the heart, the heart becomes clean and soft.

*suddha-sattva-visesatma*  
*prema-suryamsu-samya-bhak*  
*rucibhis citta-masrnya-*  
*krd asau bhava ucyate*

“When devotional service is executed on the transcendental platform of pure goodness, it is like a sun-ray of love for Krsna. At such a time, devotional service causes the heart to be softened by various tastes, and one is then situated in *bhava* \[emotion\]. ” (Brs. 1. 3. 1)

This *suddha-sattva-visesatma* is the primary characteristic (svarupa-laksana) of this stage of *bhava-bhakti.* It is like a ray of sunlight of love for Krsna. When the heart becomes soft and these emotions are manifest in our heart, this is described as the threshold of *prema,* love of God.

**Question: What is the best impetus to help us begin practicing devotional service?**

The best impetus is to hear from a devotee.

*nitya-siddha krsna-prema ‘sadhya’ kabhu naya*  
*sravanadi-suddha-citte karaye udaya*

“Pure love for Krsna is eternally established in the hearts of the living entities. It is not something to be gained from another source. When the heart is purified by hearing and chanting, this love naturally awakens. ” (Cc. Madhya 22. 107)

Pure love for Krsna is eternally situated in every living being’s heart and does not come from any other source. When the living entity hears about Krsna from the right source, that dormant love is awakened. There is no more powerful method than hearing from a devotee about Krsna. Simply hearing the holy name from a Vaisnava can awaken some desire to chant it.

When the desire to chant the holy name is awakened, gradually the commitment to execute *sadhana-bhakti* is also awakened. But it can take many lifetimes of association with devotees before one develops the commitment to practice *sadhana-bhakti* oneself. But as Srila Prabhupada explains in *The Nectar of Devotion,* even if one has had no previous contact with *bhakti,* but somehow has some taste for hearing *Bhagavad-gita* and *Srimad-Bhagavatam* in the association of devotees, then one’s devotional service can begin. And for such persons, devotional service is easier to take up than for those who are accustomed to mental speculation and argumentation.

According to the learned scholars’ general opinion, a person may become governed by certain convictions derived by his own arguments and decisions. Then another person, who may be a greater logician, will nullify these conclusions and establish another thesis. In this way the path of argument will never be safe or conclusive. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* recommends, therefore, that one follow in the footsteps of the authorities. *(Nectar of Devotion,* p. 19-20)

A person may be known as a great sage, but his arguments on the meaning of scripture may differ from another great sage’s arguments. Thus neither “great sage’s” arguments are conclusive. The conclusive truths of scripture can only be understood when they are revealed by the Lord’s self-realized devotees. Therefore we should accept whatever path the *mahajanas* have revealed us to follow. The *mahajanas* are authorities in devotional service who are well-established in Krsna conscious realization and whom the Supreme Lord has empowered to present sastric truth.

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* explains this point in relation to Srila Rupa Gosvami: Just as Lord Krsna entered Lord Brahma’s heart and spoke the conclusive truth about Krsna’s transcendental position, so Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu entered Srila Rupa Gosvami’s heart and made him a fully realized soul whose every action was consistent with the conclusive truth taught by the scriptures and the previous *acaryas.* When the Lord empowers a devotee, that devotee both directly experiences the truth of *sastra* and has the potency to present it to others. Krsna says, *vedanta-krd veda-vid eva caham:* “By all the *Vedas,* I am to be known. Indeed, I am the compiler of *Vedanta,* and I am the knower of the *Vedas. " (Bg.* 15. 15)

This does not mean that devotees reject logic and argument, however. Certainly we have to use logic and argument to come to the point of accepting the statements of scripture and the spiritual master as conclusive. In the end, however, all logic and argument has to be transcended, because neither engender pure devotion. Faith is the only real basis of devotion. Therefore the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* explains that Sri Krsna, the son of Mother Yasoda, is easily accessible to those who engage in spontaneous— fully faithful—devotional service to Him. Srila Rupa Gosvami therefore says that logic and argument, while important, do not form the safest path to *bhakti;* rather, like the *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* he recommends that we follow in the footsteps of the previous *acaryas.*

It is the duty of the *acarya* to somehow get his disciples to fix their minds on Krsna. The rules and regulations can come later. Srila Prabhupada set the example of a guru trying to fix his disciples’ minds on Krsna. Even at the beginning, when Prabhupada started the Krsna consciousness movement, he attracted people by speaking about Krsna. It was quite some time before he introduced a new idea: “Anyone who lives in the *asrama* must follow certain rules and regulations. ” By the time Prabhupada introduced the rules and regulations, we were already so attracted to Krsna that we could not refuse to follow. When the mind is attracted to Krsna, we will naturally want to follow whatever regulations help us make progress toward Him.

The beginnings of devotional service are difficult to practice. Ordinary people cannot understand *sadhana-bhakti.* As *Bhagavad-gita* states, out of many thousands of persons trying to achieve the perfection of human life, only a few will become perfect and realize the self. Out of many thousands of such self-realized persons, only one will be able to understand Krsna. Srila Prabhupada gave us the method to elevate us to this rare understanding: If we simply follow in the footsteps of Lord Caitanya *(gaurangera duti pada)* and the previous *acaryas*—the process of chanting Hare Krsna—we can easily understand devotional service.

Lord Caitanya appeared in this world and enacted pastimes to make devotional service accessible to the people in the fallen age of Kali. The Bhagavatam (1. 1. 10) states:

*prayenalpayusah sabhya*  
*kalav asmin yuge janah*  
*mandah sumanda-matayo*  
*manda-bhagya hy upadrutah*

“O learned one, in this iron age of Kali men have but short lives. They are quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky and, above all, always disturbed. ”

In Kali-yuga, any type of provocation causes disturbance and fighting because people’s minds are always so disturbed. Lord Caitanya appeared and presented a medicine for the mind, the chanting of the *maha-mantra.* This medicine fills the mind with joy. Srila Prabhupada promoted the chanting of Hare Krsna as a way to deliver the mind and make it peaceful. He saw that people were suffering. Although they had so much money and power, they could not purchase peace. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states that only a devotee of the Lord can be genuinely peaceful. Caitanya Mahaprabhu gave a process to bring us to a real state of peace and to satisfy all our desires in such a way that we would never need anything else. Ultimately, Lord Caitanya came to deliver Krsna consciousness, pure love of God.

Those experiencing pure love of God have not had their ability to desire destroyed; desire is the soul’s eternal companion. But instead of desiring sense gratification, pure souls repose their desire on an appropriate object of affection. Srila Prabhupada often said that everyone is looking for Krsna. If we are looking for wealth, then we should seek out Krsna, because Krsna has the greatest wealth. If we are looking for beauty, we should seek out Krsna, because Krsna is the most beautiful. If we are looking for power, then we will find no one more powerful than Krsna. If we are looking to become famous, then who could be more famous than God? If we are looking for intelligence, then we should know that Krsna is the most intelligent. He created this entire cosmic manifestation with only a fraction of His power and without having to do anything Himself. Now everything acts according to His will.

**Question: Are the Vedic injunctions relevant to today?**

The perfection of human life is to achieve one’s eternal relationship with the Supreme, and the Vedic injunctions aim at bringing us to that perfection. The scriptural injunctions are eternally true— without exception. “Eternally” means that they are applicable for all time. It is not that there are some injunctions that were meant for an ancient society but that do not apply to a modern one.

Krsna explains in the second chapter of *Bhagavad-gita* that men of small knowledge are attracted to the flowery words of the *Vedas,* which recommend various types of sacrifices by which one can achieve elevation to the heavenly planets, resultant good birth, or material opulence. Krsna says: “Being attached to sense gratification and opulent life, they think that there is nothing more than this. ”

This means that the *Vedas* give injunctions for temporary benefits. If the living entities are attached to fruitive results, then they can perform certain types of sacrifices by which they can achieve those results. But all the boons they receive will be temporary. Therefore, from the beginning of His instructions to Arjuna, Lord Krsna establishes the difference between the temporary and the eternal. What exists, He says, is what is eternal, and for the eternal there is no cessation; the temporary will cease to be. A learned person must learn to distinguish between the eternal and the temporary.

The *Bhagavad-gita* provides us with an example. Krsna tells Arjuna, “While speaking learned words, you are lamenting for what is not worthy of grief. Those who are wise lament neither for the living nor the dead. As the embodied soul continually passes in this body from boyhood to youth to old age, the soul similarly passes into another body at death. The self-realized are not bewildered by such a change. ” Krsna tells Arjuna that lamenting that his relatives will die is foolish because their bodies are temporary and bound to death. The soul will not die. Everything material in this world is temporary, but the soul is eternal.

Krsna explains later in *Bhagavad-gita* that those who act according to their whims and do not accept scriptural injunctions achieve neither happiness nor perfection nor the supreme destination. The eternal scriptural injunctions are meant for the eternal welfare of all beings. Although certain laws or injunctions give temporary results, their ultimate purpose is to bring us to eternality. Once we become conversant with and understand God’s laws, and then act in accordance with them, we will become happy both in this life and the next. The scriptural injunctions are religious principles; they are designed by the Supreme Lord Himself. No one can manufacture some other system of religion to replace the religion given by God.

Srila Prabhupada explains that no one can sit in their homes with friends and family and say, “I will create a law today. ” Just because they may think up a law does not guarantee that it will be applied. The government creates laws, and the government has the means to enforce those laws. Neither can we manufacture a religious system, thinking, “I will serve God only in this particular way. ” Krsna tells Arjuna what he must do: surrender and fight. Our duty is simply to learn God’s laws and injunctions and then follow them.

Nowadays, it is common to manufacture a system of religion. Sometimes people with fertile minds examine all these types of religion and take a little of this and a little of that to create their own system. By their selection, they are trying to create a system of religion. But religion cannot be selected; it is the law of God. He has created eternal religious principles, principles not transformed by time, self-evident principles that are not subject to our own interpretation. The real business of human life is to know these laws of God and how to apply them.

Krsna gives *the* eternal religious principle in his concluding instructions in *Bhagavad-gita* (18. 66):

*sarva-dharman parityajya*  
*mam ekam saranam vraja*  
*aham tvam sarva-papebhyo*  
*moksayisyami ma sucah*

“Abandon all varieties of religion and just surrender unto Me. I shall deliver you from all sinful reactions. Do not fear. ”

Only Krsna can deliver us from all the reactions of sinful life, because it is He who has defined sin and purity. The universe may be full of administrators with their various responsibilities in the matter of creation, but they are never independent of Krsna. Everything in the creation rests on Krsna’s energy. Regardless of how powerful or independent the demigods or anyone else appears, no one is independent of the Lord.

To understand our dependence, Srila Prabhupada gives the example of a prisonhouse. Sometimes prisoners spend many years in prison and rise to positions of responsibility. Some may even work in the prison office or in the supplies department. Some may even be allowed to work outside the building. Each of these responsibilities gives them a greater independence than those who are confined to their cells. These more independent prisoners are compared to the demigods. The demigods are devotees of the Lord who, having executed piety, are given some responsibility for universal affairs. Perhaps they manage the sun and moon, the weather, or the material elements. If they think they are independent of Krsna, the warden, however, they are mistaken.

Responsible prisoners may have keys to the rooms where they work. If a prisoner confined to a cell stops one of these men and asks, “I heard you have some keys? ”

“Yes, I have keys. ”

“Well, use those keys and get me out of here. Do you have the key to the front door? ”

“No, I have keys to the supply closet. Do you want some soap? ” Like the warden, the demigods do not have the power to let us out of this material prison-world. They do not have keys to the front door but only to the supply closet. It is the Supreme Lord who holds the key to our escape. If we want to be liberated from all our past sinful reactions, Krsna says we will have to surrender to Him.

Krsna tells us how to find our way to the front door—and to freedom from the stringent laws of karma—in the scriptural injunctions He gives. Srila Prabhupada writes in his purport to *Bhagavad-gita* 3. 31:

In the beginning of Krsna consciousness, one may not fully discharge the injunctions of the Lord. But because one is not resentful of this principle, and works sincerely, without consideration of defeat and hopelessness, he will surely be promoted to the stage of pure Krsna consciousness.

Krsna says that He is the one who gives all punishments, but no one can punish Him. Some people have tried, but no one has been successful. For example, Hiranyakasipu tried to defeat Lord Nrsimhadeva, but he was obviously not successful. Hiranyaksa also tried to defeat Visnu in the form of Varaha, but he too was not successful. Paundraka and Kamsa tried but were killed by the Lord. Krsna can only be “punished” by His pure devotee. Anyone who is envious of Krsna will be punished *by* Krsna; those who are envious of Krsna and who do not follow His instructions are punished. Only those who have faith in Krsna can achieve perfection and remember Krsna at the time of death.

King Kulasekhara prays, “My Lord Krsna, I pray that the swan of my mind may immediately sink down to the stems of the lotus feet of Your Lordship and be locked in their network; otherwise at the time of my final breath, when my throat is choked up with cough, how will it be possible to think of You? ” (*Mukunda-mala-stotra,* 33)

Everything we have done in this life will be tested at the time of death. We have to prepare for that test in this life. We may not accept God in this life, but as Srila Prabhupada explains, everybody accepts God at the time of death. They may not surrender, but they are forced to accept Him. If they will not offer obeisances in life, they will have to offer obeisances at the time of death.

**Question: How do we become more focused in our devotional service?**

We must always remember Krsna and never forget Him. To remember Krsna is the goal of all our regulated activities in devotional service. But Krsna says, “If you can’t fix your mind on Me, then follow the regulative principles of *bhakti-yoga.* In this way, you can cultivate a desire to attain Me. ” To be focused on our service means to see Krsna present in the work. Execute your duties in proper consciousness, thinking, “Krsna has given me this duty. ” If we do not see Krsna present in our duty, how can we remember Him, think of Him, and depend on Him?

But if we are not able to see Krsna in our service, then we have to associate with those who can help us see Him. Otherwise, our service becomes mechanical. Our duties are meant to increase our attraction for hearing about Krsna’s activities and pastimes. If we try to remember Krsna while carrying them out, we will gradually become more focused.

There are higher stages of becoming more focused on Krsna. Eventually, we will come to the stage of *mantra-updsana,* whereby chanting a mantra or verse from the scripture will remind us of a particular pastime. We will then focus our mind on it and Krsna and His devotees will become fixed in our mind and heart.

In *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* Srila Rupa Gosvami calls this meditation *rupa-dhyanam*, which means meditating on the Lord’s form. It is also possible to meditate *(dhyanam)* on particular aspects of service to the Lord.

An example of such meditation is the South Indian *brahmana* who was so poor that he had nothing to offer His deity. He therefore decided to render his service by meditation. He first meditated on the Lord’s form, which manifest in his mind and heart. Then he meditated on serving that form in his mind.

First he decided to bathe Him. In his meditation, he traveled to all the sacred rivers and places of pilgrimage and gathered all the holy waters from them. He could have simply chanted mantras on his *brahmana* thread to summon those waters, but he preferred to go personally to each place. After he had gathered all the waters, he bathed the Lord.

He then gathered valuable jewels and ornaments and used them to decorate the Lord. Then he prepared sweet rice for the offering. In his meditation he checked the temperature of the sweet rice with his finger; he did not want to offer it to the Lord while it was still too hot. As he tested the sweet rice, he burned his finger. The burn was so painful that it woke him from his meditation. The *brahmana* was shocked to see that his finger was actually burned. We understand from this pastime how powerful full mental absorption in the Lord’s service is. The Lord was watching this *brahmana* from His place in Vaikuntha and sent one of His messengers to bring the *brahmana* back to the spiritual world to engage in His eternal service.

We are not capable of such intense absorption at our present stage of *bhakti.* Still, even in the beginning stages we must practice engaging the mind. Arjuna said that the mind is so restless that trying to control it is like trying to control the wind. Krsna responded, “For one who has controlled the mind, the mind becomes the best friend, but for one who has failed to control it, the mind is the worst enemy. ” *Bhagavad-gita* also states that for one who has conquered the mind, the Supersoul is already reached and the devotee achieves tranquility. For such a person, all dualities—heat and cold, happiness and distress—are the same.

Somehow or other we need to fix our mind on favorably serving Krsna. This is the goal of our preaching, our deity worship, and our chanting. It is this favorable absorption in devotion that pleases Krsna most.

We can struggle alone with material desires millennium after millennium and be defeated and disappointed birth after birth. We cannot overcome material desires by our own strength. It is essential that we develop the intense desire to somehow or other please Krsna by our activities, and that will require that we focus the mind in devotion.

**Question: How can we progress from** *sadhana-bhakti* **to** *raganuga-bhakti?*

*Sadhana-bhakti* does not exclude *raganuga-bhakti.* It does not require lifetimes of commitment to *vaidhi-sadhana-bhakti* to come to the platform of *raganuga-sadhana-bhakti.* It sometimes takes lifetimes of association with devotees to develop a strong commitment to *sadhana-bhakti,* but once we have committed ourselves, we may come to the platform of spontaneous attraction to devotional service in one lifetime. To practice *raganuga-bhakti* requires commitment and taste. If we want to awaken taste, we must associate with devotees who already experience it. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (1. 2. 16) tells us exactly how to associate with these devotees:

*susrusoh sraddadhanasya*  
*vasudeva-katha-rucih*  
*syan mahat-sevaya viprah*  
*punya-tirtha-nisevanat*

“By serving those devotees who are completely freed from all vice, great service is done. By such service, one gains affinity for hearing the messages of Vasudeva. ”

How can we serve such devotees? We can begin by hearing from them. Our qualification for advancement in *sadhana-bhakti* depends on the strength of our taste or attraction to Krsna. The more pronounced our taste, the greater our qualification. If we want to develop taste, then we must show Krsna that we want it. It is not improper to want a taste for spiritual life. But our service cannot become conditional upon whether or not we receive it. The whole purpose of service is to awaken taste.

Service should be rendered with yearning. If we do not yet yearn for Krsna with our whole heart, we should perform our service regretting that we do not yet have *bhakti.* That feeling will bring us to taste.

**Question: Can we become deserving to develop pure love by our own endeavor, or will pure love eventually be granted to us?**

Endeavor is the first stage of love. Even though our love in that first stage is obviously not pure, endeavor is an expression of affection. In the first stage of devotional service, devotional service in practice, the living entity accepts a spiritual master and endeavors to follow his instructions and the statements of scripture. As long as one’s love for Krsna remains dormant in the heart, we must first work from a platform of obligation. Srila Prabhupada said, “First there must be obedience. Without obedience there can never be love. ” We should begin our endeavors out of obedience. When Krsna sees us making such endeavors, He will bestow love on us through the mercy of His devotees. Those who are obedient to the guru will receive help. As soon as we think we are making advancement on our own, we will lose our qualification to receive pure love.

## Enthusiasm for Devotional Service

In Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura speaks on the subject of apathy, indifference, which he says is the tendency toward impersonalism. If we do not have strong faith in the holy name and in hearing of the Lord’s pastimes, and simultaneously have no attraction for fruitive work, being somewhat disgusted with the material world, we become indifferent or apathetic and therefore inert. Having no impetus to work we think, “Why should I do anything if everything I do only leads to suffering? ” Seeing only negativity and suffering on one side, and having no faith in the positive, the Lord and His pastimes on the other, we may be left with no reason to live. Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, “What can remain in a person’s heart except indifference toward everything? ”

*Bhaktyaloka* is a commentary on the first three verses in Srila Rupa Gosvami’s *Upadesamrta,* and therefore Bhaktivinoda Thakura also discusses *utsaha,* enthusiasm. He writes, “Without enthusiasm, it is very difficult to have faith in the Lord and to execute devotional service. ”

Sometimes we mistake passion for enthusiasm, thinking that it means we must always keep ourselves busy. But if the mind is not fixed on Krsna in our business, then our activities are simply in the mode of passion, which will lead to suffering. Activities performed in the mode of passion do not satisfy us, nor do they increase our faith in the Lord and His holy name. If we want to actually become enthusiastic, we must understand the purpose behind everything we do; if we are consciously offering our activity to Krsna and we remain steady in the execution of our duties, we will become enthusiastic in whatever we are doing.

Devotees are often anxious about how to balance either their temple duties as book distributor, temple president, pujari, cook, etc., with their *sadhana;* or their work for maintaining their body with their *sadhana.* Srila Prabhupada explains in the Bhagavatam that for those who are not liberated, there are two kinds of activities that must be performed—those aimed at maintaining the body and those aimed at achieving liberation. We should neglect neither. If we imitate the liberated souls and neglect the activities that maintain the body, we will fall down; false renunciation does not lead upward. Therefore we should not try to imitate liberated souls but follow in their footsteps. That means that we do have to practice both *sadhana* as well as duties connected to this world, until we are at least on the liberated platform.

This will be impossible without *utsaha* (enthusiasm). If we have no enthusiasm for our work, we will not do it. If we have no enthusiasm for our *sadhana,* we will become negligent in our worship. Negligence is born out of inactivity (laziness), apathy, or indifference. *Utsaha* conquers all three.

Lazy people like to enjoy the results of their duties by performing the least possible work. Bhaktivinoda Thakura says that such inertia is “against the nature of spirit. ” Krsna explains in *Bhagavad-gita* that all living entities have to be active; no being can be inactive even for a moment. As long as a living entity has desire in the heart, activity will follow. All living entities have desire, because desire is the soul’s eternal companion and can never be destroyed.

If we allow inertia to remain in our body or heart, how can we perform devotional service? Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes that apathy develops when we are careless in our devotion. We should therefore practice devotional service without deviating from the guru’s instructions.

Perhaps we think that there is no hurry for us to become pure in devotion. We can wait until some time in the future to become purified. Prahlada Maharaja addressed this point when talking to his classmates. He explained that one’s entire childhood is wasted in frivolous play. Fifty percent of one’s life is automatically lost during sleep. Then so much time is lost in one’s later years because one’s body is old and decrepit and cannot function properly. Putting everything in perspective, Prahlada Maharaja said that if we look at our lives and see how much time we have available to do something meaningful, we’ll see that our lives are very short. It’s better, he said, to engage in Krsna consciousness while we are young and have the energy for it. We should take to this process of hearing and chanting the Lord’s glories as soon as possible.

Srila Prabhupada often said that we can take to spiritual life at any point in life—as soon as we realize the urgency. We must realize the urgency! Because if we’re not careful, our lives will pass, and because we did not properly prepare ourselves for death, we will have to come back to this world.

Neither is it enough to think that because we are devotees, we can wait for Krsna’s mercy while we do nothing to achieve it. Srila Prabhupada warned, “One has to be careful. Don’t make your spiritual master come back and get you. Don’t simply think, ‘Because I am a devotee, at the end of this life I’ll go back to Godhead. ’”

If we want to remember Krsna at the time of death, we will have to practice enthusiastic Krsna consciousness now. Enthusiasm means we are always aspiring to advance in spiritual life. Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that spiritual advancement means first becoming properly situated in strictly following the rules and regulations according to our position, then knowing where we want to go and how to get there. But be careful not to define spiritual advancement by some material benchmark. Know the details of the path. Krsna says in *Bhagavad-gita* (6. 23):

*tam vidyad duhkha-samyoga-*  
*viyogam yoga-samjnitam*  
*sa niscayena yoktavyo*  
*yogo ’nirvinna-cetasa*

“One should engage oneself in the practice of yoga with determination and faith and not be deviated from the path. ”

Further, Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes:

In his commentary on this verse, Srila Baladeva Vidyabhusana Mahasaya has said: *atmanya yogyatva-mananam nirvedas tad* *rahitena cetasa,* “One becomes indifferent to whatever work he feels unqualified for. ” Devotional service should be performed in this mentality. (Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* p. 50)

We should not misunderstand this, thinking that if we feel unqualified for a duty assigned to us, we should become indifferent to it. Indifferent means we don’t care for it. Instead, we should become indifferent toward *karma, jnana,* and any *sadhana* practice that does not lead to an increase in our Krsna consciousness. We should be indifferent to sense gratification and should faithfully and rigidly execute the principles of *sadhana-bhakti.* This is what distinguishes a *bhakta* from a *karmi* or *jnani:* a *bhakta* has faith in the Lord’s pastimes, words, and holy name.

Enthusiasm requires faith. In the *Caitanya-caritamrta,* Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami defines faith as “the confident, firm faith that by rendering devotional service to Krsna, one automatically fulfills all subsidiary activities. Such faith is essential and favorable for the execution of *bhakti or* devotional service. ” (Cc. *Madhya 22. 62)*

Bhaktivinoda Thakura quotes *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (11. 20. 7-8) to describe how to act on the understanding one already possesses:

*nirvinnanam jnana-yogo*  
*nyasinam iha karmasu*  
*tesv anirvinna-cittanam*  
*karma-yogas tu kaminam*

*yadrcchaya mat-kathadau*  
*jata-sraddhas tu yah puman*  
*na nirvinno nati-sakto*  
*bhakti-yogo ’sya siddhi-dah*

“Among these three paths, *jnana-yoga,* the path of philosophical speculation, is recommended for those who are disgusted with material life and are thus detached from ordinary, fruitive activities. Those who are not disgusted with material life, having many desires yet to fulfill, should seek perfection through the path of *karma-yoga. If* somehow or other by good fortune one develops faith in hearing and chanting My glories, such a person, being neither very disgusted with nor attached to material life, should achieve perfection through the path of loving devotion to Me. ”

Ultimately, we want to develop faith in hearing and chanting Krsna’s glories and thus take to the path of pure devotion. Practicing pure devotion in faith gives pleasure to the Lord. Mukunda, thinking of himself as devoid of devotion, once asked Lord Caitanya, “Why is it that when I see Your face I feel so much pleasure? ” Lord Caitanya replied, “Those who have no faith in the Lord cause pain to My heart. And therefore because I feel pain, they cannot feel happiness. ” That Mukunda felt such pleasure to see the Lord’s face indicates that he did have faith in Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. The Lord wants to give pleasure to all living entities, but when the *jnanis or* impersonalists say, “The Supreme is not a person, ” the Lord finds their statement painful.

*Jnanis* always want to be independent from Krsna. Therefore they endeavor on their own to achieve the results of their activities. Devotees, however, always depend on the Lord for His mercy in anything they do. Krsna instructs us in *Bhagavad-gita* (18. 57-58):

In all activities just depend upon Me and work always under My protection. In such devotional service, be fully conscious of Me. If you become conscious of Me, you will pass over all the obstacles of conditioned life by My grace. If, however, you do not work in such consciousness but act through false ego, not hearing Me, you will be lost.

Devotees always base their enthusiasm not on independent endeavor but on faithful dependence on the Lord. Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes further on enthusiasm:

We should not lose enthusiasm because we have no strength to destroy the reactions of karma. In the very beginning of devotional service the *sadhaka* must have faith with full enthusiasm. (Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* p. 52)

**Question: Is there a difference between how people were attracted to Krsna consciousness during Sri Caitanya’s time and how they have been attracted today?**

Prabhodananda Sarasvati wrote that no one distributed *krsna-prema* the way Lord Caitanya distributed it, and no one has distributed it in the same way since. Thus it is clear that the distribution of pure love of Godhead that took place during Lord Caitanya’s presence was exceptional. Tigers embraced deer, and both chanted the holy name. When Lord Caitanya was in the water, all the wild elephants charged in and Lord Caitanya splashed them with water. The wild elephants got up and started chanting, “Krsna, Krsna! ” Srila Prabhupada told us that we are not so powerful that we can make wild animals chant and dance in ecstatic love of Godhead, but at least we should try to attract the human beings.

But although the distribution of love of God was exceptional distribution during Lord Caitanya’s presence, devotees since then who have truly relished Krsna consciousness in one another’s association has been very attractive to the conditioned souls. When we see genuine Vaisnava exchanges between devotees, we witness real displays of Vaisnava qualities like humility, tolerance, and patience. This is attractive. Such exchanges were, of course, also seen during Lord Caitanya’s time.

Speaking about Vaisnava etiquette, there are many examples of how Lord Caitanya bestowed His mercy on the Vaisnavas and how other Vaisnavas became happy to see it. When we see Vaisnava exchanges that are free from envy and pride, we see the Vaisnavas’ attraction to hearing and chanting about Krsna. All of this allows us to develop faith and attracts us to take part.

## Becoming Free from Sin

Sin has three stages: *avidya* (ignorance), the root cause of suffering; *bijam* (seed), the seed desire to commit sin; and *papam* (manifest sin), the sinful activity itself.

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura has divided the source of ignorance into five. The first is *avidya,* which means to accept what is impure as pure, what is impermanent as permanent, what is full of misery as full of bliss, and what is not the self as the self. The second is *asmita,* false ego. False ego is the attachment to “I” and “mine, ” and to think that what one perceives with one’s senses is reality. The third is *raga,* the desire to experience happiness and to avoid unhappiness. The fourth is *dvesa,* hatred, which refers to aversion for unhappiness and whatever leads to unhappiness. The fifth is *abhinivesa,* fear of death. When one is afraid of death, one clings to the conception that “sense enjoyment will give me happiness” and fears that death will take this happiness away. All of these sources of ignorance lead to *bijam,* the desire to commit sin.

There are also different types of reactions to sinful activity, namely, *prarabdha-karma* and *aprarabdha-karma. Prarabdha-karma* refers to manifest reactions (i. e., the physical and mental suffering we are experiencing now, manifest in our current physical and subtle bodies). *Aprarabdha-karma* is not yet manifest. In due course, all *aprarabdha* reactions become manifest (*prarabdha).*

Within the realm of *aprarabdha* (unmanifest) reactions is *kutam,* the continued desire to commit a sinful activity one has previously committed. Because this desire will drive us to again commit the sin *(papam),* we incur more unmanifest reactions that will cause us suffering in the future.

Devotional service, however, uproots the seeds of sinful desire. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (6. 1. 15) states:

*kecit kevalaya bhaktya*  
*vasudeva-parayanah*  
*agham dhunvanti kartsnyena*  
*niharam iva bhaskarah*

“Only a rare person who has adopted complete, unalloyed devotional service to Krsna can uproot the weeds of sinful actions with no possibility that they will revive. He can do this simply by discharging devotional service, just as the sun can immediately dissipate fog by its rays. ”

It is also stated in the *Bhagavatam’s* Sixth Canto that devotional service, beginning with chanting the Lord’s holy name, is the ultimate religious principle for the human being. Thus if we wish to uproot the seeds of sinful desire with no possibility of reviving them, we must engage in devotional service, beginning with chanting Krsna’s holy name. It is important for a devotee to remove the causes of sin and ignorance if he or she is to make further progress on the spiritual path.

As long as a devotee is dwelling on the intermediate stage between sinful life and pure life, he will keep a certain relationship to his past sins. Some neophytes on the spiritual path vividly remember their past sinful life. They reside in the gray area of not yet being fully determined to accept everything favorable for their spiritual advancement. Their actions may already be spiritualized, but their minds are still attached to previous habits. Thus it may occur that they fall back to them. However, as long as they do not commit further sins in this intermediate period, the effect of past sins will soon vanish. And the more they are absorbed in chanting the holy name, the sooner these traces will completely fade away.

*(Nectarean Ocean of the Holy Name,* Sacinandana Swami, p. 71)

This is an elaboration on *Bhagavad-gita* (9. 30), which states:

*api cet su-duracaro*  
*bhajate mam ananya-bhak*  
*sadhur eva sa mantavyah*  
*samyag vyavasito hi sah*

“Even if one commits the most abominable action, if he is engaged in devotional service he is to be considered saintly because he is properly situated in his determination. ”

In his purport to this verse, Srila Prabhupada explains that we may still have a connection with our past sinful activities due to our long association with the material energy. Because of this, even though we are determinedly engaged in devotional service, we may slip and fall, deviate, or commit a sinful act. Still, if we are rightly situated in our determination to worship Krsna purely, we will sincerely repent that sin and endeavor not to commit it again.

Of course, part of our repentance will be our willingness to accept the reaction for the sin. Actually, it is this willingness to accept the reaction that distinguishes a sincere devotee from an insincere devotee: a sincere devotee will see the reaction as both deserved and a help to remove the sinful desire from the heart; an insincere person will actually want to commit the sin again but will not want to suffer the reactions.

We have to associate with those who help us overcome these desires and avoid those who encourage us to fulfill them. Sometimes when devotees are weak, overcome by material desire, they associate with someone similarly weak, someone with the same material propensities. Then they simply encourage one another to engage in sense gratification. Instead, we should seek the association of devotees who can help us overcome our material desires. It is actually an item of sincerity to seek out proper instruction so that we can avoid committing sinful activities.

In *Caitanya-caritamrta (Antya* 1. 2), Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami says about himself, “My path is very difficult. I am blind, and my feet are slipping again and again. Therefore, may the saints help me by granting me the stick of their mercy as my support. ” If our feet are slipping on the path, we need extra support. We have to take shelter of devotees who can give us that support in the form of their merciful instructions. With extra support there is less of a tendency to slip on the path.

What if the instruction is too heavy for us to carry? But if we throw it away, we will slip on the path without it. It is therefore essential to seek out the association of devotees who can help us and thus protect us from falldown.

The best means to become free of sinful propensities is sincerely to take shelter of the holy name with a pure motivation. The holy name is eternally situated in pure goodness. Only very fortunate souls can take shelter of Krsna’s holy name. The process of chanting removes quickly the *anarthas* in the heart, and causes *hrdaya-daurbalyam,* or weakness of heart, to disappear.

*(Nectarean Ocean of the Holy Name,* Sacinandana Swami, p. 73)

**Question: Can a pure devotee fall down?**

One who is under the protection of the Lord’s internal potency cannot fall down. Those whose hearts are completely pure will not fall down. But even at *bhava-bhakti* there are traces of *anarthas,* which can lead to falldown. *Anarthas* are not *completely* destroyed until *prema.* Bilvamangala Thakura had reached *bhava-bhakti* in his previous life, but he temporarily fell from that platform.

**Question: What should we do when we see devotees fighting with one another?**

In *Caitanya-bhagavata* Srila Vrndavana dasa Thakura says that when you see two Vaisnavas fighting you should know that it is the Lord’s pastime. Therefore it is offensive to take sides. But Vrndavana dasa Thakura is talking about elevated Vaisnavas, not *aparadhis.* If you see two advanced Vaisnavas seemingly opposed to one another, consider it *lila;* don’t become an *aparadhi* by taking one side over another. And if both are *aparadhis* and are offending one another by their behavior, then it is still best not to get involved.

However, we have to be careful not to judge that something is an offense when it is actually not. Prabhupada said that we must speak the truth, calling a spade a spade. The truth is sometimes unpalatable, but we should not refrain from speaking it. But if you see that a person is actually being offensive in their behavior, then “taking sides” may mean that at least you try to get the dispute resolved by someone who can help. Simply taking sides and even starting a defensive movement is not the way to resolve problems. We do not solve problems by numbers or by creating a *vox populi.* Rather, we should resolve problems from the spiritual platform. That is, we should seek Krsna conscious solutions. Therefore we should look for people who have the most Krsna conscious influence on all parties and place the dispute in their hands.

## Spiritual Strength in Executing Sadhana-bhakti

We all require nourishment in spiritual life, and if we do not get it, we become weak. That weakness makes us vulnerable to the influence of the external energy. It takes spiritual strength to fight *maya,* because *maya* is constantly waging war against each and every one of us.

We have to take nourishment in whatever form we can assimilate, but in its most generalized sense, we are nourished by *sadhu-sanga,* which affords us the opportunity to hear *hari-katha,* descriptions of the Supreme Lord’s glories. Vaisnava association will also allow us the opportunity to hear and chant about the glories of the holy name, and to take part in *kirtana.*

Srila Prabhupada explained that one of the purposes of the Mayapur festival we hold each year is so that the devotees can come together, recharge their spiritual batteries, and get strength to preach around the world. Prabhupada was always concerned about the devotees, because he was so much pushing them to preach and spread Krsna consciousness. He knew that his disciples faced *maya* continuously in their efforts to push back Kali’s influence. However, Kali’s influence is not only present externally, it is present internally, in our own hearts. Certainly, we go out to preach Krsna consciousness because we can see Kali-yuga manifesting everywhere, but if we really want to conquer Kali, we can also look into our own hearts and see how attracted we are to *maya.* To the degree that we suffer from such attraction, it will be that much more difficult to fight *maya.* We require strength. The *Mundaka Upanisad* (3. 2. 4) states:

*nayam atma bala-hinena labhyo*  
*na ca pramadat tapaso vapy alingat*  
*etair upayair yatate yas tu vidvams*  
*tasyaisa atma visate brahma-dhama*

*“A* person devoid of strength in devotional service cannot achieve the Supreme Soul, the Lord. Only one who is eager to practice devotional service through the process of chanting His holy names can enter the supreme abode of the Lord. ”

Srila Baladeva Vidyabhusana explains in his commentary that this verse explains the right means by which we should strive to achieve Krsna. The “right means” relates to cultivating spiritual strength. When we are strong enough, we should then soberly and carefully strive to achieve Krsna while performing appropriate austerities. He equates spiritual strength, *bala,* with devotional service. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (9. 4. 66) states:

*mayi nirbaddha-hrdayah*  
*sadhavah sama-darsanah*  
*vase kurvanti mam bhaktya*  
*sat-striyah sat-patim yatha*

“As chaste women bring their gentle husbands under control by service, the pure devotees, who are equal to everyone and completely attached to Me in the core of the heart, bring Me under their full control. ”

This is what spiritual strength is; ultimately, it means the strength to control Krsna. Rupa Gosvami called this *sri-krsnakarsini.* We begin to practice devotional service by engaging in activities for Krsna’s satisfaction.

But even to do that we need strength. Where will we get it? To gain spiritual strength, we must seek it from someone who already has it. The source of all spiritual strength is Lord Balarama.

Lord Balarama is the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s first expansion. Balarama is the *vaibhava-prakasa* expansion of the Lord. There is actually very little difference between Lord Balarama and Lord Krsna. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states that the only difference between them is that Lord Balarama has a white complexion, while Lord Krsna is dark. Of course, Krsna plays a flute while Balarama plays a buffalo horn, and especially, Lord Balarama has the *abhimana or* conception of being Krsna’s servant, even though He is practically nondifferent from Krsna.

Lord Balarama renders service to Krsna in the spiritual world in a variety of ways. He even creates the spiritual world. This means that Goloka Vrndavana and everything that has form there is manifest from Lord Balarama. Balarama also serves Krsna by expanding into the material universe from Balarama to Maha-Sankarsana and then further into the Visnu expansions. Thus Lord Balarama also serves the Lord in the material creation. And, of course, He serves as Ananta Sesa, who is supporting unlimited universes on His hoods.

In *Caitanya-bhagavata* Srila Vrndavana dasa Thakura describes that as Ananta Sesa, Lord Balarama is holding up unlimited universes on his hoods, but He does not stand on anything. He has no support, yet by His strength He supports the unlimited universes.

Therefore we understand that He is the source of spiritual strength, and that a devotee who wants to make progress in devotional service must seek spiritual strength from Him or from His representative, the spiritual master. It is only by the spiritual master or through the devotee that one can attain devotional service, which is the only means to control Krsna. Krsna cannot be controlled by any other way. Krsna tells Uddhava *(Bhag.* 1. 14. 20):

*na sadhayati mam yogo*  
*na sankhyam dharma uddhava*  
*na svadhyayas tapas tyago*  
*yatha bhaktir mamorjita*

“My dear Uddhava, the unalloyed devotional service rendered to Me by My devotees brings Me under their control. I cannot be thus controlled by those engaged in mystic yoga, Sankhya philosophy, pious work, Vedic study, austerity, or renunciation. ”

Just as we cannot practice devotional service except by a devotee’s mercy, so we cannot gain spiritual strength except by a devotee’s mercy. In order to have the strength to see Krsna and to enter the spiritual world, we must firmly hold on to the instructions of the Lord’s devotee. Srila Prabhupada gives the example that if we are drowning in the ocean, we can only be saved by someone who is not drowning. If that person throws us a life preserver, he or she can pull us out of the ocean as long as we don’t let go.

We must hold on to the mercy; that much we have to do. If we do not hold on, we will never be pulled out. Thus if we want to come to the stage of devotional service, we have to hold on tightly to the Vaisnavas’ instructions by constant remembrance and constant endeavor to apply them. Only then will we find the spiritual strength to allow ourselves to be rescued.

**Question: What steps are most important in the initial stages of** *sadhana-bhakti* **if we want to gain spiritual strength?**

Srila Prabhupada explains in *Caitanya-caritamrta* that we must erect a fence to protect the seed of our *bhakti.* That fence is to always keep ourselves in the society of devotees. This fence will protect the seed once it has been planted and has started to grow, and it will keep out mad elephants. Of course, we may ourselves invite mad elephants to trample our creeper—such as when we offend devotees—but the fence will protect us if we prosecute our devotional service carefully.

Once the seed of *bhakti* is planted, we must water it continuously with hearing and chanting if we want it to grow. While watering, however, we must be careful to pull out any weeds that grow alongside the creeper. That means we have to learn to discriminate between weeds and the devotional plant. The weeds are compared to *anarthas,* unwanted desires in the heart, that can choke the growing plant. Therefore the most important step one must take in the initial stage of *sadhana-bhakti* is to associate with those devotees who help us become free from the influence of our anarthas and who strengthen our faith in pure devotional service.

**Question: When we feel that we are in a hopeless situation, where will we get the strength to continue?**

Devotees never think they can overcome *maya* by their own strength. If ever they think they can overcome *maya* on their own, they will immediately be defeated. Maya is rendering good service to Krsna; her service is never obstructed. She is expert at what she does.

Maya has two potencies, *praksepatmika-sakti* and a*varanatmi-ka-sakti,* the “covering” potency and the “throwing” potency. The covering potency makes us forget ourselves and our life’s purpose, and the throwing potency keeps throwing out suggestions that distract us from surrendering to Krsna. This means that whenever we try to free ourselves from her influence, she will say, “I have a proposal for you! ”

Don’t doubt that Maya is doing her service expertly and that she is powerful. The moment we think, “Okay, Maya, I know exactly what you are doing, and I know how to defeat you! ” Maya laughs and says, “Oh? You think you can defeat me? ”

We cannot defeat Maya on our own strength because she is so much more powerful than ourselves and always works under Krsna’s control. Krsna Himself says that Maya acts perfectly according to His will. Someone who acts perfectly according to Krsna’s will cannot be obstructed by anyone else. Only Maya’s original source of strength, Krsna, can defeat her.

When Hiranyakasipu asked Prahlada where he got his strength, he said, “My dear father, I have gotten my strength from the same place you have gotten yours. ” Prahlada knew the source of his power; his father did not. Because Prahlada got his strength by surrendering to Lord Visnu, he was able to overcome *maya.* Hiranyakasipu also received his strength from Lord Visnu, but he could not overcome *maya* because he did not take shelter of that source of his strength. Rather, he was destroyed because he did not surrender to Krsna.

To illustrate our dependence on the original source of strength, Srila Prabhupada tells the story of *punar musika bhava,* “Again Become a Mouse. ” One day, a foraging mouse was being chased by a cat. To save himself, he approached a mystic sage who was meditating in the forest. The mouse said, “I have heard that you can grant a benediction. ” The mouse described his plight, and when the sage asked what he wanted, he said, “I will not have to fear the cat if I myself am a cat. Please change me into a cat. ” Said the sage, “Become a cat. ” The mouse turned into a cat and thought, “Finally I will be free of the botheration of cats. ”

But the next time the cat was out hunting, he was chased by a dog. Immediately he thought of the sage and wondered, “Why should I suffer like this? I will ask the sage to turn me into a dog. ” Said the sage, “Become a dog. ”

Again the mouse thought, “No cats! No dogs! This is almost liberation! ” So fearlessly, the mouse in the dog’s body went hunting. But a jackal began to follow him, howling and chasing him, and he was afraid. Again he approached the sage, this time requesting to become a jackal. Again the sage granted his wish. While in the jackal’s body the mouse felt undefeatable. He wandered the forest seeking his food. Suddenly, a tiger leapt out of the undergrowth and began to chase him. How frustrating to always be chased by something more powerful than himself!

Again he returned to the sage, this time asking to become a tiger. ” The sage agreed.

But instead of wandering away in search of food, the mouse in the tiger’s body stopped and thought, “Why do I need to seek food?

*A* tiger can eat humans. I will simply eat the sage. ” But the sage said, “Oh? You want to eat me? *Punar musika bhava*—again become a mouse! ” And the powerful tiger, who could not appreciate or surrender to the source of his strength was defeated.

Srila Prabhupada compares the mouse to the materialists who are given position, strength, and power by the Supreme Lord, yet think, “Let me use this power against God. ” And even worse are the materialists like Hiranyakasipu who want to use their God-given power against the Lord’s devotees.

Those who try to use their God-given strength against God will always be defeated by Him. Devotees know that if they want strength to overcome *maya,* the only way to get it is to go to Krsna in surrender—or to those who are carrying Krsna’s strength to deliver to the living entities.

## Being Careful to Avoid Anisthita-bhakti
