There are two types of *bhajana-kriya: anisthita* (unsteady) and nisthita (steady). I would like to analyze *anisthita-bhakti* and how to bring ourselves to the stage of steady devotion.

*Anisthita-bhakti* is marked by six characteristics or somewhat progressive stages. The first is *utsaha-mayi,* false enthusiasm. A *brahmana* boy may begin his study of scripture and think he has become a learned scholar worthy of everyone’s praise. Similarly, those who are just beginning devotional service may have the audacity to think they have mastered the process and attained a high state. The scripture calls that *utsaha-mayi*—we are puffed up with enthusiasm. The problem is that such enthusiasm is coming from a false conception of ourselves. We think, “I’m chanting Hare Krsna, wearing *kanti-mala* and devotional clothing. I’m a great devotee. I have been a member of the society of devotees for some time, and I know a few *Bhagavad-gita* verses. I can also play fancy *karatalas.* Compared to everyone else around here, I’m an advanced devotee! ” We draw enthusiasm from thinking we are more advanced than everyone else. Therefore it is called “false” enthusiasm.

And we see that such false enthusiasm does not last. As soon as we are no longer recognized for our so-called advancement, we lose heart to practice Krsna consciousness. When we do not get the respect we think we deserve, we feel something is missing in our spiritual life. “I am advanced, but no one else seems to recognize me for what I am. I’m the only one who can see it. ” False enthusiasm is another face of pride and must be given up.

The next stage is called *ghana-tarala,* which literally means “thick-thin” or sporadic endeavor. Sometimes we are enthusiastic and think, “I feel good about my practice of *bhakti.* I am getting the respect I deserve and the results I am earning. Everything is going so nicely. ” But then things change: “This Krsna consciousness is too difficult. I have no taste, and no one respects me. ” We lose our enthusiasm.

Sometimes when we lose our enthusiasm, we also lose our ability to practice regulated *sadhana.* We get absorbed in material consciousness, then begin to suffer, and take up the chanting again. We gain a little steadiness, then stop again, because we have not discovered the importance of chanting or the truth about ourselves. Such is *anisthita bhakti.*

The third stage of *anisthita-bhakti* is *vyudha-vikalpa,* indecisiveness. We become indecisive when we are trying to find the best way to surrender, yet remain under the sway of our material attachments. We search continuously for a more comfortable way to surrender— a way that still allows us to fulfill our material desires. We remain indecisive, and in the end do nothing for Krsna.

The fourth stage is *visaya-sangara,* struggle with the senses. Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura quotes: “One whose heart is absorbed in materialism is far from obtaining devotion to Visnu. How can a man by going east obtain something that is in the west? ” Struggle with the senses indicates that we want to satisfy our material desires. But we also want spiritual advancement. If we try to satisfy the desires, then how will we make spiritual advancement? “How can a man by going east obtain something that is in the west? ”

Srila Prabhupada gave a different analogy: How can a person hope to ignite a fire if he pours water on it at the same time? We have to give up our material attachments.

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura quotes a nice verse in this connection to help those who are struggling:

Having awakened faith in the narrations of My glories, being disgusted with all material activities, knowing that all sense gratification leads to misery, but still being unable to renounce all sense enjoyment, My devotee should remain happy and worship Me with great faith and conviction. Even though he is sometimes engaged in sense enjoyment, My devotee knows that all sense gratification leads to a miserable result, and he sincerely repents such activities. *(Bhag.* 11. 20. 27-28)

The fifth symptom of *anisthita-bhakti* is *niyamaksama*, vowing to serve Krsna. While *visaya-sangara* refers to making vows to renounce whatever is unfavorable to *bhakti, niyamaksama* means making vows to increase one’s service, such as chanting twenty-five rounds on Ekadasis, but being unable to keep them.

The last characteristic of *anisthita-bhakti* is *taranga-rangini,* enjoying the facilities *bhakti* offers. We may chant Hare Krsna for some time and gradually become steady. This may bring us respect or even position. Then we begin to like the position and facilities our practices give us, and become more attached to the facilities than to *bhakti* itself.

Lord Caitanya explained to Rupa Gosvami that while watering the devotional seed in the heart, sometimes it is found that the weeds *(anarthas)* grow faster than the devotional creeper. These unwanted desires or *anarthas* can choke the devotional plant if allowed to continue growing.

Lord Caitanya therefore tells Rupa Gosvami that we have to learn to discriminate between the devotional creeper and the weeds, and then cut away the weeds. This is too difficult a task to perform alone. Rather, Krsna removes the *anarthas* from the hearts of those who have developed the urge to hear about Him. The more eager we are to hear about Krsna rather than to meditate on our material desires, the more Krsna will act from within the heart to remove the material desires from our heart. There is actually no other way to remove *anarthas.*

Be attentive while watering the devotional plant. If we find ourselves focusing more on the weeds than the *bhakti-lata,* then we are guilty of *taranga-rangini,* enjoying our own *anarthas* in the name of practicing *bhakti.*

The *acaryas* have explained these categories of unsteady devotional service only to help us look at ourselves. Don’t spend time trying to categorize the advancement or perceived lack of advancement of other devotees. Try to understand how to make spiritual advancement. Having come in contact with *bhakti-yoga,* be very, very careful that you do not lose this rare chance to love Krsna, which is available only in this human form of life.

**Question: How can we determine our own level of advancement?**

We have to look into our own heart. If we see that we are exhibiting symptoms of *anisthita-bhakti,* we must continue our work on the stage of *anartha-nivrtti.* What are the *anarthas?* Perhaps we are fault-finding, duplicitous, envious of others, or we desire to be worshiped. Or perhaps we are neglecting the instructions of scripture. By looking at our own heart, we should be able to see the truth of our situation.

Even though we may not be able to clearly understand the higher level of *nisthita-bhakti,* if we want to advance in devotional practices, we have to at least recognize our own *anarthas* and decide that they should be removed. Otherwise, we are subject to the *anartha* called *tattva-vibhrama* (ignorance). That is, we are ignorant of the obstacles we must overcome to make advancement in spiritual life. Don’t waste time trying to figure out what is in anyone else’s heart; focus on your own. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura says (as quoted in *Leadership Excellence Newsletter,* Issue 24):

The world stands in no need of any reformer. The world has a very competent person for guiding its minutest happenings. The person who finds that there is scope for reform of the world himself stands in need of reform. The world goes on in its own perfect way.

No person can deflect it by the breadth of a hair from the course chalked out for it by providence. When we perceive any change being actually affected in the course of events of this world by the agency of any particular individual, we also know very well that the agent possesses no real power at any stage. The agent finds himself driven forward by a force belonging to a different category from himself.

... The scriptures declare that it is only necessary to listen with an open mind to the name of Krishna from the lips of a bona fide devotee.

As soon as Krishna enters the listening ear, He clears up the vision of the listener so that he no longer has any ambition of ever-acting the part of a reformer of any other person, because he finds that no one is left without the very highest guidance. It is therefore his own reform by the grace of God, whose supreme necessity and nature he is increasingly able to realize by the eternally continuing mercy of the Supreme Lord.

**Question: Is it proper for us to try to understand the details of our service to Srimati Radharani?**

Before we can serve Srimati Radharani, we have to be qualified enough to know what our service is. What is most important for us is to understand the moods of the servants of the residents of Vrndavana. And the most important point to understand about Srimati Radharani’s service is that no one can give Krsna more pleasure than Srimati Radharani gives Him.

Krsna cries when He thinks of Srimati Radharani, because Her service and devotion to Him is so unconditional. The *gopis’* mood in service, especially Srimati Radharani’s, is that they will not be happy unless they see that everything will lead to Krsna’s happiness. Of course, all the devotees in Vrndavana please Krsna, but there are different stages of perfection.

The Lord’s pastimes in Navadvipa are *audarya* (full of magnanimity). It is described that Krsna’s pastimes in Dvaraka are perfect, in Mathura more perfect, and in Vrndavana most perfect. They are most perfect because in Vrndavana the residents engage in spontaneous devotional service. The mood of sweetness, *madhurya,* predominates and permeates all the relationships in Vrndavana, whereas in Dvaraka and Mathura the devotees serve with an awareness of Krsna’s *aisvarya* (divine position). Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura describes that the sweet nature of Krsna’s Vrndavana pastimes is due to the fact that His humanlike activities are never destroyed by the majestic understanding of His opulence. The residents of Vrndavana have no such consciousness. Their level of understanding of Krsna is based on their particular relationship with Him. Their highest realization is that “Krsna is my friend, ” “Krsna is my son, ” or “Krsna is my lover, ” and by these feelings they possess the Lord. Their pure *bhakti* cannot be disturbed under any circumstances.

Srimati Radharani’s service to Krsna is considered the highest because She satisfies all of His desires. All the Vrajavasis have selfless, spontaneous, ecstatic love for Krsna filled with spiritual emotions. They know nothing but service to Krsna. We should want to serve them. We should understand that if we want to please Krsna, we must please His devotees. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* (Cc. *Madhya* 13. 80) states *gopi-bhartuh pada-kamalayor dasa-dasanudasah:* our actual identity is as servants of the servant of the servant of the servant of the servant—a million times removed—of Krsna, the master of the *gopis.*

The details of our service to Srimati Radharani can only be revealed by a resident of Vrndavana when we are ready to perform that service. If we think we can understand our service before we become qualified, we are fools and will fall down.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that such an attempt is like trying to reach the ripe fruits at the top of a tree by jumping from the ground. If we want the ripe fruits, we will have to climb the tree. Jumping from the ground will accomplish nothing.

If we think we are ready to have our service to Srimati Radharani revealed, we should know that we are jumping for the ripe fruits. The very minimum qualification to even approach this topic is that we are situated on the steady platform, between *anartha-nivritti* and *nistha.*

Sometimes devotees imagine that they are closer to *bhava* than they really are simply because they have begun to hanker to serve Krsna in a particular mood. But *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* describes the nine characteristics of *bhava-bhakti* for our benefit: (I) *Avyartha-kalatvam,* proper utilization of time; (2) *Ksanti*, perseverance— a devotee always remains tolerant and patient even in the face of many disturbances; (3) *Virakti,* detachment or indifference toward the objects of sense gratification; (4) *mana-sunyata,* Pridelessness, or always thinking oneself to be very low and humble even though one is greatly advanced; (5) *asa-bandha,* hope against hope—*bhava-bhaktas* have firm faith that Krsna will elevate them to the topmost position of devotional service although they always feel themselves to be very low and humble and incapable of achieving it by their own efforts; (6) *Samutkantha,* intense eagerness to achieve the stage of loving devotional service to Krsna; (7) *nama-gane sada rucih,* constant attraction for chanting the Lord’s holy name; (8) *asaktis tad-gunakhyane,* attachment to always glorifying the Supreme Lord’s qualities; and (9) *pritis tad-vasati-sthale,* a love for living in the holy *dhama.* In this regard, Srila Prabhupada states:

Furthermore, we should also increase our inclination to live in a place where Krsna lives, *pritis tad-vasati-sthale.* In the vision of higher devotees, Krsna actually lives everywhere, but because we are in a lower condition, we should know that for us Krsna lives in the temple. Because we do not see Krsna everywhere, we should come to the temple to see Krsna, who kindly appears there, by His mercy, in a manner in which we can see Him.

*(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 150)

Do we have all the characteristics of *bhava-bhakti?* It takes the intense desire and the qualification of *bhava-bhakti,* the threshold of *prema, to* enter that stage of pure love of God where we can offer actual service to Srimati Radharani. If we are trying to offer such service before that time, we are jumping for the ripe fruit. We will not be able to attain it.

Better to move progressively up through the branches. When we arrive on the branch where the ripe fruit is hanging, we will see that it will fall right into our hands. Service will be revealed when we are ready for it to be revealed. Until we attain that stage, we should hanker for it to be revealed, but we should not intrude in the higher realm. The *acaryas* warn that anyone who tries to enter the higher realm without qualification will be an intruder and will simply cause a disturbance to the Lord and His devotees.

Therefore, try to understand the mood of the Vrndavana servants. What is the nature of their unalloyed love? How does Srimati Radharani’s love for Krsna surpass that of all the other residents of Vrndavana? Strive to become a servant of a perfect servant of Krsna, or become a servant of a perfect servant of Srimati Radharani. By becoming a servant of a perfect servant of Srimati Radharani we can come to assist Srimati Radharani in giving pleasure to Krsna. Until we get that opportunity, however, we should accept a bona fide representative of such a servant—a servant of the servant of the Lord—and by cultivating a selfless mood of service in that person’s association, which will include hearing descriptions of the Lord’s pastimes, gradually make ourselves qualified.

## Glories of the Holy Name

## The Greatest Gift

*a-brahma-bhuvanal lokah*  
*punar avartino ’rjuna*  
*mam upetya tu kaunteya*  
*punar janma na vidyate*

\[Krsna says\]: “From the highest planet in the material world down to the lowest, all are places of misery wherein repeated birth and death take place. But one who attains to My abode, O son of Kunti, never takes birth again. ” *(Bg.* 8. 16)

The living entity simply wanders throughout the planets looking for permanence. It is our nature to be sheltered by something permanent. But the material world is temporary and filled with suffering; by its nature it does not allow for permanence. Therefore, whatever relationship we establish with this world will not fully satisfy the self, because the self is satisfied only by permanence. Thus Srila Prabhupada encouraged us to go back home, back to Godhead. In *Bhagavad-gita,* Krsna says:

*paras tasmat tu bhavo ’nyo*  
*’vyakto ’vyaktat sanatanah*  
*yah sa sarvesu bhutesu*  
*nasyatsu na vinasyati*

“Yet there is another unmanifest nature, which is eternal and is transcendental to this manifested and unmanifested matter. It is supreme and is never annihilated. When all in this world is annihilated, that part remains as it is. ” *(Bg.* 8. 20)

He also tells us what we must understand in order to attain the world of permanence (Bg. 4. 9):

*janma karma ca me divyam*  
*evam yo vetti tattvatah*  
*tyaktva deham punar janma*  
*naiti mam eti so ’rjuna*

“One who knows the transcendental nature of My appearance and activities does not, upon leaving the body, take his birth again in this material world, but attains My eternal abode, O Arjuna. ”

This place of permanence, where we can have all our desires completely fulfilled, is the spiritual world. The scriptures describe the spiritual world as composed of *Cintamani,* transcendental touchstone. There the trees are all *kalpa-vrksas* or desire trees, and Krsna is known as the reservoir of all pleasure. He alone can satisfy all our desires. When all our desires are fully satisfied to the complete degree, we will have nothing left for which to hanker. This is the state of existence living entities in the spiritual world experience. All they know is Krsna. We in the material world have forgotten Him and the true source of our happiness, and therefore do nothing *but* hanker.

Krsna calls Himself the source of everything in the *Bhagavad-gita* (10. 8):

*aham sarvasya prabhavo*  
*mattah sarvam pravartate*  
*iti matva bhajante mam*  
*budha bhava-samanvitah*

“I am the source of all spiritual and material worlds. Everything emanates from Me. The wise who perfectly know this engage in My devotional service and worship Me with all their hearts. ”

What is this Krsna like? Lord Brahma states:

*isvarah paramah krsnah*  
*sac-cid-ananda-vigrahah*  
*anadir adir govindah*  
*sarva-karana-karanam*

“Krsna, who is known as Govinda, is the Supreme Godhead. He has an eternal, blissful, spiritual body. He is the origin of all. He has no other origin and He is the prime cause of all causes. ” (Bs. 5. I)

And:

*advaitam acyutam anadim ananta-rupam*  
*adyam purana-purusam nava-yauvanam ca*  
*vedesu durlabham adurlabham atma-bhaktau*  
*govindam adi-purusam tam aham bhajami*

“I worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Govinda \[Krsna\], who is the original person—absolute, infallible, without beginning. Although expanded into unlimited forms, He is still the same original, the oldest, and the person always appearing as a fresh youth. Such eternal, blissful, all-knowing forms of the Lord are usually not understood by even the best Vedic scholars, but they are always manifest to pure, unalloyed devotees. ” (Bs. 5. 33)

Krsna has a transcendental body and always appears as an ever-fresh youth. He manifests unlimited universes from His transcendental body, but He is eternally situated in His transcendental realm, Goloka Vrndavana. The entire cosmic manifestation is a transformation of His energy; He Himself never has to become involved in creation, maintenance, or destruction. But He chooses to descend into this world once in a day of Brahma. *Bhagavad-gita* (4. 6) states:

*ajo ’pi sarin avyayatma*  
*bhutanam isvaro ’pi san*  
*prakrtim svam adhisthaya*  
*sambhavamy atma-mayaya*

“Although I am unborn and My transcendental body never deteriorates, and although I am the Lord of all living entities, I still appear in every millennium in My original transcendental form. ”

It is due to Krsna’s kindness that He appears, and we should never mistake His form for something material. Rather, He always appears in His original transcendental form. In the age of Kali, the Lord mercifully appears in the form of His name.

*kali-kale nama-rupe krsna-avatara*  
*nama haite haya sarva-jagat-nistara*

“In this age of Kali, the holy name of the Lord, the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra,* is the incarnation of Lord Krsna. Simply by chanting the holy name, one associates with the Lord directly. Anyone who does this is certainly delivered. " (Cc. Adi 17. 22)

Krsna has invested all His energies in His name, making Himself easily approached. If we chant the Lord’s holy names, our relationship with Krsna becomes revealed. Without this conscious understanding of our true identity, we are forced simply to have a relationship with Krsna’s material energy, and we feel lost in all the temporary experiences that living with matter entails.

The Lord and His holy name are nondifferent. As the Lord has eternal pastimes in the spiritual world, therefore, His holy name also has eternal pastimes in the spiritual world. We will begin to understand this as we learn to see the Lord in His name. When we have pure love for the Lord and that love enters our chanting, we will experience the Lord’s presence. The Hare Krsna mantra is not simply a sound vibration. The Lord has willingly agreed to invest His holy name with all His potencies. To the degree that we have faith in this, we will gain experience of it.

Therefore, the holy name is the source of all devotional relationship in this age, and it is the cornerstone practice in *sadhana-bhakti.* Those who wish to serve Krsna’s holy name purely should receive it from a pure devotee at the time of initiation, and make a serious commitment to chanting it. It is therefore an important point in a person’s life to come to the platform of accepting Vaisnava initiation. But initiation is not the end of one’s practice; it is the beginning. Devotees sometimes misunderstand this point; they think that initiation is the main achievement. They see initiation as the goal, and when they receive it, imagine that they will be carried back to Godhead without any endeavor on their part. Those who have received initiation soon realize that they have only just begun their spiritual life.

Srila Prabhupada explains in *The Nectar of Devotion* that if devotees do not chant seriously, or they commit offenses or give up their vows, then they will not achieve perfection simply because they are initiated. It is not simply the acceptance of vows that enables us to continue chanting Krsna’s holy name. Prahlada Maharaja describes *(Bhag.* 7. 7. 51):

You cannot please the Supreme Personality of Godhead by becoming perfect *brahmanas,* demigods or great saints, or by becoming perfectly good in etiquette or vast learning. None of these qualifications can awaken the pleasure of the Lord. Nor by charity, austerity, sacrifice, cleanliness or vows can one satisfy the Lord. The Lord is pleased only if one has unflinching, unalloyed devotion to Him. Without sincere devotional service, everything is simply a show.

Although accepting vows is not the end of the process, still, doing so before the spiritual master, the sacrificial fire, and the deity is certainly a big commitment. The most important vow we take is to chant Hare Krsna a fixed number of times a day, because even if we fall while chanting the Lord’s holy names, if we continue to chant sincerely, the holy name will forgive us. Knowing this, of course, should not give us an excuse to fall, because such a mentality constitutes the seventh offense in chanting. This is the most serious offense, and is difficult to rectify (Elaboration of this seventh offense against the holy name will be given further in this section on the *Glories of the Holy Name).*

Still, sincere chanting even from a fallen condition can reinstate us in devotional service. Therefore, to chant the holy name is the most important vow we make.

The other vows we take at initiation deal with the four regulative principles. These principles are important, but they really only bring us to the platform of human life. That alone will not elevate us to the transcendental platform. It is only the Lord’s holy name that can bring us to Krsna’s lotus feet.

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* describes that sincere chanting of our *diksa* mantra will deliver us from material existence. However, *krsna-nama,* the holy name of Krsna, is what delivers us to Krsna’s lotus feet. It is Krsna’s name in the Gayatri-mantras that makes those mantras significant. Therefore, we should never give up chanting Krsna’s names. We should understand the real value of chanting. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (11. 5. 36) states:

*kalim sabhajayanty arya*  
*guna jnah sara-bhaginah*  
*yatra sankirtanenaiva*  
*sarva-svartho ’bhilabhyate*

“Those who are actually advanced in knowledge are able to appreciate the essential value of this age of Kali. Such enlightened persons worship Kali-yuga because in this fallen age, all perfection of life can easily be achieved by the performance of *sankirtana. "*

And Lord Caitanya says:

*yuga-dharma pravartaimu nama-sankirtana*  
*cari bhava-bhakti diya nacamu bhuvana*

“I shall personally inaugurate the religion of the age—*nama-sankirtana,* the congregational chanting of the holy name. I shall make the world dance in ecstasy, realizing the four mellows of loving devotional service. ” (Cc. *Adi* 3. 19)

We too can dance in ecstasy by chanting the Lord’s holy name. Such ecstasy is the perfection of chanting. Chanting allows us to realize our eternal relationship with the Supreme Lord and to engage in that service. But to achieve the highest ecstasy, we should be sure to chant the Lord’s holy name with a proper attitude, for this congregational chanting is the great mercy Lord Caitanya came to deliver.

In his *Padyavali,* Srila Rupa Gosvami quotes a nice prayer: “I pray that I will always be able to chant Your holy name in the same way that a lover reads aloud a letter from his long separated beloved. ”

When a soldier is away at war, he becomes eager to receive letters from his family members from whom he has been separated for a long time. When a letter finally arrives, a soldier reads and rereads every word with great relish. Srila Rupa Gosvami prays that he can chant the Lord’s name with the same attention and eagerness. He adds, “I pray that the sight of Your transcendental form will be as pleasing as the beauty of a young bride is to her husband. I pray that the narrations of Your sweet pastimes will become as sweet to my ears as the sound of a young girl’s voice is to a materialist. I pray that the remnants of your foodstuffs will become as palatable to me as is the taste of a young woman’s lips to a materialist. ” We should pray to have such feelings when we chant.

Srila Prabhupada says that it was with this feeling that Lord Caitanya taught His devotees to worship the Lord in this age. He said that devotees never worship the Lord with feelings of union but with feelings of separation. Lord Caitanya exemplified His own teachings, tasting the ecstasy of separation from the Lord in the mood of Srimati Radhika. He also mercifully distributed the same taste to others.

Lord Caitanya appeared in this world to both distribute the holy name and to help us realize the four loving mellows of devotional service experienced in Vrndavana. He presented the chanting of the holy name as the *abhidheya,* the means to achieve spiritual perfection. Our goal, therefore, is *kirtaniyah sada harih, to* always be chanting the Lord’s holy names. Each of us who has committed through initiation to chant the Lord’s name should understand the importance of the vow and especially the great value of chanting.

There is a story to help us appreciate the importance and value of the holy name. Once, a disciple approached his spiritual master and asked to receive a confidential instruction from him. The spiritual master said, “Chant the Hare Krsna maha-mantra. ”

When the disciple heard this instruction, he accepted it as something secret and confidential. With great enthusiasm, he remembered the instruction in his heart and left his spiritual master’s *asrama* with “Hare Krsna” on his lips. But while walking along the road he saw some women washing clothes and chanting Hare Krsna. This surprised him, because they knew his secret mantra. As he continued to walk, he saw a group of fishermen pulling in their nets while chanting Hare Krsna. Again, he was surprised. Further down the road he saw young boys playing and also chanting Hare Krsna.

He wondered, “I asked my spiritual master for a confidential instruction and he told me to chant Hare Krsna. If this mantra is so confidential, why are all these people chanting it? ” He decided to return to his spiritual master and ask him about this.

Back at the *asrama* he explained the whole situation to his spiritual master and asked him for clarification. But instead of speaking, his spiritual master told him, “Here is a valuable stone. Take it, return to all the people who are chanting, and try to get them to trade it for something of equal value. ”

Confused but obedient, the disciple approached the women who were washing their clothes. These were simple village women, and they did not know anything about the stone’s value. The disciple said, “Here is a valuable stone. Please accept it and give me something in return for it. ”

But the washerwomen said, “What will we do with such a stone? Maybe we can use it to wash our clothes. ” (In India, people beat their clothes with a stone or piece of wood while washing them. ) The disciple realized that these women truly could not appreciate the stone’s value and he left to seek out the fishermen.

But he met with the same result; the fishermen also did not know the value of the stone. They asked if they could use it to weight their nets, but then decided that it was too light.

Finally, the disciple approached the young boys and met a similar disappointment. The boys only thought they could use the stone as a ball in their play. They had no sense of the value of the stone.

When the disciple returned to the *asrama,* his spiritual master asked, “Were you able to get something of equal value in return for the stone? ”

“No, no one could understand the stone’s value. ”

The spiritual master said, “Similarly, many people may chant Hare Krsna, but they remain indifferent to the holy name. One must not remain indifferent to the holy name of the Lord but must understand its great value. The holy name is the greatest gift. ”

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes that there are only two realities in this material world, the *jiva* and the Lord’s holy name. By the mercy of the holy name the whole spiritual world is revealed, and one can fully understand who Krsna is along with His qualities, and pastimes. His beautiful form will also manifest, attracted by His own holy name. We should have faith that everything is present in Krsna’s holy name.

Chant the holy name understanding its value. Make a firm vow to always chant at least sixteen attentive rounds every day. And if you cannot be attentive, at least make the effort to be attentive.

Srila Prabhupada fulfilled Lord Caitanya’s prophecy that the holy name would appear in every town and village throughout the world. Before Srila Prabhupada came, who knew of the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra*? In such a short time, Srila Prabhupada made “Hare Krsna” a household word. And the lives of all those who came in contact with the holy name have been changed. Many of them have developed god-like qualities.

Whenever Srila Prabhupada was interviewed by journalists or on the radio or television, he emphasized how his followers had previously been addicted to all kinds of sinful activities, but now they were becoming lovers of God. Such is the power of the Lord’s holy name.

When Srila Prabhupada came to the West to try to spread Krsna consciousness throughout the world, he felt he had little time to fulfill the mission the Lord and his spiritual master expected of him. He even received a sign that he might not live much longer during his first journey overseas in the two heart attacks he suffered on the voyage. But Srila Prabhupada had to plant as many Krsna conscious seeds—seeds of love of Godhead—as possible. Those seeds were complete in themselves, and as long as they fell in fertile soil and were nourished, they would grow nicely.

Srila Prabhupada took care of the seeds he planted: he sowed the seeds, watered the garden, helped to remove some of the weeds, and harvested the fruits. Srila Prabhupada is a *sat-guru;* he is fully on the liberated platform. And he is conscious of all of his disciples and followers.

It is the disciple’s duty to also be fully conscious of his or her spiritual master. Those disciples who meditate on Srila Prabhupada’s desires and follow his instructions, as he gave them when he was present, can be empowered to continue planting, watering, weeding, and harvesting the seeds of *bhakti* that he planted.

Srila Prabhupada said that the age of Kali constitutes an emergency, and sometimes during an emergency, concessions can be made in how one gets things done. For example, a sheriff may go to various citizens, deputize them, and thus give them the authority to represent him. Perhaps these citizens have never been trained, but the sheriff chooses outstanding citizens to represent him during the emergency. Similarly, Lord Caitanya gave out badges and authorized His followers to serve in whatever way required during the emergency.

Srila Prabhupada’s emergency work was to distribute the Lord’s holy name to all people. He emphasized that our whole life should be dedicated to serving Krsna’s holy name. Those who carefully chant the holy name in a serious mood will experience a transformation of heart.

Krsna’s holy name is self-effulgent and identical with Krsna. Just as Krsna is eternal, has no material qualities, and is the reservoir of all transcendental *rasa,* so Krsna’s holy name has the same qualities. Just as Krsna is eternally situated in Goloka Vrndavana and eternally engaged in unlimited pastimes, so Krsna’s holy name is eternally manifest in the spiritual world. When the holy name is manifest in the material world, we should know it has been imported from the spiritual world.

When a devotee on whose lips the holy name is vibrated appears in this world—a devotee who has a taste for the holy name, who sees Krsna present in His holy name, and who loves Krsna—imports the holy name for the benefit of others. When such a devotee chants, he gives everyone an opportunity to come in contact with Krsna.

Krsna’s holy name is like the gradual blossoming of a flower. First the bud appears, which is compared to Krsna’s form. When the bud opens, one can discern its fragrance. This fragrance is compared to Krsna’s qualities. Then the bud flowers fully, and this flower is compared to Krsna’s pastimes.

The holy name opens gradually like this flower; while chanting the Lord’s holy names, the Lord’s qualities, form, pastimes, and one’s eternal relationship with Him are gradually revealed. The holy name contains everything. Therefore chanting Krsna’s name is the primary activity of *sadhana-bhakti.* All the other devotional service we render is meant to help us progress in chanting. We should develop this attitude about our chanting.

Prabhupada often said that we cannot possibly understand our great fortune. It is such fortune to have come in contact with Krsna’s holy name. The holy name is the most precious gift that one can possibly receive. Narottama dasa Thakura calls this gift *prema-ratana-dhana,* the “treasure-trove of pure love. ” Anyone who comes into contact with the holy name—even once hearing it— immediately becomes fortunate.

Prabhupada also warned us, however: “Now having obtained this fortune, you should always endeavor to discover its real value. ”

**Question: Caitanya Mahaprabhu came and gave us the holy names. Chanting is the yuga-dharma for all Kali-yugas, but He does not come every Kali-yuga. What happens during those Kali-yugas when He does not come?**

Caitanya Mahaprabhu comes as Gaura-Narayana in every Kali-yuga to distribute the Lord’s holy names, but in this particular Kali-yuga, He not only comes to distribute the holy name, but to give the sweetest, most ecstatic, spontaneous loving devotional service as it is rendered by the residents of Vraja. Just as Krsna comes once in every day of Brahma, Lord Caitanya comes to give *vraja-prema* once in every day of Brahma.

In other ages, He comes to give congregational chanting so that the conditioned souls can become free of sinful reaction and achieve liberation. But in *this* Kali-yuga, He gives *vraja-prema,* which is the highest type of *prema.*

## Understanding the Potency of the Holy Name

*ayur harati vai pumsam*  
*udyann astam ca yann asau*  
*tasyarte yat-ksano nita*  
*uttama-sloka-vartaya*

Both by rising and by setting, the sun decreases the duration of life of everyone, except one who utilizes the time by discussing topics of the all-good Personality of Godhead. ” *(Bhag.* 2. 3. 17)

This human form of life is so rare and valuable. And besides having taken birth as humans, all of us have to varying degrees had the rare opportunity and great fortune to come into contact with the Lord’s holy name. Having contacted the holy name gives us the opportunity to achieve perfection. All success can be achieved by chanting.

“Success” may be perceived differently according to one’s mentality. One person may consider success the achievement of great wealth. Another may consider success the achievement of influence and position. Someone may consider success the achievement of liberation, and another may consider success as becoming free from sinful reaction. But real success means to achieve the topmost goal of human life, ecstatic love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead—something that is achieved by chanting the Lord’s holy name.

When we hear about the pastimes of Lord Caitanya and His devotees, it seems like these pastimes of chanting, dancing, and feasting go on without obstacles. Sometimes the *kirtanas* continued for seventy-two hours at a stretch.

Lord Caitanya manifested *sata-prahariya bhava,* twenty-one hours of ecstasy, in which the Lord and His associates continuously chanted Krsna’s holy name. At other times the Lord would engage in *kirtana* for much longer than that.

Sometimes in the association of Lord Nityananda, the devotees would hold *kirtana* and forget to eat for a month. They were so absorbed in the holy name that they derived all their nourishment from chanting.

We cannot imitate their pastimes. And we see that if we try to chant and dance continuously, we run into many obstacles. We may certainly feel the desire to chant and dance all the time, but there are always other things that we must do that prevent us from continuous absorption in *kirtana.* And is it even possible for us to chant and dance all the time, even if we don’t have other duties? We will inevitably get tired or hungry or find ourselves thinking about other bodily necessities. It is difficult to transcend the body. Without real taste for the Lord’s holy name, it is difficult to continue chanting it.

Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami spent only ninety-six minutes a day on eating, sleeping, and anything else his body needed. The rest of his time he spent following Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s instructions with great ecstasy. He was chanting the Lord’s holy name and worshipping Radha and Krsna in his heart. He was completely absorbed, experiencing the bliss of chanting the holy name, that sometimes he even forgot to sleep.

Even when we have the opportunity to spend such time free of other demands, if we don’t hanker for real taste, we cannot do it. It’s too difficult to chant continuously. The dirty things in our heart prevent us and block our ability to relish chanting.

What does it mean to taste the holy name? One time, the great *nama-acarya,* Srila Haridasa Thakura, was invited by one Vaisnava, Balarama Acarya, to come to the home of two devotees, Hiranya and Govardhana Majumadara. At that time, Haridasa Thakura was chanting 300, 000 names daily. We chant only sixteen rounds daily, which is approximately 28, 000 names, but Haridasa Thakura was chanting 300, 000 names, about 192 rounds, and that took most of the day. It requires great devotion to be so absorbed in chanting the holy name. Since Haridasa Thakura was chanting purely, he was, of course, experiencing the Lord’s presence in His name.

When Haridasa Thakura arrived at the home of Govardhana and Hiranya, Balarama Acarya introduced him to everyone present, including the many learned scholars, *brahmanas*, and saints who had assembled to meet him. When Balarama Acarya mentioned that Haridasa Thakura chanted 300, 000 names a day, the assembly decided to discuss the holy name’s glories.

Various people spoke. One person said, “The holy name can free one from all the reactions of sinful life. ” Someone else said, “The holy name can bring one to the platform of liberation. ”

But Haridasa Thakura protested. “Actually, liberation and freedom from sinful reactions are simply by-products of chanting. One can gain them by only once chanting the Lord’s holy name without offense. The real goal of chanting is to achieve ecstatic love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By chanting the holy name without offense, one can awaken his ecstatic love for Krsna’s lotus feet. ” Haridasa Thakura then recited a verse:

As the rising sun immediately dissipates all the world’s darkness, which is deep like an ocean, so the holy name of the Lord, if chanted once without offenses, dissipates all the reactions of a living being’s sinful life. All glories to that holy name of the Lord, which is auspicious for the entire world. *(Padyavali,* 16)

Haridasa Thakura added, “O learned scholars, please explain the meaning of this verse. ”

But the audience thought it better for Haridasa Thakura to explain it. Therefore, Haridasa Thakura said:

With the first glimpse of sunlight, fear of thieves, ghosts and demons immediately disappears, and when the sun is actually visible, everything is manifest, and everyone begins performing his religious activities and regulative duties.

Similarly, the first hint that offenseless chanting of the Lord’s holy name has awakened dissipates the reactions of sinful life immediately. And when one chants the holy name offenselessly, one awakens to service in ecstatic love at the lotus feet of Krsna.

(Cc. Antya 3. 184-185)

Haridasa Thakura then described how even Ajamila was liberated by chanting his son’s name at the time of death. But a devotee does not aspire for liberation from sinful reactions. Liberation is simply an automatic by-product of chanting.

Present among the assembled scholars and saints was a tax collector named Gopala Cakravarti. Haridasa Thakura’s statements made him angry, and he said, “How is it possible that one can achieve liberation simply by a slight glimpse of the holy name?! One may not achieve liberation even after millions of births of full knowledge of the Absolute Truth. If by once offenselessly chanting the Lord’s holy name one does not achieve liberation, I will cut off Haridasa Thakura’s nose! ”

Haridasa Thakura replied, “If the slight glimpse of the holy name of the Lord does not give one liberation, I will cut off my *own* nose! ” Everyone present was disturbed by Gopala Cakravarti’s words. It was obvious that Gopala Cakravarti did not understand Haridasa Thakura’s exalted position, but thought him only some emotional devotee whose statements were not based on *sastra.*

But Haridasa Thakura assured Gopala Cakravarti that he was speaking from *sastra,* then added, “If you do not accept *sastra,* then you will never be able to understand the Lord’s holy name. ”

Three days later, Gopala Cakravarti contracted leprosy for his offense to Haridasa Thakura, and everyone could understand that one has to be careful to not commit offenses to great Vaisnavas, and certainly not to the Lord’s holy name.

This discussion took place because people had different conceptions of the holy name and the actual goal of chanting it. Haridasa Thakura established the real goal of chanting. He explained that everyone who comes into contact with the holy name derives benefit, but if one wants to derive the *full* benefit and achieve love of God, one has to chant purely, without offense.

People chant for different reasons. Some people chant Hare Krsna to become famous, wealthy, or peaceful. Such people are attached to fruitive gain from chanting and think, “If I am chanting Hare Krsna, I can achieve my goal. ” But their goals are material. They do not know the real goal of chanting. The scripture calls this mentality *pratibimba-abhasa*, in which the chanter is in touch with only a reflection of the holy name.

When the sun in the sky is reflected on water, its reflection is distorted. Similarly, the Lord’s holy name, when reflected on a materialistic conception, is distorted and is not the Lord’s real holy name. To know the true glories of the holy name, one has to hear of them in the association of devotees.

Anyone who experiences Krsna’s personal *darsana* experiences unlimited ecstasy. The Vaisnava literature contains many examples of devotees receiving Krsna’s *darsana,* and they cannot contain their bliss.

When the Lord appeared before Dhruva Maharaja, for example, Dhruva experienced ecstasy. When Lord Caitanya revealed His *maha-prakasa* form to Kholaveca Sridhara, Sridhara fell to the ground overcome by ecstasy. In other words, seeing the Lord produced unlimited ecstasy in him. If the Lord and His name are nondifferent, and if we were chanting the pure holy name, we would be receiving Krsna’s *darsana* and feeling such ecstasy. If we are not experiencing the ecstasy of Krsna’s *darsana,* it stands to reason that we must not be chanting the pure holy name.

The material world is compared to a blazing forest fire. It requires huge quantities of rainwater to extinguish such a fire. Krsna’s holy name is just like that rainwater, and it pours down from the spiritual world to put out the blazing fire of material existence.

Therefore relief from material suffering is another by-product of chanting.

The *Siksastaka* gives the analogy of a white lotus touched by moonlight *(sreyah-kairava-candrika-vitaranam). Sreyah* means “good fortune”; *kairava* “white lotus”; *Candrika* “moonlight”; and *vitaranam* “spreading. ” When a sincere chanter comes into contact with the moonlight of Krsna’s holy name, the white lotus of auspiciousness blossoms in his or her heart. That white lotuses bloom in the moonlight is an observable phenomenon; some flowers open in sunlight and some, such as the white lotus, open in moonlight. Like the white lotus, our hearts will open to the moonrays of Krsna’s name, and our life will become completely auspicious.

Srila Rupa Gosvami describes in the *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* that something is accepted as auspicious if it produces four effects: it can benefit everyone, it can attract everyone, it produces good qualities, and it bestows a superior form of happiness. Because Krsna’s holy name is nondifferent from Krsna Himself, it produces these four effects.

Srila Prabhupada gives the example that fire burns indiscriminately. Fire will burn a child who does not know the properties of fire as easily as it will burn an adult who does. Similarly, if one comes into contact with Krsna in the form of His holy name, the holy name will act and auspiciousness produces; immediately one will begin to develop all-good qualities. The Bhagavatam (5. 18. 12) states:

*yasyasti bhaktir bhagavaty akincana*  
*sarvair gunais tatra samasate surah*  
*harav abhaktasya kuto mahad-guna*  
*manorathenasati dhavato bahih*

“All the demigods and their exalted qualities, such as religion, knowledge and renunciation, become manifest in the body of one who has developed unalloyed devotion for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Vasudeva. On the other hand, a person devoid of devotional service and engaged in material activities has no good qualities. Even if he is adept at the practice of mystic yoga or the honest endeavor of maintaining his family and relatives, he must be driven by his own mental speculations and must engage in the service of the Lord’s external energy. How can there be any good qualities in such a man? ”

And something else happens: In the heart of devotees who chant Krsna’s holy name, Bhaktidevi, the wife of Lord Krsna, appears along with her two sons, Knowledge and Detachment.

In addition, chanting the Lord’s holy name increases the ocean of transcendental bliss. Just as a large tree can grow from a small seed, the whole ocean of bliss can manifest by coming into contact with devotional service. But it will not fully manifest until we remove the covering of ignorance over our hearts. Still, as we chant we will at first gain a slight glimpse *(abhasa-rupa)* of that ocean, and gradually, as we become increasingly steady in our devotion and chanting, the whole ocean of bliss will manifest.

Srila Rupa Gosvami describes in his *Upadesamrta* that the holy name, form, pastimes, and activities of the Supreme Personality of Godhead are naturally sweet like sugarcane. But for one afflicted by jaundice *(avidya),* nothing tastes sweet. Still, it is wonderful that simply by carefully chanting these sweet holy names every day, the tongue gradually begins to relish them and the disease of *avidya* is destroyed at the root.

Careful daily chanting means attentive chanting. It means meditating on the true glories of the holy name. At every step— from the beginning to the end—a careful chanter can taste nectar *(purnamrtasvadanam).* That nectar is like a cool, refreshing bath during the heat of summer. We have been burning up in the fire of our own lust for sense gratification; the holy name can free us from this fire.

It is our good fortune (*sreyah*) that we can experience the cooling effects of the holy name. The Bhagavatam (12. 3. 51) states:

*kaler dosa-nidhe rajann*  
*asti hy eko mahan gunah*  
*kirtanad eva krsnasya*  
*mukta-sangah param vrajet*

“My dear King, although Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, there is still one good quality about this age: Simply by chanting the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra,* one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom. ”

The more our attraction for chanting increases, the more the blazing fire of material existence will be destroyed in our lives. We will then begin to experience the bliss found in the *sankirtana* of the Lord’s holy names.

**Question: What is the difference between** *gosthanandis* **and** *bhajananandis?*

These are the two types of advanced devotees. One of the characteristics of the highly elevated soul is that he is always engaged in chanting the glories of the Lord. That is their life. B*hajananandis* are always chanting, but they do it in solitude. Even though they may only be chanting for their own benefit, still others are benefiting by their chanting because the very ether that we are living in is purified by their chanting of the holy name of the Lord.

However, *gosthanandis,* who are also always chanting, are always absorbed in spreading the glories of the holy name of the Lord. One can chant by glorifying the holy name of the Lord, glorifying the pastimes of the Lord, or glorifying the qualities of the Lord, because the holy name of the Lord is non-different from the Lord. Therefore, to glorify the pastimes, form, and qualities of the holy name is done by chanting the holy name of the Lord. Such a person’s tongue is always decorated with the name of the Lord by their continuous preaching.

In the Bhagavatam, it is described by Devahuti (3. 33. 7):

Oh, how glorious are they whose tongues are chanting Your holy name! Even if born in the families of dog-eaters, such persons are worshipable. Persons who chant the holy name of Your Lordship must have executed all kinds of austerities and fire sacrifices and achieved all the good manners of the Aryans.

To be chanting the holy name of Your Lordship, they must have bathed at holy places of pilgrimage, studied the Vedas and fulfilled everything required.

We can either chant for our own satisfaction or absorption in the holy name of the Lord, or we can chant for the benefit of others. However, Srila Haridasa Thakura describes that when we chant loudly the holy name of the Lord, then there is much more benefit, since others will hear the transcendental vibration of the holy name.

Although the holy name is non-different from the Lord, it is more powerful when heard from the lips of a pure devotee, because a pure devotee invokes the presence of the Lord by his pure chanting. Therefore, if we come in contact with the glorification of the Lord from the lips of His pure devotee, it can act upon our hearts. The Lord infuses the holy name with the potency to act upon the hearts of others. Thus if we even once hear the holy name of the Lord from a pure devotee of the Lord, we can be infused with a greater potency to chant, and our spiritual life can actually begin and progress.

Ultimately, we will obtain the benediction that Caitanya Maha-prabhu gives, which is to come to the platform of *kirtaniyah sada harih,* always chanting the holy name of the Lord. That is considered to be the perfectional stage of life. Sometimes this perfectional stage is not easily understood by non-devotees, because they cannot imagine what benefit one derives from continuously chanting the holy name of the Lord, nor what benefit anyone else will derive from that continuous chanting.

## Faith—The Price for the Holy Name

I am singing news of the greatest happiness! At the place known as Surabhi Kunja in Sri Navadvipa, the marketplace of the holy name has now been opened and Lord Nityananda Himself is the proprietor. Such wonderful things are going on in that blissful marketplace! Sri Nityananda Prabhu is selling the pure holy name wholesale, merely for the price of one’s faith. Seeing the assembly of devotees eagerly waiting to purchase the name, Lord Nityananda first examines each of them to test their qualification; then He sells them the name by bargaining for His price accordingly.

O my dear friends! If you really want to buy this pure holy name, then just come along with me, for I am now going to meet with this Nityananda Mahajana. Thus you will finally be able to acquire the pure holy name. I will also take my due commission, and in this way all three of us will fulfill our desires. Sri Nityananda Prabhu is so extraordinarily merciful—accepting only one’s faith in the holy name, He bestows the topmost divine bliss. When Nitai sees a tear welling in someone’s eye upon chanting the name of “Gaura! ” He instantly gives His support to that person; indeed, He bestows all divine opulences. He gives that person genuine realization of the pure teachings of Sri Krsna as found in the *Bhagavad-gita* and *Srimad-Bhagavatam.*

While displaying all this inconceivable mercy, He pays no attention to one’s caste, material wealth, mundane knowledge, or physical ability. Now, dear friends, please reject all of *maya’s* entangling snares. If you are a householder, then just remain at your home; if you are renounced, then just live in the forest. Either way, nothing more will trouble you. We no longer need to fear the terrible Age of Quarrel, for the most merciful Lord Nityananda gives the holy name to anyone and everyone—even to the lowest among men.

Bhaktivinoda loudly calls out and proclaims to all, “OTHER THAN THE LOTUS FEET OF LORD NITYANANDA, THERE IS NO SHELTER! ” (*Dalaler Gita)*

*Dalaler Gita* is the “Song of the Marketplace and the Broker of the Holy Name. ” When we think of a marketplace, we think of a place where people buy and sell goods. Nothing in a marketplace is free; everything comes at a price. A marketplace also has brokers, agents who go out to bring in customers to the wholesalers. Brokers usually work on commission, getting a percentage of each sale.

But in the marketplace described in the *Dalaler Gita,* there is only one good—the holy name—and one currency. You cannot purchase the holy name with your strength or material wealth or intelligence. Nothing material can invoke the Lord’s presence or the presence of His holy name.

We often hear that we are meant to distribute the holy name freely. Why, then, would it be found in a marketplace, where goods are bought and sold? But the phrase “marketplace of the holy name” is used to illustrate something else about how the holy name is distributed. That is, although the Lord freely distributes the holy name, if we want to attain its full benefit, we must purchase it with the currency of *Sraddha,* faith.

The broker (or sellers or traders) distribute only the pure holy name. But Bhaktivinoda Thakura also describes that if one tries to purchase that pure holy name with material desire—counterfeit currency—then the holy name itself will become counterfeit. One cannot purchase the holy name with material desire. Lord Nityananda, who is the principal trader in this marketplace, is therefore looking for only one thing when He distributes the pure holy name. He pays no attention to caste, material wealth, mundane knowledge, or physical ability (*jati*, *dhana, vidya, bala na kore apeksa).* All these are irrelevant. He seeks only those who can pay the correct currency of their faith in the holy name.

Although Lord Nityananda is the principal trader, there are other workers in the marketplace. There is the *dalal,* or broker, for example. There are also traveling salesmen who take the goods from the trader on credit and then go out and distribute them, bringing back their earnings and receiving their commission. Or, if the traveling salesmen themselves possess sufficient currency, they can purchase the goods in advance from the broker, and then go out and sell the holy name to others, trading it for *their* currency.

And there is no currency exchange in the market. You cannot exchange money, intelligence, or anything else for faith. By nature every living being has faith, but where do they place it? Those who have faith in the material world trust fallible soldiers. Lord Nityananda specifically searches for those who have even a little faith in the holy name.

In this marketplace there are barbers, tailors, and street sweepers. Bhaktivinoda Thakura considers himself a street sweeper. Anyone can work in this marketplace, but to become qualified for even menial work, one must become free of material motive. When one goes out to beg alms on the Lord’s behalf, one must beg alms in the way that Nityananda and Haridasa Thakura begged alms. And when one goes out to sell the holy name, one should only want one profit: an increase in one’s own faith. Thus it is described that workers in this marketplace discuss only topics related to *bhakti* because such talks enhance their faith.

Additionally, everyone working in the marketplace must wear a badge with Gaura’s name on it. That is, each person has to chant Gaura’s name. There are even workers in the marketplace who check to make sure that everyone’s currency is authentic.

And if we are faithless? We should know that faith is awakened in the association of devotees.

*nitya-siddha krsna-prema ’sadhya’ kabhu naya*  
*sravanadi-suddha-citte karaye udaya*

“Pure love for Krsna is eternally established in the hearts of the living entities. It is not something to be gained from another source. When the heart is purified by hearing and chanting, this love naturally awakens. ” (Cc. *Madhya* 22. 107)

The faith that lives naturally in our heart is brought out when we associate with devotees who themselves have strong faith and realization. Therefore, if Lord Nityananda sees that someone has even a *little* faith, He is anxious to give that person the holy name.

Furthermore, Lord Nityananda is so merciful that His method of distribution itself invokes faith in those who do not have it. This is in the mood of the instructions Lord Caitanya gave Lord Nityananda: *“bolo ’krsna, ’ bhajo krsna, koro krsna-siksa. ”* Lord Nityananda goes out, and with tears in His eyes, begs everyone, *prabhura ajnay, bhai, magi ei bhiksa:* “My dear brothers, please, I beg of you, this is the only alms that I beg: Please chant Krsna’s name, worship Krsna, and make Krsna your life. ” Anyone seeing Nityananda would immediately become overwhelmed by His mood and faith would be created in his or her heart.

Such people would think, “Here is someone who is begging something from me, but the only thing He wants is for me to chant Krsna’s name and to worship Krsna. ”

The *acaryas* explain that when Nityananda goes out to sell the holy name, first He gives a free sample of His ecstasy of love for Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Sometimes people go out to the streets to hand out free samples of shampoo, laundry detergent, or whatever. The purpose of such distribution is to entice you to come back and purchase a bigger bottle at the full price. Lord Nityananda used the same method, giving out small samples of His deep compassion and His intense service attitude toward Lord Caitanya. He wanted to give everyone the holy name, and this intensity caused anyone who saw Him to become affected by His desire. Just seeing Lord Nityananda awakened faith in others’ hearts. Thus He gave both the holy name and the faith required to taste it.

After one had simply chanted the holy name once with even a little faith, Lord Nityananda would say, “Now he has become the possessor of the holy name. ”

*ek-bar dekhle cakse jal,*  
*’gaura’ bole’ nitai den sakala sambal*

“When Nitai sees a tear welling in someone’s eye upon chanting the name of ‘Gaura! ’ He instantly gives His support to that person; indeed, He bestows all divine opulences. ” (*Dalaler Gita,* 7)

Depending on the amount of currency one has, Lord Nityananda gives the holy name in that proportion. But Lord Nityananda does something even more wonderful. If the purchaser of the holy name chants it the prescribed number of times with *Sraddha,* Lord Nityananda returns to him everything he paid! In this way, the purchaser now has the currency to come back and purchase more of the holy name, because his chanting with *Sraddha* increases his faith. And when Lord Nityananda gives him back what he paid, he then has *nistha. Nistha* means *naiscalya,* steadiness.

With the currency of *nistha* not only do we obtain the holy name, but we see the form of the holy name, which is Krsna’s original form. When one’s mind is fixed, steady, and controlled while chanting, the Lord’s form appears in the mind. We thus experience both the holy name and its form. As we continue to chant with *nistha,* our faith increases, Lord Nityananda returns to us what we paid, and we then begin to chant with *ruci* (real taste).

On the platform of *ruci,* we not only experience the holy name and its form but also the Lord’s qualities. Then at *asakti* we experience all of this along with the Lord’s pastimes.

After *asakti* comes *bhava,* where we experience the holy name, its form, qualities, and pastimes, and our own *rasa or* relationship with Krsna. Finally, when we chant on the platform of *prema,* we experience all of the above along with all the ecstatic emotions natural to our relationship with Krsna. This can all be obtained by those who begin to chant with *Sraddha,* the currency required to purchase the pure name *(suddha-nama).*

Automatically by having firm faith, chanting the holy names, and engaging in devotional service, all our subsidiary duties are fulfilled. This type of faith is favorable for the execution of devotional service, and it is awakened in the association of devotees, where one gradually becomes convinced about the devotional process. It is very difficult to develop faith without the association of devotees.

In his famous verse, Srila Rupa Gosvami lists the progressive stages of spiritual advancement. In his discussion on faith, he says that new faith may not be firm; it may still be soft and pliable. Soft faith leaves us open to nondevotional influences. What gives strength to pliable faith is when that faith is placed in the value of *sadhu-sanga.* We must have at least a little faith in the importance of associating with devotees, and *sadhu-sanga* is the stage of advancement he lists next.

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura writes that there are two means by which such faith is awakened: by previous contact with devotional service, we have spontaneous faith in hearing the teachings of devotional service from the scriptures; and by a devotee’s forceful preaching. Whichever way we examine it, however, faith is awakened by the association of devotees either in this life or in a previous life.

From the devotees, we hear about the process of devotional service, and gradually, as we become convinced, we enter the stage of *bhajana-kriya.* Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that there are different levels of *bhajana-kriya,* which he refers to as the *panca-samskaras. Samskaras* are purificatory acts that must be performed if we are to gain gradual elevation in spiritual life. Due to contact with matter, our existence in this material world is impure; therefore it is every human being’s duty to voluntarily accept acts of purification.

The five *samskaras* are *tapah, pundra, nama, mantra,* and *yaga. Tapah* refers to austerities. We must voluntarily accept inconvenience or physical austerity for a higher purpose. Sometimes we think that austerities mean we have to put ourselves into a suffering condition, but Srila Prabhupada explained in a lecture in 1976 that one need not place himself in a circle of fire in the middle of the summer, or submerge himself in cold water in the middle of winter, in order to perform some austerities. Rather, our austerity is to strictly follow our spiritual master’s instructions. The results of all other austerities are then automatically achieved. The purpose of all austerity is to reduce the bodily conception of life, which is the cause of material bondage. It is extremely difficult to overcome *ahankara,* false ego. Because of false ego we identify ourselves with the body, its extensions, and to everything we feel we require to satisfy the body’s needs. Of course, when we satisfy the Lord by our austerities, we feel spiritually satisfied and automatically our bodily conception of life decreases. This is actually the definition of *Sraddha: to* have faith that by pleasing the Lord, all our subsidiary duties are automatically fulfilled.

*Pundra* (referring to *urdhva-pundra)* means to decorate the body with Vaisnava *tilaka.* It also means “effulgence. ” When we reduce our bodily conception of life by increasing our attachment to the Supreme Lord through hearing and chanting, we become happy, our anxieties diminish, and we develop an effulgence.

Those on the bodily platform experience so much anxiety, and sometimes foolish people think that the only way to free themselves from anxiety is to renounce everything. But that is not how to become free of anxiety—especially if one maintains material desires in the heart. We are supposed to have faith in the process of devotional service, which will gradually awaken renunciation in the heart. But if this faith in the devotional process has not yet been awakened, and if we simply renounce whatever troubles us in our life, it will only make our heart hard. Renunciation is something that must awaken in the heart with the dawn of devotional attachment.

So if we don’t renounce the things that bother us, what do we do? Krsna says that nonpermanent happiness and distress disappear in due course. The temporary appearances of happiness and distress are like the appearance and disappearance of winter and summer seasons. “They arise from sense perception, O Scion of Bharata, and one must learn to tolerate them without being disturbed. ” (Bg. 2. 14) These disturbances come and go, but because devotees have faith in chanting and hearing, they continue to chant and hear with tolerance of the disturbances.

For example, Kholaveca Sridhara sold banana leaf cups and plates for a living. Every day he set up his wares, then took fifty percent of his earnings to maintain himself and used the other fifty percent to worship the Ganges. At that time as now, selling banana leaf cups and plates was not a lucrative business. There are many banana trees in Bengal and Sridhara had a lot of competition. Still, he set up shop each day and sold what he could. He was not disturbed by his meager profit; he was peaceful. Actually, he was not drawing his satisfaction from selling banana leaf cups and plates but from chanting Hare Krsna. He sold his wares simply as a matter of duty, and he remained detached from the fruits of his work. Thus he had no material anxiety, even though he was poverty-stricken.

By the power of devotional service, our material anxieties gradually decrease. Why? Because gradually we learn to find our true shelter in hearing and chanting about Krsna. If we chant and hear attentively with devotion, we will easily tolerate the upheavals of the material world.

If we awaken our taste for chanting by faith and by associating with those who have firm faith, and if we voluntarily accept inconveniences to reduce our bodily conception, we will gradually experience internal satisfaction and become effulgent.

The third *samskara* is nama, which refers to becoming initiated into the chanting of the holy name. We are meant to take up *tapah* and *pundra* before initiation. We should also have firm faith in the chanting before formally accepting the holy name at initiation.

The *sastras* advise that the holy name not be given to those who are faithless. The faithless tend to offend the holy name and thus drag themselves down. The spiritual master who initiates faithless persons is also dragged down by his own ninth offense to the holy name. It is therefore the spiritual master’s duty to imbue disciples with faith in the holy name before initiating them.

Why would anyone preach to the faithless? Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that sometimes persons want to take initiation to gain prestige, to remove past sins, or for material gain. Such persons are not approaching the guru or the holy name out of faith but with material motive. The spiritual master should not initiate such persons. Having no concept of the holy name’s true value, such persons will only remain indifferent to the holy name, and that will be an offense.

Thus before accepting *nama* we must have both faith and at least some taste for chanting, at which point we can accept the vows to chant a minimum of sixteen rounds of the Hare Krsna mantra a day. When we take our vows, we should note the word “minimum” and always try to increase.

At initiation, disciples are also given a spiritual name along with *nama.* This name is intended to help them remember Krsna. Later, if we go on chanting the holy name with faith and steadiness, we can receive the *diksa-mantra* (Gayatri mantras). These Gayatri mantras help us overcome *anarthas* in the heart and to move toward continuous chanting of the holy name. When we accept these *diksa-mantras,* we also accept the vow to chant them regularly and at specified auspicious times. Accepting these mantras qualifies us to perform *yaga* (or deity worship), which is the fifth samskara. By worshipping the Deities we establish and increase our relationship with the Supreme Lord in a mood of service. All of this takes place at *bhajana-kriya.*

Thus at *bhajana-kriya* we engage in hearing, chanting, remembering, worshipping, offering prayers, and serving the Deities. And because we have faith in these devotional processes and we continue hearing and chanting with that faith, the *anarthas* in the heart are gradually removed. It is not possible for us to clear our own *anarthas,* but if we have faith in hearing and chanting about Krsna, then Krsna will act from within the heart and remove them. Again, *Sraddha* is the most important ingredient.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes in *Harinama Cintamani* that if we do not awaken faith, and through *nama* initiation we are given the holy name, we will inevitably commit offenses to the Lord’s holy name. Giving initiation to someone who does not possess faith is like giving fancy clothes to a monkey; it will simply rip them up. If one places pearls before swine, they will trample them.

Have faith in chanting the holy name and understand its value. Don’t see initiation as a way to gain prestige. Don’t look for recognition as a Vaisnava. If you seek recognition, you will soon want people to serve you. And you will think you don’t have to worry about whatever bad reputation you had in the past, because now you are in a perfect position for your own enjoyment. Such a mentality means that you want to engage the holy name in your own service. It means you have no appreciation for the real value of the holy name—that it can take you to the transcendental platform and establish you in your eternal relationship with Krsna.

Krsna has created this temporary material world by His own will to provide the living entities a place to exercise their minute independence and to enjoy separately from Him. Krsna has nothing to do with the material world, although He is interested in the suffering living entities who live in it. As Krsna has nothing to do with the material world, so the living entities have nothing to do with it either. They are meant only to live eternally with Krsna in the spiritual world. But under the influence of Krsna’s illusory energy, they identify themselves as residents of this world. Persons who have no faith in the holy name want only to remain here, engaging the Lord’s holy name in their own service and thus fulfilling their desires to enjoy matter. They obviously have no understanding.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura says that just as Lord Caitanya first awakened faith in Jagai and Madhai and then initiated them, it is the duty of the Vaisnava preachers to first awaken faith in others and then to initiate them into the chanting of the holy name. It is actually our duty to give people in whom faith has been awakened the holy name.

And at initiation, we should not foolishly think that now that we *have* the holy name, we have somehow increased our prestige. Rather, have firm faith and execute your prescribed duties, primary among them to chant the holy name. Perform all your duties prescribed according to your position—whether you are a *brahmacari, grhastha, vanaprastha*, or *sannyasi,* without disturbance. If you perform your Krsna conscious activities with firm faith in hearing and chanting about Krsna, you will see that gradually you will lose the natural attachment connected to these duties and become more attached to Krsna.

How does all this fit in with the idea that the holy name should be freely distributed? Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that the Lord’s holy name can be given to anyone. Lord Caitanya Himself did not discriminate in His distribution of krsna-nama. The holy name can be given to everyone and everyone can chant (think of the disciple who saw the washerwomen, fishermen, and boys chanting the holy name). But to receive *nama,* initiation, means that one must first follow a process that ultimately awakens one’s faith. And when it is time to accept *nama,* a follower may offer wealth, words, or intelligence to the spiritual master and the spiritual master may accept it for Gauranga’s service, but all the spiritual master is really seeking is essence: faith.

A spiritual master who becomes attracted to the material offerings is actually fallen. If a spiritual master is looking at a potential disciple and measuring his or her wealth or knowledge, he will think, “Should I be interested in this person? What does he have to give me? ” Such motivation prevents one from seeing a person’s true qualification; such a guru would not be able to recognize genuine faith.

When Lord Caitanya sent Lord Nityananda and Haridasa Thakura out to the streets, He told them, “Tell everyone you meet to worship Krsna, chant Krsna’s name, make Krsna their life, and teach others about Krsna. ” He then instructed His preachers, “Go out every day, and when you come back in the evening, tell Me how many people have taken the goods you distributed. ”

On Lord Caitanya’s order, the two would go out to the streets and beg charity: “O people of faith! O people of faith! By the order of Lord Gauranga, O brothers, I beg these three requests of you: Chant ‘Krsna! ’, worship Krsna, and teach others about Krsna. ” They would then return to Lord Caitanya and report who had accepted the holy name.

It is with this kind of faith that we also have to distribute the holy name. Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, “Householders can also go out and distribute the holy name, but a characteristic of their faith is that in their private discussions, they talk only about *bhakti* and Krsna. ”

Of course, householders need to have some way to maintain themselves; that is, they may sell other goods during part of the day and not necessarily say to those around them, “Please give me your faith. ” But when they go out to sell the holy name, then they should ask only for people’s faith. If they ask for anything else in exchange for the holy name, it proves that they themselves do not have faith in the holy name. How, then, will they distribute it? Faith is the only currency that can be accepted during sales, purchases, and when receiving one’s commission.

We have come to understand the word *Sraddha to* mean *faith,* but there is a much deeper explanation of the word. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura uses an analogy to explain this deeper meaning: When *Sraddha* is born, it is like the birth of a baby girl. When a newborn girl appears, her parents are careful to protect her from severe cold, extreme heat, too much exposure to sun, harmful creatures, hunger, and thirst. In the same way, *Sraddha* must be protected from the harmful misconceptions of karma (desire for fruitive gain) and *jnana* (impersonal conceptions).

Srila Rupa Gosvami describes the most important characteristic of devotional service *(Brs.* 1. 1. 11):

*anyabhilasita-sunyam*  
*jnana-karmady-anavrtam*  
*anukulyena krsnanu-*  
*silanam bhaktir uttama*

“The primary characteristic of *bhaktas* offering *uttama-bhakti,* pure devotional service, is that they are performing favorable activities meant only for Krsna’s satisfaction. Such devotional service must be rendered *anyabhilasita-sunyam,* free from any motivation other than the desire to please Krsna, including motivations such as the desire for fruitive results or the desire for relief from suffering (liberation). ”

Desire is an eternal companion of the soul; therefore we must direct it properly. The only *genuine* desire of a pure spirit soul is to satisfy the Lord by rendering Him favorable service for His pleasure. Any admixtures are compared to the bad elements from which the newborn girl must be protected.

**Question: How does faith take birth in a living entity’s heart?**

*Sraddha* cannot awaken in the heart except through the association of devotees. That association does not even have to be consciously sought out. Even accidental association (*ajnata-sukrti*) can allow us to hear from a devotee or render him or her some service. The *Prabhupada-lilamrta* gives an example of that: When Srila Prabhupada was still living at 26 Second Avenue, a drunk walked in, and because he had previously seen that there was no toilet paper in the bathroom, brought several rolls.
