After putting the toilet paper into the bathroom, he left. Srila Prabhupada said, “Oh, just see, his devotional service has now begun. He was not in order, but he has given something to Krsna. ”

When we come into a devotee’s association after suffering so much distress in life, we learn that Krsna is the ultimate goal of life and that there is nothing higher than Krsna’s lotus feet. Our faith has awakened. We then begin to execute devotional service with the strength we have received from that devotee. And by the continued association of devotees who are more advanced than us, our *Sraddha* will be protected and increase. Once *Sraddha* has taken birth in a living entity’s heart, that living entity must always remain in the association of devotees at every stage of his or her devotional service—even up to the topmost platform of *prema*—in order to nicely protect Sraddhadevi.

**Question: How should householders achieve steady faith?**

In order to achieve steady faith, householders should hear about Krsna every day in the association of devotees. Maharaja Yudhisthira asked this same question of Narada Muni. He said, “I am a *grha-mudha-dhi,* I have the tendency to forget the goal of life, and you are *brahmacari.* Please, therefore, describe to me the means by which one can achieve salvation in this world according to scriptural descriptions. ” Narada Muni gave Yudhisthira Maharaja the same instruction I have just given: householders must hear daily about the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s pastimes in the association of devotees. By hearing about the Lord’s superior activities, the householders will gradually awaken from their dreamlike condition, just as persons gradually wake from their dreams at the end of night.

The relationships of this world are simply a perverted reflection of the eternal relationships in the spiritual world, but without hearing about spiritual relationships in relation to Krsna’s pastimes, it’s very hard to understand that. People tend to accept the contents of their dreams as real while they are in the midst of them. Similarly, when we come together with friends, family, and society, and are face-to-face with the daily obligations and commitments these relationships evoke, it all seems so real to us. But they are not real because they are based on the body; these relationships are actually temporary. We need to awaken from our dreamlike absorption in our mundane relationships and seek out steady faith. The only way to strengthen our faith is to hear about Krsna in all *His* relationships: His relationship with Nanda Maharaja, with Mother Yasoda, the cowherd boys, the *gopis,* and with the transcendental realm of Vrndavana. All of these relationships are permanent, eternal, and fully satisfying to the soul. We are meant to participate in them. Having once achieved this understanding, and with strong faith and full absorption in Krsna’s family and village life, we will never have to come back to this world of repeated birth and death, where we taste mundane relationships sometimes in human families, sometimes in dog families, and sometimes in elephant families.

This is why Narada Muni says that the primary responsibility of householders is to hear the Lord’s pastimes in the association of Vaisnavas. Doing this will increase their faith and become their *sadhana.* It is on the strength of hearing such descriptions that one gains the conviction to chant Hare Krsna.

It is not necessary for householders to renounce their family relationships, however. *Jnanis* practice renunciation because they see everything as a source of misery and lust and want nothing to do with either. Devotees do not renounce anything that can be engaged in Krsna’s devotional service. Rather, they can care for their families and possessions if they see that by maintaining them the body remains fit for hearing and chanting about Krsna. This will allow them to achieve perfection. It is this striving for perfection that gives householders their impetus to work; it is the root of their enthusiasm, because they know their work will not be a source of misery. As householders live in this way, focused on hearing and chanting about Krsna, their faith increases. It is important that householders adopt activities that increase their faith.

**Question: Once we have faith, are we then free from all suffering and punishment?**

There are so many examples in the scriptures of devotees who had this faith in the Lord and who accepted the Lord’s punishment as His mercy. Think of Haridasa Thakura being threatened by the Kazi. The Kazi said, “You were born a Muslim. Why are you walking the streets chanting ‘Krsna Krsna’? Give it up! If you don’t give it up, I will have you beaten! ”

Haridasa Thakura told him, “All religious principles are ultimately meant for the Supreme Lord’s satisfaction, and chanting God’s names is the ultimate religious principle. ”

The Kazi responded, “If you do not give up this chanting, I will beat you! ”

But Haridasa had such firm faith that he said, “You can cut my body to pieces, but I will never give up chanting Krsna’s name. ”

Angered, the Kazi gave the order: “Have him beaten in all the marketplaces! ” Haridasa was then taken and whipped in twenty-two different marketplaces so that everyone could see what happens to those who give up the Muslim faith and chant Krsna’s names. But although they tried to kill Haridasa, Haridasa did not die. He accepted the beatings, and remained absorbed in chanting the holy name with firm faith.

After some time, Haridasa Thakura could see that the guards who were beating him were distressed, and he asked them why. Imagine being beaten and being more concerned about those who are beating you than about yourself!

The guards said, “We have been ordered to beat you to death, but you won’t die. If you don’t die, *we* will be punished. *We* will be beaten like we are beating you. ”

“Oh, that’s all you want—for me to die? Then let me die. ” And Haridasa immediately manifest all the external symptoms of someone who has died.

Thinking they had finally killed him, the guards wanted to throw him in the Ganga, but when they tried to pick up his body, it was so heavy that none of them could lift it. Even several men together could not lift Haridasa’s body. This is because the Supreme Lord had entered Haridasa Thakura’s body. Finally, a large group of men, by Krsna’s mercy, were able to pick up Haridasa’s body and throw it into the river.

Of course, Haridasa Thakura wasn’t really dead—he was still absorbed in his chanting of the holy name—but in the meantime his body floated down the Ganga. Eventually, after his body had washed ashore and the symptoms of life had returned to his body, Haridasa began to chant aloud. Everyone witnessed this, including the guards who called both the king and the Kazi to witness it as well.

The king immediately understood that Haridasa was no ordinary soul, and he said, “I have made a grievous mistake. ” He bowed before Haridasa Thakura and said, “I can understand that you are a genuine saintly person. From this day on, you may continue chanting Krsna’s names. ”

Haridasa Thakura then left and continued chanting. The *brahmanas* who had witnessed the beating stopped him and expressed their sorrow at the pain he had incurred. However, Haridasa replied, “Why are you lamenting? There is no need. I deserve to be punished much more than I was. I was taken to prison and I stood there and listened to blasphemy and did not give up my life. Therefore, the Lord was merciful to me! He only gave me a small token of what I should have suffered. ”

Before being beaten, Haridasa Thakura had been dragged to prison in chains. Being chained, he was helpless to move even his hands or his legs. Still, Haridasa Thakura accepted the beating as the Lord’s mercy, even though he had been helpless to avoid what he considered his crime—listening to blasphemy and not acting on it. He felt no resentment whatsoever for the punishment he received, but simply accepted it as the complete mercy of the Lord. A devotee has such faith.

Another example of a devotee with such faith is the *brahmacari* who Srivasa Pandita brought into the *kirtana* in his home. Lord Caitanya became angry at the *brahmacari* and reprimanded him: “Who does this *brahmacari* think he is to come into My *kirtana!* Does he think that simply by performing austerities and drinking milk that he is qualified to see My *kirtana! "*

The *brahmacari* thought, “This is so wonderful! I am fortunate to be reprimanded by the Lord. What great fortune have I earned in my previous life to be punished by the Lord in this way? I am such an offender. I *deserve* to be punished. ” Understanding the heart of the *brahmacari,* the Lord then accepted him as one of His associates and placed His lotus feet on his head.

This kind of faith is the difference between devotees and nondevotees. Devotees never resent being punished by the Lord, but they work sincerely without consideration of defeat or hopelessness. Such devotees will surely be promoted to the stage of pure Krsna consciousness.

**Question: What is the difference between a** *sraddhabhasa* **and a** *sraddhavan?*

*Kanistha-adhikaris, or* devotees still on the lower platform of devotional service, are known as *sraddhabhasa* because they have no firm faith. Statements about renunciation, austerities, exclusive devotion, or full surrender are theoretical to such persons. They do not have enough faith to understand that surrender, renunciation, and austerities can bring about the result they desire.

Of course, its not that renunciation or austerities or conditional surrender produce *prema.* In order to be successful in achieving *prema-bhakti,* devotees must have unflinching faith in the Supreme Lord. The Seventh Canto describes that even though we may be expert in all brahminical activities—we may be religious, moral, detached, humble, tolerant, prepared to offer sacrifices, and know the scriptures—if we are proud of our position and have not surrendered body, mind, and words to the Supreme Lord, we are considered lower than a person who, although born in a dog-eater family, has completely dedicated everything to Krsna’s service. False *brahmanas* cannot even purify themselves, what to speak of others, whereas those born in families of dog-eaters but who have surrendered body, mind, and words to the Lord’s service can purify not only themselves but their entire family for generations before and after themselves.

Those who possess such firm faith and who sincerely want to dedicate their lives to serving Krsna are called *sraddhavan.*

**Question: The ninth offense in chanting is to give the holy name to unqualified people. But how can we save others without giving them the holy name?**

The ninth offense is to *instruct the intimate glories of* the holy name to the faithless. It is also an offense to initiate faithless persons into the chanting. But to present the Lord’s holy name to others in order to give them the opportunity to hear it and be purified is not offensive to the holy name. If one hears the Lord’s holy name in the association of devotees, that association will help one develop the desire to hear the holy name’s intimate glories. But we should be careful: It is an offense to describe the intimate glories of the holy name and the Lord’s intimate pastimes to persons with no faith. The holy name and Krsna Himself are nondifferent. Therefore, the holy name gradually flowers into the Lord’s intimate pastimes. But only persons with full faith in the holy name should hear such intimate pastimes.

## Chanting Purely

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura explains that if we are not chanting the Lord’s holy name in complete ecstatic love for Him, manifesting symptoms of *prema,* it is because we are committing offenses and still have *anarthas.* Therefore we should examine our devotional service in relation to our chanting to judge whether we are making progress. If we are not tasting the holy name but are still trying deliberately to chant to free ourselves from offenses, then such chanting will give us the means by which we can become free. Chanting is not a mindless activity.

Srila Prabhupada says that the Lord’s holy name transcends the mind and intelligence because it has descended from the spiritual world and is nondifferent from the Lord. However, if we want to progress in chanting, we have to engage the mind and use our intelligence to follow the correct process. If we do not follow this process, we can chant mindlessly for many births and never attain the real goal of chanting.

Whatever you do—whether you are selling books, cooking, or worshiping the Deities—you should know that your duties are meant to help you develop your attraction to hearing and chanting. It is the Lord’s holy name that has the power to clean the heart. Our duties are meant to be performed in relation to chanting. The real fruit of these duties is an increased attraction to and meditation on the Lord’s holy name.

**Question: When chanting with the intention of trying to increase our attraction to and meditation on the holy name, how can we remain attentive?**

It is always good to chant in an atmosphere where the mind cannot be distracted. In fact, Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura describes that there are different obstacles to be overcome while chanting. One is called *laya* (the tendency to sleep). Another is called *viksepa* (the tendency to be distracted during the performance of *kirtana or japa).* This tendency, of course, also includes distractions arising from the mind, so both external and internal distractions. The internal distractions are the unwanted thoughts that continuously pull us away from hearing Krsna’s name. These distractions are harder to control than external distractions. Next there is *kasaya,* the innate tendency toward pride, anger, greed, and lust. Such people are also free from *rasasvada,* the tendency to engage in sense gratification rather than to be absorbed in *kirtana.* The last is *apratipatti,* which really means lack of taste even when there are no distractions or fatigue.

Before we begin chanting, we must first convince ourselves that any thoughts that arise while chanting that are not helping us become more fixed on the holy name are unwanted. Identifying them may be a challenge in itself, since our tendency is to justify them, especially when they are connected to our service. Nonetheless, we must convince ourselves that they are unwanted while we are chanting.

Actually, there are progressive steps to chanting. First we should concentrate simply on hearing the holy name. By hearing continuously very attentively, meditation on the holy name will deepen to meditation on the Lord’s form. While we may now find ourselves meditating more on our service in this world, we should know that there are more elevated levels of service that we can perform that reveal themselves to us when we learn to deeply meditate on Krsna’s name—service such as assisting the Vraja-gopis by perhaps making flower garlands, carrying messages, or some other type of personal service rendered to the Divine Couple. Such elevated meditation cannot be forced. Still, if we want to achieve it, we must begin with the first step of focusing the mind in attentive hearing.

Actually, focusing the mind is the second step. The first steps begins before we pick up our beads: we have to first identify what for us constitute unwanted thoughts, and then convince ourselves that they are indeed unwanted. This takes work, and then whenever a thought comes to mind that we have classified as unwanted, we have to deliberately remove it from our attention and return to attentive hearing of the holy name.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura lists three things that are required if we are to make progress in chanting: chanting in the association of devotees who have taste, freeing ourselves from both internal and external distractions, and steadiness and determination.

To free ourselves from external distractions, Bhaktivinoda Thakura suggests that we chant in a place conducive for concentrated chanting. Don’t chant where there the surroundings will stimulate thoughts about the other things you have to do. Obviously, don’t chant in front of the television, or with a magazine or newspaper open in front of you, or in front of your computer, or in front of *anything* that steals your attention away from Krsna’s name.

Usually the most conducive place to chant is in the temple room, because the temple room is dedicated for worshiping the Lord. If it is not easy for you to control your mind, it is good to chant in the association of other devotees. One has to have self-discipline to chant alone, because we have a natural tendency to relax the body and mind when we are by ourselves.

The great endeavor to control the mind may actually contribute to fatigue while chanting, but what especially contributes to fatigue is that we have no taste. If there is no taste, chant anyway as a matter of duty and taste will eventually come. We will not be able to force taste for the holy name. Taste comes only by the Lord’s will. To the degree that we are determined to chant despite a lack of taste or that we become tired by the effort, we will gradually begin to feel progress in our chanting. To help focus the mind, we can look at the deity or a photo of the deity.

When we choose to chant is also important. Chanting should ideally be done early in the mornings. The early morning hours are in the mode of goodness, daytime hours are in the mode of passion, and night hours are in the mode of ignorance. If you don’t chant during the early morning hours, you should know that you are not chanting during a time conducive to mode-of-goodness activities.

It is also a good habit to be determined that we will not do anything else in the day until we have completed our *japa* quota. Not finishing all our rounds in a sitting should be the exception rather than the rule. Some people’s work schedules may make such a thing impossible, but we should chant as many rounds at one time as we can, and in the case of the person with the job, the rest of the rounds should be completed when he or she returns from work in the evening.

Further, we should analyze our *japa* habits. Sometimes we speak to others during *japa,* but we would be better to speak to them after we have finished our rounds. If everyone agrees not to disturb anyone else’s *japa,* then everyone will be free to chant their quota without distraction. If we are motivated, we will find the means to chant without distraction. And if we want to go beyond simple listening to real meditation, we have to at least start with listening and learn to control the mind. Srila Prabhupada said that there are three important ways to fix the mind on the holy name: listen, listen, listen.

Attentive chanting leads to meditation on the Lord’s qualities, form, and pastimes. If we chant with a deep intention and eagerness to listen attentively, gradually we will appreciate the Lord’s qualities, then His form, and eventually, His pastimes. When each of these three aspects of the Lord’s form manifests, they will become part of our meditation and we will be able to remember them while we chant. But we cannot force these aspects to appear. That is why Srila Prabhupada emphasizes the importance of attentive hearing while chanting. If we can simply learn to hear the holy name, the mind will be clear and stable.

The Lord can manifest by His own sweet will. Perhaps at first we will only experience a temporary or flickering manifestation, but as we continue with attentive chanting, we will feel more and more attraction to Krsna and He will show Himself more and more. Mold your life in such a way that you can give more attention to the holy name. To chant purely is the spiritual master’s most important instruction. By thinking deeply about this instruction, you can immediately think of ways to adjust your life and give more attention to chanting. For some, this may require juggling the duties you perform during the day. It is worth doing that. Those who give the required time to chant will be given the sense of responsibility to be even more attentive in their chanting.

By chanting attentively our lives can become perfect. The more we become conscious of Krsna, the more detached we will become toward all other duties. We must perform our duties, but we should do so in a spirit of detachment.

Don’t renounce your duties artificially, however. Artificial renunciation is in the mode of passion and will not help us. Performing duties in the spirit of detachment does not mean renouncing them nor indifference toward the responsibilities we bear. Detachment means knowing that the ultimate goal of our various duties is to achieve taste for chanting Krsna’s holy name. Our duties and our chanting are intimately connected. Since we do not yet have pure attachment for the holy name, we have to perform our duties. If we had pure, exclusive attachment to the holy name of the Lord, there would be nothing else for us to do; we would be completely satisfied. A person who is fully satisfied has no duties. The order to chant is the most important instruction the spiritual master has given.

**Question: If we are chanting properly and listening attentively, why do we not see more positive results in our other activities?**

Krsna wants to show us that we are not the doers; He wants to make us more dependent on the holy name. The holy name is not dutybound to bestow anything on us, such as better results in our activities. Rather, the real result of our other activities—whether we are doing book distribution or other services—is to increase our dependence on the holy name. Don’t think that just because you are chanting the holy name you will automatically be able to distribute a lot of books. Book distribution can become an external activity if we are not careful. Certainly it is purifying to distribute books, but it can also become external if we lose our sense of dependence on Krsna and are thinking more of the results than the service itself.

It can be discouraging if we try to please guru and Krsna yet do not see results. However, it is not the external results that satisfy guru and Krsna. Rather, they are satisfied when they see us becoming more dependent on Krsna. If we think only of results, our service can become mechanical, and we will gradually begin to think about recognition, attachment to results, and profit. We have to carefully uproot such motivations. Results come when Krsna sends them. When He sees that our motivation is sincere, He will give us whatever results He likes. Everything is completely His choice. All we can choose is whether or not we wish to serve Him voluntarily.

Srila Prabhupada gave us the practical service of distributing his books, and therefore we are able to assist him in spreading Lord Krsna’s glories everywhere. That service is not meant to make us proud or to draw us away from the heart of our practice; it is meant to help us increase our attraction for hearing and chanting. By hearing and chanting, our hearts will become cleansed.

*dharmah svanusthitah pumsam*  
*visvaksena-kathasu yah*  
*notpadayed yadi ratim*  
*srama eva hi kevalam*

“The occupational activities a man performs according to his own position are only so much useless labor if they do not provoke attraction for the message of the Personality of Godhead.” *(Bhag.* 1. 2. 8)

Any service that helps us increase such attraction is purifying. Distributing books is particularly purifying because it forces us to speak about Krsna.

To explain further how to make progress in chanting and to become purified, during initiations, while giving his disciples the holy name, Srila Prabhupada would often speak about the ten offenses to overcome. The Lord’s holy name is an all-fulfilling desire tree. It can bestow anything a person who chants it wants. If one wants material success, opulence, liberation, or freedom from sinful reactions, the holy name can bestow it. However, none of these are the goal of chanting. Chanting the holy name has a specific goal, and devotees should always be conscious of it. The real fruit of our chanting is to develop love for Krsna.

To develop this love, it is necessary to receive the holy name in proper disciplic succession or *parampara.* Although we may receive the mantra, if we do not receive it from a bona fide guru in a proper disciplic succession, our chanting will not have the desired effect. A proper disciplic succession means that line of spiritual masters who have understood that the ultimate objective of chanting is to develop love of God.

The Lord’s holy name is the means by which we can achieve perfection in Kali-yuga. It is up to us to take the holy name and embrace it in the core of our hearts, and to chant it with a proper attitude.

Srila Rupa Gosvami tells us that the holy names are very, very sweet because they are nondifferent from Krsna. Krsna is *akhila-rasamrta-murti,* the transcendental form of unlimited *rasa* (relationships) with His devotees. Thus the holy name, when chanted properly, can reveal one’s eternal relationship with Krsna.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains in his commentary on *Saranagati,* in his final prayer about the holy name, that the holy name will ultimately bring us back to the spiritual world. Krsna will then keep us by His side and reveal our eternal relationship with Him. When we actually have our relationship with Krsna revealed to us in the spiritual realm, we will understand the holy name’s true sweetness, because we will see everything present in the holy name—including our own relationship with Krsna.

**Question: Although we may be chanting, why do we still feel distress? Why aren’t we always happy?**

Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes in his song A*tma-nivedana:*

All the troubles encountered in Your service shall be the cause  
of great happiness, for in Your devotional service joy and sorrow  
are equally great riches. Both destroy the misery of ignorance.

(Song 8, verse 4)

Having fully taken shelter of the Supreme Lord, even if one has to undergo some great difficulty that might appear a cause of distress, devotees will see that difficulty as a source of happiness, because they have transcended the temporary happiness and distress of this world. They have realized that real happiness in spiritual life is found in pleasing Krsna. However, the ignorant suffer from *dvesa:* they hate unhappiness and anything that appears to cause it.

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura describes *abhinivesa,* the last form of ignorance, as still instinctively clinging to the idea that the body can provide enjoyment and that death means the end of our enjoyment. All types of ignorance are meant to be destroyed at the root by chanting the holy name.

Devotees must be aware of the possible offenses that can be committed while chanting. Always try to chant in such a way that you overcome your material attachments, knowing that you cannot do it by our own endeavor. Devotees know they are always dependent on Krsna. And the more we progress in chanting, the more we will realize how dependent we are on the holy name. In the *Bhagavad-gita,* Krsna describes such a dependent devotee as the highest *yogi:*

*yoginam api sarvesam*  
*mad-gatenantaratmana*  
*sraddhavan bhajate yo mam*  
*sa me yuktatamo matah*

“Of all *yogis,* the one with great faith who always abides in Me, thinks of Me within himself, and renders transcendental loving service to Me—he is the most intimately united with Me in yoga and is the highest of all. That is My opinion. ” *(Bg.* 6. 47)

One must learn the truth and the holy name’s transcendental position by hearing the revealed scriptures from the Vaisnavas. There is no other way to taste the holy name’s sweetness.

Srila Rupa Gosvami cries, tears flowing from his eyes in ecstasy, “I do not know how much nectar the two syllables ‘Krs-na’ have produced. When the holy name of Krsna is chanted, it appears to dance within the mouth. We then desire many, many mouths. When that name enters the holes of the ears, we desire many millions of ears. And when the holy name dances in the courtyard of the heart, it conquers the activities of the mind, and therefore all the senses become inert. ” *(Vidagdha-madhava,* 1. 15) He does not want anything else. Imagine what taste he must be experiencing if he is praying for that.

Devotees hearing this verse understood how to properly hear the holy name’s glories. And by hearing the revealed scriptures from the mouths of Vaisnavas, we will begin to taste the sweetness of the Lord’s holy name.

**Question: Is there a difference between chanting** *japa* **while thinking about material duties and chanting while thinking about devotional service we are supposed to execute?**

There is a difference, obviously, between trying to hear and pray attentively, fully dependent on the Lord’s holy name. Srila

Prabhupada says we should chant as a child cries for its mother. One should not chant while thinking of practical engagements involving the physical body or the mind. Attentive and prayerful chanting cannot be compared to chanting while meditating on one’s work—whether that work be service or mundane. Therefore, if we need to plan our activities, then we should do it, but not during *japa.*

In *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* Srila Rupa Gosvami describes different levels of *dhyana* (meditation). There he lists *lila-dhyana, guna-dhyana, nama-dhyana*, and *krida-dhyanam,* or meditation on service. An example of a devotee performing *krida-dhyanam* is the South Indian *brahmana* who burned his finger when he tested the sweetrice he had cooked only in his mind. Being able to serve the Lord in the mind, he was at an elevated stage of devotional service. We too may come to that point one day and derive the same benefit, but for now, it is important simply to hear the sound vibration when we chant Hare Krsna. Gradually, when we learn more and more to control the mind through strict hearing, our worshipful Lord will manifest in the heart. We cannot force that to happen. Until it happens, control the mind while chanting as much as possible by listening to the sound of the holy name.

We also have to have a proper conception of the Lord’s holy name. The heart is not cleansed simply by hearing the sound of our own chanting. *Caitanya-caritamrta* explains that we must learn about the holy name’s transcendental position and beauty by hearing the revealed scriptures from Vaisnavas.

**Question: Should we work on improving the quality of our chanting or increasing our** *japa* **quota in order to taste the sweetness of the holy name?**

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes:

One must diligently complete the prescribed number of holy names according to his vow, and he must always check that he chants his rounds sincerely. Those who chant distractedly are always eager to somehow complete the fixed number of holy names and be done with it. It is important to concentrate on the quality of the chanting and not on trying to artificially increase the number of holy names. The name of the Lord should be pronounced distinctly. Only by the grace of the Lord can this be achieved. Thus one should pray to the Lord that he never falls victim to the wiles of the illusion of distraction, and that he can continue to taste the full nectar of the holy name. (Sri *Harinama Cintamani,* Chapter 12)

Better to concentrate on quality instead of artificially increasing the quantity of *japa.* Unfortunately, sometimes we think that we become happy simply by finishing our rounds and having time left to do the things we *really* want to do. We often think there are more important things to do besides chanting our rounds.

Of course, we should be happy that we completed our rounds, because we vowed to chant a fixed number every day. But did we emphasize the quality of our chanting? Try to chant sincerely and attentively, pronouncing each name distinctly. Although chanting like this takes hard work, we should accept that work happily. *This* is a devotee’s happiness; devotees who chant with attention know that they will make progress in devotional service. Certainly the goal is to always be happy in Krsna consciousness, but when we are at the stage of *sadhana-bhakti* we cannot always expect such happiness. We should instead be happy to accept whatever is most beneficial for us. Bhaktivinoda Thakura also says that we should pray to the Lord to never fall victim to the illusion of distraction (*viksepah),* the tendency to think something is more important than *kirtana or bhajana.* Don’t consider anything more important than your *japa.* Be attentive; chant seriously. Don’t be whimsical, and don’t talk to other devotees while chanting. Chanting is not something we do to keep ourselves entertained. Don’t allow distraction in simply because you think attentive chanting is too difficult.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura adds:

Distraction in chanting produces a type of illusion, causing serious offenses against the holy name that are very difficult to overcome. This illusion leads to craving for wealth, women, position, success and even cheating. When these attractions cover the heart, the neophyte gradually loses interest in chanting of the holy name.

One must \[therefore\] make a constant effort to drive these thoughts away from the mind, and diligently follow the rules of Vaisnava etiquette. This endeavor will bring back his good fortune. (Sri *Harinama Cintamani,* Chapter 12)

Chanting Hare Krsna takes concentrated effort. If we become weak and lose our determination, the mind will begin to meditate on illusion, which, as Bhaktivinoda Thakura says, leads to material cravings. When we meditate on these cravings for prolonged periods of time, they further dry up our determination and we will lose interest in chanting.

Sometimes devotees say, “I cannot chant. I *try* to chant, but my mind is too distracted. I chant one or two rounds, but it is so difficult. I cannot chant anymore. Why has this happened to me? ” It has happened because the devotees were not careful. They became weak, overcome by their material cravings, and now their determination has dried up and they can no longer chant.

But chanting is our lifeline in spiritual life. It is how we cultivate our devotional attitude, surrender, and service to the holy name. Everything is achieved by chanting. Therefore, Bhaktivinoda Thakura emphasizes the quality of our utterance over increasing the quantity of rounds. If we have time to chant more, certainly we should, but don’t sacrifice the quality for quantity. At the least we should chant our promised quota of sixteen rounds.

**Question: If we have already given up chanting our rounds, what should we do?**

If you come to me for instruction, I will tell you to again chant a fixed quota with quality. You will again be able to chant sixteen rounds if you at least start chanting *some* rounds with quality.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura also writes *(Sri Harinama* Cintamani, Chapter 12):

This endeavor will bring back his good fortune \[if at least he tries to chant attentively, and tries to kick out unwanted thoughts from the mind\]. He must begin by observing *ekadasi* vows, appearance days of the Lord, and other important festival days. He should spend the entire festival day and night chanting and singing the glories of the Lord in association of saintly devotees. The ideal way to observe these festivals is to be in one of the *dhamas* (Navadvipa, Vrndavana, Puri, etc. ) with *sadhus* \[pure devotees in the line of Srila Rupa Gosvami\] reading and discussing *sastra* \[Vedic literature like *Bhagavad-gita, Srimad-Bhagavatam,* and other Vaisnava scriptures\]. He should wholeheartedly participate in these festivals without anxiety or hesitation. These festivals will gradually rekindle the dying spark of spiritual taste. Thus he will gradually again become attracted to the pastimes of the Supreme Lord.

Narada Muni says *(Bhag.* 4. 29. 39-40):

My dear King, in the place where pure devotees live, following the rules and regulations and thus purely conscious and engaged with great eagerness in hearing and chanting the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in that place if one gets a chance to hear their constant flow of nectar, which is exactly like the waves of a river, one will forget the necessities of life—namely hunger and thirst—and become immune to all kinds of fear, lamentation and illusion.

If we want to become free from the bodily and mental conceptions, we have to bring the consciousness to the transcendental platform. We have to become convinced that meditating on sense gratification produces nothing but anxiety. But we are not convinced. We still look for an injection of sense gratification, even though it creates only a temporary illusion of forgetting distress— when the injections’ effect wears off, the mind does not remain peaceful, but plunges into even more anxiety.

It is just like a person who thinks having a nice dream is happiness and a bad dream distress. When such a person wakes from the bad dream, the distress disappears and he thinks himself happy. Unfortunately, when he wakes from the nice dream he feels distress. Neither his happiness nor his distress have any connection with reality. Sense gratification is like that—it has no connection with reality. We have to become convinced that meditation on sense gratification produces nothing but anxiety. If we become convinced, we will kick out mundane thoughts while we are chanting. If we have any hope of one day tasting the complete nectar of the holy name, it will only be by making these efforts.

Devotees know they cannot progress by their own endeavors, but they are compelled to try because they want to express their desire for Krsna’s mercy. The Lord is always ready to give us His mercy, actually, but we have to want it.

**Question: Why do we lose taste for chanting?**

Losing taste for chanting is caused by offenses. However, Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura also explains in *Madhurya-kadambini* that when we lose our faith in and our taste for hearing and chanting, we can regain it, although only after some suffering.

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura explains that those who lose taste for chanting are just like persons with fever who cannot eat. Those who are intelligent will not wait for the suffering that follows a fever but will do what they can to cure the disease. Regardless of how we treat the fever, however, when the fever begins to come down, and after we have suffered intensely, we will need *pusti,* nourishment. In the same way, those who have lost their taste for chanting must be nourished again by proper hearing. Such hearing will help them cure the disease. Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura refers to hearing as an invalid’s diet and medicine.

Although we suffer before we can regain our taste for chanting, we should understand that the *acaryas* have not cursed us to suffer. When Lord Nityananda falls to the ground with tears in His eyes, He is begging us not to choose suffering. When Narada Muni saw Mrgari, the hunter, he begged, “Please, don’t half-kill these animals. You are going to suffer. ” He was not cursing him, but merely pointing out the law of nature. We should never think we are above nature’s laws.

The *acaryas* have given us the holy name, which is the right medicine to relieve our suffering. Take this medicine and chant it attentively. Strive for the day when you will get even a little glimpse of the pure holy name. That will awaken such happiness!

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes the proper mood we should adopt in his *bhajana, “Kabe ha’be bolo, ”*—“When will that day be mine when my offenses ceasing and the taste for the holy name increasing? When in my heart will Your mercy shine? When, oh, when will that day be mine? ” He is expressing his hankering for taste, for mercy, and for the nullification of his offenses, and he is begging for *suddha-nama ruci, to* taste the pure holy name. Our heart is considered steel-framed if when chanting our body does not tremble in ecstasy, tears don’t flow from our eyes, our hair does not stand on end, and we do not feel an overflow of love from the heart. A steel-framed heart is hard. By the time we attain *bhava* the heart will be soft. If our heart is still steel-framed, we are still committing offenses to the holy name. Bhaktivinoda Thakura has assured us that until we have given up our offenses, we will not attain taste.

From Narottama dasa Thakura’s *Lalasamayi Prarthana,* Verse 1:

When will that opportune moment come to me when there will be shivering of the body as soon as I chant Lord Gauranga’s name? And after the shivering, while chanting Hare Krsna, when will there be tears pouring down from my eyes?

The great Vaisnavas’ prayers teach us through example that if we want to experience the pure holy name, we have to want to experience it. The holy name is unlimitedly tasty. It is so satisfying to the self that upon chanting it, devotees forget all else.

Haridasa Thakura exemplified this truth. Once, Ramacandra Khan sent a prostitute to tempt Haridasa Thakura into falldown, but he was so absorbed in the holy name that he was not at all attracted. Then Mayadevi herself came before Haridasa Thakura while he was sitting alone in his cave, and she showed him her most attractive form, using all her feminine gestures to attract his mind. But even Mayadevi could not draw him away from the holy name. Afterwards, she told Haridasa Thakura that the reason she had come was to herself taste ecstatic love for the holy name, and she knew that if she were going to receive it, she would have to approach a devotee like Haridasa Thakura. Even Mayadevi was eager to take part in Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s pastimes and receive the holy name from Haridasa Thakura! This pastime illustrates the great strength and power of the holy name. It is so satisfying that one forgets about the entire material world.

Don’t doubt that the holy name is full of ecstasy. Lord Caitanya told Prakasananda Sarasvati that He didn’t study philosophy because Isvara Puri considered Him to be a fool and had instructed Him simply to chant the holy name. He then said that when He began chanting, tears flowed from His eyes and His body trembled. He wondered, “What kind of mantra has my spiritual master given Me? ” He even lost external consciousness.

Lord Caitanya wanted us not only to experience the ecstasy of chanting the Hare Krsna maha-mantra, but to distribute it widely. To give us the strength and encouragement to do this, He has given us this nice *sadhana.* If we practice good *sadhana* every day, we will not only become absorbed in our own hearing and chanting but awaken the desire to distribute Krsna consciousness for others’ benefit.

**Question: Why should we chant our rounds on beads?**

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura gives one reason why we specifically chant on beads (and not clickers): Beads allow us to see how we are increasing. When we chant on beads, we can see practically how we are increasing our *japa* by the amount of times we go around on our beads. Even Lord Caitanya chanted on beads and thus counted His rounds. Bhaktivinoda Thakura adds that Tulasidevi is auspicious, and when we hold our *tulasi* beads, we are holding her in our hands.

**Question: Can we chant japa while driving?**

If you are a passenger, yes, but not if you are the driver! How can you concentrate on the holy name while driving? There is nothing wrong with chanting Hare Krsna while you are driving, but do not count that chanting toward your minimum *japa* quota.

## Removing Anarthas and Becoming Free from Offenses

*Nama-aparadha* is the first stage of chanting the holy name of the Lord. The second stage is *nama-abhasa,* and the third stage is *suddha-nama.* In the beginning, we may chant the holy name of the Lord in ignorance, while committing offenses. We do not know what the offenses are. We begin chanting and we feel such a taste of the holy name. That is because in the beginning we are chanting in *nama-abhasa.* But when we start committing offenses, we come down to the platform of *aparadha.* Then when we become aware of offenses and knowingly commit them, having no faith in the holy name of the Lord, we are considered a *nama-aparadhi.*

One who is chanting on the platform of *namabhasa* is one who wants to become free from his offenses and clean his heart from *anarthas,* and thus be forgiven. He therefore chants very attentively and very deliberately to the Lord. But even this is not enough.

Lord Caitanya also wants us to become free from the influence of *nama-aparadha* and *nama-abhasa.* The word *abhasa* means partial, and can be of the following types: light, shadow *(chaya),* and reflection *(pratibimba).* At *nama-abhasa,* although one can get a little glimpse of the holy name, he does not experience the holy name completely; he only partially experiences it.

There are two types of *nama-abhasa: chaya* and *pratibimba. Pratibimba,* being a reflection, has no connection with the object. Just as you can see the reflection of a tree in the water, there is no connection between the two, and the reflection of the tree on the water is distorted.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that a person who is either chanting *pratibimba namabhasa* or a person who is engaged in *pratibimba bhaktyabhasa* actually is more attracted to achieving either fruitive gain or impersonal liberation. He thinks that by engaging in *bhakti,* he will be able to achieve his goal, which is similar to *jnana-misra-bhakti,* where the desired goal for the *jnanis* is liberation.

*Chaya,* being a shadow, is not as bad as *pratibimba* because *chaya* does have a connection with *bhakti,* the object. However, *chaya* still a form of *bhaktyabhasa* does not bring one to pure devotional service. A person who is chanting on the platform of *chaya namabhasa* is a person who is weak. Although he wants to make progress in chanting the holy name of the Lord, either due to foolishness or weakness of heart, he slips. He is not overwhelmingly distracted by intense attachment to fulfill some material goal, and he is not chanting the holy name of the Lord in order to fulfill his material ambitions. He is just weak. Such a person who may be weak is in a better position than the person who is addicted to fulfilling his material goals/ambitions.

It is described that there are two symptoms that are achieved by *namabhasa*: relief from all distress and eradication of all sinful reactions. *Namabhasa* can also liberate one from the effects of Kali-yuga. Having received a little glimpse of these symptoms, because they are all-consuming and one’s distress immediately goes far away, he does not even think about anxiety.

Although the holy name of the Lord is only partially manifest on the *namabhasa* stage, if we continue chanting in this way, it becomes more frequent, we become more steady in our chanting, and then gradually some taste is awakened. This is what we should strive for. Krsna is inviting all of us to come and experience the joy of Krsna consciousness.

If we are not rendering pure devotional service, we are rendering what is called *bhakti-abhasa,* which is partial devotional service.

Similarly, a devotee whose heart is still filled with many *anarthas* and attachments is known as *bhakta-abhasa,* or *vaisnava-abhasa.* He is not really a Vaisnava; he is next to Vaisnava.

What are these *anarthas!* Chanting Hare Krsna simply to gain the respect of others, fault-finding, cheating, attachment to anything that has no relation to Krsna, and envy. If we are maintaining *anarthas,* we are chanting on the offensive stage, and such chanting will give rise to more *anarthas.* We’ll find ourselves desiring even more respect, or perhaps the inclination to cheat will increase. We should examine ourselves carefully and root out these unwanted desires.

The desire for recognition and material attachment can cause us to offend the holy name or the Vaisnavas. Then *anartha* becomes *aparadha.* The etymology of *aparadha* is “to be turned away from the service of Srimati Radharani and Her maidservants. ” The *sastras* list four categories of *aparadha:* offenses to the holy name *(nama-aparadha),* offenses to devotees *(vaisnava-aparadha),* offenses to the deity *(seva-aparadha),* and offenses to other living entities *(jana-aparadha).* (One offends other living entities through envy or by wanting to cause pain to others. )

An example of how *anarthas* become *aparadha* and tend to multiply is in the story of Ramacandra Puri. When Ramacandra Puri offended his guru, the seed of fruitive desire grew in his heart. He was then not only offensive to his own spiritual master, Madhavendra Puri, but to Lord Caitanya as well as many other Vaisnavas.

*Anarthas* coming from offenses are particularly difficult to eradicate; they are not eradicated until one achieves *prema.* Even at the beginning stages of *bhava* we are left with traces of *anartha.* Even though one no longer commits offenses at *nistha,* having completed *anartha-nivrtti,* and certainly not at *bhava,* residual *anarthas* that were the result of *aparadha* remain. The *Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi* 8. 16) states:

*bahu janma kare yadi Sravana, kirtana*  
*tabu ta’ na paya krsna-pade prema-dhana*

“If one is infested with the ten offenses in the chanting of the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra,* despite his endeavor to chant the holy name for many births, he will not get the love of Godhead that is the ultimate goal of this chanting. ”

In order to develop our love, we have to be careful to avoid *anarthas.* Be just as careful to avoid the *aparadhas* and asat-trsna (the thirst for material objects). *Asat-trsna* leaves one attached to sense gratification, elevation to the heavenly planets, the acquisition of mystic powers, and liberation.

*Anarthas* are compared to clouds, while the holy name is compared to the sun. As the sun burns hotly in the sky but the clouds can prevent us from experiencing both its heat and light, so Krsna is fully manifest in His name, but we do not “see” Him because our hearts are covered with the clouds of *anarthas.*

To help free us from *anarthas,* Lord Caitanya left behind eight instructions in His *Siksastaka* prayers. The *Siksastaka* contains a particularly important verse that teaches us how to chant offenselessly:

*trnad api su-nicena*  
*taror iva sahisnuna*  
*amanina mana-dena*  
*kirtaniyah sada harih*

“One who thinks himself lower than the grass, who is more tolerant than a tree, and who does not expect personal honor yet is always prepared to give all respect to others can very easily always chant the holy name of the Lord. ”

If we find ourselves unable to chant, it is because we lack the qualities mentioned in this verse. These qualities are required; we should practice to develop them. They are fully manifest in the heart of one who chants the holy name purely. Until we come to that stage, we will have to practice chanting offenselessly and cultivating humility.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that Lord Caitanya gave us this verse to stress the importance of becoming free of *nama-aparadha* and *nama-abhasa.* It also tells us the nature of those who can chant the holy name purely: They always feel themselves humble—lower than straw in the street, more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and ready to offer all respects to others without expecting respect for themselves.

Tolerance is an aspect of humility. Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami explains that if someone comes to cut down a tree, the tree will not protest but will still provide that person fruits and shade. Similarly, even though someone may offend a Vaisnava, that Vaisnava will remain kind and compassionate toward that person. Vaisnavas always act for others’ welfare. Trees do not ask for water, even if they are parched. If someone comes and breaks their branches, they never complain. This is tolerance. If we can learn such humility and tolerance, it will be natural for us to offer respect to others. If we do not learn these important qualities, we will chant with *anarthas.*

Sometimes devotees think that they must only avoid offending elevated devotees, but that it is all right to offend “ordinary” devotees or nondevotees. But Lord Caitanya said that we should offer all respect to others—to *all* living entities. Be careful not to offend other living entities whether through violence or envy. We are meant to respect all living entities, knowing that the Supreme Lord is in their heart.

And while cultivating humility, we must learn to give up the desire to be respected in return. The desire for respect is a great obstacle for devotees. Krsna is not pleased when we demand respect from others. Krsna *is* pleased when we walk away from false respect. In any case, respect automatically follows devotees who want only to please the Lord. Such devotees do not have to endeavor for respect, because if the Lord is pleased by their activities, then their activities serve as an example for everyone else. Lord Caitanya promises that those who follow His edict will be able to chant Krsna’s name constantly *(kirtaniyah sada harih).*

Time is quickly passing, but we should each take the time to examine ourselves to see what we are trying to accomplish with our life. We tend to create so many goals for ourselves—we want to wake up early tomorrow, to chant sixteen rounds; to become serious about associating with devotees, to control the tongue, to make a lot of money, to build a temple—but what is the goal of these activities? *Srimad-Bhagavatam* describes the greatest success:

*etavan sankhya-yogabhyam*  
*sva-dharma-parinisthaya*  
*janma-labhah parah pumsam*  
*ante narayana-smrtih*

“The highest perfection of human life, achieved either by complete knowledge of matter and spirit, by practice of mystic powers, or by perfect discharge of occupational duty, is to remember the Personality of Godhead at the end of life. ” *(Bhag.* 2. 1. 6)

The best way to remember the Lord is to always glorify Him. Make absorption in such glorification the goal of life and do not let your life pass by without remembering it.

**Question: If we try our best to avoid offenses but still commit some, are we chanting** *namabhasa?*

We may continue to commit offenses due to weakness of heart *(hrdaya-daurbalyam) or* ignorance. But devotees are always trying to avoid offenses.

It is important to distinguish between intentional and unintentional sins. If a devotee commits a sin without purpose, a so called “accidental fall down, ” this sin is only temporary and will be washed away by the power of the holy name. But if a person commits intentional sins, thinking the bad effect will be washed off by the power of the holy name, then he cannot be regarded as a serious devotee. He’s a cheater. He’ll have to feel the bitter result of his offenses, such as fear, lamentation, and eventually, death.

Even the mere thought of committing a sin with the intention to annihilate it later through the strength of the holy name cannot be compensated by any remedy. What to speak of someone who actually commits the offense? He is not more than a show-bottle devotee, for only a materialist can think like that. Such a person cannot be engaged in devotional service. In no case is it allowed to commit sins, thinking they will be forgiven by the strength of the holy name. We must not think of it at all.

Where is the border line between the two kinds of sin? Whereas a smaller or greater blunder is unintentional. We speak of intentional sins if they’re premeditated, or planned in advance. In the previously mentioned offenses there were always possibilities to neutralize them. Here for the first time the consequences are very severe.

It is through the process of devotional service that one attains perfection. But if one rejects this process, especially by abusing the unique strength of the holy name for the fulfillment of sinful desires, then his life span, mind, and words will be ruined.

No one should think himself to be in an unimpeachable position due to his chanting and due to being situated in spiritual life. These thoughts and their succeeding actions have disastrous effects. Sometimes a neophyte devotee may consider himself to be in an especially privileged situation. He may think that now he’s being protected by the power of devotional service, and therefore he can do whatever his mind and senses dictate to him. This is the “I’m saved” syndrome.

As long as he is on the stage of *namabhasa,* he has to be aware of this offense. If we’re in bad association, then we’re likely to return to our old habits.

*(Nectarean Ocean of the Holy Name,* Sacinandana Swami, p. 71-72)

If we accidentally deviate, and then realize our mistake, then we must repent. Thinking ourselves fallen, knowing that we deserve punishment so that we will not commit it again, we should increase our chanting and try to avoid the same offense again.

Although you may commit unintentional offenses, do not remain ignorant. Learn to recognize the offense so that you can know how to pray to the Lord. You must pray, “I am weak, and so have fallen under the powerful influence of Your illusory energy. I am very tiny and I cannot overcome Your powerful energy by my own efforts. Please give me strength. ” Pray to Krsna for the strength to overcome offenses; do not remain passive.

**Question: If the holy name is more merciful than Krsna, why is** *nama-aparadha* **more dangerous than** *seva-aparadha,* **and what should we do if we have offended the holy name?**

*Seva-aparadha* means it is simply an inadvertent neglect in the worship of the Lord; it is not intentional. But as soon as the *seva-aparadha* is committed with the intention of counteracting its reaction by chanting, it is no longer *seva-aparadha*; it is *nama-aparadha.* Although Srila Bhaktivinoda explains that the holy name and the Lord are nondifferent, there is one secret difference. That is, the holy name is even more merciful than the Lord in other forms. Both Bhaktivinoda Thakura and Srila Rupa Gosvami explain that if you offend the *arca-vigraha* form of the Lord, if you take shelter of the holy name, by the holy name’s mercy you can be forgiven.

Regarding *namaparadha,* the Padma Purana states:

*namaparadha-yuktanam*  
*namany eva haranty agham*  
*avisranti-prayuktani*  
*tany evartha-karani ca*

“Even if one chants the Hare Krsna mantra offensively, one can nullify these offenses by continuously chanting without deviation. One who becomes accustomed to this practice will always remain in a pure transcendental position, untouchable by sinful reactions. ” (B*rahma-khanda* 25. 23)

Chant Hare Krsna with the consciousness that you are an offender. Pray continuously to the holy name to allow you to become free from your offenses and from the *anarthas,* the obstacles to devotional service. If you take shelter of the holy name like this and constantly chant, the holy name will forgive you. But if you falsely think you have not committed offenses, you will not even be able to chant *namabhasa.* Rather, your chanting will remain at the *namaparadha* stage.

**Question: Do we advance more quickly after receiving initiation, or do we continue at the same pace?**

There is no guarantee that after initiation, advancement will be quicker. So much depends on attitude. But at initiation, the disciple makes a commitment and should then draw strength from that commitment. Initiation helps disciples increase their faith and to seek to strengthen their commitment to spiritual life. Certainly those who deepen their commitment can expect to make advancement. But we should be careful not to commit offenses; even though we may make advancement, if we commit offenses, those offenses will engender *anarthas* in the heart and our advancement can become stifled.

*Anarthas* arise from four sources: sinful activity, pious activity, offenses, and *bhakti.*

**Question: How does** *bhakti* **produce** *anarthas***?**

While chanting Hare Krsna, a person is often given respect, position, and power and may become attached to them.

**Question: How can we remove** *anarthas***?**

It is not possible to free ourselves of *anarthas* by ourselves. *Anarthas* are cleaned away by associating with something pure. As long as we are impure, we cannot on our own remove impurities from our heart. Therefore, we are very much dependent on the association of more advanced devotees and on the Lord’s holy name. And because Krsna is completely pure, when we associate with His holy name, we also become pure. If we are not in a mood of dependence, we cannot clean our heart. Therefore we must pray to Krsna with a mood of helplessness: “I am fully dependent on You. I’m very fallen. I have no qualifications. If I try to remove these *anarthas* from my heart, I will only become more entangled in their network. I am fully dependent on Your mercy. If I can’t depend on You, then on whom *can* I depend? ”

Many of the prayers of the Vaisnava *acaryas* emulate this mood. We can pray to Krsna, “Please send me the association of Your devotees whose words can enter my heart and give me the strength to overcome my obstacles. Please send those who can help me to become free from lust, anger, and greed. ” This constant sense of dependence will purify the heart.

The more we submit ourselves to the holy name and take shelter in chanting, the more these *anarthas* will be cleared. Lord Caitanya says *(Siksastaka* 2):

O my Lord, Your holy name alone can render all benediction to living beings, and thus You have hundreds and millions of names like Krsna and Govinda. In these transcendental names You have invested all Your transcendental energies. There are not even hard and fast rules for chanting these names. O my Lord, out of kindness You enable us to easily approach You by chanting Your holy names, but I am so unfortunate that I have no attraction for them.

Lord Caitanya also said, “That I am still living in My body despite being separated from You is proof that I have no love for You. If I had any real love for You, how could I continue to live? I would give up this body in a moment. ”

Lord Caitanya prayed like this and taught us that we should feel unqualified and fully dependent on Krsna. Try to cultivate that attitude. And the best way to cultivate it is to repeat the prayers of realized Vaisnavas while continuing to offer some prayers ourselves.

*Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* describes the various types of prayers we can offer. There is *vijnapti,* humble entreaty in submission to the Lord. There is also *dainyabodhika,* which means to come before the deity and express one’s complete insignificance. Another is called *samprarthanatmika,* in which one prays for advancement and spontaneous devotional service. Another is *lalasamayi,* prayer for one’s service to the Lord to be perfected. (This last prayer can only be offered when one has attained an elevated stage. )

**Question: Are** *anarthas* **removed instantly?**

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura explains that there are five stages to clearing *anarthas:* partial, pervasive, almost complete, complete, and absolute. The *anarthas* that arise from offenses are partially cleared at *nistha.* One retains residual *anarthas.*

The next stage is called pervasive. An *anartha* that one has developed due to offenses comes to pervasive clearing at *asakti. Asakti* is an elevated stage at which one has deep attachment to the Lord. At *ruci* one has a taste for devotional activities, but at *asakti* one gets one’s taste from Krsna directly. Even at this stage, the *anarthas* in the heart from previous offenses are only cleared pervasively. There are unlimited desires in the heart, and they are only almost completely cleared at *bhava.* But even at *bhava* they are not completely cleared.

Bharata Maharaja serves as an example. He was practicing *bhava bhakti,* but due to a lingering past offense, he became attached to a deer and in his next life took birth in a deer’s body. Although one may think this terrible, Bharata Maharaja was able to remember his previous birth even while in the deer’s body, and he therefore went to listen to the pure recitation of the Lord’s pastimes at the sages’ *asramas.* Thus in his next life he took birth as Jada Bharata, a pure devotee. Offenses are serious; it is offenses that cause us not to be able to experience *suddha-nama,* the pure holy name. *Anarthas* arising from offenses will not be absolutely cleared until we attain *prema.*

Lord Caitanya exemplifies *anartha-nivrtti* in His the example of the Gundica-marjana pastime (cleansing the Gundica temple). First He cleaned all the dirt from the temple by removing it and placing it in a big pile. Then He told the devotees to clean the temple again. “This time”, He said, “you should try to find the tiny particles of dust and dirt and throw them out. ” These finer particles of dust are compared to the various *anarthas.*

When the devotees cleaned the Gundica temple, they were cleaning the throne and temple where Lord Jagannatha would sit. Thus Lord Caitanya taught us to clean the heart so that it can become a nice place for the Lord to sit. Of course, the Lord is already present in everyone’s heart as the Supersoul, but the cleaner we make our heart, the more we will see our worshipful deity manifest. Pure devotees do not see the Supersoul in their heart, but rather they see their worshipful deity. If we wish such purity and such a *darsana,* we have to clean our heart of *anarthas.*

Although we cannot clear our own *anarthas* alone, we have to endeavor to perform *sadhana-bhakti* with our tongue, eyes, and ears—that is, by hearing and chanting about Krsna. This will ultimately evoke *suddha-sattva,* pure, unalloyed goodness.

**Question: Are we committing the seventh offense to the holy name if we commit sinful activity due to our weakness, but are prepared to suffer the reactions?**

The seventh offense, which is to commit sinful activities on the strength of chanting the holy name, is extremely severe. We can neutralize any other offense to the holy name, but not this one. It is very difficult to neutralize the seventh offense, because the consequences are so severe.

However, there are some deliberate activities one can perform to become free from this offense. The seventh offense means that one deliberately commits sin and then tries to neutralize both the sin and any suffering that may be incurred by chanting Krsna’s name. Persons committing this offense do not intend to stop their sinful activity, but think only that they will purify themselves and avoid reaction through chanting. Such persons cannot claim that they are innocent, or that they committed the sin by accident.

In his purport to *Bhagavad-gita* 9. 30, Srila Prabhupada explains that no one should take Krsna’s words as an excuse to commit sinful activities. The verse states:

*api cet su-duracaro*  
*bhajate mam ananya-bhak*  
*sadhur eva sa mantavyah*  
*samyag vyavasito hi sah*

“Even if one commits the most abominable act, if he is engaged in devotional service he is to be considered saintly because he is properly situated in his determination. ”

However, when devotees commit sinful acts out of weakness, perhaps overwhelmed by material desires, if they are sincere, they will sincerely repent their act and voluntarily accept any suffering that results.

**Question: To perform devotional service means to act for Krsna’s pleasure. When we are chanting at the beginning, we are not chanting the pure name. How is Krsna pleased by our impure or even offensive chanting? How can we understand that our chanting, even in the beginning stages, is pleasing to Krsna even if we are not chanting the** **pure name?**

This question reminds me of the story of the illiterate *brahmana* who was told by his spiritual master to read *Bhagavad-gita.* This *brahmana* did not let his illiteracy get in the way of his service. Every day he would sit with the *Bhagavad-gita* and attempt to read it. Sometimes he would even be holding it upside down. When Lord Caitanya saw the *brahmana* crying while holding the *Bhagavad-gita,* He asked, “Why are your eyes full of tears? ”

The *brahmana* said, “My spiritual master instructed me to read *Bhagavad-gita* every day, but I do not know how to read. ”

“If you can’t read what it says, why are you crying? ”

The *brahmana* replied, “When I look at the picture of Krsna and Arjuna, and see how Krsna is serving Arjuna as his chariot driver— and I think of how Krsna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, subordinates Himself completely to the orders of His devotees, I cry. ”

Lord Caitanya then embraced the *brahmana* and said, “You are the real knower of *Bhagavad-gita. ”* Similarly, we have been ordered to chant Hare Krsna without fail every day, even though we cannot chant purely. Like the illiterate *brahmana* we should realize, “I cannot chant purely, but still I have been ordered to chant. ” And then we should chant with firm faith that the holy name can deliver the fruit of pure love of Godhead; the pure holy name can awaken on the tongue of one who follows the guru’s order.

We should pray, “I have no qualification. My heart is like coal. I have so many *anarthas, so* many desires. But Your devotee, my spiritual master, has given me this wonderful name based on his faith that it can deliver me. He has told me that if I go on chanting the holy name every day, with faith, then some day I will love You. Right now I have no devotion, no love, no knowledge. I have nothing. I am completely dependent on You. Somehow, please manifest Yourself. I have faith that this name has the potency to purify even a heart like mine. ”

**Question: If we realize that we have lost the mercy of the Lord’s holy name, what should we do?**

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura gives the example that if the subordinate of a wealthy and capable master is offensive to that master who maintains him, the master will neglect the servant. The subordinate will suffer from the neglect and become impoverished. After some suffering, however, the servant may then realize that he should not have been offensive. He then may become prepared to do anything the master asks without hesitation.

Thus those who have lost the Lord’s mercy may suffer for some time, but after suffering may realize that they should enthusiastically render service to guru, *sadhu,* and *sastra.* By enthusiastically and determinedly serving them, they will obtain Krsna’s mercy—and naturally, the holy name’s mercy. The holy name will also remove the sinful tendency from the heart, which caused them to offend in the first place.

**Question: Is apathy a form of** *nama-aparadha?*

In Sri *Hari-bhakti-vilasa,* carelessness is counted among the offenses against the holy name. There the word *pramada* is defined as “inattentiveness. ” *Sri Hari-nama-cintamani* has divided inattentiveness into three: apathy, inactivity (inertia), and distraction. Until we become free from these three types of inattentiveness we cannot perform devotional service.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura had already explained that apathy develops from carelessness, and laziness and inertia are referred to as inactivity. When we feel enthusiasm, laziness and inertia cannot remain. Enthusiasm and inertia are diametrically opposed.

Even if all other *nama-aparadhas* are given up, if we are still inattentive, we will never become attracted to the holy names. If we have enthusiasm at the beginning of devotional service and we don’t let our enthusiasm wane, then we will never become apathetic, lazy, or distracted while chanting.

Therefore enthusiasm is the only support for all types of devotional service. By enthusiastically performing devotional service, we can give up *anisthita* (unsteady) service in a short time and attain *nistha.*

**Question: What can we do about complacency and a lack of interest in spiritual life?**

To relieve ourselves of complacency we should hear from more advanced devotees who are not complacent. When we hear from and associate with such persons, it should wake us from our own complacency and make us realize the work we must do if we want to become Krsna conscious. The Supreme Lord does not show Himself to those who have no spiritual strength, who are careless or wild, or who perform inappropriate austerities. Rather, the Supreme Lord personally reveals Himself to those who strive to reach Him by right means. Such persons enter the spiritual world.

We will not become Krsna conscious simply by thinking we are devotees or because we have accepted initiation. Rather, those who become Krsna conscious strive to do so. And if we do not have the spiritual strength to strive, we should associate with those who do.

## The Lord Protects Those Who Glorify His Name

In many of his letters in the late ’60s, Srila Prabhupada emphasized that chanting the Lord’s holy name is most important. On several occasions he said that the most important instruction the spiritual master gives is that the disciple chant Hare Krsna. Repeatedly throughout those letters one gets a sense of how much Srila Prabhupada stressed chanting Hare Krsna to protect us from the dangerous material world. What are those dangers? They are related to our unnatural condition brought about by our forgetfulness of our eternal relationship with Krsna.

Srila Prabhupada often spoke of the material world as situated in the middle of a vast ocean, and how the middle of the ocean is always troubled by turbulence. Similarly, there is always turbulence in this world. However, few people have understood the real shelter available in Krsna’s holy names. This ignorance is due to the powerful influence of the Lord’s external energy, which maintains the living entities forgetful of their eternal relationship with God.

In one letter (dated 1969), Srila Prabhupada said that we should pray to Krsna to be able to serve Him nicely, by His grace. When we chant the Hare Krsna mantra, we are addressing Radha and Krsna and praying to Them for Their service. “Hare Krsna” means “O Hare, O Radha, please engage me in Your service, so that I can get relief from *maya’s* service. ” Just as a person resigns from an inferior post and accepts a superior post, so we pray to Krsna to find relief from the inferior post of serving *maya* and to be engaged in the superior post of serving Krsna. Every living entity has the choice to serve Krsna or *maya.*

Srila Prabhupada said that *maya* will test us when we come to Krsna consciousness to discover whether we want to serve or disturb Krsna. Of course, Krsna is never disturbed under any circumstances, but *maya* still tests us. Prabhupada said that when we first come to Krsna consciousness we will experience so many disturbances. Just as a boat in the middle of the ocean is tossed by the waves, so we will be tossed by our mind and senses. Although we will have many tests, if we take shelter of Krsna, we will cross the ocean of difficulties.

*Sri Navadvipa-dhama-mahatmya* describes how Mayadevi approached Lord Caitanya and asked Him why she could not be part of His pastimes—why He leaves her in the material world to punish the living entities. In response, Lord Caitanya blessed Mayadevi (in her form as Parvati) the benediction to remain in Navadvipa-dhama. He said, “You are *yogamaya,* and in the spiritual world you serve Me by creating so many pastimes for My enjoyment. But in the material world you serve Me as *mahamaya,* and your service is to punish the conditioned souls who want to forget their relationship with Me. Still, I will allow you to remain in Navadvipa as *praudha-maya,* or protector of the *dhama. ”* Mayadevi therefore protects the *dhama* and does not allow anyone to enter who will disturb the Lord. When someone wants entrance who wishes to serve the Lord, then Mayadevi makes all arrangements for that service.

In the same book, Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura also explains that some people may think themselves in the *dhama,* but if they are deceitful, they can never enter past the guardianship of *praudha-maya.* Thus even though we may go to Navadvipa, where Lord Caitanya appeared and displayed His most munificent pastimes of distributing this holy name, we may never have entered the *dhama* at all. We must clear our hearts of deceit.

One who offers service to Lord Caitanya in the mood of *trnad api sunicena* can become free of deceit and qualified to enter the *dhama.* This means that *praudha-maya* helps sincere devotees free themselves of *maya* if they chant the holy name with a desire to become humble and prideless, and to offer respect to everyone without expecting honor for themselves. The simple desire to surrender to the Lord’s holy name qualifies one to enter the holy *dhama.*

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura also explains that when we are willing to accept the Lord’s punishment as correction, we become qualified to enter the *dhama,* and the meaning of the *trnad api sunicena* verse becomes revealed in our heart.

Ultimately a devotee must take shelter and pray for constant protection from *maya,* because *maya* is always present to test our advancement. She never goes away. She may be there as the material energy, or as our own gross attachment to matter. It is our attachment to physical matter and the desire to enjoy that matter that turns the living entities’ heart from Krsna. After taking up the chanting, it is *aparadhas* toward Krsna or His devotees that turns our heart away from Him.

“Hare” refers to Srimati Radharani, the energy of the Lord. Krsna is the Supreme Lord; Rama is the Supreme Lord as the reservoir of pleasure. Krsna is therefore also known as Radha-ramana, “one who seeks to give Srimati Radharani pleasure and thus gives her unlimited pleasure. ” Krsna is capable of bestowing unlimited pleasure, and we can taste some of that pleasure when we come under the protection of His internal potency. Thus devotees sincerely pray from the heart for the Lord’s protection and shelter, and especially protection and shelter under His internal potency.

Prabhupada adds that performing regulated activities, such as attending *arati,* chanting our rounds, and studying devotional literature on our own and in classes will keep us fit to preach effectively in the field. Regulated activities help us maintain a high standard of purity, a standard that must be kept if we do not want to lose spiritual strength and then fall down. As part of our purity, we should be thoroughly convinced that the Hare Krsna mantra has the power to protect us in all circumstances. If we chant with such faith always and without offense, we will make swift advancement and gradually come to see things clearly. Then when we find ourselves spontaneously engaged in the Lord’s service and anxious to avoid mundane activities, we will know that we have achieved a little taste in Krsna consciousness.

If, however, we lose faith in chanting, then tears should come to our eyes, and we should cry and pray to Krsna, “Please do whatever is necessary to restore my faith in Your holy name. ” These tears are the price that we must pay to stay under the internal potency’s protection. Prabhupada says that if these tears don’t come to our eyes along with the fervent desire to pray for the Lord’s protection, *maya* can take us in a moment.
