It does not take long for the living entity to fall away from the protection of Krsna’s internal potency.

For example, the *Bhagavatam* explains that Ajamila fell from Krsna consciousness simply by seeing a prostitute and a *sudra* embracing. The enjoying spirit is deeply rooted in the heart, and it is that enjoying spirit that turns us from Krsna’s service and causes us to commit offenses to both the Lord and His devotee. If we can simply remain sincere, even though we may have material attachments, we will remain humble. As soon as we think, “I have wealth, intelligence, fame, power, and beauty and I want to enjoy them, ” we will fall down. It is our attachments, and ultimately our pride, that drive us to commit offenses to the Lord and the devotees who always try to reduce our pride.

Srila Prabhupada gave us a wonderful prayer to offer:

My dear Krishna, please remind me to always chant Your Holy Name, please do not put me into forgetfulness. You are sitting within me as Supersoul, so You can put me into forgetfulness or into remembering You. So please do not put me into forgetfulness. Please always remind me to chant, even You send me into the hell, it doesn’t matter, just so long as I can always chant Hare Krishna.

(Letter to Devananda, Los Angeles, November 23, 1968)

Anyone can begin chanting at any time in life, but the goal is that once having begun, never stop. Chant in a proper state of mind, and be protected from *maya.*

**Question: If we are constantly engaged in chanting, how will we take care of our material needs?**

We may become bewildered, thinking, “How will I put food in my belly and in the bellies of my family members if I am always chanting? How do I go to work and tell my boss, ‘Hare Krsna Hare Krsna’? He will not want to listen to anything I say. ” We may even begin to think that it is too idealistic to think that we can always chant the Lord’s holy name. However, constant chanting is the perfectional stage of life. One who is purely situated in such perfection is liberated.

*iha yasya harer dasye*  
*karmana manasa vaca*  
*mikhilesvapyavasthasu*  
*jivan-mukta sa ucyate*

“Anyone who, by his actions, mind and words, lives only for the transcendental loving service of the Lord, is certainly a liberated soul, even though he may appear to be in a condition of material existence. ” (Brs. 1. 2. 187)

The Lord personally cares for such liberated souls, who are fully absorbed in continuous chanting. The Lord provides all their necessities, because they do not think about their own needs but only about Krsna’s pleasure and glorification. Such devotees do not glorify the Lord because they have to or because they are afraid of the Lord’s punishment. They have no motivation for their glorification other than that they cannot help it; they are spontaneously attracted to Krsna. They are no longer conscious of their physical body or the need to maintain it. The Lord always recognizes such devotees.

Nothing is hidden from the Lord because He is in every being’s heart. He knows our desires better than we do. Krsna is the Supreme

Being, and when we are fully dependent on Him, He will protect us. Krsna says in *Bhagavad-gita* (9. 31), “O son of Kunti, declare it boldly that My devotee never perishes. ” The Lord provides all necessities.

Of course, for a person exclusively dependent on the Lord’s holy name, these necessities are meager; they need only enough to keep body and soul together. And their attachment to the Lord is so deep and intense that even if body and soul are not kept together, they have firm faith that they will always be with Krsna. They feel, “Krsna, if You want to maintain this body, then please maintain it. ” Otherwise, whether they are in a body or not makes no difference to them; they are liberated from the body. If the Lord wants them to stay in the world and preach His glories, then they will do so, and if the Lord wants them to return to the spiritual world to associate eternally with Him, then they are ready to offer that service. The devotees are so surrendered that they even say to the Lord, “If you want me to take another birth—or another million births—I will do so. Whatever You want is fine with me, as long as I can serve You. ” Lord Brahma prays to Krsna in the same way: “O Lord, whether in this life or in some other life, or in some lower species of life, I simply beg one benediction from You: Let me be counted as one of Your devotees. That is all I want. ”

*narayana-parah sarve*  
*na kutascana bibhyati*  
*svargapavarga-narakesv*  
*api tulyartha-darsinah*

“Devotees solely engaged in the devotional service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Narayana, never fear any condition of life. For them the heavenly planets, liberation and the hellish planets are all the same, for such devotees are interested only in the service of the Lord. ” *(Bhag.* 6. 17. 28)

Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, “I would rather take birth as an insect in the house of a devotee than as Lord Brahma. At least in the house of a pure devotee I will always hear Your glories, since Your devotees always chant about You. ”

The characteristics of highly advanced devotees who have fully surrendered their lives to the Lord are that they are fully maintained by the Lord and that they are fearless. They never think, “I am always chanting, so how will I get any work done and make enough money to support myself? ” The Lord personally cares for such advanced devotees.

*ananyas cintayanto mam*  
*ye janah paryupasate*  
*tesam nityabhiyuktanam*  
*yoga-ksemam vahamy aham*

“Those who always worship Me with exclusive devotion, meditating on My transcendental form—to them I carry what they lack, and I preserve what they have. ” (Bg. 9. 22)

Thus the Lord cares for those who are fully surrendered to Him. But of course, it takes faith to trust Him. We have to have this firm faith in the holy name and then chant continuously. But we should know that the Lord certainly cares for those whose faith is strong and who are fully surrendered to His feet. One who has firm faith will accept their circumstances as the Lord’s will.

This firm faith was exhibited in a pastime where a group of devotees were traveling with Lord Caitanya. After traveling for several days, Lord Caitanya asked everybody, “Have you made any arrangements for preparing food to eat? ” The devotees said, “No, You did not tell us to, and we won’t do anything unless instructed by You. ” Lord Caitanya, speaking in the role of a devotee rather than the Lord said, “You are all renunciants. Actually, one has to have this faith that the Lord will provide. ”

The goal is to always be able to hear and chant about Krsna. However, as long as we are not liberated from our material bodies, and as long as we are not on that platform of such firm faith that we can completely neglect these bodies, then we have to perform the necessary duties in relationship to the body so that we can use it for developing an attraction for hearing and chanting about Krsna. We will develop an attraction for hearing and chanting about Krsna, in the association of devotees. If we set chanting very attentively the holy name of the Lord as a priority, then we have a purpose to maintain this body. We can use our ears for hearing that holy name of the Lord from the mouths of the devotees, and we can use our tongue for chanting the holy name. In such a way, one who is still conditioned can constantly chant the holy name and maintain his body. When executing his work, he can speak as required to get his work done, with the meditation that he is maintaining his body and family so that they can hear and chant about Krsna together. That is the goal of his work.

*kamasya nendriya-pritir*  
*labho jiveta yavata*  
*jivasya tattva-jijnasa*  
*nartho yas ceha karmabhih*

“Life’s desires should never be directed toward sense gratification. One should desire only a healthy life, or self-preservation, since a human being is meant for inquiry about the Absolute Truth. Nothing else should be the goal of one’s works. ” *(Bhag.* 1. 2. 10)

Thus we should accept whatever is necessary to maintain body and soul together, and we should be very careful that we do not become so diverted that we forget what the whole goal of human life is. Not using this body for becoming Krsna conscious in this life is a waste, simply being a *krpana* (miserly person). If we simply maintain this body for sense gratification, at the end of this life, the only thing that we can take with us is our sinful reactions. But, if we use this human form of life for hearing and chanting about Krsna, then that is the purpose for maintaining this body.

**Question: If we have a health problem, how do we seek the Lord’s protection?**

You have endeavor to protect your health. Srila Prabhupada makes this point clear in the purport to Bhagavatam 7. 9. 19: “Although as a matter of routine duty one must of course accept other remedial measures. ” We have this duty to perform, because the body does not belong to us; it belongs to Krsna. Therefore we must depend on the Lord for the outcome of our effort to heal the body. Pray to Krsna, “If for some reason You wish me to remain in this world, please protect me, but if You want to take me, then take me. ”

**Question: What if there is an extreme situation where one is approaching death and medicine cannot help. Are we still supposed to fight to stay alive?**

Certainly we should fight for our lives if we have a purpose to fulfill in this world. Srila Prabhupada was very ill and sometimes not so inclined to continue fighting. Then he would see how much his disciples wanted him to stay and how dependent they were on his presence. He would then think, “I must stay and help them. I have something that I have to try to finish while I am still here. ”

Devotees would even tell Prabhupada that he had not finished translating the *Bhagavatam or* that they were not advanced enough to survive in Krsna consciousness without him. Prabhupada would say, “All right, then I will endeavor to remain.” And he would endeavor to stay because there was something for him to stay for. Ultimately, of course, when Krsna decided Srila Prabhupada should leave, only then did he leave.

## Qualities of a Devotee

## Humility

*trnad api su-nicena*  
*taror iva sahisnuna*  
*amanina mana-dena*  
*kirtaniyah sada harih*

One should chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking oneself lower than the straw in the street; one should be more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and ready to offer all respect to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly. ” *(Siksastaka 3)*

*dharmas ca satyam ca damas tapas ca*  
*amatsaryam hris titiksanasuya*  
*yajnas ca danam ca dhrtih srutam ca*  
*vratani vai dvadasa brahmanasya*

“A *brahmana* must be perfectly religious. He must be truthful, and he must be able to control his senses. He must execute severe austerities, and he must be detached, humble, and tolerant. He must not envy anyone, and he must be expert in performing sacrifices and giving whatever he has in charity. He must be fixed in devotional service and expert in the knowledge of the *Vedas.* These are the twelve qualifications of a *brahmana. ”* (*Mahabharata*)

All these qualities are the ornaments decorating *brahmanas;* they are the qualities by which *brahmanas* are supposed to be directed. Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that a devotee has to give up pride of position and attachment to material opulences. Devotees who reject false designations can preach, because they consider themselves the Lord’s servant. They also see everyone else as a potential servant of the Lord and do not look at others for exploitation—as somehow meant to boost their false prestige. Engaging in devotional service or chanting Hare Krsna simply to garner respect for ourselves is a form of *anisthita-bhakti* called *taranga-rangini.* But we should also know that Krsna is always merciful toward His devotees and will try to clean our hearts from such attachment. If Krsna sees that we have become attached to position, power, fame, or social status, He will help us by removing them from our lives. In any case, He will take them away from us at death, even if He permits us to maintain false prestige while we are alive. At death we lose all social status, wealth, fame, and everything else we may think we possess. A devotee is defined as one who voluntarily gives these things up simply out of devotion for Krsna.

Although we will have to renounce, voluntarily or involuntarily, all these material attachments, we will not lose the devotional service we have rendered or the true devotional qualities we have acquired in the Lord’s service. Qualities like humility, Pridelessness, tolerance, and simplicity are natural to the soul.

Although I began by listing the qualities of a *brahmana,* we do not aspire to become *brahmanas* but Vaisnavas who possess those wonderful qualities. Prahlada Maharaja says that if one is ornamented with all the qualities of a *brahmana* but is not a devotee, he cannot purify himself due to a position of false prestige. As if to exemplify this point, Srila Prabhupada was once sitting on a *vyasasana* in Sweden, giving a lecture at a university about *varnasrama-dharma,* and in the midst of his lecture, he spoke about the qualities of a *brahmana,* stating that *brahmanas* were the first or top class in society. There was a man in the audience who had perhaps become envious. He seemed to think that Srila Prabhupada was indirectly glorifying himself, so he said, *“You’re* a *brahmana,* right? Is that why you sit on a fancy high seat? ”

Srila Prabhupada said, “No, I’m not *brahmana,* a first-class man. I am actually a *fifth-class* man, because I am a servant of all the other classes. ” His humility disarmed the man. Thus humility is so powerful.

Devotees should never think themselves superior to anyone; rather, they should offer respects to all. They think themselves servants, and that their service is to give Krsna to everyone they meet. Devotees realize that if they are going to share Krsna, they must respect their hearers, recognizing that Krsna is present in everyone’s heart. Devotees think, “Somehow, let me try to preach in a way that will help these people to follow Krsna’s supreme direction. ” They always see themselves as servants and are always conscious of the Lord’s presence in the hearts of everyone. With this humble consciousness one can preach.

**Question: How can we give up pride and become humble?**

There is no question of *becoming* humble. Humility is the natural quality of the soul, and therefore we must simply restore this natural condition. Pride is actually a demonic quality, a quality that drags us down to mundane consciousness where we think ourselves the doer and superior to others. Pride is unnatural to the soul. It causes us to think ourselves advanced simply because we have material skills; it drives us to find fault with other devotees or to make fun of them. If we deride devotees, the Lord will never accept anything we offer. Offending devotees drags us down into a fallen condition, allowing the seed of fruitive desires to again grow in our heart.

To restore our natural humility, we have to associate with and serve those who are humble. We should also look for opportunities in which the devotees will correct or instruct us. Serving devotees gives us an opportunity to see others as being on the spiritual platform. Krsna will go directly to those who have developed such pure vision.

Once, when Lord Caitanya went to Jagannatha Puri, Haridasa Thakura, considering himself degraded, thought, “I should not come before Lord Caitanya. ” Therefore he stayed at a distance and offered his *dandavats to* Lord Caitanya from afar. But when Lord Caitanya came to where the devotees were, He looked around and immediately asked for Haridasa. Someone pointed out, “Haridasa is over there, offering his obeisances to You from a distance. ”

Lord Caitanya immediately walked over to Haridasa, picked him up, and embraced him. He arranged a place for Haridasa near where He Himself was staying. Then Lord Caitanya pointed to the *cakra* on top of the Jagannatha temple, and told Haridasa that he could worship Lord Jagannatha every day simply by seeing that *cakra.* Then daily Lord Caitanya took *darsana* of Haridasa Thakura, bringing him *maha-prasada* from the temple.

But Haridasa Thakura continued to think himself fallen. One day he said, “O my Lord, I am so fallen. Please do not touch me. How can You look on me? I am so contaminated. I am not worthy of Your mercy. ” This is humility.

Similarly, when Rupa Gosvami and Sanatana Gosvami came before Lord Caitanya at Ramakeli, they fell down in *dandavat* at a distance, straw between their teeth, feeling themselves lower than that straw. When Lord Caitanya approached them they said, “O Lord, we are too fallen. We are millions of times more fallen than Jagai and Madhai. They are far more elevated than we are. They chanted Your name and became free of all offense, but we are constant offenders because we cause others pain by serving *mlecchas* \[meat-eaters\]. Please don’t touch us. ”

Lord Caitanya replied, “O Rupa and Sanatana, your humility is killing Me. It breaks My heart to see your humility. ” As with Haridasa, Lord Caitanya picked them up and embraced them, then initiated them into the chanting of the holy name. All the Vaisnavas present became ecstatic to see the two brothers receive the Lord’s mercy, and they chanted, *“Haribol! Haribol! "*

*A* Vaisnava is never envious of other devotees who receive the Lord’s mercy. Instead, they become ecstatic and chant, “Haribol! ” Ecstatic tears come to their eyes, because they experience bliss to see Krsna working in the lives of His devotees.

Prabhupada warned that in our Krsna consciousness movement many people may dress as Vaisnavas who are actually nondevotees. He said, “Therefore we must be careful to only associate with those devotees who have Vaisnava qualities. ” We should look for those devotees who have the qualities exemplified by our *acaryas.*

**Question: How can we become free of our critical mentality?**

The source of the critical mentality is usually pride and envy. We become proud of our material position and envious of others who are successful. And we look for faults in others in order to gain more respect and recognition for ourselves.

The faultfinding mentality is described as an *anartha* and one of the weaknesses of heart, and it obstructs our progress on the *bhakti* path. To remove this *anartha,* we must pray to Krsna to take it away.

One quality of a Vaisnava is *adosa-darsi,* one who sees no fault. Everywhere that devotees possessing this quality look, they see no faults. There are many examples of such devotees in *Caitanya-caritamrta*—Raghunatha dasa Gosvami and Haridasa Pandita to name two. Wherever these two devotees looked, they could see no faults.

Of course, it takes time and practice to develop this vision. *Kanistha-adhikaris* see *only* the faults in others, but if they are to remain in devotional service, they will have to make progress from this vision. Rising to the platform of *madhyama-adhikari* requires broadening one’s vision and actually feeling respect for others. When we feel respect, we can also feel compassion, and when we feel compassion we can preach. From a platform of respect, *madhyama-adhikaris* can distinguish between the innocent, to whom they preach, their equals with whom they make friends and share Krsna consciousness, the atheists and demons, whom they avoid, and the Supreme Lord, whom they worship. Even though the *madhyama-adhikaris* avoid atheists and demons, their compassion compels them to pray for their deliverance and well-being. They only neglect them (*upeksa*) so that they will not have the opportunity to commit further offense.

Lord Caitanya explains in the *Siksastaka* prayers that in order to chant constantly, we have to learn to respect all living beings. Pride prevents us from doing so. We must give it up.

Srila Jiva Gosvami explains that because those who are still prideful cannot respect other beings, their preaching is useless. How can we preach to nondevotees if we cannot even respect the devotees? Therefore he says such persons cannot preach; they have no preaching potency. Preaching means seeing that others have the potential to perform devotional service; it means being able to see their good qualities waiting to be employed in Krsna’s service. It means overlooking bad qualities, knowing that devotional practice will destroy such qualities at their root, especially when their good qualities are fanned.

The desire to render devotional service is present in every living entity’s heart. A preacher’s duty is to see that spark of desire and fan it. Preachers always see themselves as the servants of everyone. This requires humility. In a humble state of mind, thinking oneself lower than straw in the street, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and ready to offer all respects to others, one can preach and chant constantly.

In the beginning stages we have to practice, because we have not fully realized our constitutional position as Krsna’s servants. We can only stay in Vaisnava association if we learn humility. If over time we do not become humble, Krsna will simply weed His garden and pick us out.

Externally, we may think people give up Krsna consciousness for any variety of reasons, but no one leaves Krsna consciousness unless he or she has committed offenses. To protect us, Krsna will give each of us the opportunity to become humble. If we do not take advantage of that opportunity, Krsna’s garden will be weeded.

And we have to learn to see the good qualities and the potential to perform devotional service in nondevotees. Devotees are those who can make even demons devotees. They are *audarya,* magnanimous, and they reflect the magnanimity of Lord Caitanya. By Lord Nityananda’s mercy even offenders become devotees.

Pray to become a devotee, develop humility, and keep yourself in the association of devotees who possess humility, hearing from and serving them. Gradually, you will see your own nondevotional qualities and become disgusted by them. Then you will be able to constantly pray to the Lord to weed your heart so that He does not weed *you* out of the association of His devotees. Krsna has given us this one choice: Have your heart weeded or be weeded out yourself. Make the correct choice.

It is the characteristic of those at *bhava-bhakti to* be *mana-sunyata,* very humble and low despite their great advancement. Such humility allows the *bhava-bhakta* to be open to receiving the Lord’s mercy. Thinking ourselves the servants of the Vaisnavas is really the safest position.

**Question: What specific prayers should we offer to remove the fault-finding mentality?**

It is good to first offer prayers spoken by the *acaryas,* but devotees can also pray in their own words, saying, “I am fallen. I am finding faults with Vaisnavas. I know that if I continue to fault-find You may remove me from their association, and I can think of nothing more unfortunate than to be kicked away from the association of Vaisnavas. Understanding my fallen condition, My Lord, I open my mind and heart to You. Please teach me the ways of devotional service. Teach me how to become a Vaisnava. Please enter into the core of my heart and uproot these sinful desires. Please protect me so that I can always live in the association of Your devotees. Please help me to overcome these obstacles. By my own efforts I can achieve nothing. Only by Your mercy can I possibly overcome the obstacles to my spiritual life. And although I have no qualification to even ask this of You, still, I have no other hope. If I cannot come to You and reveal my heart, then I have no other hope in my life and cannot make progress in devotional service. I therefore surrender myself to Your will. If You so desire to help me overcome these obstacles, please enter into my heart and rip them out. I know when You begin to rip them out, it will be painful, but if that pain comes as a result of Your mercy, it will be my happiness. Please be merciful to me. ” Pray like this before beginning your *japa* in the morning. Place yourself in a position of complete dependence on the holy name. Doing so is how a devotee overcomes obstacles in devotional service. The Lord’s holy name will act, being the most merciful manifestation of Krsna.

## Forgiveness

Forgiveness is an important devotional quality. The Ninth Canto of *Srimad-Bhagavatam* states, “The duty of a *brahmana* is to culture the quality of forgiveness, which is illuminating like the sun. The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Hari, is pleased with those who are forgiving. ” *(Bhag.* 9. 15. 40)

In his purport to this text, Srila Prabhupada quotes Canakya Pandita:

The cuckoo bird, although very black, is beautiful because of its sweet voice. Similarly, a woman becomes beautiful by her chastity and faithfulness to her husband, and an ugly person becomes beautiful when he becomes a learned scholar. In the same way, *brahmanas, ksatriyas, Vaisyas* and *sudras* become beautiful by their qualities. *Brahmanas* are beautiful when they are forgiving, *ksatriyas* when they are heroic and never retreat from fighting, *Vaisyas* when they enrich cultural activities and protect cows, and *sudras* when they are faithful in the discharge of duties pleasing to their masters. Thus everyone becomes beautiful by his special qualities. And the special quality of the *brahmana,* as described here, is forgiveness.

Someone may say, “I did not take second initiation and am therefore not a *brahmana.* This order to forgive does not apply to me. ” We can look for as many excuses as we wish to avoid becoming forgiving, but devotees—even first-initiated devotees— are supposed to be higher than *brahmanas.* We are supposed to remain aspiring devotees, what to speak of becoming *brahmanas.* The *real* purpose of the Krsna consciousness movement is not to produce *brahmanas* but Vaisnavas. The *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* (2. 12) states how this is possible:

*yatha kancanatam yati*  
*kamsyam rasa-vidhanatah*  
*tatha diksa-vidhanena*  
*dvijatvam jayate nrnam*

“As bell metal is turned to gold when mixed with mercury in an alchemical process, so one who is properly trained and initiated by a bona fide spiritual master immediately becomes a *brahmana. ”*

Devotees are automatically supposed to acquire the quality of forgiveness. It is our tendency to blame others for our misfortunes, but as the *Bhagavatam* states, there is a higher principle we should be striving to achieve, and if we actually want to become devotees, we must develop devotional qualities.

Dharma, the bull of religion’s discussion in the *Bhagavatam’s* First Canto, exemplifies the quality of forgiveness. When Kali was beating Dharma, he broke three of his legs. Pariksit Maharaja, the protector of religious principles, asked Dharma, “Who has done this to you? ”

Dharma replied, “If I point out the perpetrator of my misfortune, then I am just as guilty as the perpetrator, because nothing happens without the Lord’s sanction. Obviously, the Lord has sanctioned this act. What use would it be to point out its agent? ”

Hearing Dharma respond like this made Pariksit Maharaja appreciate Dharma’s position and character, and he said, “You must be religiosity personified, and I am a protector of religious principles. Therefore, I will find out who perpetrated this act. ”

For those who cultivate Krsna consciousness, forgiveness manifests automatically in the heart. It is difficult for nondevotees to forgive others, because without Krsna conscious vision, one sees only duality, and in duality we see things only as good or bad according to our own mind. Such “religion” is called “manodharma, ” the religion of the mind. We have to transcend the conception of good or bad, right or wrong. The only way to transcend such duality is to become Krsna conscious and to see Krsna present everywhere and in every act.

Of course, one may ask, “What if someone did something to me that I didn’t deserve? ” Renounce your tendency to justify things by thinking you didn’t deserve them! Krsna is behind everything that happens in this world. Remember Krsna and you will never lose.

Krsna consciousness contains no duality. Everything is good, because Krsna is all good. There is no bad in anything Krsna does. Srila Prabhupada gives the example that when we face the sun, everything is bright, but when we turn our back to the sun, we see darkness in the form of our own shadow. Krsna is all-good, but when we turn away from Him, we see evil, darkness and ignorance.

If you struggle with forgiveness, then remember how Haridasa Thakura forgave those who beat and tormented him. Prahlada Maharaja also forgave his father, who tried to kill him, and even asked Lord Nrsimhadeva to bless him. The Pandavas forgave Dhrtarastra, even though Dhrtarastra made so many plans to destroy them. Srila Prabhupada even mentions Lord Jesus Christ in a Fourth Canto purport: While on the cross Jesus prayed, “O Lord, forgive them for they know not what they do. ”

These examples are there to teach us how to cultivate the quality of forgiveness. And by so doing, we can also change the heart of a person who may have even done something wrong. Just like when Lord Brahma and Daksa went to Lord Siva to pacify him. Because Lord Siva was so Krsna conscious, immediately Daksa’s heart became pacified, having come in contact with a Vaisnava who was forgiving. Forgiveness is described to be a very beautiful ornament of a devotee.

**Question: Lord Caitanya mentions** *hati mata,* **the mad elephant offense, saying that when we commit it, we lose everything in Krsna consciousness. Does this offense uproot the creeper of devotion? Do we lose everything if we commit this offense? Or can we return and begin again from where we left off?**

There is no loss in any Krsna conscious endeavor, but if we commit Vaisnava *aparadha,* our advancement will be checked; we will not be able to make any more advancement. And if we cannot make more advancement, the seeds of fruitive desire may sprout. Ramacandra Puri is an example of this misfortune. He offended Madhavendra Puri (and later, Lord Caitanya), and because of these offenses he became attracted again to material desires. If we commit such an offense, we will fall from our position. But we can also be redeemed from our offense if the Vaisnava we offended is forgiving.

**Question: What should we do if we approach a Vaisnava we have offended, but his or her anger does not subside?**

Bhaktivinoda Thakura discusses this in Sri *Harinama* Cintamani: “If anyone offends a devotee in a moment of delusion or madness, he must fall at the devotee’s feet and repent bitterly and, weeping and full of contrition, beg forgiveness. He should declare himself a fallen wretch, in need of that devotee’s grace. A devotee is very merciful; his heart will soften and he will embrace the offender, thus exonerating him from his offenses. ” With this mentality he should take full shelter of the holy name, increasing his chanting. Continuing to feel himself fallen, he may become relieved from the offense. Of course, a Vaisnava may not be so quick to forgive because he may understand that we are seeking only relief from the reaction and do not feel true repentance. Therefore, we should cultivate a genuine feeling of repentance while we chant and pray to become free of the tendency to commit such offenses in the first place.

## Overcoming Adversity

## Voluntarily Accepting Suffering for a Higher Cause

Sinful activity, which originates from ignorance and fruitive desire, leads to suffering. Intelligent persons do not try for material happiness because they know that whatever happiness they are destined will come automatically, as will misery. We know that our past sinful activities produce physical and mental suffering, but they also produce the potential to undergo more suffering in the future, because they produce seeds of material desire that then lie dormant in our heart until they can fructify. We are forced to suffer whenever we misuse our independence. Narada Muni says that when we try to counteract the karma brought on by fruitive activity by performing other fruitive activities—activities devoid of Krsna consciousness—it is like trying to counteract a bad dream with a hallucination. We may awaken from the dream only to find ourselves in the middle of an illusion. Hallucinations do not relieve bad dreams.

Narada Muni says that if we want to free ourselves from the suffering that results from fruitive activity, we must awaken our Krsna consciousness. When we awaken Krsna consciousness, our journey in the material world will feel simply like a bad dream.

**Question: Can a person be happy in spiritual life and suffer simultaneously?**

Krsna says in *Bhagavad-gita, su-sukham kartum avyayam:* this process is everlasting and joyfully performed. We can therefore experience happiness even in the midst of suffering. When devotees see their suffering in connection with Krsna they are joyful because they understand that their suffering helps them come closer to Krsna.

Krsna says to Uddhava: “Having awakened faith in the narrations of My glories, being disgusted with all material activities, knowing that all sense gratification leads to misery, but still being unable to renounce all sense enjoyment, My devotee should remain happy and worship Me with great faith and conviction. Even though he is sometimes engaged in sense enjoyment, My devotee knows that all sense gratification leads to a miserable result, and he sincerely repents such activities. ” *(Bhag.* 11. 20. 27-28)

By such faith, devotees never give up Sri Krsna’s lotus feet, and their suffering simply brings them closer to Krsna. This is the devotees’ real happiness; they are not interested in the false happiness of this world. To be truly happy, we have to have this kind of faith. Therefore Krsna says, “Be happy and continue to worship Me with faith. ” If we have faith in the purpose of our suffering, we will be pleased to have an opportunity to be recognized by Krsna. However, if our suffering drives us to take shelter in sense gratification, then where is our real happiness? We will be living only in illusion.

There are two kinds of suffering: forced suffering and voluntary suffering. Forced suffering is the kind of suffering that results from karmic activity; it always leads to more suffering because we tend to try to counteract our suffering by performing more karmic activity. We do not go to Krsna for shelter. Voluntary suffering is when we recognize that we are undergoing suffering for the Lord’s pleasure. Devotees don’t try to counteract voluntary suffering by karmic activity but go to their true shelter, Sri Krsna.

Prahlada Maharaja says *(Bhag.* 7. 5. 30-32, as quoted in purport to *Bhag.* 3. 9. 17):

Persons who are determined to totally rot in false, material happiness cannot become Krsna-minded either by instructions from teachers, by self-realization or by parliamentary discussions.

They are dragged by the unbridled senses into the darkest region of ignorance, and thus they madly engage in what is called “chewing the chewed. ”

Because of their foolish activities, they are unaware that the ultimate goal of human life is to achieve Visnu, the Lord of the cosmic manifestation, and so their struggle for existence is in the wrong direction of material civilization, which is under the external energy. They are led by similar foolish persons, just as one blind man is led by another blind man and both fall in the ditch.

Such foolish men cannot be attracted towards the activities of the Supreme Powerful, who is actually the neutralizing measure for their foolish activities, unless and until they have the good sense to be guided by the great souls who are completely freed from material attachment.

We are bound by the ropes of our own fruitive activities and the only destination we attain is our own suffering. Therefore, voluntarily accept inconvenience to satisfy the Supreme Lord. The fruit of all our austerities, inconveniences, and suffering in this world should be the Lord’s pleasure.

If we have implicit faith in the Lord’s words, we can still be happy even if we are suffering. Mukunda had such faith when he was told that he would only attain the Lord’s *darsana* after ten million births; he felt only ecstasy and did not feel resentment or even pain at hearing it would take him ten million births before again seeing the Lord. We must have faith like Mukunda’s. Then if we suffer by the Lord’s will, we will accept it as our means of repentance and will continue to worship the Lord, trusting that He will forgive us for our offenses.

This world was created to provide a place of sacrifice for the *jivas.* Krsna told Brahma, “O sinless Brahma, you may know from Me that it was I who first ordered you to undergo penance when you were perplexed in your duty. Such penance is My heart and soul, and therefore penance and I are nondifferent. ” *(Bhag.* 2. 9. 23)

Also in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (5. 5. 1) Lord Rsabhadeva tells his sons:

*nayam deho deha-bhajam nrloke*  
*kastan kaman arhate vid-bhujam ye*  
*tapo divyam putraka yena sattvam*  
*suddhyed yasmad brahma-saukhyam tv anantam*

“My dear boys, of all the living entities who have accepted material bodies in this world, one who has been awarded this human form should not work hard day and night simply for sense gratification, which is available even for dogs and hogs that eat stool. One should engage in penance and austerity to attain the divine position of devotional service. By such activity, one’s heart is purified, and when one attains this position, he attains eternal, blissful life, which is transcendental to material happiness and which continues forever. ” Lord Rsabhadeva makes it clear that one who has accepted a human form of life must use it to perform austerities for the Lord’s pleasure. The Lord becomes pleased when He sees a devotee willing to undergo personal inconvenience on His behalf.

The Lord is sometimes called Soka-satana, “the destroyer of lamentation. ” He is also known as Hari, “one who takes away. ” The Lord, by His kind nature, takes away His devotees’ lamentation, and that is one of the reasons Lord Caitanya appeared in this world. The Lord is never happy to see the living entities suffering and is thus eager to relieve their pain. But because they are bound and conditioned, they identify with their body and the illusory energy and thus suffer the pain of material attachment.

To illustrate this point, Visnujana Maharaja once told a story about a married man who had a paramour. When the husband returned home, his wife asked him, “I have heard that you are seeing another woman. What is it that she does that I don’t do? ”

The husband replied, “I am attracted to the way she cooks for me. ”

The wife then decided to prepare a special meal for her husband to attract him back to her. The next time her husband returned, his wife presented him with a feast fit for the demigods. The husband was overwhelmed by her efforts, and the wife thought, “Now he will never leave me. How can his paramour surpass this? ”

But even after eating the feast, the husband did not return home the next night. The wife was surprised. When she saw her husband next she asked, “Do you mean she cooks better than I did? ”

“No, your cooking was much better. ”

“Then what has attracted you to her? ”

“Actually, it’s not just the way she cooks, but the way she dresses. ”

Hearing this the wife thought, “All right, I will capture him with my beautiful dressing. ” She then purchased the most costly clothing, ornaments, and make-up, and decorated herself.

When the husband returned home, his wife greeted him with an embrace. He was captivated by her beauty and therefore stayed home that night. But again, the following night, he went to see his paramour.

His wife became frustrated the next time she saw her husband. “Does she dress better than I did? ”

“No, your beauty quite surpassed hers. ”

“Then why did you go to her again? What does she have that I don’t have? ”

“It’s the way she kicks and bites me. ”

“Kicks and bites you?! You want to be kicked and bitten? I can’t do that. I love you. If you want to be kicked and bitten, you will have to go to her. What can I do? ”

Like the faithful wife, Krsna tries to attract us by showing us the glories of the spiritual world, sending His devotees who reveal His wonders, and giving us His own holy name. As Lord Caitanya He came to deliver the most fallen, and Lord Nityananda, His servant, was so merciful that even if the living entities did not want the

Lord, He would beg them with tears in His eyes, “You must chant these names and make your life successful. ” Is it that we are too enthralled by Maya’s kicking and biting to respond to Their mercy? Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda are *parama karuna, pahun dui jana*—the two most merciful Lords. We should take what They have come to offer: the pure holy name and relief from our suffering.

**Question: In the Bhagavad-gita Krsna says that one who follows the regimen of eating and sleeping gets relief from the fearful material miseries. But He also mentions that one who turns to** *bhakti* **is relieved from the miseries of this world. Are these two methods mutually exclusive? Do we need to follow both statements or just one of them?**

In the *Bhagavad-gita,* Krsna speaks about regulating the senses, which is required. But devotees can automatically regulate their senses by using them in Krsna’s service. The scriptures contain information about prescribed and prohibited activities. As long as we are practicing *vaidhi-sadhana-bhakti* we must honor both categories. That is, we must accept whatever is favorable for our cultivation of Krsna consciousness and reject whatever is unfavorable. The most favorable activity is hearing about Krsna. To simply regulate the senses will not give us a higher spiritual taste.

Srila Prabhupada gives many examples of *yogis* and *munis* who fell from their position, although they were strict in their performance of austerities and especially in their habits of eating and sleeping. Why did they fall down? Because they did not associate with Vaisnavas or hear the Lord’s wonderful pastimes, thus enabling them to develop an attraction for the Lord’s lotus feet. We must both regulate our senses so that we can focus on hearing about Krsna and restrict ourselves from whatever hinders our Krsna conscious development.

**Question: How can we become completely free from anxiety?**

The spiritual world is called Vaikuntha, the place of no anxiety, and the material world is Kuntha, the place of anxiety. There is no question of becoming free from anxiety in the material world as long as we are in material consciousness.

But Srila Prabhupada says that two kinds of anxiety exist in this world: material anxiety and spiritual anxiety. Material anxiety causes bondage, and spiritual anxiety drives us forward toward liberation. Prabhupada says that material anxiety is just like a rich man who must always worry about his money. It’s a miserly mentality. Spiritual anxiety, however, develops when a person is anxious to obtain something for Krsna’s service. Such persons don’t think about relieving their anxiety; they simply feel the anxiety in service to Krsna. Thus spiritual anxiety is liberating.

## Overcoming Obstacles

*bhaktih paresanubhavo viraktir*  
*anyatra caisa trika eka-kalah*  
*prapadyamanasya yathasnatah syus*  
*tustih pustih ksud-apayo ’nu-ghasam*

Devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord, and detachment from other things—these three occur simultaneously for one who has taken shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in the same way that pleasure, nourishment and relief from hunger come simultaneously and increasingly, with each bite, for a person engaged in eating. ” *(Bhag.* 11. 2. 42)

Srila Prabhupada uses this verse to help us analyze our devotional service and to see if we are making spiritual progress. If we want to know that we are advancing in Krsna consciousness, we should analyze whether we are becoming detached from matter. The more we see Krsna’s presence in our lives, the more we remember Him, and the more we become free from material entanglement, we can say that we are making advancement. If we are always dependent on Krsna, we will become free from all the obstacles of conditional life by His grace. And we can learn to depend on Him if we endeavor to always please Him.

Lord Caitanya describes one of the symptoms of Madhavendra Puri’s intense love: he did not consider personal convenience in serving the Lord but thought only of the Lord’s pleasure. The Lord then states (Cc. *Madhya* 17. 185):

*dharma-sthapana-hetu sadhura vyavahara*  
*puri-gosanira ye acarana, sei dharma sara*

“A devotee’s behavior establishes the true purpose of religious principles. The behavior of Madhavendra Puri Gosvami is the essence of such religious principles. Madhavendra Puri’s example shows how a devotee is ready to undergo personal inconvenience simply for the Lord’s pleasure. ”

For example, when Madhavendra Puri was in Remuna and Gopinatha stole the sweet rice for him to sample, he was thinking that crowds of people would come to honor him. Not being interested in *pratistha* (reputation), he left the vicinity of the temple and went somewhere secluded to chant. He considered *pratistha* an impediment to his devotion. In fact, Madhavendra Puri always traveled alone, without a companion, to facilitate his *bhajana.*

Not long after this incident, Madhavendra Puri was continuing on his way to Jagannatha Puri to find camphor and especially sandalwood to bring for his Gopala’s pleasure. When he arrived in Jagannatha Puri, however, and people saw his ecstatic symptoms, they gathered around him in crowds to see this exalted devotee. Madhavendra Puri thought, “Again I must leave, but if I leave, how will I be able to accomplish the task I have set out to do? ”

Madhavendra Puri meditated on only one object: the Lord’s satisfaction. To serve the Lord he stayed this time despite crowds. He was never motivated by the desire to gather respect for himself. In fact, he considered the offered respect an austerity. It was simply one of the inconveniences he had to undergo in his service to Gopala.

Madhavendra Puri accepted many inconveniences on that trip. He wanted a large amount of sandalwood, which was not easy to obtain since sandalwood was under government control. But somehow he was able to explain what he needed and arrangements were made. After he received the required amount, he then had to travel out of Orissa through dangerous tracts of land governed by Muslims. Although he had the necessary documents to release the sandalwood, he had to pass through various tolls. He never considered the dangers or obstacles on his path. In fact, he considered these a source of pleasure, and in the Lord’s service, he happily embraced them.

Service should be rendered selflessly, without desire for recognition or return. The greatest source of satisfaction devotees can feel is when the Lord accepts their offerings. All obstacles to such service can be removed by the Lord’s grace, just as Gopala removed all obstacles for Madhavendra Puri. When Madhavendra Puri arrived in Remuna, the Lord appeared to him in a dream for the third time. He told Madhavendra Puri, “You are here in Remuna, and My deity form as Gopinatha is also here. He is nondifferent from Me, so you should take the sandalwood and camphor that you have been carrying with great difficulty and arrange for it to be offered to Gopinatha. When the sandalwood and camphor are applied to Gopinatha’s body, My own temperature will reduce. ”

Gopala had arranged this for Madhavendra Puri because He understood that he would undergo great inconvenience by having to travel through so many difficult areas with the sandalwood. The Lord therefore personally removed the obstacles from His devotee’s path. The Lord wants to see how much we desire to please Him, and Lord Caitanya later said that Gopala had tested Madhavendra Puri. Of course, Madhavendra Puri passed the test wonderfully. The Lord uses such pure devotees to show us the definition of selfless service.

Those of us who are still aspiring to achieve selfless service can look at and meditate on these examples. We can pray to achieve such devotion by praying to Krsna to remove whatever obstacles in our hearts obstruct us from rendering real service. A devotee never stops endeavoring to please the Lord. It is the Lord Himself who manifests wonderful results in reciprocation.

But as soon as we serve, motivated by the desire to become famous, our offering is spoiled and the Lord is disinclined to accept it. If we *really* want to offer something for Krsna’s pleasure, we have to pray for the internal and external obstacles to be removed and for our offering to become pure.

In *Mukunda-mala-stotra*, King Kulasekhara states:

O tongue, please constantly chant the mantra composed of Sri Krsna’s names. This is the only mantra for destroying all enemies, the mantra worshiped by every word of the *Upanisads,* the mantra that uproots *samsara,* the mantra that drives away all the darkness of ignorance, the mantra for attaining infinite opulence, the mantra for curing those bitten by the poisonous snake of worldly distress, and the mantra for making one’s birth in this world successful.

If we want to be successful, we have to keep our hearts pure by chanting the holy names attentively in the association of devotees. Whatever obstacles are there, we should be willing to overcome them.

## The Lord’s Protection

The symptoms of a really Krsna conscious person are that he knows that Krsna is the Supreme master, he always thinks himself in danger without Krsna, and by taking shelter of Krsna’s lotus feet he always feels safe. Krsna says, *kaunteya pratijanihi na me bhaktah pranasyati* “You may declare to the world that My devotee is never vanquished. ” *(Bg.* 9. 31) If one becomes a pure devotee of Krsna, there is no question of danger. Of course, Krsna gives protection to everyone, for without His protection no one can live even for a single moment. But we should not think, “If Krsna is giving protection to everyone, what is the use of becoming a devotee? ” A king gives protection to every one of his citizens, for that is his duty, but he especially protects his own circle of men. This is not unnatural. If one directly engages in the service of the President, when he is in some difficulty he is especially protected. Although the President gives protection to all the citizens, those who personally associate with him, giving him service, receive special consideration. That is not actually partiality. It is natural. When a gentleman loves all children but has special love for his own children, no one will say, “Oh, why are you loving your own children more than others? ” No, that is natural. Similarly, Krsna says in *Bhagavad-gita, samo ’ham sarva-bhutesu* “I am equal to everyone. ” Krsna, being God, loves everyone because everyone is part of Him. Nonetheless, He takes special care of His devotees. Therefore He says, *kaunteya pratijanihi na me bhaktah pranasyati:*

“My devotee will never be vanquished. ” *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 151)

Devotees achieve peace when they have firm confidence that Krsna will always protect them. Pure devotees do not even ask for the Lord’s protection; they ask only for the opportunity to remember Krsna. For those who remember Krsna under all circumstances, Krsna’s protection is automatic. Devotees know that however Krsna deals with them is in their best interest. Bhaktivinoda Thakura prays, “O, Krsna, kill me or protect me—whatever You like You can do. ” Devotees understand that even if they are killed, Krsna will bring them to Him because they are not meant to live in the material world in the first place. If the devotees remember Krsna, Krsna will not forget them. Krsna says *(Bhag.* 9. 4. 68), “The pure devotee is always within the core of My heart, and I am always in the heart of the pure devotee. My devotees do not know anything else but Me, and I do not know anyone else but them. ” This kind of reciprocation is natural.

If, however, someone never thinks of Krsna, then Krsna says, “Since this person never thinks of Me and always wants something other than Me, I do not have to be personally involved in his life. If he wants something from material nature, then he can earn it by performing pious activities. Material nature, working under My direction, will care for his needs. ”

There are thirty-three million demigods who act according to Krsna’s direction and who provide all of the living entities’ necessities. Why should Krsna have to get personally involved in fulfilling the living entities’ material needs? He can simply enjoy as the *parama-purusa,* the Supreme Enjoyer.

When Krsna wants to enjoy, He exchanges with His devotees through the heart. The devotees thus feel satisfied, seeing Krsna everywhere. This is how Krsna gives His devotees pleasure, satisfying all of their desires and protecting them from the material world.

Once, when Srila Prabhupada was still in *grhastha* life, he was riding on a train with his young son. The conductor came over to Prabhupada’s son and played a trick, asking, “Where is your ticket? ” The boy said, “I don’t have a ticket. ”

“You don’t have a ticket? Then you must get off the train! ”

The son’s face immediately turned white, and he said, “How can I get off the train? It’s moving. ”

But suddenly he looked up and said, “He is my father. ” His anxiety was gone, and he smiled. For a moment, he had forgotten that his father was with him, but as soon as he remembered, his fear disappeared. Similarly, devotees faced with dangerous situations always remember Krsna as their protector.

What of those who don’t remember Krsna? Sukadeva Gosvami tells us that those ears which do not hear the Lord’s pastimes are like the holes of snakes; those eyes that do not see the Lord’s beautiful form are compared to the eyes on peacock feathers; that tongue that is used to speak about mundane subject matters is like a croaking frog beckoning death; and that head that does not bow down before the Lord’s deity is like a king with a big crown that causes him to drown because of its heavy weight. Such a king drowns in false pride and his life is thus wasted. Sukadeva Gosvami adds: “What is the value of a prolonged life which is wasted, inexperienced by years in this world? Better a moment of full consciousness, because that gives one a start in searching after his supreme interest. ” *(Bhag.* 2. 1. 12) Such a devotee always thinks himself in danger without Krsna, and therefore always takes shelter in remembering Him.

Srila Prabhupada explains that Krsna considers His devotees valuable jewels—jewels to which He holds on very tightly. He always protects His devotees, and the devotees always feel His protection.

In the *Adi Purana,* Krsna tells Arjuna, *jagatam guravo bhakta, bhaktanam guravo vayam:* “The Vaisnavas are the spiritual masters of everyone, and I am the spiritual master of the Vaisnavas. As the Vaisnava is the spiritual master of everyone, so I am also the spiritual master of everyone. The devotees are My friends, and I am the friend of My devotees. The devotee is My guru, and I am His guru. O son of Prtha, I go wherever My devotee goes. ”

He also tells Uddhava in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (11. 14. 16), “With the dust of My devotees’ lotus feet I desire to purify the material worlds, which are situated within Me. Thus, I always follow the footsteps of My pure devotees, who are free from all personal desire, rapt in thought of My pastimes, peaceful, without any feelings of enmity, and of equal disposition everywhere. ”

**Question: In Bhagavad-gita, Krsna confirms that He protects His devotees who devote their lives to satisfying Him, so why do we see devotees like Haridasa Thakura being beaten and punished?**

The greatest danger we face in this world is to forget Krsna. Therefore Krsna says that He protects His devotee, and that His devotee will always remember Him and never forget Him. Whether he stays in the material world or returns to Him, Krsna always protects His devotee.

Haridasa Thakura shows us how a devotee does not give up his Lord under any circumstance. Although Haridasa was beaten in twenty-two marketplaces, he did not give up chanting Hare Krsna. We should not give up the chanting either. And although Haridasa was beaten, the Lord protected him, even though Haridasa did not ask for such protection, by protecting Haridasa’s devotional remembrance. It may appear that Haridasa was suffering, but he was absorbed in thoughts of Krsna and did not experience the pain.

There are many examples of Krsna protecting His devotees. For example, Krsna entered Uttara’s womb to save Pariksit Maharaja from Asvatthama’s *brahmastra.* Kuntidevi also mentions a number of instances in which the Lord protected the Pandava family. Then she prays, “O my Lord, You have executed all duties Yourself. Are you leaving us today, though we are completely dependent on Your mercy and have no one else to protect us, now when all kings are at enmity with us? ” *(Bhag.* 1. 8. 37)

Krsna also protected Arjuna during the Battle of Kuruksetra. When Bhisma was about to kill Arjuna, Krsna broke His vow not to fight on either side and rushed, chariot wheel upraised, to protect him. Krsna declares that His devotee will never be vanquished, and in this circumstance, because Bhisma had vowed to fight so fiercely against Arjuna that Krsna would be forced to take up arms, Krsna protected both Arjuna and Bhisma’s vow.

The Lord always protects devotees who surrender fully to Him. This protection does not always mean that their bodies will be protected, but they will be saved from having to fall again into any position in the material world.

Srila Prabhupada gives the example that a devotee is like a king’s son, always under the watchful protection of his father; nondevotees are like orphans with no one caring for them. Even if the king’s son leaves the kingdom, the king will always eagerly seek him out and provide for his needs. Similarly, those who take to devotional service come under the Lord’s watchful protection.

Srila Prabhupada also explains that in the material world, it is the highest misfortune to be forced into dependence on another’s mercy. This is because no one but the Lord can give proper protection. Similarly, Sukadeva Gosvami states that people tend to take shelter of fallible soldiers for protection, but fallible soldiers cannot protect us against the greatest types of danger. They may appear to help, but they cannot give ultimate protection—or *any* protection—without the Lord’s sanction.

Prahlada Maharaja prays, “My Lord Nrsimhadeva, O Supreme, because of a bodily conception of life, embodied souls neglected and not cared for by You cannot do anything for their betterment. Whatever remedies they accept, although perhaps temporarily beneficial, are certainly impermanent. ” *(Bhag.* 7. 9. 19)

Those living entities who desire to be sheltered by the Lord’s external energy are placed completely in her care. Although Krsna remains with each living entity as the Supersoul in the heart, He does not interfere with the living entity’s desires, but simply sanctions and bears witness.

Devotees desire only to be dependent on the Lord’s mercy. There is no other protection. The Pandavas were all highly qualified warriors, powerful *maharathas,* yet in all their endeavors they remained dependent on Krsna for the result.

The story of Maharaja Ambarisa provides a similar example of the power of a devotee’s dependence on the Lord. Maharaja Ambarisa was an exalted Vaisnava because he depended on the Lord’s mercy. Everything he did was offered in devotion. The *Bhagavatam* describes that he engaged all of his senses in the Lord’s service. He engaged his eyes in seeing the deity’s beautiful form; he engaged his ears in always hearing *krsna-katha*; he engaged his tongue in always glorifying the Lord’s activities and accepting *prasadam;* he engaged his arms in always cleansing the Lord’s temple; he engaged his mind in always thinking of the Lord’s activities and pastimes; he engaged his sense of touch by touching the bodies of Vaisnavas, who themselves were completely purified, and taking dust from their lotus feet; and he engaged his legs in always walking to the temple. Maharaja Ambarisa thus lived the ideal Vaisnava life—the life of someone who has given everything for the Lord’s service.

Maharaja Ambarisa had been performing an Ekadasi *vrata* for the Lord’s pleasure for one year. When it came time to break his Ekadasi fast, however, he had to consider how to both satisfy the terms of the vow and Durvasa Muni. Maharaja Ambarisa then consulted with *brahmanas,* who instructed him that by drinking water he was both breaking his fast and not breaking it. But when Durvasa Muni returned to the palace, he realized that Ambarisa Maharaja had broken his fast before feeding his guest, and he became angry. He reprimanded the king, “What kind of host are you who eats before his guest? What kind of *king* are you? You follow irreligious principles! ”

Of course, Durvasa Muni is famous for being feisty. In this situation, he became so angry that he tried to curse Maharaja Ambarisa. He yanked out a tuft of his own hair and threw it to the ground while chanting mantras to create a demon. Immediately, a huge, fiery demon, powerful enough to destroy the creation, appeared. Maharaja Ambarisa, however, took shelter of Krsna and remained undisturbed. Although he was capable of it, he did not counteract Durvasa Muni’s curse and instead prepared himself to die. The Bhagavatam states (1. 18. 48):

*tiraskrta vipralabdhah*  
*saptah ksipta hata api*  
*nasya tat pratikurvanti*  
*tad-bhaktah prabhavo ’pi hi*

“The devotees of the Lord are so forbearing that even though they are defamed, cheated, cursed, disturbed, neglected or even killed, they are never inclined to avenge themselves. ”

To protect Durvasa, the Lord invoked His Sudarsana *cakra.* The Sudarsana *cakra* destroyed the demon and then chased Durvasa Muni for his offense toward the sinless Maharaja Ambarisa. Durvasa Muni ran for his life. The Sudarsana *cakra* was capable of consuming whole universes, and Durvasa was afraid. He tried to take shelter of the hills, valleys, and mountains, then ran to Lord Brahma for shelter. Lord Brahma explained that he could not protect Durvasa from Krsna. Durvasa Muni next went to Mount Kailasa to take shelter of Lord Siva, but Lord Siva, too, could offer no protection.

Durvasa Muni then went to Vaikuntha to take shelter of Lord Narayana, but the Supreme Lord said, “I cannot possibly protect you. I am completely under the control of My devotees and am not at all independent. Because My devotees are free from material desires, I always sit in their hearts. Even those who take shelter of My devotees are very dear to Me. ”

In his commentary on this section of the Bhagavatam, Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura writes, “Durvasa Muni went before the Lord to ask for His protection, but the Lord told him He wasn’t independent. Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura then poses a question on Durvasa Muni’s behalf: How is it that You are dependent and not independent?

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura then provides the Lord’s answer: It is My nature to be completely dependent on My devotees.

Durvasa Muni: Yes, but You are always merciful to those who are suffering, and whenever You see others’ suffering, Your heart melts.

The Lord: Actually, My mind does not belong to Me. I don’t even belong to Myself! You are asking Me to protect you and to think about your suffering, but My mind cannot think about your suffering because it has been stolen by My devotees. They have taken Me into their hearts where they serve Me. I am always in their hearts and they are always in My heart. The devotees do not know anyone but Me and I do not know anyone but them. If I were to say to them, ‘You have pleased Me very much by your service, so please, let Me do something for you, ’ My devotees will always say, ‘I don’t want anything from You. I only want the opportunity to serve You. ’ I therefore feel so indebted to them and want to give them something valuable in exchange. Therefore I give them the most valuable thing I have—My heart. They don’t ask Me for it, but still I give it to them. Just as a chaste wife brings her husband under control by her service, so My pure devotees, who are always absorbed in thoughts of Me and who are equal to everyone, bring Me completely under their control by their service. Therefore, I am helpless and completely dependent on the devotees; they have stolen My heart and mind. Durvasa, I cannot think about your suffering. If you want My consideration, you have to go to My devotee and ask him for his forgiveness. ”

Certainly it is a devotee’s nature to be forgiving, but the Lord places the power to forgive in the devotees’ hearts by placing Himself in their hearts. He thus becomes completely dependent on their desires and decisions. This is His *svabhava.* If the devotee wishes to extend mercy, the Lord’s mercy will follow.

Krsna therefore extends His mercy through the mercy of His devotees. If we wish to get the Lord’s mercy, getting the mercy of the devotees is our only hope.

Dhruva Maharaja provides another example of a devotee who was dependent on the Lord. When it was time for Dhruva Maharaja to give up his body, and death personified appeared before him, Dhruva Maharaja stepped on the head of Death to mount the Vaikuntha airplane. Death is no obstacle for a devotee. Rather, it provides a step that takes the devotee closer to the spiritual world.

Those who always remember the Lord in dependence, are assured of His protection, and the Lord helps them come back to Him. Krsna states in *Bhagavad-gita* (9. 22):

*ananyas cintayanto mam*  
*ye janah paryupasate*  
*tesam nityabhiyuktanam*  
*yoga-ksemam vahamy aham*

“But those who always worship Me with exclusive devotion, meditating on My transcendental form—to them I carry what they lack, and I preserve what they have. ”

When Arjunacarya read this verse, he tried to change the word *vahami* to *karomi.* He thought, “I am a poor *brahmana,* and here Krsna says He will personally carry. How can this be right? How could Krsna personally carry anything for someone like me?”

Thinking there was a mistake in the book, he crossed out *vahami* and replaced it with *karomi,* which means to arrange through agents.

Later, after he had gone off to bathe and beg alms, two boys arrived at his home burdened by bundles of food. They urged Arjunacarya’s wife to quickly take what they had brought.

The woman couldn’t understand their hurry, but the boys insisted, “Please, take it right away! We have to run before your husband comes back and strikes us again. ”

Arjunacarya’s wife was astonished. “What are you saying? My husband is a gentle *brahmana.* He would never strike anyone. ”

One boy said, “No, your husband struck me. ” He then turned around and showed her the marks on his back and said, “Now please take this food. We must run before he returns. ”

The wife took everything the boys had brought and prepared a nice meal. When Arjunacarya returned and saw the meal, he asked, “Where did all this food come from? ”

The wife told him about the two boys, and then asked how it was possible he had beaten them. She said, “I tried to defend you by saying that you would not possibly strike anyone, but the boy turned around and showed me his back. His back was cut in a cross pattern. They were afraid of you and wanted to be gone before you got back in case you should strike them again. ”

Arjunacarya thought about this strange event and then suddenly understood. He said, “You are very fortunate. You have received the Supreme Lord’s mercy. Krsna has personally come to provide these things for us. Just before I went out, I was studying *Bhagavad-gita* and reading *tesam nityabhiyuktanam, yoga-ksemam vahamy aham.* I thought that Krsna could not possibly carry what His devotees lacks. I could not understand why Krsna would carry anything for someone like me; I am unworthy of His attention. Now I can understand that when I struck the word *vahami* from the book He took it as a personal offense, just as if I had struck His body. Therefore, He came to uphold His statement in *Bhagavad-gita. ”*

We can thus understand that the great power of devotion attracts the Lord, and He will personally carry whatever His devotees lack. If we simply depend on Him fully, He assures us that He will carry whatever we lack in our attempt to return to Him at the end of this material life.

## Passing Krsna’s Test

There is a pastime in the Eighth Canto describing Bali Maharaja’s arrest by the Lord. This whole pastime is a perfect illustration of the devotee’s surrender to the Lord’s will. As Srila Prabhupada explains in the Introduction to the chapter, the Lord sometimes uses His devotees in order to advertise their glories to benefit others. The Lord arrested Bali Maharaja—in fact, He cheated him—in order to glorify His devotee.

Srila Prabhupada often said, “No one can cheat better than Krsna. ” But Prabhupada also explains that whether Krsna is cheating or bestowing benedictions, because He is all-good, both His cheating and benedictions are always for everyone’s ultimate benefit. When Krsna cheats His devotees, it simultaneously establishes their true glory. And the devotees’ response to Krsna’s cheating captures the Lord’s heart. Devotees are those who are prepared to accept whatever circumstances into which the Lord puts them. To test their faith, the Lord sometimes gives the devotees trouble. If our faith is weak, it will be difficult for us to make spiritual progress.

Devotees may find it difficult to pass the Lord’s test, but the difficulties they encounter are not the Lord’s fault. The Lord can never be blamed for what He does because He has no purpose in cheating or testing other than to benefit the devotees and set an example for others to see. Krsna tested the *gopis* when He called them to the forest in the middle of the night. He also tested them when He left the *rasa* dance. Their surrender to His will was good for them and has also taught all devotees since. Because of their surrender, the Lord said, “I cannot possibly repay the debt for your surrender to Me. You have defied my statement in *Bhagavad-gita* where I say, ‘As one surrenders unto Me, I will reward him accordingly. ’ I *cannot* reward you accordingly. I have nothing to offer you in exchange for Your great love. Therefore be satisfied with the glory of your own activities. ”

But the Lord still felt that He had a debt to pay. He repaid His debt to the *gopis* by appearing as Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. He said, “I wanted to show everyone My devotees’ surrender. To benefit the entire creation, I appeared to be distant from the *gopis.* I tested you, allowing you to think Me absent when I left the *rasa* dance. Now, to repay My debt for your wonderful surrender, I will appear as Caitanya Mahaprabhu and freely distribute the same love you feel to others. ” Caitanya Mahaprabhu therefore fulfilled Krsna’s debt by crying tears of separation and giving pure love of Godhead to everyone He met, teaching all how to love Krsna in the mood of the Vraja *gopis.*

How does testing His devotees benefit others? By seeing how merciful Krsna is toward His pure devotees, when we are tested we will remember that mercy and the wonderfully surrendered response of the devotees. The Lord wants everyone to surrender to His will; no one is exempt. Therefore every living being must be tested. And when our faith is tested, by Krsna’s grace we have examples to look to.

Once, a feather-weight boxer was about to have his championship fight. Realizing it was the most important fight of his career, he decided to prepare for the fight more than he had ever prepared for any other fight. He increased his exercise and altered his diet so that he was eating more calories and protein. He was sure he would be successful in the bout. But when the day of the fight arrived, he stepped into the ring and found that his opponent was a middle-weight. Because of his increased exercise and protein intake, he had moved into the middle-weight class.

This is similar to how *maya* tricks us. We prepare ourselves to overcome small difficulties in the feather-weight class and imagine we have defeated our opponent. Then suddenly we find another opponent in a higher class waiting to defeat us.

This means that *maya* tests us in proportion to our spiritual advancement. In the beginning, the tests are small because our faith is small. When our faith grows, we will have a test in proportion to our growth. How else will we progress toward full surrender? Ultimately, we want to surrender our entire selves to Krsna. We will have to build ourselves up gradually.

Srila Prabhupada explained this point on a morning walk (Teheran, August 11, 1976):

**Atreya Rsi:** Is it true, Srila Prabhupada, that Krsna is testing us every day? We’re having tests, opportunities \[... \]

**Prabhupada:** No, just like if you want responsible post, then there is question of test. If you want to become a vagabond, remain vagabond. Where is question of test?

**Atreya Rsi:** No, for devotees.

**Prabhupada:** Ah, for devotees, there is test. For devotees there is test. We see from the devotee’s life, Prahlada Maharaja, how much severe test he had to pass through. Bali Maharaja, Narada Muni.

**Atreya Rsi:** And the most advanced the devotee is, the more severe the test is, the more chance.

**Prabhupada:** No, after you pass the examination, there is no more test. But before coming to the post of recognized devotee, Krsna tests very severely. That one has to pass.

**Hari-sauri: I** read once in the *Bhagavatam* in one of your purports, you said that *maya* is there to test the sincerity of the candidate.

**Prabhupada:** Yes, we see from the life of Bali Maharaja, how he was put into difficulty. Even his spiritual master cursed him. (break) \[... \] put into test and still he remains in his determination, then he’s passed. That is very natural. And there is a word, “acid test”?

**Hari-sauri:** Yes.

**Prabhupada:** For ascertaining real gold, the acid test one has to pass to become real gold.

**Nava-yauvana:** (break)... this test depends on one’s faith in Krsna?

**Prabhupada:** Faith in Krsna? What do you mean by faith?

**Hari-sauri: If** you want to pass the test, he says, does that depend on one’s faith?

**Prabhupada:** What does that faith mean?

**Atreya Rsi:** Conviction.

**Prabhupada:** What is that conviction? Describe it.

**Atreya Rsi:** That Krsna is the controller.

**Prabhupada:** Krsna is controller. You may have faith or no faith, that doesn’t matter.

**Hari-sauri: If** one is convinced that Krsna will always protect him.

**Nava-yauvana:** When he’s convinced to abide by the instructions of \[... \]

**Prabhupada:** Faith means that you are meant for giving some service to Krsna. You should stick to that service, that path, in spite of all impediments. That is the passing of test. Generally, just like we are meant for preaching Krsna consciousness. So there may be severe test, but still we shall remain determined. That is wanted. There may be so many impediments, punishment, still you should do that. That is wanted. That is test. Not that as soon as there is some difficulty I give it up. There may be severe test, but still we shall not give it up. We must go on. That is determination.

**Nava-yauvana:** Passing the test means executing the order of the spiritual master.

**Prabhupada:** Yes, that is spiritual life. One has to take order from the spiritual master and execute it, despite all impediments. That is determination.

There will always be tests for devotees, and if we want to become recognized as great devotees, we will have to pass more severe tests. If we still feel ourselves tested, we should know that the more severe tests are still ahead.

But all tests have the same solution: take shelter of the Lord and find your attachment to Him strengthened. Lord Caitanya calls the *gopis* the topmost devotees and their example the highest because their attachment to Krsna is the deepest of any devotees. So much “acid” was poured on them and they still passed the test.

Bali Maharaja lost all his possessions, fell from his position, was defeated and arrested by enemies, rebuked and deserted by relatives and friends, and cursed by his spiritual master, yet being fixed in his vow, he did not give up his truthfulness.

Bali Maharaja was even cheated when the Lord, who asked for three steps, took everything in two. Bali’s spiritual master tried to convince him to go back on his word, saying, “Don’t you see that this is Lord Visnu? He is here to favor the demigods and is going to take everything from you! It is not a religious principle to lose everything. According to religious principles the householder has a right to take what he needs to fulfill his obligations. You have obligations to your family, society, and spiritual master. Even though promised, you did not preface your vow with om, so you are not actually bound. ”

But Bali said, “No. I must be truthful. ” He then quoted a verse that states that Mother Earth can tolerate anything but a liar. “Even though He may be the Supreme Lord Visnu, I must be truthful to my word. ”

Sukracarya then cursed him: “Become bereft of all your opulence. ”

Vamanadeva then took possession of the universe, and Bali Maharaja was arrested in front of his wives, grandfather, Prahlada Maharaja, and other family members. He felt ashamed before Prahlada Maharaja, and hung his head. This was Bali’s test.

The Lord had come with the intention to cheat Bali Maharaja. Nevertheless, Bali Maharaja, being a perfect devotee, realized that he should request the Lord’s third step on his head; he realized that if one has great wealth and possessions, then the possessor must be greater than what he possesses. Bali had owned the universe before Vamana had taken it in his first two steps; he himself must be greater than what he owned. Krsna was pleased with Bali’s surrender.

Krsna says, “If you surrender to My test, I will award you with the intelligence to overcome it. ” Everything is gained by surrender to the Lord. The Lord will always protect the devotee who is surrendered to Him, and Lord Vamanadeva protected Bali Maharaja’s promise by giving him the intelligence how to fulfill the condition of the promised third step.

The Lord became so indebted to Bali Maharaja that He rewarded him with the planet Sutala. He said, “In this place there will be no suffering like on the planets in the material world. So that you can always see me, I will be your doorkeeper. ” He even told Prahlada Maharaja, “You should go with your grandson to Sutala, where you will always see Me. ”

Bali Maharaja responded by completely accepting the Lord’s will. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (1. 18. 4) states:

*tiraskrta vipralabdhah*  
*saptah ksipta hata api*  
*nasya tat pratikurvanti*
