*tad-bhaktah prabhavo ’pi hi*

“The devotees of the Lord are so forbearing that even though they are defamed, cheated, cursed, disturbed, neglected or even killed, they are never inclined to avenge themselves. ”

Another devotee who was defamed and cursed was Maharaja Pariksit. When Samika Rsi realized that his son, Srngi, had cursed this great devotee-king, he felt terrible. He thought the only thing that would counteract his son’s karmic reactions for such an act would be if Pariksit Maharaja would countercurse him. But he knew Pariksit Maharaja would never do such a thing. He therefore prayed to the Lord, “Please punish my son in whatever way necessary to absolve him from the sinful act of cursing such a great devotee. ” These examples of devotees passing tests are for our benefit. When we see these examples in *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* we should take shelter of them and understand that they have been told so we can follow. *Mahajano yena gatah sa panthah*—one should follow the path of *mahajanas* such as Bali Maharaja, Prahlada Maharaja, and Narada Muni. Anyone who follows in their footsteps can achieve the results they achieved. Queen Kunti says *(Bhag.* 1. 8. 25):

*vipadah santu tah sasvat*  
*tatra tatra jagad-guro*  
*bhavato darsanam yat syad*  
*apunar bhava-darsanam*

“I wish that all those calamities would happen again and again so that we could see You again and again, for seeing You means that we will no longer see repeated births and deaths. ”

Srila Prabhupada writes:

Generally the distressed, the needy, the intelligent and the inquisitive who have performed some pious activities worship or begin to worship the Lord. Others who are thriving on misdeeds only, regardless of status, cannot approach the Supreme due to being misled by the illusory energy. Therefore for a pious person if there is some calamity, there is no other alternative than to take shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord. Constantly remembering the lotus feet of the Lord means preparing for liberation from birth and death. Therefore, even knowing there are so called calamities, they are welcome because they give us an opportunity to remember the Lord, which means liberation. *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 43)

**Question: How can we prepare ourselves for Krsna’s tests?**

Prabhupada said (Teheran, August 11, 1976), “Faith means that you are meant for giving some service to Krsna. You should stick to that service, that path, in spite of all impediments. That is the passing of test. ” In other words, devotees must be determined and know that the perfection of all their activities is to satisfy the Lord.

Just as Queen Kunti was fully dependent on the Lord, we see that Srila Prabhupada was also fully dependent. He described himself as a useless soul, and questioned why he had been brought to America. He knew he wanted to preach in the West—to attract the Westerners to the mellows of devotional service—and thus said, “I am simply praying for mercy so that I will be able to convince them about Your message. ”

Srila Prabhupada embarked on a completely unknown journey. Yet the faith in and conviction about the purpose of his journey was strong. He said (Ma*rkine Bhagavata-dharma,* Verse 15), “I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Krsna. I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now, if You like, You can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta. ” The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states that the simplest thing becomes difficult if one forgets Lord Caitanya, and the most difficult task becomes easy if one remembers Him.

## Association

## Jana-sanga—Association with Worldly-minded Persons

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that as we are progressing along the path of *bhakti,* we have to carefully and attentively protect our *bhakti-lata-bija.* He explains that until we come to the stage of *uttama-bhakti,* the topmost stage of devotional service, we can still be influenced by bad association—and not only influenced, but so influenced that the root of our *Sraddha* (faith) will wither. Association with gross sense gratifiers and Mayavadis, attachment to the opposite sex, and offenses committed toward Vaisnavas can all contribute to the withering of our faith in the *bhakti* process.

Even a *madhyama-adhikari* is at risk. A *madhyama-adhikari* is someone with firm faith in the objective, but who is not so fixed on the path that he or she cannot fall down. Even at the *madhyama* level one can lose faith in the devotional process if he or she associates with worldly-minded people.

The *Bhagavatam* (3. 25. 20) defines good association:

*prasangam ajaram pasam*  
*atmanah kavayo viduh*  
*sa eva sadhusu krto*  
*moksa-dvaram apavrtam*

“Every learned man knows very well that attachment for the material is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But that same attachment, when applied to the self-realized devotees, opens the door of liberation. ”

Associating with Krsna’s devotees will inspire our *bhakti.* Because Krsna’s devotees are attached to Krsna, if we become attached to them, we will naturally also become attached to Krsna. A *sadhaka* or aspiring devotee always wants such strong Vaisnava association.

Devotees who have qualified themselves to perform pure devotional service still risk danger if they remain in the company of unwanted association or if they commit offenses. Such devotees can degrade their *bhakti to* the platform of *chaya bhaktyabhasa* (shadow devotion) or even *pratibimba bhaktyabhasa* (reflected devotion). If we become entangled in *bhaktyabhasa,* we will not find our way out. Such is the danger of *jana-sanga.*

There are two types of *sanga,* association and attachment. Regarding attachment, the *Bhagavad-gita* (2. 62) states:

*dhyayato visayan pumsah*  
*sangas tesupajayate*  
*sangat sanjayate kamah*  
*kamat krodho ’bhijayate*

“While contemplating the objects of the senses, a person develops attachment for them, and from such attachment lust develops, and from lust anger arises. ”

If we associate with our attachments, we are engaged in a dangerous form of *sanga.* By contemplating sense gratification, the determination to perform devotional service can dry up. Sense desires are like reeds along a lakeshore; they gradually suck the water out of the lake, leaving only a dry hole. Again the *Bhagavad-gita* (2. 44) speaks:

*bhogaisvarya-prasaktanam*  
*tayapahrta-cetasam*  
*vyavasayatmika buddhih*  
*samadhau na vidhiyate*

“In the minds of those who are too attached to sense enjoyment and material opulence, and who are bewildered by such things, the resolute determination for devotional service to the Supreme Lord does not take place. ”

Lord Caitanya describes (Cc. *Madhya* 22. 87):

*asat-sanga-tyaga, —ei vaisnava-acara*  
*‘stri-sangi—eka asadhu, ‘krsnabhakta’ ara*

“A Vaisnava should always avoid the association of ordinary people. Common people are very much materially attached, especially to women. Vaisnavas should also avoid the company of those who are not devotees of Lord Krsna. ”

We must renounce *asat-sanga,* bad association, including all our material attachments. Of course, it is not easy to completely renounce both bad association and attachments, so we must take shelter of the Vaisnavas. The Vaisnavas are called *sat,* eternal and good. If we associate with the *sat,* then gradually the *asat* will be eradicated or transformed.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains in further detail how material attachment increases by prohibited association. He refers to *sanga-tyaga,* the renunciation of bad association, and includes the dangers of association with the opposite sex. We have to be strict and prohibit ourselves from any association that will not help our Krsna consciousness, which includes improper association with the opposite sex. Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes:

Every practicing devotee should know the various kinds of worldly people. Therefore we are hereby most unwillingly enumerating these people. These worldly people are of seven kinds:

(1) Mayavadis and atheists, (2) sense enjoyers, (3) those who are fond of the sense enjoyers, (4) women, \[this actually means excessive attachment to the opposite sex\] (5) womanizers, (6) the hypocritically devout, and (7) the ill-behaved foolish outcastes. ”

*(Sri Bhaktyaloka,* p. 32-33)

Bhaktivinoda Thakura speaks first about Mayavadis.

The Mayavadis do not accept the eternal name, form, qualities, and pastimes of the Supreme Lord. They consider the living entity as a product of *maya*; therefore in their opinion the living entity has no eternal existence. (Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* p. 33)

The Mayavadis think *bhakti* is something one adopts temporarily in order to achieve the higher goals of knowledge and ultimately, liberation. They think liberation is perfection and that *bhakti* is not practiced in perfection since the living entity as an individual no longer exists. They believe that the body, mind, and false ego are destroyed at liberation. Furthermore, they think that variety, including individual identity, is a material phenomenon; there is no variety (or individual identity) on the spiritual plane.

This means that the Mayavadis practice *bhakti,* ultimately to extinguish Krsna’s existence, their own existence, and the relationship between themselves and the Supreme.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura compares this to Vrkasura’s mentality. He thought, “Let me please Lord Siva so that I can get a benediction to touch people’s heads and make them explode. ” When he received his benediction he thought, “Let me touch Lord Siva’s head and kill him! ” This is not *bhakti.* One cannot think of pleasing the object of worship if ultimately one wants to destroy him. Mayavadis have this same mentality.

Sometimes we may see Mayavadis chant Hare Krsna with apparent devotion, tears streaming from their eyes. Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes their emotion as *pratibimba bhaktyabhasa.* He writes that while they may feel some temporary ecstasy, it is only a reflection of real *bhakti* induced by their association with real Vaisnavas. It is not real *bhakti* itself.

Similarly, Krsna says in the *Bhagavad-gita (Bg.* 7. 24):

*avyaktam vyaktim apannam*  
*manyante mam abuddhayah*  
*param bhavam ajananto*  
*mamavyayam anuttamam*

“Unintelligent men, who do not know Me perfectly, think that I, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Krsna, was impersonal before and have now assumed this personality. Due to their small knowledge, they do not know My higher nature, which is imperishable and supreme. ”

They think the Supreme is impersonal, void, and undifferentiated, and that when He appears in this world, He accepts a material body. That is why they are called Mayavadis—they think that Krsna is controlled by maya.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains why it is dangerous to share loving exchanges with Mayavadis *(Sri Bhaktyaloka,* p. 33):

They do not consider devotional service eternal; rather, they consider it a temporary method to cultivate knowledge. All the Mayavadi conclusions are opposed to the principles of devotional service. Therefore by associating with Mayavadis, one’s devotion gradually disappears. Sri Svarupa Damodara gave the following instruction in *Caitanya-caritamrta* *(Antya* 2. 94-95):

*buddhi bhrasta haila tomara gopalera sange*  
*mayavada sunibare upajila range*  
*vaisnava hana yeba sariraka-bhasya sune*  
*sevya-sevaka-bhava chadi’ apanare ‘isvara’ mane*

You have lost your intelligence in the association of Gopala, and therefore you are eager to hear the Mayavada philosophy. When a Vaisnava listens to the *Sariraka-bhasya,* the Mayavada commentary upon the *Vedanta-sutras,* he gives up the Krsna conscious attitude that the Lord is the master and the living entity is His servant. Instead, he considers himself the Supreme Lord.

Such is the Mayavadi conception. However, Srila Prabhupada gives different arguments for us to use in our dealings with Mayavadis. He says, “If you think you are supreme, then why have you come to this material world to suffer? ”

The Mayavadis respond, “This is my pastime. I am simply enjoying my *lila. ”*

Prabhupada says, “If you meet such a Mayavadi who is enjoying *lila* in this way, then tell him, ‘Enjoy my boot in your face! Why are you trying to stop me? This is also *lila,* isn’t it? *Lila* means it is just simply enjoyment. You say you have come to this world simply to enjoy. Then enjoy my boot in your face! ”’

When Mayavadis say, “I can become God. You are God. We can all become God and that is perfection, ” the devotees are pained to hear it. The Mayavadis are so envious of Krsna that they want to destroy Him. How can we have loving exchanges with such persons?

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* *(Madhya* 19. 149) states:

*krsna-bhakta—niskama, ataeva ‘santa’*  
*bhukti-mukti-siddhi-kami—sakali ‘asanta’*

*“Bhuktis,* those who desire material opulence, and *muktis,* those who desire liberation, are *kami*—still filled with lusty desires. Therefore they can never be peaceful. The *krsna-bhaktas,* however, are *niskama;* they have no desires separate from their desire to please Krsna. Therefore they can be peaceful. ”

In *Caitanya-caritamrta,* Svarupa Damodara speaks to Bhagavan Acarya, an elevated devotee. Bhagavan Acarya expresses his affection toward the Lord in the mood of friendship because in *krsna-lila* he was a cowherd boy. As Bhagavan Acarya he often invited Lord Caitanya to his home for *prasadam.* Bhagavan Acarya was practically in the renounced order of life and had no inclination toward worldly affairs. But he had a brother, Gopala Bhattacarya (not Gopala Bhatta Gosvami). Gopala Bhattacarya had gone to Varanasi to study Vedanta and had learned Sahkaracarya’s commentary on *Vedanta-sutra,* the *Sariraka-bhasya.* This is, of course, a Mayavada commentary and it therefore describes how the Lord comes under *maya’s* influence, how the living entity is imaginary and does not actually exist, and that when the living entity becomes liberated he completely loses his individuality. Gopala

Bhattacarya stayed in Varanasi for quite some time and then returned home.

When Bhagavan Acarya received his brother at home he thought, “Let me introduce him to Lord Caitanya. ” He therefore brought Gopala Bhattacarya to see Lord Caitanya. But Lord Caitanya is never happy to meet anyone who is not a pure Vaisnava, what to speak of a Mayavadi filled with so many philosophical and spiritual misconceptions. Thus He was not happy to meet Gopala Bhattacarya and did not want to share loving exchanges with him. But because Bhagavan Acarya was His devotee, Lord Caitanya feigned pleasure to please His devotee.

Later, Bhagavan Acarya was speaking to Svarupa Damodara, and said, “My brother has been studying in Varanasi. Will you please hear the *Vedanta-sutra* from him? ”

Svarupa Damodara replied, “What are you saying? You have lost your intelligence in your brother’s association! Now you seem to be eager to hear Mayavada philosophy! Do you not know that when a Vaisnava listens to the *Sariraka-bhasya* he gives up the Krsna conscious attitude that the Lord is the master and the living entity His servant? He then begins to consider himself the Supreme Lord. ” Bhagavan Acarya replied, “Our devotional service is fixed at the lotus feet of Krsna. We will never give up Krsna’s lotus feet by hearing such commentaries! ”

But Svarupa Damodara explained, “Still when one hears statements like that the Supreme Personality of Godhead comes under *maya’s* influence and that the living entity’s existence is illusory, a Vaisnavas feels pain. A devotee never thinks *bhakti* is a temporary practice! *Bhakti* is eternal, the living entity is eternal, and Krsna is eternal. The perfection of life is to love Krsna eternally. How can you listen to someone who wants Krsna destroyed? ”

When Bhagavan Acarya heard Svarupa Damodara’s words, he went home and told his brother, “Go someplace else! ” He no longer wanted anything to do with him.

A similar exchange happened when Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya spoke *Vedanta for* seven days continuously. As Lord Caitanya did not want to speak to Gopala Bhattacarya, so He simply sat without listening to Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya. Finally, Sarvabhauma asked, “I have been speaking for seven days. Certainly You must have something to say about everything I have told You. ”

Lord Caitanya said, “I have been trying to listen to the essence of your words, extracting it from your interpretations, which cloud the real meaning of Vedanta. I cannot listen to what you are saying. ” Lord Caitanya listened to Vedanta, but not to Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya.

When Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya finally asked the Lord for His association, he was defeated and gave up his Mayavada conception. He then became a confidential servant of the Lord and composed a hundred prayers (*Sarvabhauma-sataka*) glorifying Lord Caitanya. He became so overwhelmed by the ecstasy of devotional service that when he heard the following verse, he wanted to change *mukti-pade to bhakti-pade:*

*tat te ’nukampam susamiksamano*  
*bhunjana evatma-krtam vipakam*  
*hrd-vag-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te*  
*jiveta yo mukti-pade sa daya-bhak*

“My dear Lord, one who earnestly waits for You to bestow Your causeless mercy upon him, all the while patiently suffering the reactions of his past misdeeds and offering You respectful obeisances with his heart, words and body, is surely eligible for liberation, for it has become his rightful claim. ” *(Bhag.* 10. 14. 8)

When he quoted this verse to Lord Caitanya using the word *bhakti* instead of *mukti,* he explained, “To hear the word *mukti* now breaks my heart. ”

Lord Caitanya told Sarvabhauma that he could not change the *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* but he convinced the Bhattacarya that

Krsna is also known as *muktipada,* one whose lotus feet bestow liberation.

Lord Caitanya was pleased with Sarvabhauma’s devotion and said, “Sarvabhauma, previously you were so interested in hearing the word *mukti* that it became your constant meditation. I am happy to see that you no longer have any attraction whatsoever for liberation. ”

While we may love to hear such pastimes as Lord Caitanya converting the Mayavadis, we should ourselves be careful about associating with Mayavadis. Even an elevated devotee can fall down by hearing Mayavada philosophy. We should not think that we have the power to convert them by showing them affection. Bhaktivinoda Thakura warns, “Don’t try to satisfy a materialistic person. ” If we try to satisfy all their desires, we might find ourselves being drawn into those desires. The only way a person can become fully satisfied is to place Krsna in the center of their lives.

About Mayavadis Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes:

Those who do not accept the Supreme Lord, who is glorified by the *Vedas,* are called *nastikas,* or atheists. Their minds have been polluted by false arguments; therefore one’s devotion is lost in their association. (Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* p. 33)

The atheists’ minds are polluted by false arguments. *Krsna-bahirmukha hand bhoga-vancha kare* (*Prema-vivarta, Jiva gati):* As soon as the living entity becomes inimical to Krsna and desires sense gratification, the illusory energy throws him down. The root cause of his falldown is his enmity to the Lord caused by his desire to satisfy his senses. Krsna, the *purusa*, is the supreme enjoyer, and everything is meant to give pleasure to *His* senses; Mayavadis and atheists want things for their own enjoyment.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura also speaks about the *dharmadvaji,* the hypocrites, as the most dangerous type of association. Hypocrites speak about devotion but are materially motivated. Sometimes they present themselves as spiritual teachers or masters to fulfill their nondevotional objectives. Association with the *dharmadvaji* must be avoided.

And the last category of association we should avoid is the atheists—those who are completely against worship of the Supreme Lord.

Atheists think they are independent and do not have to answer to anyone. However, Krsna always shows them that their so-called independence cannot save them from death. Srila Prabhupada says that if you do not want to offer your obeisances *(dandavats) to* God during your life, then you will have to do it at the time of death.

**Question: What is the difference between the Mayavadis and the Brahmavadis?**

Mayavadis think the Lord’s form is *maya,* illusory, whereas Brahmavadis accept the Brahman as Absolute. Mayavadis emphasize that Krsna’s body is a temporary manifestation made of the material energy. They also say that devotion is *maya* since Krsna, the person, does not really exist. The Brahmavadis, however, simply speak of Brahman as supreme. The Mayavadis want to become one with Krsna, and the Brahmavadis want to enter the impersonal Brahman. They are similar in that respect.

**Question: Those who come to the material world already possess the seeds of** *jnana* **and karma and are therefore easily contaminated by their influence. How can we avoid this contamination in our devotional service and in our life?**

We have to understand the characteristics of karma and *jnana,* and we have to learn to distinguish between *karma-misra-bhakti, jnana-misra-bhakti,* and *suddha-bhakti.* We should also recognize the desires in the heart for *bhukti* and *mukti.* The only pure desire the living entity has is to please Krsna. Almost all other desires come under the categories of *bhukti* and *mukti,* the desire to enjoy sense objects or to become free from suffering.

Prahlada Maharaja states in the Seventh Canto that in the material world every materialist is endeavoring for happiness and he is trying to mitigate his distress. And therefore he is acting accordingly. Actually we do not become happy by endeavoring for happiness; as soon as we try for happiness, our distress begins.

Srila Rupa Gosvami describes three sources of happiness: sense enjoyment, liberation, and devotional service. There are no other sources. Those who want *bhukti* follow the *karma-kanda,* while those who want *mukti* engage in *jnana.* But *bhukti* and *mukti* are two pots of poison because they separate us from Krsna.

We should examine our hearts to see our own tendencies for these two poisons, and then we should avoid anything that leads us to want to taste them. As long as we are still on the *vaidhi-bhakti* platform, we must discriminate between favorable and unfavorable activities.

Mayavadis, *muktis,* are in the first category of persons whose association we should avoid. The next category contains the gross sense enjoyers, the *bhuktis.*

Association with sense enjoyers is harmful. Those who are always busy associating with sense enjoyment are filled with blasphemy, envy, and violence. Quarrel, argument, and hankering for sense enjoyment is their life. The more they enjoy, the more their thirst for sense enjoyment increases. The sense enjoyers do not get time to speak or hear topics of Krsna. Whether they engage in pious activities or sinful activities, the sense enjoyers always remain far from knowledge of the self. (Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* p. 33)

Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes some of the desires that fall under the category of *bhukti:* the desire for fame, victory, followers, to enjoy wealth, and to eat only palatable food. Even those who want elevation to the heavenly planets or to take a high birth are described as *bhuktis.* Such persons may perform sacrifices, but their sole objective is sense gratification, whereas devotees think only of Krsna’s enjoyment.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura then explains that greed for material enjoyment causes the living entity to become subordinate to six enemies, headed by lust and anger. He adds that envy takes over and rules the heart. The six enemies are *kama, krodha, moha, matsarya, mada,* and *lobha* (lust, anger, greed, bewilderment, madness, and envy). Usually an envious person is maliciously inclined.

Krsna describes that demoniac persons define their enemies as anyone who tries to obstruct them from their enjoyment. However, one who engages in regulated sense gratification according to the concession given in the scriptures is not counted amongst the gross sense enjoyers, and one need not give up their association.

Lord Rsabhadeva says:

*nunam pramattah kurute vikarma*  
*yad indriya-pritaya aprnoti*  
*na sadhu manye yata atmano ’yam*  
*asann api klesada asa dehah*

“When a person considers sense gratification the aim of life, he certainly becomes mad after materialistic living and engages in all kinds of sinful activity. He does not know that due to his past misdeeds he has already received a body which, although temporary, is the cause of his misery. Actually the living entity should not have taken on a material body, but he has been awarded the material body for sense gratification. Therefore I think it not befitting an intelligent man to involve himself again in the activities of sense gratification, by which he perpetually gets material bodies one after another. ” *(Bhag.* 5. 5. 4)

The materialist thinks, “I am suffering now, so I will relieve my suffering with sense enjoyment. ” But such persons are blind. They cannot see that their suffering is caused from this very mentality and the efforts it drove them to in a previous life. Lord Rsabhadeva calls such persons *pramattah,* mad.

They are like asses. When you want an ass to carry a heavy burden, you put blinders on it so that it cannot see the grass to either side. The ass walks a long distance, seeing only what is directly in front of it, and does not realize that it could have satisfied its hunger anywhere along the road.

An asslike person is called a *mudha.* The *Bhagavad-gita* compares fruitive workers to the ass:

*na mam duskrtino mudhah*  
*prapadyante naradhamah*  
*mayayapahrta-jnana*  
*asuram bhavam asritah*

“Those miscreants who are grossly foolish, who are lowest among mankind, whose knowledge is stolen by illusion, and who partake of the atheistic nature of demons do not surrender unto Me. ” *(Bg.* 7. 15)

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* tells the story of an astrologer, a *Sarva-jna.* The astrologer asked a poor man why he was lamenting. “Don’t you know you have a great fortune? Your father has departed and left behind a fortune for you. ”

The Sarva-jna told him how to find this fortune: “If you dig on the southern side, the bees and drones will arise and you will not get your treasure. If you dig on the northern side, you will be swallowed by a serpent. If you dig on the western side, you will be haunted by ghosts. But here on the eastern side, if you dig just a little bit, you will find great wealth. ”

The side where the bees and drones are waiting is compared to fruitive activities, because persons perform sacrifices to attain pleasure in a future life, but when their desires are fulfilled, they are stung by more desires. Thus they are never satisfied.

Those who are swallowed by the black serpent are like Mayavadis who want *mukti.* They may achieve liberation and merge into the impersonal Brahman, but then they will gradually fall down from their position of imagined superiority because they failed to awaken their attraction to Krsna’s lotus feet.

Persons haunted by ghosts are like those wanting mystic yogic perfections. They may have some power but still not have achieved full shelter at Krsna’s lotus feet. The astrologer thus tells the impoverished man, “You have great wealth. You are so fortunate! You dig on the eastern side and you will find your treasure. ” The wealth he is referring to is the wealth of *bhakti.*

The Vaisnava *acaryas* explain that the astrologer was giving the poor man *sambandha-jnana,* knowledge of his relationship with his own buried wealth. We too have to first understand *sambandha-jnana,* knowledge of our relationship with Krsna, before we can pursue the goal. *Sambandha-jnana* includes knowing who Krsna is, understanding that He is nondifferent from His holy name, to understand the nature of the the living entity, the material nature, and how they interact, and to understand our relationship with the Supreme Lord. Why is *sambandha-jnana* important? Because just like the impoverished person, if we know what and where our wealth is, then we will have the drive to dig for it.

Now that we have come into contact with the Krsna consciousness movement, the greatest wealth, we should learn not to dig on the northern, southern, or western sides and thus avoid karma, *jnana,* and the desire for mystic perfection. Rather, we should learn to dig only on the eastern side and obtain the wealth of *prema.* If we maintain this proper understanding, we will be able to avoid becoming a gross sense enjoyer.

In the *Caitanya-caritamrta (Antya* 6. 278) Srila Raghunatha dasa Gosvami describes one way one becomes a gross sense enjoyer:

*visayira anna khaile malina haya mana*  
*malina mana haile nahe krsnera smarana*

When one eats food offered by a materialistic man, one’s mind becomes contaminated, and when the mind is contaminated, one is unable to think of Krsna properly.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains how even devotees can be sense enjoyers:

Those persons who externally engage in sensual activities and accept sense objects just to maintain body and soul together but internally engage in their constitutional position as servants of Krsna are not counted among the sense enjoyers, who are attached to the fruits of their activities.

The sense enjoyers and persons fond of sense enjoyers are both worldly-minded. Persons who are fond of sense enjoyers are themselves also sense enjoyers, because they always think of sense enjoyment within their hearts. There are some people who are not themselves sense enjoyers, but they take pleasure in associating with sense enjoyers. Their association should also be renounced, because they will soon become sense enjoyers and bad association themselves. There are two types of sense enjoyers—those who are extremely attached to sense enjoyment and devotees who are attached to sense enjoyment. Associating with those who are extremely attached should be totally rejected. The devotees who are attached to sense enjoyment are also of two types—those who have included the Lord as a part of their sense enjoyment and those who have kept the Lord in the center of their sense enjoyment. Association with the latter type is preferable to association with the former. Although those who engage in sense enjoyment according to religious codes are better than the sinful sense enjoyers, still the practicing devotee should not associate with them until they become devotees. Simply by wearing the dress of a renunciate one does not become a devotee free from sense gratification, because renunciates often collect and save objects of sense enjoyment. On the other hand, many persons resemble sense enjoyers but utilize everything in the worship of Lord Hari in the mood of detachment. Considering all this, the practicing devotee should give up the association of sense enjoyers and those fond of them and engage in devotional service in a secluded place or, if he is fortunate, in the association of genuine devotees. (Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* p. 34-35)

Gross sense enjoyers are not only those who have no conception of anything but sense gratification; there are other categories of such people: persons who are fond of associating with gross sense enjoyers, those who are extremely attached to sense enjoyment, and devotees attached to sense enjoyment.

**Question: How should we preach to and interact with others taking into consideration the instructions regarding** *jana-sanga?*

Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that that means the devotees should not engage in the activities mentioned in the fourth verse of the *Upadesamrta* with worldly people. The *Upadesamrta* (4) lists the six loving exchanges to be shared among devotees:

*dadati pratigrhnati*  
*guhyam akhyati prcchati*  
*bhunkte bhojayate caiva*  
*sad-vidham priti-laksanam*

“Offering gifts in charity, accepting charitable gifts, revealing one’s mind in confidence, inquiring confidentially, accepting *prasada* and offering *prasada* are the six symptoms of love shared by one devotee and another. ”

One should not share such affectionate exchanges with nondevotees even while preaching. Rather, we have to give up all material motivation in order to preach effectively. This means we should not want anything in return when we give Krsna to others except that the persons to whom we preach simply accept Krsna.

When preaching to nondevotees, we should also not reveal confidential topics such as Krsna’s intimate pastimes with the *gopis.* Prabhupada said that when we are preaching to nondevotees, there are five subject matters that should be discussed: the holy name,

Lord Caitanya’s *sankirtana* movement, that we are not the body, Sanatana *dharma,* and that Krsna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Preaching means awakening attraction to Krsna in others. However, a *madhyama-adhikari* preaches to the innocent but avoids the atheists.

Prabhupada also warns that we should not try to imitate *mahabhagavatas* or *uttama-adhikaris,* thinking that we are capable of loving everyone. An *uttama-adhikari* can love everyone, but until we come to that platform, we have to protect our *Sraddha* by avoiding bad association.

In addition, Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura specifically explains in Sri *Bhaktyaloka* how we can differentiate between favorable association and association that should be avoided:

What is association and giving up association? Many people have doubts about this. Doubts may be there, because if just by coming close to a materialist or material object is considered associating with them, then there is no way to give up this association. As long as the material body is there, how can one give up the proximity of these things? How can a *grhastha* Vaisnava give up his family members? One cannot give up the association of deceitful persons, even if they are renunciates. One will have to come across materialists in one’s life, whether one is a *grhastha* or a renunciate. (Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* p. 33)

Although we have to be careful about associating with materialistic people, this does not mean we abandon them. If we look back at ourselves, we will probably see that we once shared their qualities. Where would we be today if the devotees avoided our association? We have all come to Krsna consciousness because of Vaisnava association received to one degree or another. Without that association, and without knowing anything about devotional service, how could we have lived anything *but* a materialistic life?

Thus when Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes giving up materialistic association, he does not mean that we should have nothing to do with them. Rather, he wants us to be compassionate toward them. He writes:

O *sadhakas!* One must accept proximity with both bad and good people as one passes his life. This equally applies to householders and renunciates. Proximity must be there, nevertheless one should not engage in bad association. Giving in charity, accepting charity, revealing one’s mind, hearing one’s mind, accepting foods, and giving foods—if done with love, these are called *sanga,* or association. Giving some foodstuffs to a hungry person and accepting some charity from a pious man is done out of duty, not out of love. Even if they are materialists, this type of engagement is not considered association. But if they are pure devotees, then such activities are performed out of love. When acts are performed out of love, then it is association. Therefore giving charity to pure Vaisnavas and accepting items or wealth from them becomes *sat-sanga.* Giving charity to a materialist or accepting charity from one, if done out of love, becomes *asat-sanga.* *(Sri Bhaktyaloka,* p. 112-113)

Here he defines association as something that increases our love. Love is not stagnant—experienced once and living on in memory. Love is dynamic. And love is the eternal nature of the soul. Therefore, love should be reserved for Krsna and the Vaisnavas.

## Sadhu-sanga—Association with Devotess

Lord Krsna appears in five forms: as the devotee, as the expansion of the devotee, as the incarnation of the devotee, as the devotional energy, and as the pure devotee.

*panca-tattva-eka-vastu, nahi kichu bheda*  
*rasa asvadite tabu vividha vibheda*

“Spiritually there are no differences between these five *tattvas, f*or on the transcendental platform everything is absolute. Yet there are also varieties in the spiritual world, and in order to taste these spiritual varieties one should distinguish between them. ” (Cc. *Adi* 7. 5)

Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami states that on the transcendental platform there is no difference between these five *tattvas* since everything is Absolute. We distinguish between them only to taste variety in the spiritual world. Lord Caitanya’s association with Lord Nityananda, Advaita Acarya, Srivasa Pandita, and Gadadhara Pandita created a variety of loving exchanges.

Lord Caitanya did not appear alone; He appeared with His associates (both the other members of the *Panca-tattva* and the many other associates who descended to Navadvipa). He went with these associates into the streets of Navadvipa and attracted the nondevotees to Krsna by revealing the devotees’ loving exchanges. When Krsna comes, He is accompanied by His eternal associates and similarly attracts people to Himself by showing His transcendental reciprocation with the residents of Vraja. When worldly persons come into contact with devotees and see their loving exchanges, their faith in devotional love is awakened and they too become devotees.

This is the great value of *sadhu-sanga.* The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (1. 18. 13) states:

*tulayama lavenapi*  
*na svargam napunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-sangi-sangasya*  
*martyanam kim utasisah*

“The value of a moment’s association with the devotee of the Lord cannot even be compared to the attainment of heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which are for those who are meant for death. ”

Lord Kapiladeva says:

*ta ete sadhavah sadhvi*  
*sarva-sanga-vivarjitah*  
*sangas tesv atha te prarthyah*  
*sanga-dosa-hara hi te*

“O My mother, O virtuous lady, these are the qualities of great devotees who are free from all attachment. You must seek attachment to such holy men, for this counteracts the pernicious effects of material attachment. ” *(Bhag.* 3. 25. 24)

If we do not fully understand the value of *sat-sanga* and what happens to the heart as a result of such association, we will not fully appreciate the dangers of *asat-sanga.*

Bhaktivinoda Thakura continues *(Sri Bhaktyaloka,* p. 32):

The scriptures often state that without the association of the Lord’s devotees, Bhaktidevi does not manifest in the heart. It is most necessary for *sadhakas* to associate with devotees. Therefore, in this context, the word *jana-sanga* is understood to mean persons who are devoid of devotion. That is why Srila Rupa Prabhu has included the instruction to give up worldly association as one of the limbs of devotional service. In the *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu* (1. 2. 43) it is said: *sanga tyago vidurena bhagavad vimukhair janaih*—“One who desires to attain devotion to Krsna should carefully give up the association of worldly people. ”

We should instead seek out the association of devotees who are more advanced than we are and hear from them. The *Bhagavatam* states that when we see superior Vaisnavas, we should receive them happily and then hear about Krsna from them; when we see Vaisnavas who are on the same level as ourselves, we should become their friends; and when we see Vaisnavas who are not as advanced as ourselves, we should be kind and show them mercy by encouraging them in devotional service. Srila Prabhupada explains this point in his purport to *Upadesamrta* (4): experienced devotees speak while inexperienced devotees hears from them.

By the nature of loving exchanges, when we offer something with love, the person to whom we are offering it will accept it. That acceptance is the person’s reciprocation with our offering. The best service we can render the senior Vaisnavas is to donate our ears— to give submissive, aural reception. By giving our ears and eagerly hearing their *katha,* our own desire to serve Krsna will awaken more and more.

Srila Prabhupada, of course, understood this principle. Therefore he presented his *Krsna* book in such a way that it would attract the readers. Sometimes people read Krsna book thinking they are reading only nice stories. Although they have no faith, they are building their *sukrti* because they are associating with a pure devotee.

*Sukrti* is piety, and *sukrti* for *bhakti* is gained through association with transcendental persons. Real *sukrti* gives rise to an intense desire to engage in devotional service. There are three types of *sukrti: bhogonmukhi, moksonmukhi,* and *bhakty-unmukhi.*

*Bhogonmukhi sukrti* means that by performing pious activities, one gets *bhoga,* a higher standard of material enjoyment—elevation to the heavenly planets. *Moksonmukhi sukrti* refers to pious activities that lead to liberation. *Bhakty-unmukhi sukrti* refers specifically to the good fortune of Vaisnava association, which leads one to practice *bhakti.*

We must have engaged in *ajnata-sukrti*, unknown *bhakty-unmukhi sukrti,* in order to have come to devotional service. Through the association of the Vaisnavas, and because we would have also had to have experienced an increase in the mode of goodness and a decrease in illusion in such association, gradually we were able to execute devotional service by our own will.

Visvanatha Chakravarti Thakura states that by engaging in pious activities, the mode of goodness gradually becomes prominent. When the mode of goodness is prominent, the influence of the other modes decreases. When delusion, which is a product particularly of the mode of ignorance, decreases, then we come into steadier Vaisnava association and learn about *bhakti.*

The *Bhagavad-gita* (7. 16) describes four kinds of persons who are eligible for *bhakti:*

*catur-vidha bhajante mam*  
*janah sukrtino ’rjuna*  
*arto jijnasur artharthi*  
*jnani ca bharatarsabha*

This verse lists the four types of persons eligible for *bhakti* as: the distressed, the inquisitive, those desiring economic gain, and those seeking knowledge. Krsna explains that it is their *sukrti*—association with transcendental personalities and their intense desire to engage in *bhakti*—that qualifies these four types of persons for *bhakti.* But Rupa Gosvami explains that when these four persons receive the mercy of the Lord or His devotees, they lose their material motivation and become eligible for pure devotional service.

Different examples are given for each category of persons eligible for *bhakti.* For those who are distressed, Gajendra is mentioned. Gajendra was an elephant who had been King Indradyumna in his previous life. This elephant entered a lake in the heavenly planets where he resided and was caught by a crocodile. The crocodile held his leg tightly, preventing him from getting free. The crocodile, being in its natural element, gradually began to defeat Gajendra, who became weaker. The two fought for a thousand celestial years. Gajendra became excessively distressed. He then remembered prayers from his previous life as Indradyumna and began to recite them. The Lord then personally appeared before him and severed the crocodile’s head, relieving Gajendra’s distress. At that time, Gajendra became eligible for pure devotional service, and his desire to become free from distress left his heart. Receiving a connection with a transcendental personality—in this case, the Lord Himself— awakened in him an intense desire to serve the Lord.

The second category mentioned is the *jijnasu,* the inquisitive, and the example given is of Saunaka Rsi. Saunaka Rsi gathered with the sages at Naimisaranya and heard from Suta Gosvami. All the sages were afraid of Kali-yuga’s influence, so they asked Suta Gosvami what was most beneficial for people to do in this age. In Suta Gosvami’s association, the sages then heard the Supreme Lord’s glories in the form of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* including the glories of pure devotional service. Thus because of their inquisitiveness, they received the association of Suta Gosvami, had their inquiries fully satisfied, and became eligible for pure devotional service.

Dhruva Maharaja is given as the example of the third category, the *artharthi.* Dhruva Maharaja wanted a kingdom greater than his great-grandfather’s, and came into contact with Narada Muni, who instructed him on how to gain the Lord’s *darsana.* That is, he achieved the association of a pure devotee, then the Lord’s *darsana,* and these caused him to lose his desire for kingdom and wealth. He too became eligible to perform pure devotional service.

The four Kumaras serve as examples of the fourth category, the *jnanis.* The four Kumaras were *nirvisesa jnanis, or* impersonalists, but by once smelling the fragrance of the *tulasi* leaves emanating from the Lord’s lotus feet, they became personalists and thus eligible for pure devotional service.

As long as material motivation remains, one cannot become eligible to practice *suddha-bhakti.* Nonetheless, the four examples described above illustrate the importance of association. The *Srimad-Bhagavatam (3. 25. 25)* states:

*satam prasangan mama virya-samvido*  
*bhavanti hrt-karna-rasayanah kathah*  
*taj-josanad asv apavarga-vartmani*  
*sraddha ratir bhaktir anukramisyati*

“In the association of pure devotees, discussion of the pastimes and activities of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is very pleasing and satisfying to the ear and the heart. By cultivating such knowledge one gradually becomes advanced on the path of liberation, and thereafter he is freed, and his attraction becomes fixed. Then real devotion and devotional service begin. ”

And *(Bhag.* 7. 5. 32):

*naisam matis tavad urukramanghrim*  
*sprsaty anarthapagamo yad-arthah*  
*mahiyasam pada-rajo-’bhisekam*  
*niskincananam na vrnita yava*

“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaisnava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Krsna conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination. ”

**Question: In this regard, Prahlada Maharaja told his father, “You are asking how is it that I can resist your torments. And how is it** **that I can always remember the lotus feet of the Lord. It is only because I have taken the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaisnava. ” If we have been polluted by the association of nondevotees, but our heart becomes cleaned by the association of devotees, does it mean that our faith has been restored?**

We may commit an offense that causes us to stop hearing and chanting. But after some suffering we realize, “I need nourishment. ” We therefore take our sick man’s food in the form of *sadhu-sanga* and again begin the process of *bhakti.* But we do not start from the beginning again. Rather, we begin with the same faith we had when we stopped practicing. If we have become polluted by bad association, we simply need good association—and to take advantage of it by lending our ears to hearing and taking nourishment through association and chanting so that we can become strong again and thus be reinstated.

**Question: Is there danger of becoming too familiar with advanced devotees?**

Srila Prabhupada spoke about this in relationship to one of his disciples. He said that if a disciple is not careful, then familiarity will begin to breed contempt. When we become familiar with an advanced devotee, we may begin to think he or she is an ordinary person. We may even look for faults in that Vaisnava. It is our tendency to look for faults or shortcomings in others. But if we look at the devotee’s qualities with the proper vision, we should be able to see many good qualities. If we cannot, then we need to *practice* seeing good qualities in devotees.

**Question: It is easy to see good qualities in advanced devotees, but what can we do if we are always associating with devotees who are not very advanced?**

According to scripture, even a moment’s association with an advanced devotee can change our heart and awaken our desire to develop love for Krsna. Therefore, if we have the great fortune to associate with an elevated Vaisnava, our consciousness is supposed to change. When the consciousness changes, it means our perspective changes. That changed perspective includes seeing everyone around us and everything that happens to us differently. We can test whether the quality of our consciousness is improving by seeing if we are able to discern the good qualities in others.

**Question: What can we do to serve elevated Vaisnavas?**

If we want to become effective servants of elevated Vaisnavas, assist them in the mission they have been given by Srila Prabhupada. To be an effective instrument in spreading Krsna consciousness, appreciate others’ good qualities. We should be able to learn to do that by our association with advanced Vaisnavas.

How do advanced devotees teach us? They identify the spark of spiritual desire in our heart and then fan it, awakening our desire to serve Krsna. If our desire has truly been fanned, we will want to reciprocate with them and share what they have given us with others. If you become inspired, spread the inspiration. Krsna consciousness should be infectious.

To inspire others to become Krsna conscious requires seeing their good qualities, because that means you can see their potential for performing devotional service. We must have that vision. Every living entity has a potential to perform devotional service, and if we cannot see that, it is a defect in our own vision.

Lord Kapiladeva says that we should seek out the association of devotees who inspire us in Krsna consciousness, but seeking out that association means we should actually be eager and greedy, to take the inspiration and then to share it with others. However, if we lose our appreciation for what they are giving and become overfamiliar, we will become withdrawn in their association and our consciousness will stay at a low level. At that point, we should examine ourselves rather than finding faults in others.

When associating with devotees—and for making advancement in spiritual life at all—we should always feel ourselves very low. If we do not build our Krsna consciousness on humility, it will be difficult to make progress. We will not be able to appreciate Srila Prabhupada’s great gift to us: the society of devotees.

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* tells the story of Kalidasa, who was always anxious to eat the remnants of the Vaisnavas’ food. He would not discriminate between advanced Vaisnavas or neophytes, Vaisnavas born in *brahmana* families or Vaisnavas born in *sudra* families. It made no difference to him, because he considered himself to be very low. As long as someone was a Vaisnava, he wanted their remnants. He defined a Vaisnava as anyone who chanted Krsna’s name.

Kalidasa used a variety of tricks to get those remnants. He would bring offerings, then wait outside behind a tree until the Vaisnava had honored what he had brought. He would always bring the type of food that would have something left over—mangos have pits and skins, for example. When the remnants were thrown out, he would come out of hiding and relish them.

It was said of Kalidasa that there was no Vaisnava anywhere in Bengal whose remnants he did not honor. Lord Caitanya, understanding the faith Kalidasa had in the Vaisnavas, gave him much mercy. At the Jagannatha temple, Lord Caitanya used to walk down the steps, and just before entering the temple, Govinda would wash His feet. Lord Caitanya had a strict rule: No one was allowed to take the water that had washed His feet. This was part of His *sannyasa* principles. Of course, the devotees would try to get the water anyway.

One day, Kalidasa was standing by the Garuda-stambha just as Lord Caitanya’s feet were being washed. Kalidasa caught the water in his cupped palms and drank it right in the Lord’s presence. Lord Caitanya did not stop him. Kalidasa then drank a second time, then a third. Finally Lord Caitanya said, “Stop. I cannot satisfy your desires any further. ” Thus Lord Caitanya personally gave him the mercy of allowing him to take that water, because He understood his faith in the Vaisnavas.

Later, Lord Caitanya returned to His place of residence and had lunch. But He did not eat everything on His plate. He indicated to Govinda that Kalidasa was waiting outside the door. Govinda brought Kalidasa the remnants of the Lord’s food. Again Lord Caitanya bestowed such great mercy on Kalidasa. Kalidasa is an ideal example to show us how necessary it is to appreciate all Vaisnavas.

Srila Prabhupada explains in the *Madhya-lila of* the *Caitanya-caritamrta* that when Rupa Gosvami and Sanatana Gosvami were recognized by Lord Caitanya and given their initiation and names, all the Vaisnavas standing around became ecstatic and chanted aloud, “Jaya! Jaya! ” Prabhupada comments that a real Vaisnava is someone who is always enlivened to see another devotee being recognized by the Lord. He writes that one should not be envious when it is somebody else recognized by the Lord. Vaisnavas are never envious of other Vaisnavas. They are always happy to see others getting recognition and glorification. This is a common theme in the *Caitanya-bhagavata.*

Lord Caitanya said that He would rather be in a cage with a tiger or be surrounded by flames than to associate with nondevotees. Similarly, when Lord Caitanya asked Ramananda Raya what would cause the most suffering, Ramananda Raya replied that the most painful thing is to be bereft of the association of Vaisnavas. He said he could not think of anything more painful than this.

On all levels of Krsna consiousness—whether we are neophytes or advanced—we need Vaisnava association. Simply remaining in the association of devotees gives us the impetus to raise our consciousness.

**Question: What about those devotees who go into** *samadhi* **in seclusion?**

Such elevated Vaisnava *acaryas* who go into *samadhi* are not alone. They may not be associating externally with other Vaisnavas, but they are associating internally with all the intimate devotees in Vraja and rendering service to the Divine Couple with them. There is association between Vaisnavas at every stage.

The *acaryas* explain that Krsna can be compared to Mount Everest. If we stand at a distance and see Mount Everest, *all* we can see is Mount Everest, because the mountain is higher than everything around it. As we come closer, however, we will see that Mount Everest is surrounded by subordinate mountains, and among those subordinate mountains are valleys and trees. But from a distance, we cannot recognize anything but Mount Everest. Similarly, if we think we are alone with Krsna, it means we are still standing at quite a distance from Him.

Do not stay so far away. Come closer and see Krsna with His devotees. Unless we associate with Krsna’s devotees, we will never be able to associate with Krsna.

**Question: While some Vaisnavas appear externally to give up the association of other devotees, why do some Vaisnavas appear to associate only with sense enjoyers or even to be sense enjoyers themselves?**

Pundarika Vidyanidhi was an example of a devotee who both appeared to associate with sense enjoyers and be a sense enjoyer himself, surrounded as he was by such opulence. Even physically he looked like a sense enjoyer. When Mukunda brought Gadadhara Pandita to meet this saintly Vaisnava, Gadadhara Pandita thought, *“This* is an elevated soul? ” Seeing his mentality, Mukunda then recited verses from *Srimad-Bhagavatam so* that Pundarika Vidyanidhi could hear him glorifying Krsna and His kindness. As soon as Pundarika Vidyanidhi heard these verses, tears of love flowed from his eyes and he manifested symptoms of ecstatic love. He fell to the ground and lost external consciousness. When Gadadhara Pandita saw this he said, “Oh, I have committed an offense in my mind! I judged him from his appearance, thinking him a sense enjoyer. The only way I can rectify this offense is to take shelter of this elevated Vaisnava and accept initiation from him. ” Gadadhara Pandita later approached Pundarika Vidyanidhi and begged for *diksa.*

Why would an elevated Vaisnava appear to be a materialist? Sometimes they take on this persona to avoid fame. For example, Gaurakisora dasa Babaji placed a young sweeper woman in front of his *bhajana-kutira so* that everyone would think he was enjoying with her at night and disrespect him. He did not want cheap adoration, and he did not want people to come to him only to ask for material benedictions. He preferred that people simply thought he was a materialist and left him alone. In reality, however, he was completely renounced.

Often people approach saints only for worldly benedictions. One materially attached Vaisnava mentioned in scripture was Raghunatha dasa Gosvami’s father, Govardhana Majumadara, and he once sent his son to Haridasa Thakura to receive such a benediction. Govardhana was a wealthy landowner, and he treated his son like a king. He was very attached to him, and wanted only the best of everything for him. Raghunatha dasa was not allowed to even see poverty or disease. When he was young, his father would not let him out of the palace; he wanted him to see only beautiful things. He also assigned him a number of personal servants, and when he was older, he would be carried wherever he wanted to go on a palanquin, the seat strewn with flower petals. It is described that there were always three palanquins waiting to carry Raghunatha dasa. The palace had three exits, and Govardhana had arranged for one palanquin to be waiting at each exit. The palace walkways where Raghunatha strolled were covered with lotus petals.

Govardhana Majumadara was a Vaisnava *abhasa.* He gave charity to Vaisnavas and *brahmanas,* but he was also quite attached to wealth. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states that he was collecting 200, 000 gold coins in taxes every month. He then gave half to the king and kept half for himself. This is an enormous sum of money for those days in Bengal.

Haridasa Thakura, on the other hand, was a mendicant. He lived in a hut just large enough for one person to sit down, and there he sat and chanted the Lord’s holy name 300, 000 times a day. He bathed in the river, and would not eat until he had finished his rounds. He wore only a loincloth and was very renounced.

One day, Govardhana Majumadara heard that Haridasa Thakura was staying nearby at Balarama Acarya’s home. Balarama Acarya was one of Govardhana’s family priests and had taken initiation from Haridasa. Govardhana Majumadara thought it was auspicious that such a saintly person was nearby and thought, “It will be nice for my son to go and receive Haridasa’s benediction. ” He therefore asked Balarama Acarya to take Raghunatha dasa with him to see Haridasa. They arrived at Haridasa’s *bhajana-kutir* accompanied by ten horsemen and servants and a number of priests and ministers.

Govardhana Majumadara had sent Haridasa valuable gifts, but Haridasa Thakura was not attracted by them. He asked, “What will I do with these things? ” He asked that they be distributed.

When Balarama Acarya brought Raghunatha dasa before Haridasa Thakura and introduced him as Govardhana Majumadara’s son, Haridasa Thakura learned that Govardhana wanted Haridasa to bless him.

Haridasa Thakura turned to Raghunatha and said, “I bless you to renounce the whole world! ” When Balarama Acarya heard what Haridasa was about to say, he sneezed loudly so no one else could hear. Govardhana Majumadara would be shocked to hear what kind of blessing Haridasa had given.

When the party returned to the palace, Govardhana Majumadara asked the servants, “What benediction did Haridasa Thakura give my son? ”

The servants said, “We didn’t hear what he said because Balarama Acarya sneezed at the exact moment the blessing was pronounced. ”

Govardhana next asked Balarama Acarya. Balarama Acarya replied, “He blessed him that he would possess everything in the world. ”

Govardhana was ecstatic to hear this and said, “So wonderful! He will become even greater than me. I possess this kingdom, but he will have the whole world! ”

Balarama Acarya did not lie to Govardhana; he spoke something logical: how could Raghunatha renounce the world unless he first possessed it?

Some saints try to avoid people who approach them for mundane benedictions. Although these saints are completely detached and fully Krsna conscious, they may appear worldly simply to confuse people and thus avoid those who want only worldly blessings from them.

In another incident, Balarama Acarya once told Govardhana, “There is a great saint living in Santipura. He is always surrounded by hundreds of thousands of followers. I want to see him. Maybe I should go and also take your son. ”

Balarama Acarya was referring to Lord Caitanya, and when Balarama Acarya brought Raghunatha to see Him, Raghunatha got his first opportunity to associate with the Lord and to render Him some service. He was even able to take the remnants of His food. From that time, a very, very strong desire to serve Krsna awakened in his heart. He vowed that someday he would give his entire life to the service of the Lord’s lotus feet.

After that, Raghunatha became like a madman. Although he was growing up in his father’s home like a prince, nothing of this world attracted him. Raghunatha wanted intensely to give his life to the Lord’s service, but the Lord had told him when they had met, “Do not be a *markata-vairagya,* a false renunciant. M*arkata-vairagis* are like monkey renunciants who appear renounced but who simply go from one tree to the next for sex with the female monkeys. Cultivate attachment to the Lord in your heart and everything else will follow. If you follow My advice, you will soon be with Me. ”

But Raghunatha was alive with spiritual desire. He returned home, but did not want to remain there. His father tried to lock him in his room, because he was always trying to run to the Lord’s shelter. His mother even suggested they tie him to the bed.

Finally Govardhana realized, “What good will that do? Our son has become mad. He is simply mad to serve Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Even if we tie him up, he will somehow get away. ”

During this time, Raghunatha went to visit Lord Nityananda, who was nearby. Govardhana sent twenty servants with his son to ensure that he would return. When Raghunatha saw Lord Nityananda, he offered his obeisances from afar, but someone recognized him and pointed him out to the Lord.

Lord Nityananda said, “Raghunatha dasa! Why are you acting like a thief and keeping your distance from Me? You should come here immediately. ”

Raghunatha dasa came before Lord Nityananda, and the Lord told him, “I must punish you for being a thief. My punishment is that you should prepare a wonderful festival and see that everyone is fully satisfied. ”

Raghunatha dasa took this wonderful service opportunity and purchased large quantities of chipped rice, yogurt, and condensed milk. Then the Vaisnavas held a big festival, and Lord Nityananda was pleased with Raghunatha dasa.

Raghunatha dasa then begged the Lord, “Please give me the benediction that I will be able to obtain the shelter of Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet. ” In response, Lord Nityananda placed His lotus feet on Raghunatha’s head. “Certainly you will be able to achieve the fulfillment of your cherished desire. ”

Later, Raghunatha did get the opportunity to break away from his family. One night, Yadunandana Acarya came to look for

Govardhana. He was having difficulties with the *pujari* and wanted Govardhana’s help, but Govardhana was not there. He looked for Hiranya, but could not find him either. Only Raghunatha was present.

By this time, Raghunatha was being quite careful to show his father that he had returned to his normal state of mind and was a responsible son, and his father was feeling more confident in Raghunatha and therefore less attentive. Therefore, there were only a few guards present, and they had fallen asleep. Yadunandana Acarya presented the problem with the *pujari to* Raghunatha and asked for his help.

Raghunatha agreed to accompany Yadunandana, but after they had walked some distance, Raghunatha told Yadunandana Acarya, “I will take care of everything. You can go home now. ” Raghunatha realized that this was the moment he had been waiting for to make his escape.

However, it was the time of year when the devotees go to Puri from Santipura along the main road. Raghunatha thought, “If I walk on the main road, my father will search for me and certainly find me. ” Raghunatha decided to take the more difficult but hidden route through the forest. What normally would have taken him twenty days took Raghunatha twelve, so eager was he to obtain *darsana* of the Lord’s lotus feet.

On his first night out, Raghunatha slept in a cowshed and was offered a small quantity of milk to drink. Throughout the journey, he managed to eat on only three out of the twelve days. Finally he arrived in Jagannatha Puri, lean and haggard. When he came to the place where the Lord was staying, someone announced, “Look, there is Raghunatha dasa. ”

Raghunatha dasa offered his prostrated obeisances before the Lord and the Lord picked him up and embraced him. He could see that Raghunatha had undergone severe austerity to come to Him, and asked the devotees to take Raghunatha somewhere where he could bathe and eat. He then entrusted Raghunatha to the care of Srila Svarupa Damodara Gosvami. Soon after, Raghunatha began to beg his food with the other Puri beggars outside the temple’s Simha-dvara gate, begging only enough to keep body and soul together.

Govardhana had sent many servants out to find his son. Later, he realized that Raghunatha must have gone to Puri, and he sent servants and letters to beg Raghunatha to return home. When he heard that Raghunatha was begging outside the temple gate, he sent messengers to pour large quantities of money into his begging bowl.

Raghunatha accepted the money, thinking, “I will not use this money for myself. Instead, I will use it to cook for Lord Caitanya. ” Therefore, every night he invited the Lord for *prasadam,* and the Lord agreed for some time. Then one day, Raghunatha thought, “Why should I accept money from a materialist? I don’t think the Lord is satisfied with this. ” He then stopped inviting the Lord, and the Lord understood why and was pleased.

Then Raghunatha stopped begging; there didn’t seem to be any way to avoid his father’s messengers. Raghunatha decided to instead take food from the place where it was distributed freely. He did this for some time, and when the Lord heard about it, He was again pleased.

Then Raghunatha dasa thought, “Why should I continue to eat like this? ” He decided it would be better to simply eat the rejected *maha-prasadam* rice from the Jagannatha temple. This rice was so rotten that even the cows would not eat it. Raghunatha gathered that rice from the kitchen drains and washed and ate it.

Once, Svarupa Damodara saw Raghunatha’s rice-gathering and said, “Why are you eating so opulently and not sharing it with anyone? ”

Svarupa Damodara then told Lord Caitanya, and Lord Caitanya personally came and repeated Svarupa Damodara’s words: “Raghunatha, you are feasting every day and you are not inviting

Me? ” Lord Caitanya tried to take some of the rice, but Svarupa Damodara said, “No, Lord, this rice is not fit for You to eat! ”

“What do you mean it is not fit? This rice is tasty! It will satisfy everyone. If it is good enough for Raghunatha, it is good enough for Me. ”

In this way, Raghunatha became so dear to the Lord by so fully embracing His instructions: Do not speak like ordinary people. Never be interested in hearing *gramya-katha.* Never run after palatable food. Never wear costly clothing. Never expect honor for yourself, but always be prepared to offer all respect to everyone. Always chant the holy name of the Lord, and worship Radha and Krsna always in your heart.

These were the only instructions Lord Caitanya ever directly gave to Raghunatha, who maintained this standard of renunciation throughout his life. Srila Prabhupada says that those who possess the Lord’s lotus feet, as Raghunatha did, possess the most valuable gift. We should try to possess this gift and keep it in our own hearts. We should approach the Vaisnavas, who are always merciful, and pray, “Please give me the benediction that somehow the lotus feet of the Lord will manifest in my heart. ”

This is the real benediction a Vaisnava has to offer. Once the Lord’s lotus feet become firmly planted in the heart, one remembers the Lord, the heart is cleansed by transcendental knowledge, and we gain everything. Raghunatha dasa Gosvami is an ideal example of one who had everything.

Another example of a person who appeared to be a materialist but who was actually completely detached is Devananda Pandita. Devananda Pandita had previously had Lord Caitanya’s association, but he did not develop any inclination to serve Him. Later, he got the association of Vakresvara Pandita, who was always absorbed in ecstatic love. When Vakresvara Pandita came to Devananda Pandita’s *asrama,* Devananda became attracted to him and desired to render him some service. Vakresvara Pandita was often seen dancing in ecstatic love. Sometimes he lost external consciousness and fell to the ground. Devananda Pandita was so attracted by his symptoms of devotion that he desired to follow him wherever he went, if only to catch him if he fell. He would then take the dust from Vakresvara Pandita’s body and smear it on his own. And when Vakresvara Pandita danced, Devananda Pandita sometimes stood nearby to protect him from others who might disturb the dancing. By rendering such service to Vakresvara Pandita, Devananda Pandita was able to get Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

Vrndavana dasa Thakura quotes a relevant verse: “The mercy from the Lord one may or may not get, but by the mercy of a devotee one will always get it. ” Not only did Devananda Pandita get Vakresvara Pandita’s mercy and through him the mercy of Lord Caitanya, so Jagai and Madhai received Lord Caitanya’s mercy through the compassion of Lord Nityananda.

**Question: There are devotees who make Krsna a part of their gratification, and there are those who put Krsna in the center of everything. Should we avoid the association of either of these people?**

Bhaktivinoda Thakura says that we do not need to avoid the association of either class of devotee, but he says that it is better to associate with those who place Krsna in the center of everything, including their enjoyment. It is the gross sense enjoyers who should be avoided. These people are not devotees. A devotee is one who has faith in hearing about Krsna. Although they may not be completely pure, such devotees still have faith in the process of hearing and chanting. The process will work and these devotees will become purified.

**Question: In the** *Nectar of Instruction,* **Rupa Gosvami said that we should open our heart and ask about confidential things. How can we develop trust toward devotees so that we can reveal our mind?**

If you find someone you trust, then open your mind to that person. There is no way to find people you can trust except by associating with them. We usually begin such association by hearing from someone, and we also examine whether the person has qualities we would like to develop or a better understanding of the philosophy than we do or something in them that makes us want to inquire further. Obviously, our inquiry will not be confidential during the first exchanges, but if we ask questions and receive satisfactory answers, then trust is built. With that initial trust, we can become more confidential. There is no other way to approach the development of trust.

Think about it like this: A person wants to ice-skate on the river but does not know if the ice is strong enough to hold him. Therefore he is cautious; he does not immediately skate out onto the river in case he falls through quickly and does not come up again. But if he stands on the bank and simply looks at the ice, he cannot skate. Instead, he puts a little weight on the ice and tests it. He keeps one foot on the bank and the other on the river. If after his initial test the ice holds, he adds a little more of his weight. If it still holds, he adds more weight. Once he has two feet on the ice, he can test it even further. If it still holds, he puts on his skates. There is no other way to test a relationship than to engage in it. We cannot just stand by and ask, “Can I trust him or not? ” and expect to know.

**Question: We are supposed to hear from advanced devotees, but how can we know who is advanced?**

It is every *acarya’s* duty to represent the previous *acaryas* and show how their devotional conclusions are in accord with the previous *acaryas’* conclusions. Srila Prabhupada often gave the example of a lawyer in a courtroom: When a lawyer presents a case before the judge, it is natural and responsible of him to represent the conclusions accepted in previous courts. A person who is able to do so is considered a good lawyer. Often when the Supreme Court rules on a particular subject, that ruling is held up as the topmost ruling of the highest court in the country. When presenting arguments before a judge, therefore, a lawyer will always try to follow precedent to authenticate his points.

Similarly, Srila Prabhupada always presented the conclusions of the previous *acaryas* in his *Bhagavad-gita, Caitanya-caritamrta* and *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* thus building a case to convince us— the residents of Kali-yuga, who may have so many doubts—about the authenticity of *bhakti-yoga.* We are not interested in being authenticated by worldly moralists or materialists. Prabhupada often said, “We stand on the authority of Krsna Himself and the entire disciplic succession. ” Therefore, when we read from the works of Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura, Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura, or Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, we should understand that Srila Prabhupada has already given many of these conclusions in his books. And when he introduced the Krsna consciousness movement to the western world, he was not introducing something new or recently fabricated, but something authentic, something introduced and supported by the previous *acaryas,* Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu five hundred years ago, and Krsna five thousand years ago. We can expect an advanced devotee to understand these conclusions. Advanced devotees stand on solid ground, and that solid ground is the conclusions of scripture and the previous *acaryas.* An advanced devotee is a *sraddhavan,* one who possesses such faith.

**Question: How do we properly pray to Krsna, following in the footsteps of previous acaryas?**

We should pray to increase our taste in the holy name, or for an increased realization that Krsna is present in His name. Pray to the holy name to help you increase your attachment to devotional service and to Krsna’s pastimes. Pray for help in overcoming *anarthas.* There are so many prayers that we can offer, and the scripture is full of nice prayers. Certainly we should recite the prayers of the previous *acaryas,* but we should not try to imitate their *bhava.* We can also offer prayers in our own words.

Rupa Gosvami explains that we should come before the deity and express our insignificance. Doing so is one type of prayer. We can pray about our fallen condition and open our heart to the deity. If we pray to ask something from the Lord, we should be careful to know what to ask for. We should not make the mistake of asking for the wrong thing. Therefore, it is good to learn to pray from the previous *acaryas.*

Lord Caitanya asked Kesava Bharati, “Who is better, the devotees or the impersonalists? ”

Kesava Bharati replied, “I can understand that the devotees are far better, ” and he began to recite prayers, including the prayers spoken by Lord Brahma. He said, “I see from the devotees’ examples that their minds are never diverted from Krsna, and whenever there was an opportunity to ask something from Krsna they ask only for devotion. ” He quoted one of Lord Brahma’s prayers: “My dear Lord, I therefore pray to be so fortunate that in this life as Lord Brahma or in another life, wherever I take my birth, I may be counted as one of Your devotees. I pray that wherever I may be, even among the animal species, I can engage in devotional service to Your lotus feet. ” *(Bhag.* 10. 14. 30)

Lord Caitanya was pleased by Kesava Bharati’s words and he said, “Had you answered that the impersonalists were better, I would have drowned myself in the Ganges. Devotees are certainly greater. ” Kesava Bharati then himself became an example of how devotees ask Krsna only for the opportunity to remember and serve Him.

We can also compose our own prayers: “Krsna, please give me whatever I need so that I can remember You. Please take anything You want from me so that I can remember You. Please just let me remember You. ”

Sometimes the devotees ask, “Please let me take birth in the association of Your devotees. ” Bhaktivinoda Thakura prays:

*kita-janma hao yatha tuwa dasa bahirmukha brahma-janme nahi mora asa*

“Let me become an insect in a place where Your devotee is present, because if I fall down in the dust of the feet of a devotee my life will be successful. ” Bhaktivinoda Thakura says, bahirmukha brahma-janme nahi asa: “I would not want to be a Brahma and not be a devotee of Krsna. ” (As quoted in *Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 132)

Devotees pray for the opportunity to remember the Lord and to remain in the association of those devotees who will help them remember Him. Such devotees are *akama,* free of desire.

The demigods are *sakama* devotees; they have desire. They will describe the Lord’s many wonderful qualities, but because they are *sakami,* they will ask Krsna to protect their position, body, and wealth. We should try to look for examples of unalloyed devotees and pray as they do. The unalloyed devotees give us proper prayers and a proper mood in which to offer them. Follow in the footsteps of the *mahajanas.* That is your safest position.

**Question: In what proportion should we spend our time with senior devotees, junior devotees, and devotees that are equal to us?**

If we have the association of devotees who are more advanced than us—even if it is only a moment of real association—we will naturally become more appreciative of the association of devotees who are our equals. The real value of advanced association is that someone touches our heart and helps us to see our *anarthas* and the obstacles we need to overcome—which really means that we get help to focus on our own shortcomings rather than on the shortcomings of others. We know we are associating with more advanced devotees when we become more conscious of our own obstacles.
