Whatever percentage of association with advanced devotees it takes to allow us to appreciate the good qualities in our equals and overlook their shortcomings we should take. That may mean associating with advanced devotees for five percent of our time, two percent, or even *lava-matra,* one-eleventh of a second.

If we have the proper eligibility to take advantage of the association of advanced devotees and hear from them submissively, then we will find that our heart becomes cleansed in their company and we will begin to realize the goal of life. Association does not mean only getting personal attention. Simply being greedy for personal attention will not carry us forward.

Having proper association with elevated Vaisnavas can allow us to associate nicely with equals. We will not run into difficulties in such relationships at all, because wherever we look we will simply be looking for whatever association can help us cleanse our own heart. This means we will seek out only the good in someone and will be disinterested in the shortcomings.

One is equal to us if we have the same faith and thus can help us see what is in our heart. Such persons can act like mirrors and show us our *anarthas.* Furthermore, although we may have peers, we can treat them as superiors. Of course, our peers will never think themselves superior, but if we treat them as if they are with sincere desire and while praying to Krsna, then our peers may give us their mercy and reciprocate by offering us friendly affection.

**Question: What do we do when we get inspired by association and want to render some service, but we feel we have no qualification to serve Krsna?**

We cannot obtain anything unless we have *adhikara* (qualification) for it. The *adhikara* for *bhakti* is faith. Although we may not have the qualification to please Krsna—and we should never think we do—we can still have the desire. And although we cannot please Krsna, we should try to please those who inspire us to please Krsna. By doing so, we can awaken the qualification to do something for Krsna’s pleasure. Even Prabhupada said his only qualification was that he tried to please his spiritual master.

**Question: How did the** *gopis* **and cowherd boys attain their position?**

*Krta-punya* (previous pious activities) brought the *gopis* and *gopas* into the association of *nitya-siddha* devotees. That is how they became qualified to become cowherd girls and boys. Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura explains that the *gopis* come from different categories of devotees. Some were *municari gopis* and some *sruticari gopis.* By the association of the *nitya-siddha gopis, purva-raga* was awakened in their hearts and they became qualified to enter the *rasa-lila.* The same thing can be said of the cowherd boys. After many pious activities, they got the association of the *nitya-siddha* devotees and became qualified.

**Question: What happens if we give up the association of devotees?**

As soon as we give up the association of devotees, *maya* will catch us and say, “Be in *my* company. ” No one can remain neutral or without company. One must either associate with *maya* or associate with Krsna and His devotees.

Narottama dasa Thakura sings, “Why am I living? I have not associated with the devotees, I have not taken part in the sankirtana movement, I do not understand what is Krsna, I do not understand what is Lord Caitanya. Then why am I living? What is my happiness? Why did I not die long, long ago? ” (Srila Prabhupada’s purport to *Gaura Pahu)* We should think in the same way.

But Narottama’s lamentation is not mundane. Hankering and lamentation are usually the symptoms of material life, but Narottama is expressing spiritual lamentation. What evokes this spiritual lamentation is when we see the superiority of spiritual life. Lamentation purifies us because it makes us realize that we must renounce our materialistic way of life and, as Srila Narottama dasa Thakura describes, the only way to do that is to remain in the association of Vaisnavas. Srila Prabhupada has spoken extensively on this point.

Srila Prabhupada said that when we speak of Krsna, we are referring to Krsna and His devotees. Krsna is never alone; He is always with Srimati Radharani, who is with Her *gopis.* Krsna is also with the cowherd boys. We do not see Krsna alone. Krsna consciousness means to remain in the association of Krsna’s devotees.

**Question: How should householders associate with others?**

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura speaks specifically about householders who have to work. He writes that they find themselves having to be in the company of nondevotees. But actually, it is not association unless one looks at the association with nondevotees and is attracted to their materialistic qualities. Then only is it association.

When we become attracted to someone else’s materialistic qualities, we think we can improve our standard of living by being with these people. This is considered association, and it is very dangerous for spiritual life. But he adds that although one may have to work with nondevotees, he will be protected if he has attraction for the association of devotees.

The Eleventh Canto of *Srimad-Bhagavatam* explains that devotion, direct experience of God, and detachment from matter come simultaneously for those engaged in devotional service, just as pleasure, nourishment, and relief from hunger come simultaneously and increasingly with each bite for one who is eating. When people eat, their hunger diminishes. Similarly, when people associate properly with devotees, they lose their attraction for materialistic qualities in nondevotees. However, such persons always remain merciful toward the nondevotees and never become callous toward them.

Seeing the loving exchanges between devotees attracts the nondevotees; it softens and then melts their hearts. It is not that nondevotees are banned from experiencing loving exchanges. But devotees first have to learn how to have loving exchanges with one another.

**Question: What happens to devotees who avoid preaching to nondevotees?**

Sometimes even very elevated devotees will not mix with nondevotees. For example, Madhavendra Puri wandered about alone because he did not want a companion who might try to engage him in *prajalpa.* Isvara Puri even wandered in the dress of an *ekadandi sannyasi so* that he could avoid the association of materialists.

Whereas *gosthyanandi* Vaisnavas preach, *bhajananandis* simply engage in *bhajana* and worship of the Lord, and their example is purifying and beneficial for the whole world,.

Srila Prabhupada wanted his movement to be a preaching movement. We always try to follow in his footsteps. We should always aspire to satisfy Srila Prabhupada by at least becoming qualified to preach to the nondevotees.

Srila Jiva Gosvami explains in his *Bhagavatam* commentary that the *kanistha-adhikaris* cannot even show proper respect to other devotees. Because they cannot show respect to devotees, they will certainly not be able to respect the nondevotees. Therefore, they cannot preach. Jiva Gosvami describes such persons as practically useless for preaching.

We should first learn how to associate with devotees and to have friendly relationships with them. If we are incapable of loving dealings among devotees, then we should know that we are *kanisthas.* By having good relationships among devotees, we will develop taste and attraction for Krsna consciousness. Our Krsna consciousness will then fill us up and there will be no room for attraction to nondevotional qualities. Do not avoid preaching to the nondevotees; rather, develop your strength in the association of devotees so that you are strong enough not to be influenced by the nondevotees. Being fully protected from worldly minded persons, go out and preach.

In addition, Srila Prabhupada often explained that we do not have to be great scholars to develop love for God. Gaurakisora dasa Babaji was illiterate, but he was the spiritual master of Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, who was not only a pure devotee and one of the Lord’s eternal associates but a very learned person. Referring to the illiterate *brahmana* who had been ordered to read

*Bhagavad-gita* Srila Prabhupada said that he was not disqualified from attaining *prema* because of his lack of scholarship.

Although we may not be big scholars, if we are eager to understand our own position and the position of the Lord and His holy name, we will understand whatever instructions we hear. In addition, we will be able to meditate on the spiritual master’s mood and thus make progress in spiritual life.

**Question: How can we know the spiritual master’s mood?**

The spiritual master has an external mood and an internal mood. Not only should we meditate on the spiritual master’s mood in relation to his preaching or chanting, but we should meditate on his more internal mood—the one he keeps hidden in his heart. Although the spiritual master may not fully reveal this internal mood, the best way to understand it is to hear about it from him. We must not only look at the way he preaches but listen to *what* he preaches. This is the substance of understanding the spiritual master’s role. When the spiritual master speaks about the Lord and the Lord’s pastimes, he is speaking about what is in his heart.

When the Lord’s instructions or pastimes are presented with realization, it acts on our hearts, making us want to hear and actually understand. Of course, the speaker has to be potent for such inspiration to take place, but we also have to hear perfectly in order for complete transmission to take place between guru and disciple. If we hear attentively from the spiritual master, we will come to understand his mood.

In fact, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta said this about Srila Prabhupada when he remarked that Srila Prabhupada hears attentively. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura indicated that because of this attentive hearing, Prabhupada would do everything in due course. Srila Prabhupada gave the example that he once understood Srila Bhaktisiddhanta’s mind by understanding the meaning of a verse spoken by him, just as Srila Rupa Gosvami understood what was in the heart of Lord Caitanya.

Lord Caitanya once spoke a verse, and Rupa Gosvami wrote the verse down and its confidential meaning on a palm leaf and slid it into the thatch of his cottage. When Lord Caitanya found that palm leaf with Rupa Gosvami’s explanation, He was overwhelmed by the fact that Rupa Gosvami understood His heart so well. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* explains that Lord Caitanya had entered Srila Rupa Gosvami’s heart and empowered him.

Thus when the spiritual master speaks his realizations, if the disciples hear him attentively, those instructions will enter their hearts and they will understand the spiritual master’s will. When the Lord instructed Srila Rupa Gosvami, He gave him all power to execute devotional service to his guru, who was the Lord Himself. If we do not hear attentively, it is not possible to to understand the guru—certainly not by looking at what shows on his face.

**Question: What is the practical way for devotees of the opposite sex to interact when they are working together in business?**

Certain conduct is required in the marketplace of the holy name. But because devotees may be working together to distribute a commodity other than the holy name, Bhaktivinoda Thakura gives a different standard for association under those circumstances.

The standard that he gives is clearly delineated in his book, Sri *Bhaktyaloka.* He writes that in the field of one’s work, there may be different types of exchanges between one person and another, but there is to be no affection; it is simply to be done as a matter of duty. One will therefore be protected in that field of work. But in the association of devotees, when one is in the *namahatta*—exchange of the holy name, hearing about Krsna, preaching Krsna’s glories—it is done with affection and love.

At work, everything you say should be a matter of duty. When the duty is over, the conversation is over. But if one thinks,

“I have this opportunity to exchange with a woman (or man), let me embellish it with sidelong glances and frivolous talk, ” then obviously he or she has forgotten what his or her duty is.

**Question: Please clarify what it means to hear the** *Srimad-Bhagavatam* **only from the lips of a pure devotee.**

The Bhagavatam itself states that the Bhagavatam is delivered by those who taste its fruit. Sukadeva Gosvami, for example, is said to be like a parrot whose “bites” make the fruit sweeter. Of course, the bites refers to his realizations. The *Bhagavatam* is always sweeter when recited by a person who realizes its purports. Therefore hearing *Srimad-Bhagavatam* from the lips of such a pure devotee is to our greatest advantage.

When the *Bhagavatam* says that it can only be heard from the lips of a pure devotee, it means that the *Bhagavatam* can only be properly understood when it is presented purely and transparently by a pure devotee. But if a pure devotee tells someone to speak the *Bhagavatam,* then that person is authorized to speak, and so hearing from that person is also beneficial. One must be authorized.

**Question: If a devotee is authorized by a senior Vaisnava to speak** *Srimad-Bhagavatam* **but has some contamination in the heart, will the listeners regress by hearing from him?**

*Sraddha* has to be protected by the guardians of devotion. This is exactly the same point: if we associate with those whose devotional service is influenced by karma or *jnana,* especially if it is being presented as pure devotional service, then their contamination can also affect us and we will find our *anarthas* growing rather than our devotion. Certainly we should hear with discrimination, and we should always try to hear from and associate with the devotees who protect our devotional creeper and who present the *Bhagavatam* without impure motives but who are interested only in glorifying the Lord and the qualities of *bhakti.*

The *Caitanya-bhagavata* also makes this point when Devananda Pandita comes before Lord Caitanya and asks, “I speak *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* but can You please instruct me *how* I should speak it? ”

Lord Caitanya tells him that the beginning, middle, and end of the Bhagavatam—that is, the entire *Srimad-Bhagavatam*—does not but glorify pure devotional service. Anyone who speaks the *Bhagavatam* but does not glorify pure devotional service is like a mule who carries a heavy burden of useless knowledge.

Thus whoever speaks *Srimad-Bhagavatam* should glorify pure devotional service and nothing else. Suta Gosvami explains that this *Srimad-Bhagavatam* kicks out all forms of cheating religion *(kaitava dharma).* We should try to hear *Srimad-Bhagavatam* from those who have properly understood this point and who only glorify pure devotional service.

**Question: Do you mean that we should be discriminating when we attend** *Srimad-Bhagavatam* **lectures?**

Yes, we should discriminate, but by that I mean that we should see whether the person is actually speaking the *Bhagavatam or* is tingeing its message with something else. If we hear something else, we should inquire. And best we seek the association of devotees who have understood *Srimad-Bhagavatam* and who present it as Lord Caitanya instructed us to present it. If we hear *Srimad-Bhagavatam* from such persons, then we will also learn to speak *Srimad-Bhagavatam.*

Srila Prabhupada emphasized that first we hear properly, and then we can chant (speak). Prabhupada glorified pure devotional service on every page of the *Bhagavatam,* and then ordered his disciples to speak what they had read. Prabhupada even asked his disciples to speak in his presence. He would turn to a disciple and say, “You give *Bhagavatam* class today. ” Then he would sit and listen, relishing it.

**Question: What can we do to ensure that we do not lose the association of devotees?**

To come into the association of devotees is auspicious for anyone in this world. Even more auspicious is to stay in the association of devotees. In order to stay, however, we have to experience the loving exchanges described by Srila Rupa Gosvami. If we do not feel love, we will look for love among the nondevotees, because it is the nature of the living entity to look for love.

## Mercy of Guru and Krsna

## Srila Prabhupada’s Smile

Srila Prabhupada has given the precious jewel of divine loveor Krsna. He spread his *sankirtana* mission by planting this most precious jewel in the hearts of his sincere followers. In fact Srila Prabhupada was so merciful that he was anxious to plant this seed in the heart of anyone—even those who were not his followers. Certainly this shows the greatness of his compassion: his mood was fully in accord with Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s desire. It is said that Srila Rupa Gosvami understood Lord Caitanya’s heart: *sri-caitanya-mano-’bhistam*—but this verse certainly applies to Srila Prabhupada as well. He wanted only to awaken spontaneous loving devotional service in the hearts of all conditioned souls, to nourish it, and to teach whatever was required for them to discover their eternal relationship with Krsna and return to the spiritual world. A spiritual master descending from the spiritual world comes with this mission.

Srila Prabhupada gave us a method to attain Krsna that Krsna had already accepted. This is a bona fide way to satisfy the Supreme Lord. Sastra states, mahajano yena gatah sa panthah: “One should accept whatever progressive path the mahajanas advocate. ” (Mahabharata, Vana-parva 313. 117) The mahajanas are those in whose hearts we can find the solid truth of religious principles. They are both able to satisfy the Supreme Lord and teach by their example how we can do the same. If we want to truly know their methods, we must serve them and understand their hearts. Serving such persons means following their instructions. It is said that the acaryas have taken a boat to cross the river from the material world to the spiritual world, but somehow, although they have arrived safely on the other side, the boat has returned for us. That boat is their instructions.

Srila Prabhupada is such a *mahajana,* and like all *mahajanas,* he always thought of everyone’s welfare. Once, someone said to Prabhupada, “Swami, will you pray for me? ”

Prabhupada replied, “I am praying for everyone. That is my business. Otherwise, why have I come here? ” (Room conversation with Sandy Nixon, July 13, 1975, Philadelphia)

Another incident:

In the morning, Tamala Krsna told Prabhupada that on the following day, Lord Jagannatha would travel down Fifth Avenue in New York City. “Lord Jagannatha is very kind to the *mlecchas, "*Prabhupada said. “Oriyans are mostly *mlecchas,* but still they are made *pujaris.* One devotee criticized an Oriyan and Lord Caitanya slapped him: ‘Why do you criticize My servants? ’ Just see His kindness! I prayed to Krsna, ‘Anyone who has given a little service, please bless him. ’ And Krsna actually does. He doesn’t forget any service done. ” (Srila *Prabhupada-lilamrta, 52* (July 22, 1976))

Prabhupada says here that he prays to Krsna: “Anyone who renders just a little service, please bless him. ” Krsna does not forget any service.

Another incident:

Tamala Krsna inquired, “Doesn’t Radharani also pray like that to Krsna? ”

“Yes, ” said Prabhupada. “Radharani says, ‘I am not sincere. Here is Your real servant. ’ That is *maha-bhava.* Krsna becomes a servant to His servant. Therefore a disciple’s first duty is to be submissive to his *guru. ”*

“Srila Prabhupada, ” said Tamala Krsna, “all your devotees are very much grateful to you. ”

Srila Prabhupada suddenly became immersed in thoughts of his devotees, and he became very ecstatic. Closing his eyes and rocking his head, he spoke with a choked voice and tears. “Oh, your intense love for me. I am living for you. All over the world everything is going on—money is coming and being spent—and I don’t have to worry. I am so much indebted. And I am taking so much service from you all. ”

“It is we who are indebted, ” said Tamala Krsna. “There is no way we can ever pay this debt to you, Srila Prabhupada. ”

(Srila *Prabhupada-lilamrta, 52* (July 22, 1976))

If we feel a sense of gratitude in our hearts for the great gift of Vaisnavas association we have been given, then to some degree we have understood its worth. The more we realize how valuable this gift is, the more our gratitude will increase. That is the nature of the gift Srila Prabhupada has given us: it is simply an opportunity to increase our gratitude. And as our gratitude increases, we will find ourselves wanting to offer something in return, however insignificant in comparison to the original gift.

We could see Srila Prabhupada’s own gratitude when we saw him smile. That smile was the most captivating experience for a devotee, and we would not want to miss seeing it. Prabhupada had a smile that radiated and extended from ear to ear. His smile was life itself. In the movement’s early years, the devotees struggled and performed great austerities just to receive one of Prabhupada’s smiles. That was all they wanted in return for their hard work. Just seeing Prabhupada’s gratitude, the devotees wanted only to do more to please him. And Prabhupada always smiled again.

We should pray that some day we too can make Srila Prabhupada smile like those devotees that made Prabhupada smile. The more we appreciate our own fortune at having the opportunity to be in the association of such wonderful Vaisnavas who know how to make Prabhupada smile, the more dedicated we will become to helping Srila Prabhupada and these devotees continue the *sankirtana* mission of spreading Krsna consciousness to every corner of the world. What makes Srila Prabhupada smile? He smiles when he sees Krsna consciousness expanding. We do not have to do something big to make Prabhupada smile. Prabhupada can be satisfied with even the smallest endeavor.

When we have the desire to please Prabhupada, then we can know that Prabhupada ultimately takes what we do and offers it to Krsna. He wants to offer a garland of devotees to be worn around Krsna’s neck. Each time he does so, Krsna smiles on him, Prabhupada smiles on us, and those whom we assist in the preaching also smile on us. We need nothing else to go back to Godhead.

At the same time, this work cannot be done cheaply. We have to struggle and overcome many difficulties. But Prabhupada is pleased by our efforts to daily go give Krsna consciousness to others and to become Krsna conscious ourselves.

When Srila Prabhupada left behind his method for satisfying Krsna, which had already been accepted by Krsna, he was thinking about everyone he had left behind. In the *Caitanya-caritamrta* he discusses the Vaisnava’s depth of compassion, referring specifically to Vasudeva Datta, who thought only of saving everyone but himself. Vasudeva Datta was prepared to accept the sinful reactions of every living entity just so they could all go back to Godhead. Srila Prabhupada explains that personalities like Vasudeva Datta are so merciful that they are ready to accept the sins not only of their followers but those who are not their followers. Lord Jesus Christ, for example, accepted only his followers’ sins; Vasudeva Datta’s compassion extended beyond the compassion of Lord Jesus Christ. He was willing to accept the sinful reactions of all living beings in the creation.

Prabhupada often quotes a verse spoken by Prahlada Maharaja when Lord Nrsimhadeva was offering him a benediction:

My dear Lord Nrsimhadeva, I see that there are many saintly persons indeed, but they are interested only in their own deliverance. Not caring for the big cities and towns, they go to the Himalayas or the forest to meditate with vows of silence \[*mauna-vrata\].* They are not interested in delivering others. As for me, however, I do not wish to be liberated alone, leaving aside all these poor fools and rascals. I know that without Krsna consciousness, without taking shelter of Your lotus feet, one cannot be happy.

Therefore I wish to bring them back to shelter at Your lotus feet. (Bhag. 7. 9. 44)

Srila Prabhupada had a mood similar to that of Prahlada. He often told us that if we took care of others, Krsna would take care of us.

In the Eighth Canto there is a verse that states that the highest method for satisfying the Supreme is to voluntarily accept inconvenience and suffering for the sake of those who are suffering. Again, this was Srila Prabhupada’s method of worshiping the Lord. Srila Prabhupada demonstrated this mood up until the time he left this world. He also demonstrated how to fix the mind on Krsna.

Prabhupada said that because in the Western countries constant chanting was not possible, we should not artificially imitate Haridasa Thakura in our performance of *bhajana.* Rather, we should follow the method he had offered: to chant sixteen rounds a day and follow the regulative principles. These are bona fide instructions.

Of course, we should always try to increase our remembrance of Krsna, our chanting, and our preaching of the Lord’s glories, but we should also acknowledge that Srila Prabhupada left behind a method by which we should do these things.

Prabhupada said, “You should preach as I am preaching. I am teaching you how to preach. ” Prabhupada felt overwhelming compassion for the suffering, conditioned souls. We often saw him crying in the mood of compassion. Such an incident was when Srila Prabhupada was in a car and looking off into the distance where he saw some golfers. He had never seen people playing golf before, so he asked what they were doing.

A disciple said, “Srila Prabhupada, that is called golf. You see the stick in their hand? They stand outside, sometimes under the hot sun and sometimes in the rain for the whole day, and hit a little white ball into a hole with the stick. ” Prabhupada began to cry, and he said, “They have created so many ways to forget Krsna. ” Tears of compassion flowed from his eyes, and when devotees saw such tears and heard his words, they would become mad to serve him and relieve his anxiety.

Vaisnavas feel no anxiety for themselves, but when they see others going to hell, they are driven to save them. It is the duty of the servants of the compassionate Vaisnavas to help with the preaching and thus relieve the Vaisnavas’ anxiety.

When Lord Caitanya descended to this world, He also felt anxious for the conditioned souls and came to relieve them from suffering. Prabhupada told us that it is the duty of the Lord’s servants to relieve the Lord’s anxiety. Just as the Vaisnava *acaryas* come carrying a burden of preaching compassion, so those who accept the path they have left behind carry the burden of helping them to relieve their anxiety for the conditioned souls. Only one thing can relieve that anxiety: the distribution of Krsna consciousness— the distribution of the *Bhagavad-gita* verse, man-mana *bhava mad-bhakto.* Due to Srila Prabhupada’s pure love and for the benefit of all devotees who serve his mission, the Lord accepted Srila Prabhupada’s method of worship. And now we have the great fortune to worship the Lord, following in Srila Prabhupada’s footsteps on the path he too followed.

Srila Prabhupada told us that he would live always in his books and that his books would remain for the next ten thousand years. There is no question of Prabhupada’s books being written only for a certain time, place, or circumstance. The service Srila Prabhupada left behind for us is to study and distribute these books. We should take full advantage of this mercy and try to preach as Srila Prabhupada himself preached; we should try to teach others how to remember Krsna as Srila Prabhupada taught us how to remember Krsna.

By 1966, Srila Prabhupada had only been in America for a short time, but he was already speaking on confidential topics from the Sri *Caitanya-caritamrta.* He spoke with such purity and force about the spontaneous love and service rendered by the Vrajavasis. It is inconceivable how Srila Prabhupada could have been speaking on these topics at such an early time in our movement. Gradually, by understanding more his audience’s capacity, he began to preach in a way that simply attracted our hearts to him, because by doing so he awoke our gratitude for what he was giving us. Although we may not have even realized all of what he had come to give, he knew our gratitude would remain in our hearts eternally. No experience in this world would ever supplant it.

Srila Prabhupada explains that only fools think they can repay the eternal debt they own to their spiritual master. In his purport to *Srimad-Bhagavatam* 4. 22. 47 he writes:

Knowledge of Krsna is such a great gift that it is impossible to repay the benefactor.... The word *vinoda-patram*... can be understood as one word, *vinoda-patram,* which means ‘joker. ’ A joker’s activities simply arouse laughter, and a person who tries to repay the spiritual master or teacher of the transcendental message of Krsna becomes a laughingstock just like a joker because it is not possible to repay such a debt.

Srila Prabhupada bestowed such love, compassion, and kindness on all of us, and was prepared to do anything necessary to awaken the spark of devotion that lives eternally in our hearts that we owe him our lives. He preached in a way that was perfect for our understanding, and he fanned whatever sparks he could find. And it is most beautiful to realize that Srila Prabhupada continues to fan those sparks, even though he is not physically present today. Prabhupada wanted to give us the topmost perfection. We should not think he has forgotten those who have dedicated their lives to his lotus feet. He will never forget us, because he is by nature always grateful. He proved that to us time and time again. Krsna, too, is always grateful for whatever service we render, even though He does not need our service. So Srila Prabhupada is always grateful for whatever insignificant service we render his mission.

Malati Devi Dasi once told a wonderful story about how she was serving Srila Prabhupada in Mayapur, India in 1971. She gave graphic descriptions of the austerities she had to undergo to cook for Srila Prabhupada, and she was with her husband, Syamasundara Dasa, and her one-year-old daughter as well. At that time in Mayapur there were no comfortable facilities. Even the most simple act of preparing a meal was done with great inconvenience.

Malati describes how when she needed money to purchase *bhoga* for Prabhupada’s meals, the devotees would only give her a small amount each time. That meant she had to beg for money each time she needed it. And she needed it every day. To get the *bhoga,* she had to go all the way across the river to Navadvipa. Then when she came back, she had to cook over an open flame, which is difficult in India.

Every day she thought about these difficulties and thought that she would ask Prabhupada to relieve her of her service. She wrestled with these thoughts, wondering how she could ask Prabhupada such a thing. Who else would cook if she didn’t?

Then one day a group of Prabhupada’s godbrothers came to see him in Mayapur. Prabhupada asked Malati to cook for all of them. She realized that she could not possibly ask Prabhupada *that* day if someone else could cook. Instead, she went to Navadvipa and purchased the *bhoga.*

By the time the meal was prepared, Prabhupada was entertaining his *sannyasi* godbrothers. Malati realized that she was going to have to serve the lunch. She tried to be discreet—and preferred to be invisible in that company. She took her sari and covered her head, then quietly stepped into the room. She slid the plate in front of Prabhupada and immediately tried to leave.

But Prabhupada stopped her. He turned to his godbrothers and said, “This is Malati Dasi. She is my cook. She is cooking for me even though I give her so much trouble. ” Then, looking to all his godbrothers, he said, “Malati Dasi would cut her throat for me, and I would cut my throat for her. ”

Imagine saying such a thing! Prabhupada showed his godbrothers his deep gratitude to his disciples. How could we not love and give our heart to such a person? And there are so many examples of Srila Prabhupada showing his gratitude to his followers.

Once, Srila Prabhupada thanked his disciples for helping him serve his spiritual master and he broke down, crying and could not finish his statement. This was again Srila Prabhupada’s gratitude. Even though we could not serve him properly, he expressed such feelings. Knowing this naturally forces his followers to feel grateful to him. Not only did he give us the gift of love of God, but he gave us the gift of love for himself, which is the most valuable gift anyone following him could achieve. Anyone who has come to the Krsna consciousness movement should know what Prabhupada gave and should feel, deep in the heart, indebted to him. We should love him as he loved us. Although we are incomplete in our attempt to accept his gifts, we can simply pray that he will allow us to go on trying to repay him. And in that attempt through all eternity we will happily remain fools.

**Question: What pleases Srila Prabhupada the most?**

Although Srila Prabhupada worked feverishly to plant seeds of Krsna consciousness, he often said that he wanted to see us become lovers of Krsna. This is what will please Srila Prabhupada. Otherwise, why did he give so much of his life blood to spread Krsna consciousness? Prabhupada even commented on this point; he said, “It takes gallons of blood to make one devotee. ” Imagine how many gallons of blood Srila Prabhupada expended to spread Krsna consciousness. Therefore we should try to please Srila Prabhupada by continuing his mission and especially by becoming genuine servants of the Lord’s holy name.

Srila Prabhupada had a way of emphasizing how joyful he would be to see temples opening, books being distributed, and more people coming to Krsna consciousness. At the same time, if the devotees who worked hard in these efforts were to forget Krsna, Prabhupada was always there to remind us of the goal of our activities.

Once in Atlanta in 1975, many of the devotees had gathered to greet Srila Prabhupada on his arrival at the temple. Most of the devotees assembled were book distributors. Holding a book in one hand, a devotee then asked Prabhupada, “What will please you the most, Srila Prabhupada? ” He thought the answer was obvious, because for years Prabhupada had stressed the distribution of his books.

Yet Prabhupada did not say the obvious. Rather, understanding the devotee’s heart, Prabhupada said, “That you love Krsna. ” His answer was firm and conclusive, and there was no mention of book distribution. Prabhupada knew how to adjust his message according to time, place, and circumstances, ensuring that his disciples were cultivating a proper devotional attitude while executing their duties.

Yet we also saw how Prabhupada smiled brightly whenever he heard about a new temple being established. In 1976, just after we had acquired a skyscraper in Manhattan (New York), Srila Prabhupada arrived—as did devotees from around the country. Prabhupada looked like a king carrying a scepter when he entered that building, and when he sat on the roof we could see his blissful face. It felt like a first step in conquering the world. It had been Prabhupada’s long cherished desire to become firmly established in New York City, because he considered New York the most important city in the world for preaching Krsna consciousness.

Prabhupada pushed hard to see that temples were opened all over. The building purchased in Boston for a new temple was also prestigious, and we thought it would make Prabhupada feel proud to sit on its roof—he would have conquered another important city. We still have this building—it is located in a prestigious area of Boston.

Before I came to Krsna consciousness, I was skilled at carpentry and used to build houses, so when we purchased this new building in Boston, I was asked to renovate it to prepare it for Prabhupada’s visit. At that time, however, I was a book distributor, and my desire to continue distributing books caused conflict with my temple president. I tried to make him understand that Prabhupada would want me to distribute books even over renovating the temple. The temple president didn’t agree. In the end, I submitted and gave up my challenge.

So another devotee and I had to completely renovate the five-story building. We ripped out old walls and put in new ones, refinished the floors, and rebuilt the kitchen. All this had to be done with very little money. We worked on it for about six months before the work was done sufficiently to move in the deities. Once the deities were brought into the new temple and Their regular *seva* was going on, I told the temple president, “Well, Prabhu, it’s time for me to go back out on book distribution. ” He allowed me to go at that time.

Then we heard that Prabhupada was going to come to visit this new temple. The temple president called me into his office and said, “Niranjana Prabhu, Prabhupada is coming to Boston and we don’t have a proper place for him to stay. Do you know what that means? ”

I did not.

“It means you will have to get Prabhupada’s quarters together.”

I brought up the same challenge: “Don’t you think Srila Prabhupada would want me distributing books rather than fixing up his quarters? Isn’t there someone else who can do these things?”

There was no one. And the temple president added, “You should meditate on this building work as personal service to Srila Prabhupada. ”

So I did the work. It took me three months to renovate Prabhupada’s quarters. I had to strip the wood and the walls, then refinish the wood and paint the walls. I then had to put up light fixtures and fix so many other things that didn’t work.

Finally it was finished. Then we waited for Prabhupada to come. But just around that time Prabhupada got a phone call from one of his secretaries in India saying that Indira Gandhi (the Prime Minister of India at that time) would meet Srila Prabhupada. He had been anxious to meet with her to discuss the future of his disciples in India.

So instead of coming to Boston as he had planned, Prabhupada flew to India. He never came to that Boston temple. I realized from the service I had rendered to prepare for him the meaning of *Bhagavad-gita* 2. 40:

*nehabhikrama-naso ’sti*  
*pratyavayo na vidyate*  
*sv-alpam apy asya dharmasya*  
*trayate mahato bhayat*

“In this endeavor there is no loss or diminution, and a little advancement on this path can protect one from the most dangerous type of fear. ”

In this particular endeavor—preparing Prabhupada’s quarters— I came to understand that there is no loss in whatever service we render. The only way we have to please the Lord is to simply meditate on pleasing the spiritual master. Although I had worked so hard and for so long to repair the temple and prepare Prabhupada’s quarters, he never saw either the temple or his quarters. It was a lesson from Srila Prabhupada’s constant book of lessons.

Prabhupada always forced us to become internal and not to be dependent on external results when attempting to please the spiritual master. If we remain internally fixed while executing our service, and if we always meditate on the pleasure of guru, wanting nothing for ourselves, we will feel the guru’s reciprocation, because he is Krsna’s representative. Srila Prabhupada is the external manifestation of the Supersoul, so by pleasing him the Supersoul in the heart is also pleased.

Srila Prabhupada knew how to teach his disciples to become Krsna conscious. Whether we build temples, distribute books, worship the deity, or chant Hare Krsna in the association of devotees, the goal of everything we do is to meditate on the pleasure of guru and Krsna. Such meditation can be so easy, but it can also be so difficult. The heart is filled with the desire for profit, recognition, and worship, all of which obstruct us from becoming valuable instruments in the guru’s hands. Yet Prabhupada was expert, knowing exactly how to engage each of his disciples’ propensities so that they became purified. This purification was the goal of everything he did with his disciples as he pushed and pushed and pushed us. He knew we *needed* to feel his push. The Bhagavatam (1. 1. 10) states:

*mandah sumanda-matayo*  
*manda-bhagya hy upadrutah*

“In this iron age of Kali men have but short lives. They are quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky and, above all, always disturbed. ”

These are the symptoms of Kali-yuga. To help us overcome them Prabhupada would push us and say, “I want results. I want temples opened, books distributed. ” He would sometimes make devotees work *very* hard, and he knew how to use both anger and displeasure, even when he was internally pleased.

In this connection, the B*hakti-ratnakara* tells the story of Gauridasa Pandita and his disciple, Hrdaya Caitanya. Gauridasa Pandita gave Hrdaya Caitanya this name, but he was previously known as Hrdayananda and was living in Gadadhara Pandita’s *asrama.*

Once, Gauridasa Pandita went to Gadadhara Pandita’s *asrama,* approaching the Pandita with great humility. Gadadhara Pandita said, “Oh, what great fortune do I have to welcome and receive such an exalted Vaisnava? What service can I possible render to receive you properly? ”

Gauridasa Pandita replied, “I have come to ask you a favor. ” Gadadhara Pandita said, “Whatever I have is yours. ” Hrdayananda had been in Gadadhara Pandita’s *asrama* since Hrdayananda was a young child, and he was very dear to Gadadhara Pandita. But Gauridasa said, “I have come to ask you for Hrdayananda. ”

When Gadadhara Pandita heard this request, he immediately called Hrdayananda and introduced him to Gauridasa Pandita. He said, “Here, take him. Whatever I have belongs to you. ”

Gauridasa Pandita accepted Hrdayananda, and then stayed for some time more to speak to Gadadhara Pandita. They discussed *hari-katha* for several hours, and then Gauridasa Pandita prepared to leave, taking Hrdayananda with him.

Hrdayananda then went to live in Gauridasa’s *asrama,* and there he rendered all kinds of devoted service. Gauridasa Pandita was pleased by his service and initiated him.

Once, a few days before Gaura-Purnima, Gauridasa Pandita told Hrdayananda, “Soon we will have a nice festival for Gaura-Purnima. I will go door to door to beg alms for the feast. Many Vaisnavas will come to glorify the Lord. I will be back in a few days. You should stay here and carefully continue your service. ”

Gauridasa Pandita left and began to visit people’s homes, where he sat and spoke about Lord Hari. As the days passed, Hrdayananda became concerned. There was only one day left before Gaura-Purnima. “Where is my Guru Maharaja? It seems to me that we have sufficient goods here to receive the Vaisnavas properly. I don’t know why my Guru Maharaja has not come back, but we are running out of time to arrange this festival. ” He decided to send invitations to the great *mahantas,* Vaisnavas saints, in the area to announce the big festival for the following day.

Later that day, Gauridasa Pandita returned. When he learned that his disciple had taken the initiative to invite everyone before he had returned, he was pleased at heart but externally he became angry. “Why have you done this? Do you think you can act independently of your guru? I told you to wait until I got back. How will you ever make progress in spiritual life if you act independently? ”

Gauridasa Pandita then sent Hrdayananda down to the Ganges and said, “You have your own festival there and I will stay here. ”

Thus Hrdayananda went down to the Ganges and thought only of the Lord, fully accepting his spiritual master’s reprimand. While he was sitting there, a boat suddenly came to shore. A wealthy merchant disembarked. He had heard about the festival and had brought many cooking ingredients for the feast.

Not wanting to disobey his guru’s order to stay by the Ganges, Hrdayananda sent a message back to the *asrama* and told his spiritual master of the merchant’s arrival. He asked what he should do with the ingredients for the cooking.

Gauridasa Pandita replied through the messenger, “We have sufficient goods here. You keep them and have your own festival there. ”

On the following day, Gauridasa Pandita received many Vaisnavas at the *asrama* and held a big festival. Meanwhile, Hrdayananda was down at the Ganges and prepared a wonderful feast with the merchant’s gifts. He began *kirtana. A* number of Vaisnavas came to participate, and soon they too had a wonderful festival.

Hrdayananda’s *kirtana* was so ecstatic that Gauridasa Pandita could hear it from his *asrama.* Still, he did not go to his disciple. But Hrdayananda’s *kirtana* became more and more ecstatic.

Suddenly, Hrdayananda looked up in the midst of the *kirtana* and saw, standing in the inner circle, Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda. They were dancing and chanting, and were so incredibly beautiful. Their limbs were shining and devotees were all obviously feeling great ecstasy in this *kirtana.*

Meanwhile, at the *asrama,* Gauridasa Pandita told one of his disciples, Gangadasa, to go into the temple room and make the offering to the deities. Gangadasa entered the temple room, but when he opened the altar curtains, he saw that Sri Sri Gaura-Nitai were no longer there. The altar was empty! When Gauridasa Pandita heard this, he could understand what had happened. Again he was internally pleased, but he picked up a stick in mock anger and ran down to the Ganges to see what was going on. What he saw astounded him.

When Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda saw Gauridasa Pandita coming in anger, They disappeared and returned to the altar. Just at that moment, while Gauridasa Pandita was looking at Hrdayananda, he saw manifest in his disciple’s heart Lord Caitanya’s beautiful form. At that, Gauridasa Pandita completely forgot his mock anger, and dropping his stick he ran to embrace his disciple. He held him tightly in his embrace for some time, tears flowing from his eyes. He said, “From this day on, your name will be Hrdaya Caitanya, because Lord Caitanya is always in your heart. ” Thus he was pleased with his disciple, although externally he had tested him by reprimanding him.

Srila Prabhupada sometimes appeared angry with us. At various times he explained that it is the spiritual master’s duty to test his disciples. Of course, the disciple should be careful not to think that his spiritual master’s anger is only external and that he is actually pleased. When Srila Prabhupada showed his anger, his disciples would jump and run.

Once a devotee asked Srila Prabhupada what to do with rats in the temple. He wanted to know whether the rats should be killed. Prabhupada became angry with the disciple and said, “No, *you* should kill for being so dirty that the rats are coming. ” Whenever Prabhupada got angry, we could know that there was a reason behind it.

But often Prabhupada smiled. That smile illuminated the universe. Sometimes the fire of anger coming from his eyes also illuminated the universe. He showed us his anger so we wouldn’t become complacent. Prabhupada wanted results; he wanted to see temples built, books distributed, and people becoming Krsna conscious.

Sometimes he pushed us by simply sitting and speaking *krsna-katha* in a way that awakened something in our own hearts. He somehow managed to pull on our heart strings and thus give us the opportunity to remember Krsna’s wonderful pastimes. Sometimes when he would speak about Lord Caitanya’s mercy he would cry and then thank his disciples for helping him serve the Lord.

In this mood, Prabhupada once gave a four-minute lecture in Atlanta. He was explaining the purport of the *bhajana, Sri Sri Gaura-Nityanander Daya,* which discusses the mercy of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda. As he spoke he was looking at the Gaura-Nitai deities on the altar. Then he said, “They are so merciful, ” and he began to cry. His voice choked up and he could no longer speak. We could see that he was meditating on the mercy of the two Lords.

In this four-minute lecture, Prabhupada gave us the whole essence of the Krsna consciousness philosophy, making everyone attached to Sri Sri Gaura-Nitai’s mercy and making us realize our fortune at having the opportunity to attain such mercy.

So do not be complacent. There is always more and more to do to spread Krsna consciousness. We have each inherited the debt Prabhupada carried on behalf of his own spiritual master. Try to maintain the same feeling of indebtedness when you offer your own service.

Srila Prabhupada did not want the temples to simply be places where people would ring the bells and worship the deities. He wanted the temples to be places where people would gather to become Krsna conscious and to give Krsna consciousness to others. He would be pleased to sit in a new temple for the first time, with the opportunity to preach to many devotees, but he never stopped there at such victories. Rather, he continued to push us. If we come to the temple and bring others along with us, we will create a dynamic environment in the temple and Prabhupada will smile at us from ear to ear.

**Question: Is Prabhupada a resident of Vrndavana?**

Of course Prabhupada is a resident of Vrndavana. Could he be from any other place?

**Question: How does the spiritual master know the disciple and guide him or her accordingly?**

A spiritual master should know his disciples just as an Ayurvedic physician can know his patients from their pulse. The guru should know how to give appropriate instructions to each disciple according to the severity of his or her disease. The guru opens the disciple’s eyes and imparts transcendental knowledge, which helps the disciple see things from an appropriate perspective.

A spiritual master also has to cut the disciple’s attachments. A *sadhu* uses a knife and must know how to use it properly, just as a surgeon must know how to use a knife properly; both also need to know how to give the proper anesthetic.

**Question: Why do you love your disciples?**

I love my disciples because they are dear to Krsna, having come to Krsna consciousness to try to reestablish their relationship with Him and to please Him. Why shouldn’t I love them? Not only that, but my disciples are helping me serve Srila Prabhupada. I have no reason to be in this world other than to serve Srila Prabhupada’s orders. The disciple who helps the spiritual master fulfill his guru’s order becomes very dear.

## The Lord’s Merciful Glance

Lord Caitanya came to reveal, through the chanting of the holy name, the spiritual world and the mood in which the residents of that world render devotional service. He also made devotional service to Krsna attractive by showing His own absorption in it and especially in thoughts of Krsna in the mood of the *gopis.* When devotees saw Lord Caitanya’s ecstasy, they themselves would cry. Making Krsna attractive was Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

While distributing love of God, Lord Caitanya did not take into consideration the qualification of the individuals receiving it. The only qualification anyone needed was to be most fallen—which is the natural state of the living entities in this age of Kali. Lord Caitanya was so kind that He induced others to chant and thus attracted them to the spiritual world. He thought about the welfare of all living entities, which includes each of us. And the only qualification any of us need to have to receive the Lord’s mercy is that we are most fallen.

Thinking about the living entities’ conditioned state, and because of Advaita Acarya’s prayers, Lord Krsna thought, “I will go to the material world as Caitanya Mahaprabhu to relieve the conditioned souls’ suffering. I will deliver them from ignorance, which is the root cause of all suffering. ” Thus without expressing discrimination, Caitanya Mahaprabhu freely gave the most valuable gift of the Lord’s holy name and to dispel all lamentation. He also gave the highest form of ecstatic love of God. Simply by giving so much devotional love.

Srila Prabhupada explained that the method of *bhakti-yoga* Caitanya Mahaprabhu descended to give is the quickest way to go back to Godhead. He gave the example of a silversmith who uses a small hammer in his work. He must tap that hammer on the silver many, many times, and the work is tedious, but that is the only way to work carefully and precisely, analyzing his work after each strike. The process given by Caitanya Mahaprabhu is not like the silversmith, Srila Prabhupada said, but rather the blacksmith, who takes his hammer and in one blow quickly finishes his work. Caitanya Mahaprabhu did not wait for each living entity to gradually evolve through the performance of pious activities; he gave a quick and powerful method by which each living entity could become a self-realized lover of God.

Ramananda Raya explains that the price for pure devotional service is not millions of births of performing pious activities but the intense greed to attain it. By Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mercy, that greed can be awakened if we witness His revelation of the ecstatic love shared in the spiritual world through His example and teachings. It takes sincerity to attain Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s merciful glance. We will not attain it by doing any activity devoid of devotion. Sincerity is nondifferent from humility, which relates to our actual qualification to receive such mercy: we are the most fallen and therefore eligible to receive Lord Caitanya’s love.

Srila Rupa Gosvami said, “No one within the three worlds is sufficiently powerful to deliver us. You are the only savior of the fallen souls; therefore there is no one but You. If You simply deliver us by Your transcendental strength, then certainly Your name will be known as Patita-pavana, the savior of the fallen souls. ” (Cc. *Madhya* 1. 199-200) If we simply accept that we are fallen, then when Lord Caitanya’s representative comes to extend the Lord’s mercy, we will accept it and thus be introduced to the real love of the spiritual world; we will again be attracted to our eternal spiritual nature.

The story of the maidservant Dukhi shows how devotional service is not only an external activity.

Some days the Lord would sit in the courtyard after dancing and all the devotees would bathe Him there inside the house. While the Lord was dancing in ecstasy, the pious Dukhi would bring water. Sometimes tears would come to her eyes as she watched the Lord dance, then she would go again and again to bring water from the Ganges. She kept the water pots in rows on all sides. On seeing this, Sri Sacinandana became greatly pleased. The Lord asked Srivasa, Who brings water everyday from the Ganges? ’ Srivasa replied, ‘Oh Lord, Dukhi brings the water. ’ The Lord then said, ‘You should all call her Sukhi. The name Dukhi is not appropriate for her. I think she is always Sukhi. ’ \[*“Duhkhi”* means sad, and *“sukhi”* means happy.\]

On hearing such compassionate words from the Lord’s mouth, the devotees all began to cry in the happiness of ecstatic love.

By the order of the Lord, everyone called her Sukhi, and from then on Srivasa did not treat her as a maidservant. One attains Krsna simply by serving Him with love, but one cannot escape the punishment of Yamaraja simply by shaving one’s head. (CB *Madhya-khanda* 25. 10-19)

Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura comments in his purport to text 19: Simply by the external acceptance of *sannyasa* or simply by atoning for one’s sinful activities, one cannot escape the punishment of Yamaraja. If one engages in service for the purpose of acquiring love of Krsna, one attains Krsna.

We may accept the external dress of renunciation and do something to atone for our sinful activities, but unless the sinful seed of desire in the heart is uprooted, we will still be subject to Yamaraja’s punishment. So instead of practicing renunciation or atonement, we must aspire to attain devotion to Krsna. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (6. 10. 15) states:

*kecit kevalaya bhaktya*  
*vasudeva-parayanah*  
*agham dhunvanti kartsnyena*  
*niharam iva bhaskarah*

“Only a rare person who has adopted complete, unalloyed devotional service to Krsna can uproot the weeds of sinful actions with no possibility that they will revive. He can do this simply by discharging devotional service, just as the sun can immediately dissipate fog by its rays. ”

One such devotee who discharged devotional service and satisfied the Lord is Suklambara Brahmacari, who cooked rice for the Lord and received His merciful glance. Lord Caitanya told the devotees that He wanted to taste rice cooked by Suklambara, and Suklambara thought, “I am a beggar and very fallen. Lord Caitanya must be playing some trick on me. ” He said, “Please don’t play tricks with me. Why would You want to accept something from *my* hand? I am so fallen. Please tell me the truth. Why are You saying this? ”

Lord Caitanya assured him, “I want to take something from your hands. Please cook for Me. I will come for lunch today and accept food in your house. ”

Suklambara still found the Lord’s desire difficult to understand, so he went around to all the Vaisnavas and asked what he should do.

They each told him that the Lord is inclined to accept food from His devotees. “Just as He accepted food from Vidura, so He wants to accept food from Your hands. ”

Still, Suklambara was concerned about what he saw as his fallen condition. The Vaisnavas told him, “If you are so worried about the Lord accepting something from your hands, prepare the rice without touching it. ”

Suklambara agreed to that and left to gather the ingredients to prepare the most wonderful rice and ghee. As he was cooking, he became absorbed in his meditation on the Lord’s pleasure. He carefully prepared everything without ever touching it with his hands. The Goddess of Fortune, being satisfied by Suklambara’s devotion, appeared in the kitchen and immediately made everything taste nectarean.

After the rice had been cooked, and Lord Caitanya had finished His midday duties, the Lord came to Suklambara’s home with some other Vaisnavas. Immediately, with His compassionate eyes, He looked at the faces of all the Vaisnavas. Simply by glancing into their eyes He awakened their ecstatic love for Krsna.

Regarding the Lord’s glance, Srila Prabhodananda Sarasvati said (*Caitanya-candramrta* 1. 5): *kaivalyam narakayate tri-dasa-pur akasa-puspayate:* “For those who have attained the merciful sidelong glance of Lord Gaura, impersonal liberation becomes as palatable as going to hell, the heavenly cities of the demigods become as real as flowers imagined to float in the sky, the poisonous fangs of the untamable black snakes of the senses become broken, the whole world becomes full of joy, and Brahma, Indra, and all the great demigods become like tiny insects. Let us glorify that Lord Gaura. ”

Lord Caitanya glanced at all the devotees present and then sat down to accept Suklambara’s cooking. He relished the rice very much. He asked Suklambara, “How did you prepare such wonderful rice without touching it with your hands? I have never tasted such rice at any time in My life. ” He looked at Suklambara and said, “Suklambara, you are the root from which I grow. Your devotional service nourishes Me. ”

When the devotees heard this and saw how much mercy Lord Caitanya was giving Suklambara, their hearts filled with ecstatic love. Tears flowed from their eyes. They were ecstatic to see a Vaisnava receive such mercy from the Lord.

This ecstasy at another’s fortune is the quality of a Vaisnava. When we read pastimes like this one, we are meant to imbibe and embrace this quality. Vaisnavas always keep themselves back, wanting to see others recognized and receive the Lord’s mercy. Recognizing the nice service others are performing makes everyone want to become more Krsna conscious. When one becomes so inspired, then the heart fills with attraction for Krsna and *bhakti* begins to manifest.

However, sometimes we are conditional in our attempts to practice *bhakti.* We may only want to do a particular service, and if that service is not available to us, we say there is no service at all. We think if we do a service not of our choosing that we will not get credit or be recognized. We think we will not be able to maintain our enthusiasm unless our service is recognized. This is *utsaha-mayi,* false or immature enthusiasm.

But who do we want to be recognized by? Lord Caitanya is in the heart and knows when we are lamenting and why. He also knows when we have a genuine desire to serve Him, and when we are able to appreciate other Vaisnavas. If we want to be recognized by the Lord, we should learn to appreciate other Vaisnavas while cultivating a genuine desire to serve. If we really want to satisfy the Lord, then we should become humble. Lord Caitanya states in the *Caitanya-bhagavata* that anyone who is envious of other living entities will never attain devotional service to Him.

**Question: How can we reciprocate with the Lord’s merciful glance?**

If we really want to give Krsna pleasure, then we should think about what He likes the most. Whenever there is an object of service and a person, we should wonder what would most please that person. Service to Krsna, service to Lord Caitanya, and service to the devotees are synonymous, because the object of the service is to satisfy the beloved. By satisfying those persons, we too become satisfied. Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura explains this secret of success in spiritual life in his *Gurvastakam* (8):

*yasya prasadad bhagavat-prasado*  
*yasyaprasadan na gatih kuto ’pi*

“By the mercy of the spiritual master one is benedicted by the mercy of Krsna. Without the grace of the spiritual master one cannot make any advancement. ”

## Krsna’s Partiality

*samo ’ham sarva-bhutesu*  
*na me dvesyo ’sti na priyah*  
*ye bhajanti tu mam bhaktya*  
*mayi te tesu capy aham*

I envy no one, nor am I partial to anyone. I am equal to all. But whoever renders service unto Me in devotion is a friend, is in Me, and I am also a friend to him. ” *(Bg. 9. 29)*

**Question: If Krsna is equal to everyone, why does He take special interest in the devotees? This appears to be partiality on the Lord’s part.**

Generally if we are partial toward someone, it is because we feel attachment or envy. We are often partial toward others because they fulfill our interests.

Partiality causes so much trouble in the world, especially in politics. When political leaders are partial toward certain groups of citizens, other citizens suffer. We also see partiality in religious groups. Partiality can even exist in families. Sometimes siblings fight because the parents feel partial toward one child over another. That favored child is usually the source of their pride.

Krsna says He is impartial, but at the same time He calls the devotees His friends. If others are not His friends, then is He being partial? But Krsna has no vested material interest in His friends. He gets nothing material from His relationship with them. In fact, His devotees are often materially impoverished, and even from those who are not, the Lord needs no opulence. After all, He is is the source of all material and spiritual worlds. What material support could He possibly require from His devotees in order to maintain His position? He is the Supreme Lord and is Himself the support of everything and everyone. Everything rests on Him as pearls are strung on a thread. The Lord is not dependent on anyone for His position or maintenance.

The *Katha Upanisad* (2. 2. 13) states, *nityo nityanam cetanas cetanam eko yo bahunam vidadhati kaman.* Krsna is the eternal living being and is maintaining all other living beings. No one maintains Krsna. Krsna is described as *advaitam,* nondual. His body is not subject to decay; He is the source of everything and has no beginning; He is *ananta-rupam*—His form is eternal and has no end. As Lord Brahma describes, Krsna is the supreme controller and is eternal, full of knowledge, and bliss. He is the original primeval person and the cause of all causes.

We are dependent, but Krsna is independent. There is nothing for Him to do. No work affects Him, and He will never be affected by any karmic reaction. Krsna, being self-sufficient, is also self-satisfied. He does not go to anyone for anything material.

A desire tree gives fruits according to the mood of the person approaching it. To get those fruits, we actually have to take shelter of the desire tree. If we do not take shelter, the desire tree will not give anything. Similarly, although the Lord is impartial, He differentiates between those who take shelter of Him and those who do not. Those who take shelter of Him become completely attached to Him and it is natural for Him to reciprocate with them. Krsna also reciprocates with those who are not attached to Him, but He reciprocates accordingly.

Because the devotees are attached to Krsna, He is naturally attached to them, and He may show indifference, friendship, or even enmity according to the nature of their *bhakti.* But this partiality is not *dusanam*, or faulty; it is *bhusanam*, an ornament. The Lord wants to give Himself to everyone, but He is naturally inclined to those who depend on Him in affection.

For example, when Krsna went to the Kauravas to seek a peaceful resolution, He spoke to Duryodhana. Duryodhana invited Him to take food in his house, but Krsna said, “I will not eat at your house because you are an enemy of the Pandavas. Your animosity toward the Pandavas is the same as animosity toward Me. You are actually My enemy. ”

Similarly, Krsna killed Hiranyakasipu only because Prahlada was so dear to Him. Krsna was indifferent toward Durvasa Muni because He had come under the control of His devotee, Maharaja Ambarisa. Krsna’s eyes were red with anger when Asvatthama beheaded the Pandavas’ sleeping sons. The Lord feels even indifference and anger when it is connected to *bhakti* because He is *bhakta-vatsala.* This is His most unique quality. The quality *bhakta-vatsalya* is like a king who has conquered all other kings. This quality conquers all of the Lord’s other qualities and reconciles all apparent contradictions. What may appear to be a contradiction is not a contradiction when we understand that the Lord is *bhakta-vatsala.*

Thus what appears to be partiality is not. Krsna simply reciprocates with the attachment and dependency the devotees feel. This is not unnatural. Just as one would expect a parent to feel special affection or partiality toward his or her own children, so Krsna feels special affection for His devotees. If a fire breaks out in a school where there are many children, parents will try to save all children, but when they hear their own child’s voice, it is natural for them to run to that child since that particular child is completely dependent only on them.

That Krsna acts as a loving parent toward His dependent children should not be seen as a fault but rather as the Supreme Lord’s ornament. It is the quality that decorates the Lord and makes Him beautiful.

## Pleasing the Lord

When Srila Rupa Gosvami talks about loving exchanges between devotees, he is not saying that if someone gives us something, we must give something in return. In the Tenth Canto, Krsna makes it clear in His discussion with the *gopis* that this type of love is mercantile. Actually, it is not love at all. The *gopis* tell Krsna that there are three types of lovers: those who simply receivewithout giving anything in return, those who reciprocate even if the lover behaves contrarily, and those who neither reciprocate nor act in a contrary way. The *gopis* thus ask Krsna “Please tell us which You consider the best. ”

Krsna replies, “The first is like a merchant. In mercantile exchanges, someone gives you something and you say, ‘Oh, now I have to give something in exchange. If I don’t give something in return, this person will not give me anything again. ’” This mercantile exchange is not love.

In a mercantile relationship, people calculate how much to give against how much they can expect in return. If they get less than they expected, they feel there is no value to their giving. Krsna considers such exchanges less than love. The symptom of real love is to serve the beloved without expectation of return. If we feel real love, the service and the beloved’s pleasure will satisfy us. Krsna does love and reciprocate with His devotees, but He does so in a special way: by bestowing pure love of Godhead in Vraja-prema. When Krsna reciprocates, it is not because He wants something in return due to His own deficiency.

The second type of lover reciprocates even though the beloved acts in a contrary way. The closest thing to real love in the material world is the love a mother has for her child. Although a child may disturb the mother in so many ways, she will continue loving her child.

The third type of lover is either self-satisfied or ungrateful. An ungrateful person will neither reciprocate nor act in a contrary way, and a self-satisfied person does not want anything. Therefore, even though someone may render him or her service, there is no reason to reciprocate.

These categories of love can be applied to the relationship between guru and disciple. Ungrateful disciples are called *guru-druha.* Despite the fact that the spiritual master does so much to help them progress in spiritual life, they remain ungrateful. Krsna says something different about Himself, even when He appears indifferent: “Although I am self-satisfied, I am never ungrateful. ” When someone offers something to Krsna with love, Krsna is always grateful, and He wants to reciprocate. But Krsna says, “Every time I ask the devotees what they want, they say they want nothing. ” But Krsna wants to give them something! He tells them, “Ask for anything! ” The devotees insist, “I don’t want anything. I want only to serve You. ”

Krsna then says, “I become so obliged to such devotees that I feel I *have* to give them something. They don’t want wealth, power, or elevation to the heavenly planets. They don’t want *anything.* I will therefore give them the most valuable thing I possess—My heart. They have Me in the core of their hearts and are always serving Me there. I cannot forget them even for a moment. ”

And Krsna is capable of remembering every devotee simultaneously and at all times. Only Krsna is capable of such a feat of remembrance and love. During the *rasa-lila,* every *gopi* thought she was with Krsna alone. Thus Krsna satisfied each of the *gopis* personally and simultaneously.

Krsna is not forced to bind Himself to His devotees in love. Rather, He wants to be bound by them, because He loves them so much. Such love makes Him come under their control. This is the power of pure loving devotional service. In loving relationships with Krsna, the devotee is impelled to go on serving Krsna because Krsna so fully reciprocates that service, satisfying all the devotee’s desires. Such is the nature of loving relationships.

## Undersanding Queen Kunti's prayers

## Krsna, Property of the Materially Impoverished

*namo ’kincana-vittaya*  
*nivrtta-guna-vrttaye*  
*atmaramaya santaya*  
*kaivalya-pataye namah*

My obeisances are unto You, who are the property of the materially impoverished. You have nothing to do with the actions and reactions of the material modes of nature. You are self-satisfied, and therefore You are the most gentle and are master of the monists. ” *(Bhag.* 1. 8. 27)

Srila Prabhupada’s purport:

A living being is finished as soon as there is nothing to possess. Therefore a living being cannot be, in the real sense of the term, a renouncer. A living being renounces something for gaining something more valuable. A student sacrifices his childish proclivities to gain better education. A servant gives up his job for a better job. Similarly, a devotee renounces the material world not for nothing but for something tangible in spiritual value. Srila Rupa Gosvami, Sanatana Gosvami and Srila Raghunatha dasa Gosvami and others gave up their worldly pomp and prosperity for the sake of the service of the Lord. They were big men in the worldly sense.

The Gosvamis were ministers in the government service of Bengal, and Srila Raghunatha dasa Gosvami was the son of a big *zamindar* of his time. But they left everything to gain something superior to what they previously possessed. The devotees are generally without material prosperity, but they have a very secret treasure-house in the lotus feet of the Lord. There is a nice story about Srila Sanatana Gosvami. He had a touchstone with him, and this stone was left in a pile of refuse. A needy man took it, but later on wondered why the valuable stone was kept in such a neglected place. He therefore asked him for the most valuable thing, and then he was given the holy name of the Lord. *Akincana* means one who has nothing to give materially. A factual devotee, or *mahatma,* does not give anything material to anyone because he has already left all material assets. He can, however, deliver the supreme asset, namely the Personality of Godhead, because He is the only property of a factual devotee. The touchstone of Sanatana Gosvami, which was thrown in the rubbish, was not the property of the Gosvami, otherwise it would not have been kept in such a place. This specific example is given for the neophyte devotees just to convince them that material hankerings and spiritual advancement go ill together. Unless one is able to see everything as spiritual in relation with the Supreme Lord, one must always distinguish between spirit and matter. A spiritual master like Srila Sanatana Gosvami, although personally able to see everything as spiritual, set this example for us only because we have no such spiritual vision. *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 61-62)

In this verse, Queen Kunti offers her obeisances to the Lord, who is described as the property of the materially impoverished. Kuntidevi explains how Krsna allows Himself to become possessed by His devotees who love Him. That they are materially impoverished means that they have no attachment to the material nature.

This very wonderful prayer describes the characteristics of pure unalloyed devotion to the Lord. Further on in the purport, Srila Prabhupada describes how the advancement of material civilization creates a stumbling block for spiritual advancement. Such stumbling blocks are called *anarthas.* Prabhupada gives the example of lipstick to explain *anartha:* “In the present context of material advancement one uses lipstick at a cost of fifty cents, and there are so many unwanted things which are all products of the material conception of life. By diverting attention to so many unwanted things, human energy is spoiled without achievement of spiritual realization, the prime necessity of human life. ” *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 61-62)

Lipstick is completely unnecessary to maintain life. Still, we divert ourselves to such “unnecessary necessities, ” as Srila Prabhupada calls them elsewhere in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* and lose track of the goal of life.

Actually, the real necessity of life is something far beyond these temporary acquisitions. Prabhupada goes on to say that we may make much endeavor to go to the moon, but even if we get there, what benefit is there to us? Going to the moon does not solve the real problems of life, birth, death, disease, and old age. As much as we try to find stop-gap solutions to these four problems, we tend to discover that our attention is constantly diverted to something more ultimate. “What is the value of a prolonged life which is wasted, inexperienced by years in this world? Better a moment of full consciousness, because that gives one a start in searching after his supreme interest. ” *(Bhag.* 2. 1. 12)

Our job is to transform our consciousness from material to spiritual. This is a natural life progression because consciousness is by nature spiritual. The *Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya* 19. 170) states:

*sarvopadhi-vinirmuktam*  
*tat-paratvena nirmalam*  
*hrsikena hrsikesa-*  
*sevanam bhaktir ucyate*

*“Bhakti, or* devotional service, means engaging all our senses in the service of the Lord, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of all the senses. When the spirit soul renders service unto the Supreme, there are two side effects. One is freed from all material designations, and one’s senses are purified simply by being employed in the service of the Lord. ”

Srila Prabhupada describes unalloyed devotion:

Because the Lord is the property of the devotees, and the devotees are the property of the Lord reciprocally, the devotees

are certainly transcendental to the modes of material nature. That is a natural conclusion. Such unalloyed devotees are distinct from the mixed devotees. *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 63)

Narada Muni states in the Bhagavatam that the Supreme Lord is dear to those devotees who have no material wealth but who are fully satisfied by possessing only devotional service to the Supreme Lord. Indeed the Lord relishes the activities of those devotees. He becomes dear to those who are unattached to material possessions and have cultivated only one attachment in life: the full shelter of the Lord’s lotus feet. When the devotee finds something of great value in the Lord’s lotus feet, the Lord finds something of a great value in the devotee.

Prabhupada describes the devotees as valuable jewels. If we have a valuable jewel in our hand, we will hold it tightly so we do not lose it. Similarly, the Supreme Lord holds His devotees tightly in His hand because He considers them valuable. The Lord promises to always give protection especially to those devotees who have no desire other than to satisfy Him. Devotees consider devotion to the Lord the most valuable possession they could have, and therefore they are always striving to attain it.

However, by *maya's* influence, the living entities place great value on objects other than the Lord. Krsna told Brahma, “O Brahma, whatever appears to be of any value, if it is without relation to Me, has no reality. Know it as My illusory energy, that reflection which appears to be in darkness. ” *(Bhag.* 2. 9. 34)\] Everything is connected to Krsna, and if we do not see that, we are in *maya.* Krsna says in *Bhagavad-gita* (7. 25):

*naham prakasah sarvasya*  
*yoga-maya-samavrtah*  
*mudho ’yam nabhijanati*  
*loko mam ajam avyayam*

“I am never manifest to the foolish and unintelligent. For them I am covered by My internal potency, and therefore they do not know that I am unborn and infallible. ”

Everything becomes valuable when it is seen as helping us to increase our attachment to the most valuable object, Krsna. Anything that distracts our minds from Krsna has no value and should be rejected. But the materialists see the complete opposite; they ascribe value to things that take them away from Krsna.

This is described in the the Bhagavatam chapter, “The Song of the Avanti Brahmana. ” The Avanti *brahmana* had accumulated great wealth through his business, but he had also made many enemies because he was greedy and miserly. Even his own family members had become enemies.
