One day, by the arrangement of providence, he lost his wealth and the spirit of renunciation awakened in his heart. He expresses his realization in Bhagavatam 11. 23. 18-19:

Theft, violence, speaking lies, duplicity, lust, anger, perplexity, pride, quarreling, enmity, faithlessness, envy and the dangers caused by women, gambling and intoxication are the fifteen undesirable qualities that contaminate men because of greed for wealth. Although these qualities are undesirable, men falsely ascribe value to them. One desiring to achieve the real benefit of life should therefore remain aloof from undesirable material wealth.

All the qualities the Avanti *brahmana* listed are valuable to the materially greedy; this has become clear to him now that he has lost his wealth. He now realizes that he should cross over all of the insurmountable obstacles of material life by taking shelter of Lord Mukunda’s lotus feet. Such great fortune for one who can realize the great value the Lord’s lotus feet have!

The difference between devotees and nondevotees is that devotees recognize the value of shelter under the Lord’s feet and nondevotees do not. Therefore nondevotees cannot see their inevitable destruction along with everything they have considered most valuable in life. From *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (6. 17. 23):

Although the Supreme Lord is unattached to our happiness and distress according to karma, and although no one is His enemy or favorite, He creates pious and impious activities through the agency of His material potency. Thus for the continuation of the materialistic way of life He creates happiness and distress, good fortune and bad, bondage and liberation, birth and death.

However, due to their Krsna consciousness, devotees know about real value and are generally without material prosperity. Despite their apparent poverty, however, they own a secret treasure house of the Lord’s lotus feet. Therefore they see everything that happens to them as an opportunity to take shelter and increase their attachment to the Lord. Even if the Lord takes something away, they consider themselves fortunate, recognizing that the Lord has given them further opportunity to remember Him.

Further on in his purport, Srila Prabhupada writes, “To be materially impoverished is the first qualification of a devotee. ” Devotees are materially impoverished because they use everything they have, not for themselves, but for for Krsna’s service. Srila Prabhupada does not necessarily mean that we have to live in complete poverty; rather, to be “materially impoverished” means to live with the mentality that “nothing I have belongs to me. ” As soon as we think something belongs to us, we become thieves.

**Question: Sometimes our material activities and responsibilities can drain our spiritual energy. What should we do to protect ourselves?**

When Lord Brahma offered prayers for the creative potency to create the universe, he thought, “How can I create a material universe without becoming entangled by the *maya* of thinking myself the creator? ” The *Bhagavad-gita* (3. 27) states:

*prakrteh kriyamanani*  
*gunaih karmani sarvasah*  
*ahankara-vimudhatma*  
*kartaham iti manyate*

“The spirit soul bewildered by the influence of false ego thinks himself the doer of activities that are in actuality carried out by the three modes of material nature. ”

Brahma’s fear was real. We have to be careful not to become so entangled in our activities that we begin to think of ourselves as the doer or the owner of what we “create”. Prabhupada therefore writes in his purport to Bhagavatam 3. 9. 29:

Any person authorized by either the Lord or by His bona fide representative is already blessed, as is the work entrusted to him.

Of course, the person entrusted with such a responsibility should always be aware of his incapability and must always look for the mercy of the Lord for the successful execution of his duty. One should not be puffed up because he is entrusted with certain executive work. Fortunate is he who is so entrusted, and if he is always fixed in the sense of being subordinate to the will of the Supreme, he is sure to come out successful in the discharge of his work.

Our real protection is to always be conscious that we are subordinate to Krsna. Due to *ahankara* (false ego), we tend to perform our activities in forgetfulness of Krsna, thinking that the result depends on us or that we are dependent on the results.

There is only one protection against *maya’s* influence: *krsna*— *surya-sama; maya haya andhakara* (Cc. Madhya 22. 31), Krsna is like sunshine and *maya* is like darkness. Wherever there is sunshine, there cannot be darkness; similarly, wherever there is Krsna consciousness, the darkness and influence of illusion vanish.

*srnvatam sva-kathah krsnah*  
*punya-sravana-kirtanah*  
*hrdy antah stho hy abhadrani*  
*vidhunoti suhrt satam*

“Sri Krsna, the Personality of Godhead, who is the Paramatma \[Supersoul\] in everyone’s heart and the benefactor of the truthful devotee, cleanses desire for material enjoyment from the heart of the devotee who has developed the urge to hear His messages, which are in themselves virtuous when properly heard and chanted. ” *(Bhag.* 1. 2. 17)

This is another wonderful feature: Krsna steals the material attachments from the hearts of those who give their hearts to hearing His transcendental narrations. This is Krsna’s greatest mercy and the highest expression of His compassion.

Lord Brahma says we can remain in whatever social position we want, but if we simply dedicate our ears to hearing Krsna’s transcendental pastimes, which are vibrated by the Lord and the Lord’s pure devotees, we can certainly conquer Krsna, although Krsna is unconquerable by anyone in the three worlds.

Question: Rupa Gosvami says that if we cannot dovetail something in Krsna consciousness, then we do not require it. How can we learn to discriminate what can or cannot be dovetailed?

Srila Rupa Gosvami states:

*anasaktasya visayan*  
*yatharham upayunjatah*  
*nirbandhah krsna-sambandhe*  
*yuktam vairagyam ucyate*

*prapancikataya buddhya*  
*hari-sambandhi-vastunah*  
*mumuksubhih parityago*  
*vairagyam phalgu kathyate*

“When one is not attached to anything, but at the same time accepts everything in relation to Krsna, one is rightly situated above possessiveness. On the other hand, one who rejects everything without knowledge of its relationship to Krsna is not as complete in his renunciation. ” (Brs. 1. 2. 255-56)

It is true that not everyone can engage everything in Krsna’s service. But pure devotees know how to use everything in Krsna’s service. They are that expert. If we hear from a pure devotee, we too can learn how to use things in the Lord’s service.

As long as we still harbor material attachment in the heart, it will be difficult to distinguish between what we are actually using in Krsna’s service and what we are using for ourselves. Therefore, we first have to learn how to offer the fruits of our endeavors by associating with saintly persons.

## Overcoming Maya and Becoming God’s Order-Supplier

*apy adya nas tvam sva-krtehita prabho*  
*jihasasi svit suhrdo ’nujivinah*  
*yesam na canyad bhavatah padambujat*  
*parayanam rajasu yojitamhasam*

O my Lord, You have executed all duties Yourself. Are You leaving us today, though we are completely dependent on Your mercy and have no one else to protect us, now when all kings are at enmity with us? ” *(Bhag.* 1. 8. 37)

Srila Prabhupada writes in his purport:

Krsna has a completely spiritual body (*sac-cid-ananda-vigraha* \[Bs. 5. 1\]), but we do not have the eyes to see what that spiritual body is. We are accustomed to seeing material, gross things (*jada).* We can see stone, metal, wood, and other elements, and because Krsna is everything, to be visible to our imperfect eyes He appears in a form of these elements. It is not that Krsna is stone or that we are worshiping stone. We are worshiping Krsna, but because we cannot see anything except material elements like stone, Krsna kindly appears in a form carved from stone. Therefore one should be very much inclined to live within the circle of a temple environment in which the form of Krsna is worshiped.

Moreover, one should always think oneself dependent on Krsna. This is Krsna consciousness. One should always think, “Without Krsna my life is useless, and I am in danger. ” Therefore, while offering her prayers to Krsna, Kunti says, “Krsna, You are thinking that now we are safe, but I don’t think we are safe. We are always in danger. If You think we are safe, who will give us protection? We have no protection other than Your lotus feet. We are encircled by so many enemies because the sons of those who have died in the fight are now preparing to fight with us. ”

Now, although Krsna had come to Kuntidevi to take the dust of the feet of His superior, His aunt, Kuntidevi addresses Him as Prabhu, the Lord, not as her beloved nephew. She knows, “Although Krsna is playing the part of my nephew, my brother’s son, He is still the supreme master. ” *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 150-151)

Because the Lord controls all energies, He can do whatever He wishes through those energies. We can think of the Lord as the powerhouse or the energetic source. He has unlimited energies, which pervade the creation. Although He has unlimited energies, they are still classified into three primary categories, the spiritual energy, which is full of knowledge; the material energy: which is full of ignorance and is visible when one engages in fruitive activities; and the marginal energy, to which the living entity belongs (although in reality he is spiritual), because he can come under the influence of either the material or spiritual energy.

The Lord, being the fountainhead or source of all energies, creates the cosmic manifestation by transforming His own energies. Everything that exists in the entire cosmic manifestation has ultimately emanated from the supreme energetic source, Krsna, who has a form made of *sac-cid-ananda* and who is eternally situated in the transcendental realm, Goloka Vrndavana.

Krsna can expand unlimitedly, but He never becomes depleted. Prabhupada often gave the example of spiritual mathematics: one minus one equals one. This is completely contrary to mundane mathematical reasoning. According to material calculation, one minus one equals zero, but according to spiritual mathematics Krsna is the supreme One.

The Lord expands the svamsa and *vibhinnamsa.* The svamsa expansions are His plenary portions, and the *vibhinnamsa* are the living entities. Foolish people think that when the Lord creates, He transforms Himself and therefore His entire self becomes manifest in the creation. These people therefore conclude that God is everything and then extrapolate that to mean, “Therefore I am God, you are God, we are all God, because we are all simply His transformations. ” Vaisnavas who understand spiritual mathematics know that the supreme omnipotent personality of Godhead can expand unlimitedly while His eternal form remains ever unchanged.

Not only does Krsna’s form remain unchanged, but it can create everything in creation without having to be personally involved. In other words, He creates by His energies, which are always subordinate to Him and which act perfectly according to His will. Therefore, Srila Prabhupada emphasized that one of the fundamental differences between the Lord and the living entities is that the Supreme Lord can do everything simply by His will while the living entities can only serve the Lord’s will and accomplish their desires by His sanction. Srila Prabhupada explains that even when Krsna dreams, His dreaming is reality, because everything happens simply by His desire. The living entities, however, who struggle in this universe, have so many unfulfilled desires due to their impotence. Their first desire, of course, is to become the *purusa*, the enjoyer, and in order to fulfill that desire, they want to become creators. If they succeed at creating something they like, by Krsna’s grace, they then want to become maintainers. But they quickly find that the material nature does not act perfectly according to their will. Therefore they must always struggle hard with the material nature in order to overcome its laws. This is, of course, impossible; Krsna’s laws are stringent. Only Krsna is *mayadhisa,* the controller of *maya*, and the living entity is simply *tatastha,* marginal, and easily influenced by Krsna’s *maya.* Illusioned by *maya*, the living entities forget Krsna.

Although the living entity has qualities that resemble Krsna’s qualities, just as a drop of ocean water contains the same qualities possessed by the ocean, they are quantitatively different. Krsna manifests His qualities in full, but the living entities and their qualities are infinitesimal. Krsna is supremely independent while we are minutely independent. Krsna can do whatever He wishes while we have to either serve Krsna or maya. Krsna is the master, and we are forever servants. Those of us who serve maya, are bound hand and foot by the laws of material nature; those who serve Krsna experience the greatest freedom.

The Lord’s internal potency, yoga-maya, acts to help the living entities in the spiritual world to forget that Krsna is God. But they do not forget Krsna. Maha-maya makes the living entities in the material world forget Krsna Himself. That is the difference between yoga-maya and maha-maya. *Yoga-maya* makes the living entities forget that Krsna is God so that they can serve Him in a way that gives Him the most pleasure. But the same *yoga-maya* expands into the material world as *maha-maya,* accepting a form as Durga, and covers the living entities so that they forget the Supreme Lord altogether. This forgetfulness is required if we are to think ourselves independent enjoyers—the whole basis of life in the material world. The devotees in the spiritual world never want to think themselves independent of Krsna. Rather, they want to always be with Krsna and to serve Him in the most relishable way, constantly remembering Him. This remembrance and service is actually every living entity’s natural state. Life in this world is unnatural.

Prabhupada asks, “What is the value of a hand that is not connected to the body? ” Similarly, what is the value of a living entity that is not connected to the Lord? In the material world, everyone values whatever gives them pleasure; therefore they are bound in a network of actions and reactions that cause forgetfulness of God. Because the living entities wanted to forget Krsna, He provided them a playground of forgetfulness. The entire cosmic manifestation is the facility created to allow us to forget Krsna.

But the Lord never forgets the living entities. He forever accompanies the living entities in His form as Parmatma as they journey the cosmic manifestation. As the Supersoul He bears witness to their activities and desires, allowing them to fulfill some of their desires by providing the necessary sanction.

Question: Does the Lord dictate anything to the living entity, or does He simply bear witness?

Yes, the Lord dictates to everyone according to their particular desires. If the living entities have a particular desire, the Lord dictates to them how to fulfill it. But the living entities are not perfectly following the Lord’s dictations when they act.

Srila Prabhupada gives the example of a young child. Young children are very much dependent on their parents. If the child wants something, he or she is dependent on the parents to fulfill it. Sometimes young children are insistent: “Mother, I want this. I *must* have it. Please give it to me. ”

The mother may say, “No, no, you cannot have it. It’s not good for you. ” But when the child persists, the mother may give in.

This is similar to the living entities’ relationship with the Supersoul. The living entities cannot do anything without His sanction, but because we are persistent in our desires, the Lord in the heart will dictate to us how to fulfill our desires. Those desires, however, may not always be good for us.

The living entities also cannot hear the Supersoul clearly. Fire manifests differently according to the quality or condition of the wood. If the wood is wet, fire will burn dully. If the wood is dry, fire will burn brightly. Hard wood and soft wood also ignite into different qualities of fires, although fire is always fire.

In the same way, the Lord is the same Supersoul in the heart of every living entity, but He can be recognized more or less fully according to the quality of condition of the particular living entity. We are not always able to perfectly follow the Supersoul’s direction. To hear the Supersoul clearly, we first have to control the mind, which is the reservoir of sense desires. For those who conquer the mind, the *sastra* tells us, the Supersoul is already reached, and they attain tranquility. For such persons, heat and cold, happiness and distress, honor and dishonor all become the same.

The Supersoul has both “superior” and “inferior” dictation. The Supersoul’s superior dictation directs the living entity how to return to His lotus feet. The Lord says, “If a living entity desires to come to Me, I dictate in such a way that he can come. ” But we can only follow this superior dictation when we conquer the mind. Until then, we must follow the Lord’s “inferior” dictation, which is simply to hear how to fulfill our material desires.

Of course, the more conditioned we are, the more unconscious we are that Krsna is involved at all in helping us to fulfill our desires. We have no sense of service and no sense of God, but think only of our own pleasure. If we try to fulfill our desires separately from Krsna, not even recognizing the need for His sanction, then we must accept whatever reactions come under the laws of nature. Krsna does not get involved in negotiating our selfish pleasures. Rather, He leaves *maya* to take care of us. Krsna only gets involved under one circumstance: when the living entities rectify themselves and come to the point of wanting His shelter.

When Ajamila was dying and calling for his son, Narayana, Lord Narayana sent the Visnudutas to rescue Ajamila from the Yamadutas. This was because Ajamila had chanted His holy name without offense and in that moment rectified himself and atoned for his past sins. Even in Ajamila’s case, however, Lord Narayana did not come personally but sent His representatives. Krsna gets personally involved in rescuing a living entity from *maya* when that living entity cries out to take full shelter of His lotus feet. Such devotees come under Krsna’s internal potency.

Krsna assures those who have surrendered themselves fully:

*daivi hy esa guna-mayi*  
*mama maya duratyaya*  
*mam eva ye prapadyante*  
*mayam etam taranti te*

“This divine energy of Mine, consisting of the three modes of material nature, is difficult to overcome. But those who have surrendered unto Me can easily cross beyond it. ” (Bg. 7. 14)

Krsna will tell Mayadevi to leave such surrendered souls alone: “I will take them now. You have no further business with them. ” Otherwise, since everything is already arranged through Krsna’s energies, He does not interfere in the functioning of the material nature.

Until we become free of *maya* we should have a healthy fear of it. Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura says that in order for us to become absorbed in hearing and chanting, we first have to develop a very, very strong fear of the material energy. For example, before Balarama or Krsna appeared in Devaki’s womb, Devaki became pregnant with six sons whom Kamsa killed. These six sons were Marici’s sons in a previous birth. Marici represents the mind incarnate, and the six sons represent the five sense objects and the function of the mind. Kamsa is fear personified. The next child to appear in Devaki’s womb after these six sons were born is Lord Balarama, who was transferred to Rohini, followed by Krsna. Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura explains that this description is given to show us how *bhakti* is practiced. When we engage in hearing and chanting about Krsna, we meet *bhakti’s* secondary benefits— the benefits that give us the opportunity to enjoy a variety of sense objects along with our devotional service. When those secondary results manifest, sincere devotees, fearing *maya,* cry out piteously to the Supreme Lord, “Please protect me and don’t allow me to fall prey to sense gratification. ” Those who pray constantly like this become more attracted to the shelter that hearing and chanting offer. Such devotional prayers and the increase in hearing and chanting attract Krsna to appear in the devotees’ hearts, just as Krsna appeared in Devaki's womb after her first six sons were killed by fear personified. Devaki is equated with the incarnation of *bhakti.* After the sense objects are destroyed by fear, Lord Balarama, who is the source of spiritual strength, appears; when we have spiritual strength to protect us from *maya’s* constant threats, Krsna appears. This is similar to how *bhakti* appears in the heart.

So fear of the dangers of material life is essential. Narada Muni tells King Pracinabarhisat:

... No one can counteract the effects of fruitive activity simply by manufacturing a different activity devoid of Krsna consciousness. All such activity is due to our ignorance. When we have a troublesome dream, we cannot relieve it with a troublesome hallucination. One can counteract a dream only by awaking. Similarly, our material existence is due to our ignorance and illusion. Unless we awaken to Krsna consciousness, we cannot be relieved of such dreams. For the ultimate solution to all problems, we must awaken to Krsna consciousness. *(Bhag.* 4. 29. 34)

How do we awaken to Krsna consciousness? Of course, *sastra* gives numerous examples to show that the best way to free ourselves from the disturbance of illusion is to take shelter of Krsna’s holy name. If we do so, the Lord will manifest and protect us.

Devotees feel so protected simply by their remembrance of Krsna that they do not try to solve their difficulties in any way other than to take shelter of Krsna. Therefore, when they are given the opportunity to receive a benediction, they pray only to always remember Krsna. Any devotee who is afraid of *maya* will be afraid to manufacture a solution for his or her suffering that is devoid of Krsna consciousness. The devotees’ fear drives them to take shelter of hearing and chanting Krsna’s holy name. Ultimately, we want *bhakti to* manifest in our hearts, just as Krsna manifested in Devaki's womb.

But it is not only fear of *maya* that ultimately motivates us to perform *bhakti.* Yet fear is the first step. Prabhupada says, “When we place our heads on the lap of Maya, we may think we are so comfortable and that everything is very nice. But when Maya has our head on her lap, we are nicely situated for her to cut our throat. ”

How long will we sleep on the lap of the witch called Maya? Wake up to spiritual life!

Maya is there at any moment; if we are a little inattentive, we will forget our devotional objectives and waste so much time. One of the characteristics of devotional service at *bhava* is *avyartha-kalatvam:* the devotee does not want to waste even one moment. Krsna says, “I am time, ” and devotees therefore consider time valuable. If time is not used to serve Krsna, then it is wasted and *maya* can capture us.

Along with seeing Krsna as time, devotees see Him as the destroyer of everything. At the same time, they know that when they use their time properly in devotional service, time becomes an asset for their progressive advancement. A devotee thinks, “Krsna has given me so much time in this life, but it will go by quickly. If I don’t use my time to serve Him, then I will have wasted my life and will be destroyed by time. ”

Time destroys everyone in the material world, but time cannot destroy devotees who use it properly. Ways to waste time include engaging in mundane sense gratification and gossip. We will not engage in such activities if we keep the desire to engage in Krsna consciousness burning in our heart. The fire of Krsna consciousness burns up all other desires in the heart with the Lord’s help and with the help of His devotees who always glorify His name, qualities, and pastimes.

How can those who are not fully pure always engage in Krsna’s service? Srila Prabhupada explains that out of His kindness, the Lord mercifully agrees to appear in the deity. One with material vision considers the deity brass, stone, or wood, but the Lord is not any of these. Rather, the Lord has a form made of eternity, knowledge, and bliss, and for one who has the eyes to see, the Supreme Lord is manifest in the deity. Because we do not have the spiritual eyes to see His *sac-cid-ananda* form, Krsna transforms His energies for our benefit and takes what appears to be a material form, making it completely spiritual.

If the Lord can do anything He wishes, since He is not under the laws of nature, why can’t He manifest in the deity form? When the living entities think that the Lord cannot do so, it is a sign that they are under the illusory energy. Illusioned people cannot worship the deity properly. How can they feel devotion if they think they are worshiping a piece of stone, wood, or brass?

But on those who can see Him in His deity form, the Lord agrees to become dependent. Such dependence is part of the Lord’s merciful and intimate relationship with His devotees. Although He is the Supreme Being, He likes to be dependent on His devotees. This is His greatest mercy. However, if we think Him simply a statue, He will reciprocate with us accordingly.

The deity provides us with a way to see Krsna. Otherwise, how would we be able to approach Him? We do not yet have the eyes to see Him; we have to qualify ourselves to enter Goloka and serve Him there. In a lecture given on November 29, 1966, Srila Prabhupada stated:

If you want to know perfectly, then *bhakti...* It is stated in everywhere, in all Vedic literature, evidentially in *Bhagavad-gita,* which is present before us, in *Srimad-Bhagavatam, Caitanya-caritamrta.* Everywhere you will find this only way, that devotional service. *Bhaktya mam abhijanati yavan yas casmi tattvatah* \[Bg. 18. 55\], *Tattvatah* means “in fact. ” Partially you can know, but in complete... Of course, God cannot be known in complete, but the highest point a human being or a living entity can reach... That, the only process, through *bhakti... Bhaktyaham ekaya grahyah sraddhayatma priyah satam. Sraddha atma priyah satam.* That *bhakti,* that process of devotional service, is very dear to the actual transcendentalist, very dear. *Bhaktih punati man-nistha.* M*an-nistha.* To know simply “I believe in God, ” that is not sufficient.

The ultimate goal is to attain very intimate relationship or love of Godhead. That is required. Of course, to know, to believe in God, to accept God, that is all right. It is better than the atheist.

But that is not end. You must develop yourself. You must... You should not simply make God as your order-supplier, but you should be order-supplier. When I become order-supplier to God, that is my perfection. And so long I keep God as my order-supplier, that is not *bhakti.* Generally, people keep God as his order-supplier: “O God, give us our daily bread, ” “O God, I am in distress, ” “O God, I am in difficulty, ” “O God, I am... ” God supplies them. God is supplying. *Eko bahunam vidadhati kaman.* But that is not ultimate goal. The ultimate goal is that you should supply God. God will be dependent on you. That is *bhakti.*

Just like Yasodamayi. Yasodamayi, he (she) was so advanced in devotion that God became dependent on her. God appeared as his (her) child. As the child always remains dependent on the mercy of the mother, similarly Krsna before His foster mother Yasodamayi just remained just like dependent. Mother said, “My dear child, if You become more naughty, then I shall chastise You. ” And God is crying. You see? These are the relationship between God and the devotee. You see? One who chastises the whole universe, whole creation, He is afraid of His mother. He becomes dependent as He likes.

Thus Krsna is so kind that He places Himself in a dependent role with His devotees so that the devotees can become His order-suppliers and satisfy Him.

Don’t see the deity as material. Srila Prabhupada once said as he was installing the deities in Australia, “You should never think, ‘Oh, my spiritual master is giving me a pain in the neck. ’ This is not *bhakti.* The deity is Krsna. You must see that Krsna is here and how He is dependent on you. You are so fortunate. You are the most fortunate people in the whole creation! ”

You cannot have an intimate relationship with a pain in the neck. Devotion cannot come by such consciousness. It will never come! Deity worship in the proper consciousness is really a great privilege—one that gives us a glimpse into Mother Yasoda’s heart and the hearts of the devotees in the spiritual world.

That the Lord allows Himself to become subordinate to His devotee is not a fault but an ornament. Actually, it is His most *beautiful* ornament; it is what makes Him so attractive. Srila Prabhupada says, “Why become one with God when you can conquer Him? ” Impersonalists want to become one with God, but if you love Krsna, you can become greater than Him.

However, we cannot artificially manufacture love. Prabhupada said when worshipping the deities, our love should be shown first as obedience—following the rules and regulations—punctuality, cleanliness, and the desire to please the Lord.

Krsna is a*ptakama* (complete). He can satisfy all His own desires without our help. Still, He expands Himself into the unlimited living entities for His ever-increasing pleasure. Krsna is always fully satisfied in relationship with His *nitya-siddha* devotees in the three-quarters expansion of the spiritual world. He has not lost anything by our being in the material world. Still, He experiences pain to think of us being here, and that is why He comes as Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

**Question: Krsna feels pain for us?**

For us, yes. This is why Caitanya Mahaprabhu appeared; He wanted to experience Srimati Radharani’s bliss, but He also appeared to help the living entities suffering in the material world.

Srila Bhaktisiddhanta describes in his *tikas* on the *Caitanya-bhagavata* that because the devotees are merciful, they feel the living entities’ suffering, and because the devotees suffer, and the devotees keep the Lord in their hearts, the Lord feels their suffering. This is how He feels suffering for the living entities in the material world.

Prabhupada explains in the *Caitanya-caritamrta* that the devotees are those who are always trying to relieve the Lord’s anxiety. They do so by preaching Krsna consciousness, because the Lord is always anxious to accept the suffering living entities back into the spiritual world. It increases His pleasure to see them return to Him. Anything that increases the Lord’s pleasure is favorable for devotional service. And because the Lord is pleased, He says:

*ya idam paramam guhyam*  
*mad-bhaktesv abhidhasyati*  
*bhaktim mayi param krtva*  
*mam evaisyaty asamsayah*

“For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. ” (Bg. 18. 68)

## Beauty in Krsna’s Presence

*neyam sobhisyate tatra*  
*yathedanim gadadhara*  
*tvat-padair ankita bhati*  
*sva-laksana-vilaksitaih*

O Gadadhara \[Krsna\], our kingdom is now being marked by the impressions of Your feet, and therefore it appears beautiful. But when You leave, it will no longer be so. ” *(Bhag.* 1. 8. 39)

Queen Kunti speaks this while Krsna is in Hastinapura about to leave for Dvaraka. He is just boarding His chariot when Queen Kunti approaches Him and offers her beautiful prayers. Her prayers are an expression of love and deep attachment for the Lord.

Krsna appeared in the world five thousand years ago to increase the conditioned souls’ attraction and attachment to Him and to encourage them to return to the transcendental spiritual realm. To do so, He enacts a perfect display in this world of the loving exchanges that take place in the transcendental realm. The material world is a reflection of everything existing in the spiritual world, but that reflection has become perverted by the selfishness inherent in the conditioned souls. Just as a tree is reflected on a body of water, its branches reaching downward and its roots upward, so the material world reflects the spiritual world; everything we see in this world has its origin in the spiritual world, but here it is upside down or perverted.

To understand the spiritual world, we must first understand Krsna. Krsna says that both His *janma* (birth) and karma (activities) are *divyam* (transcendental). When He appears in this world, He does not take an ordinary birth but is above the laws of nature.

Lord Brahma tells us that Lord Krsna is i*svarah paramah krsnah sac-cid-ananda-vigrahah* / *anadir adir govindah sarva-karana-karanam (Bs.* 5. 1): “Krsna who is known as Govinda is the Supreme Godhead. He has an eternal blissful spiritual body. He is the origin of all. He has no other origin and He is the prime cause of all causes. ”

He is also *advaitam acyutam anadim ananta-rupam (Bs. 5. 33*), — He is nondual, His body is not subject to decay and is the source of everything; He has no beginning or end, yet He always appears as an eternally fresh youth. We cannot understand Him with our physical senses.

The *Mahabharata* explains that anything transcendental is inconceivable; if we conceive of it—which means we can debate it—it is mundane. Since mundane arguments cannot touch transcendental subjects, we should not approach transcendental subjects through material arguments. The material senses cannot conceive of the nature of transcendence. This means that we cannot understand transcendence simply by looking at it. Srila Prabhupada states that we cannot even see intelligence with our physical senses, although intelligence is a subtle material energy. We can only know intelligence by its symptoms. Then how can we expect to see something far more subtle, something purely spiritual? The only way to understand transcendental subjects is for it to be revealed by the transcendental plane.

Krsna explains in *Bhagavad-gita* (7. 25):

*naham prakasah sarvasya*  
*yoga-maya-samavrtah*  
*mudho ’yam nabhijanati*  
*loko mam ajam avyayam*

“I am never manifest to the foolish and unintelligent. For them I am covered by My internal potency, and therefore they do not know that I am unborn and infallible. ”

And in *Bhagavad-gita* 9. 11:

*avajananti mam mudha*  
*manusim tanum asritam*  
*param bhavam ajananto*  
*mama bhuta-mahesvaram*

“Fools deride Me when I descend in the human form. They do not know My transcendental nature as the Supreme Lord of all that be. ” *Bhagavad-gita* 4. 6:

*ajo ’pi sarin avyayatma*  
*bhutanam isvaro ’pi san*  
*prakrtim svam adhisthaya*  
*sambhavamy atma-mayaya*

“Although I am unborn and My transcendental body never deteriorates, and although I am the Lord of all living entities, I still appear in every millennium in My original transcendental form. ”

*athapi te deva padambuja-dvaya-*  
*prasada-lesanugrhita eva hi*  
*janati tattvam bhagavan-mahimno*  
*na canya eko ’pi ciram vicinvan*

“My Lord, if one is favored by even a slight trace of the mercy of Your lotus feet, he can understand the greatness of Your personality. But those who speculate to understand the Supreme Personality of Godhead are unable to know You, even though they continue to study the *Vedas* for many years. ” (Cc. Madhya 6. 84)

If we want to see Krsna we have to qualify ourselves. We cannot demand of God, “Please show Yourself to me. You *must* appear before me. ” Srila Prabhupada explains that we should simply accept the Vedic authority. The *Vedas* are like the mother; they consist of Krsna’s words and the words of His realized devotees. As Prabhupada points out, “If you want to know who your father is, you must accept your mother’s authority on the subject. ” She knows best who has fathered her child. We cannot discover this information through experiment.

But if we choose to continue our existence in forgetfulness of Him, there will be no real beauty in our lives. The *Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya* (3. 11) states:

*bhagavad-bhakti-hinasya*  
*jatih sastram japas tapah*  
*apranasyaiva dehasya*  
*mandanam loka-ranjanam*

“For a person devoid of devotional service, birth in a great family or nation, knowledge of the revealed scriptures, performance of austerities and penance, and chanting of Vedic mantras are all like ornaments on a dead body. Such ornaments simply serve the concocted pleasures of the general populace. ”

Aside from beauty, Srila Prabhupada also says that a human society forgetful of Krsna will be devoid of real civilization. Real civilization, he writes, directs people to understand their eternal relationship with God. Instead of doing this, modern civilization has been built on the mistaken idea that we are only the body. Therefore, although we have become so technologically advanced, we still have not discovered how to become fully satisfied. In Vedic civilization, satisfaction was found by placing Krsna at the center of existence.

Among the forgetful living entities are the foolish materialistic scientists, who would like us to believe that we come from a mundane source. The devotees cannot accept this. The devotees understand that life always comes from life, and that no combination of chemicals can ever produce life. Even if the chemicals *could* produce life, we would still be left with the question: Where did the chemicals come from? There are many theories discussing the origins of life, but no substantial evidence. It is much more logical to accept that life comes from life rather than that life comes from matter, because we have no experience of life coming from the material elements.

Srila Prabhupada explains that Krsna is our supreme father. Every person has a father, who also has a father, who has a father— and back and back until we find the original father, Krsna. Krsna Himself tells us that He is the seed-giving father of all beings. This means that every living entity has a relationship with Krsna and cannot exist without Him. What makes life meaningful is to know the nature of that relationship. People seek objects of affection in this world. If they cannot find a friend or lover, they try to exchange love with a pet. It is natural to the soul to want an object for one’s affection. Ultimately, the soul wants to exchange pure and unselfish love—love based on selfless service. But such love does not exist in this world. As Srila Prabhupada says, the so-called affection people have for family, society, or country is only an expression of selfish sense gratification. The shift in selfish desire to the desire to please Krsna is called devotional service. When Krsna becomes the object of affection, He reciprocates that affection fully in the most perfect way by becoming completely subordinate to His devotees.

Material relationships reflect those in the spiritual world, but the relationships here are temporary because they are based on bodily connections. The relationship with Krsna, however, is eternal, and therefore Krsna came to teach us how to enter our eternal relationship with Him by displaying His wonderful pastimes. He is therefore described as *akhila-rasamrta-murti,* the form and embodiment of all attractive relationships.

Krsna is at the center of the transcendental world of Goloka Vrndavana. Everyone is constantly absorbed only in thoughts of

Krsna, the cowherd boy. He has the most beautiful, soft body, and His complexion resembles a dark blue rain cloud. He has a reddish luster in seven places: His eyes, His palms, the bottoms of His feet, His palate, His lips, His tongue, and His nails. He is known as the transcendental Cupid because He can attract everyone’s mind. He is also known as Krsna, “He who can attract everyone. ”

Krsna’s friends are attracted to Krsna because He performs wonderful activities. The *gopis* are attracted to Krsna’s beauty. And when He plays His flute, His music immediately attracts everyone in the entire creation. When Krsna goes out to the pasturing grounds in the morning with His cowherd friends, all the elderly *gopis* and *gopas* walk to the edge of town with Krsna. They do not want to see Krsna leave; Krsna is their life. And the younger *gopis* stand behind the bushes, on roofs, watching Krsna from a distance. Then when Krsna is in the forest with His friends they speak about Him, always remembering Him in the core of their hearts.

Mother Yasoda and Nanda Maharaja also remember Krsna constantly. When He returns home at the end of the day with His cowherd friends, His friends return to their respective homes, feeling separation from Krsna until He blows His bugle again the next morning and they can rejoin Him. Unlimited devotees have their minds fixed only on this one person, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Of course, they do not recognize Him as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. All they know is their attraction to this most beloved boy. They do not stop thinking of Him even for a moment.

The elderly cowherd men and women consider Krsna as their son even in the presence of their own children. When Lord Brahma stole the cowherd boys and calves, Krsna *did* become their son for an entire year. They felt their affection for their sons increased a hundred times, and Krsna satisfied each of them.

The most beautiful thing about these relationships is that they are never destroyed by time. Time in the material world destroys everything, but time in the spiritual world is conspicuous by its absence. Time has no destructive features in the spiritual world; it exists only to serve Krsna’s pastimes.

Therefore Krsna wakes up in the morning, goes out with His friends, comes back in the evening, takes rest, does some mischievous things at night, and returns home early in the morning. Mother Yasoda wakes Him and feeds Him. The *gopis* cook for Krsna, but Srimati Radharani is in charge of the kitchen. She cooks for Krsna every morning. Krsna eats with His friends, then plays His bugle or flute and gathers the rest of the cowherd boys so that they can all go to the pasturing ground. The calves or cows, who have been feeling intense separation all night, now run to join Krsna. Time seems to flow like it does here, but it simply serves Krsna’s pastimes. In the spiritual world, it is always the present.

Queen Kunti, recognizing Krsna as the source of auspiciousness and beauty, therefore tells Him that if He leaves them, everything will become inauspicious and nothing will be beautiful. “It is Your presence that makes everything beautiful”, she tells Him. Then she begs, “Please do not leave us. ”

Srila Prabhupada says that Queen Kunti’s statements, made five thousand years ago, still apply. We can make Krsna the center of our lives at any time because He is ever-present. If, however, we fail to see Him, we should know that it is only because we have not invited Him to stay. If we invite Him, on the strength of our love, He will come.

Be hopeful. Even if you cannot attract Krsna by the strength of your love, you can still attract His attention by serving His devotees, who are dear to Him. Krsna confirms that He goes wherever His devotees go. He also promises that those who remember Him at the time of death will attain to His eternal nature. All reasons to hope. But we must reach out for Krsna; don’t expect anyone to do this for us.

**Question: How long will the conditioned souls wait in the material world before they get an opportunity to hear about Krsna’s pastimes and qualities from a devotee?**

There is no fixed time. Simply by coming in contact with a devotee, material life can be finished. The *Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya* 19. 151) states:

*brahmanda bhramite kona bhagyavan jiva*  
*guru-krsna-prasade paya bhakti-lata-bija*

“According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Krsna. By the mercy of both Krsna and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service. ”

But we should understand that we have been here since time immemorial. Even during the devastation, those living entities who have not gone back to Godhead enter Maha-Visnu’s body and lie dormant, waiting for the next creation. When Maha-Visnu creates the material energy, He impregnates those same living entities back into the womb of the material nature and they take up where they left off from the last creation. Prabhupada described this process often to emphasize our great fortune in having come in contact with the Vaisnavas. And not only have we experienced *that* fortune, but imagine our fortune at being present during the one time in the day of Lord Brahma when Lord Caitanya appeared to distribute ecstatic Vraja-prema. Caitanya Mahaprabhu appeared only five hundred years ago. What is five hundred years when compared to a day of Brahma, which lasts one thousand *divya-yugas* (one *divya-yuga* is 4, 320, 000 years)? This is *bhagyavan.*

**Question: Materialists could argue that if Krsna really is all-attractive, then we should already be attracted to Him.**

We cannot demand of Krsna: “You must come before me and then I will accept You. ” Krsna can choose whatever way He wants to appear before us. He says He will appear before us in sound. If we accept Him in sound, then we can purify our consciousness and eyes so that we can see His other forms. He also says that if we want to forget Him, He will facilitate our forgetfulness. Krsna wants to see us first become dependent on Him. If we become a little dependent and turn to Him in the form of transcendental sound, then we can qualify ourselves to see Him. Krsna thinks, “But more important than seeing Me is hearing about Me. ” Krsna therefore makes Himself available through His devotees’ words.

*naham tisthami vaikunthe*  
*yoginam va hrdaye ca*  
*yatra gayanti mad-bhakta*  
*tatra tisthami narada*

\[Krsna says\] “I do not stay in Vaikuntha-loka, the spiritual world, or within the hearts of the *yogis.* I stay where My devotees chant My glories. ” *(Padma Purana)*

Hearing helps us develop faith and dependence; gradually we give up our independent desires and become more surrendered at Krsna’s feet. And hearing is important because Krsna comes personally only once in a day of Brahma. When He disappears, He remains in the words of His devotees.

Queen Kunti’s prayers explain that the Lord appears to rejuvenate the devotional service of hearing and chanting about Him. And when He departs, the devotees continue speaking about Him. And if there are no devotees to speak of Him, then some devotees from the spiritual world will descend for that purpose. Thus sometimes Krsna comes Himself and sometimes He sends His empowered servants.

This goes on eternally in unlimited universes. Krsna’s appearance in each universe is like the moving of a clock’s hands from one minute to the next. Krsna comes and then departs, but remains through His devotees’ words, and then appears when the hearer’s heart becomes pure.

*atah sri-krsna-namadi*  
*na bhaved grahyam indriyaih*  
*sevonmukhe hi jihvadau*  
*svayam eva sphuraty adah*

“Therefore material senses cannot appreciate Krsna’s holy name, form, qualities and pastimes. When a conditioned soul is awakened to Krsna consciousness and renders service by using his tongue to chant the Lord’s holy name and taste the remnants of the Lord’s food, the tongue is purified, and one gradually comes to understand who Krsna really is. ” (Brs. 1. 2. 234)

## Kevala bhakti

## Krsna Does Not Want to be God

When Akrura was taking Krsna away to Mathura, everyone tried to stop Him from going. The *gopis* lay in front of the chariot, saying, “Let the chariots roll over us. Let us die. How can we live without Krsna? ” Their attachment was so deep. But Krsna left. The *gopis* then felt continuous separation from Him. The only thing that sustained them was that Krsna said he would come back.

Srila Rupa Gosvami gives the example that when ponds of water dry up under the hot summer sun, the turtles that normally live in the water have to take shelter of the mud, burying themselves simply to survive. Similarly, when Krsna left for Mathura, the Vrajavasis were thrown into the burning fire of separation and to survive took shelter of Krsna’s words: “I will come back. ”

Krsna’s devotee in Mathura, Uddhava, is described as a *jnana-misra-bhakta,* a devotee whose devotion was mixed with *jnana.* He was a disciple of Brhaspati and very dear to the Lord. The Lord wanted to teach him *kevala-bhakti,* unalloyed devotion, so He asked him to carry a message to His devotees in Vrndavana. “There in Vrndavana you will find My beloved devotees, of whom I am always thinking. ”

These devotees of Vrndavana—Nanda Maharaja, Mother Yasoda, the cowherd men and women, the cowherd boys and the *gopis*— were completely absorbed in thoughts of Krsna twenty-four hours a day. Their love for Krsna was so condensed that it could never be disturbed.

Krsna does not let the Vraja-bhaktas think that He is God. Instead, He wants them to think themselves superior to Him, to feel protective of Him, or to be equal to Him. If the devotees were to focus on His opulence and majesty, it would spoil the intimacy of the relationship. Yoga-maya thus covers everyone so that they will never suspect His Godhood. Even if the Vrajavasis see Krsna do something wonderful, such as swallow a fire or kill a powerful demon, they think He got His power from Lord Narayana.

And they appreciate Him as their dearest friend. Every day, the cowherd boys run home to tell their parents, “Do you know what Krsna did today? Listen to this! There was a big demon and Krsna finished him! Krsna is so wonderful! ”

When Mother Yasoda saw the Putana demon and how Krsna sucked out her life and killed her, she simply thought, “Krsna is safe because Nanda Maharaja and I are always praying to Lord Narayana for His protection. It is He who has protected our son. ”

Every morning when Mother Yasoda bathes Krsna, she chants mantras for His safety: “O Lord Hrsikesa, please protect my son from this side. O Lord Damodara, please protect my son from that side. O Lord Padmanabha, please protect my son from that side. ”

When Krsna was holding Govardhana Hill, Mother Yasoda actually thought Nanda Maharaja was holding it up with his stick. She could not understand how her little boy could do such a thing.

It is this deepest type of love that Krsna wanted Uddhava to witness in the Vrajavasis. Krsna therefore gave him a message of affection: “Please tell them that I am always thinking of them and remembering our activities together. I am consciously aware of the love they have for Me. Please assure them that I will come back. ” Uddhava was *sastra-jna*; He knew about Krsna and understood His glories and position as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He understood Krsna as the source of the entire cosmic manifestation, and knew that everything emanated from Him. He also knew that Krsna is no one’s son but is the seed-giving father of all beings. Krsna wanted to show him a higher understanding.

When Uddhava first arrived in Vrndavana, he saw the clouds of dust raised by the cows’ hooves as they returned from the pasturing grounds. When he entered Vrndavana, he also saw the cowherd boys. He went first to Nanda Maharaja’s home. Nanda Maharaja asked, “Please tell me, how are my brother Vasudeva and sister Devaki doing in Mathura? ” There had been so many disturbances in Mathura, but since Kamsa had been killed, the disturbances he had created were over. Vasudeva and Devaki had finally been released from prison and Krsna and Balarama were living with them.

Nanda Maharaja then asked about Krsna and Balarama. “I heard Vasudeva and Devaki have sent Them to *gurukula* under Sandipani Muni. I heard They have shaved Their heads and are now *brahmacaris,* and that They have accepted the sacred thread. They go out to collect alms on Their guru’s behalf, and They go into the forest to collect wood. How can my Krsna do such work? My Krsna is a simple boy. He should not have to undergo so much trouble to collect wood. He is my milk-fed child. How can He live through such austerities? ”

Nanda Maharaja continued, “Does Krsna remember us? Does He remember His mother, Yasoda? Does He remember how much His mother cared for Him and helped Him in so many ways? Does He remember His cowherd friends with whom He used to frolic in the pasturing grounds? Does He remember His cows and calves, and how He took them out every day and fed them sweet grasses with His own hands? Does He remember His Govardhana Hill and all His other pastime places? Wherever I go, I see Krsna’s footprints. Does Krsna still remember me? ”

Nanda Maharaja was absorbed in *vatsalya-priti,* parental affection. The primary characteristic of parental affection is *anugraha,* a feeling of compassion for and a need to protect the object of love.

Nanda Maharaja continued, “Not only that, but I have heard that there are many disturbances in Mathura. I heard that Krsna suffered much anxiety when Jarasandha attacked Mathura. He had to protect Mathura, or Jarasandha would have attacked Vrndavana. My son should not suffer such anxiety. He is only a young boy. All His life He simply drank the milk His mother gave Him. Why should He have to undergo such tribulations? ”

Uddhava tried to tell Nanda Maharaja that Krsna was not an ordinary boy but was extraordinarily intelligent. But Nanda could not understand what he was talking about. He said, “Yes, my son is extraordinary. I understand that. But He is my son. When will He come back? He told us He would come back. Does He not know that we are burning in a fire of separation? Does He not know how much we adore His beautiful face, His beautiful smile, and His beautiful eyes? We always think of His attractiveness. Our lives have become empty since Krsna left. This house is now a prison, because everywhere I look I see only Krsna. Everything reminds me of Him. When I leave the house so that I can forget about Him, I see Govardhana and remember how Krsna used to play on Govardhana Hill. I see His footprints on the banks of the Yamuna where He sported with His friends. We are burning—dying—in separation from Him. When will He return? Even in ordinary family affairs, when someone is dying, it is the general etiquette that his relatives come and see him. We are dying and still He does not come! ”

Nanda Maharaja experienced such intense feelings of separation from Krsna, and Uddhava witnessed them all. On his way to Vrndavana he had been wondering how he would console Nanda Maharaja. He knew that when an ordinary person cries over the loss of a son, it is easy to offer consolation; you can tell him that he is not the body, he is eternal. You can tell him he is part and parcel of Krsna, and that there is no reason to lament. The *sastra* is full of things to say to someone who has lost a son. But what would Uddhava say to Nanda Maharaja, whose son is Krsna? Uddhava thought, “I know *sastra,* and *sastra* says this is the goal of life—to cry and cry for Krsna. What can I possibly tell Nanda Maharaja? ‘Don’t cry for Krsna’? That would be *asastra,* against *sastra.* I can tell him only to cry more and more. ”

Then Uddhava thought, “Some things simply cannot be revealed by *sastra. ”* The pole star cannot be seen when the sun is present in the sky; one has to wait for darkness to see the pole star's light. *Sastra* is the light of knowledge and Uddhava is *sastra-jna,* the knower of *sastra.* Normally, the transcendental light of knowledge dispels all ignorance and illusion, but all the *sastra’s* light cannot dispel what Nanda Maharaja feels. There are some things that can only be seen in the darkness. Darkness for the Vrajavasis is their forgetfulness of Krsna’s godhood. The Vrajavasis’ love is never crippled by the idea that Krsna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. They are not *jnana-misra-bhaktas* but *jnana-sunya-bhaktas*—free from *jnana.*

Uddhava tried to explain all of this to Nanda Maharaja: “In one sense, Krsna is your son, but you only see things that way because of your *vatsalya-priti.* Because you think of Krsna as your son, He reciprocates and treats you like His father. So yes, in that sense Krsna is your son, but in another sense Krsna is the source of everything. He is the source of the entire material and spiritual worlds. ” Uddhava then described the glories of Krsna’s position: “You are fortunate, my dear Nanda Maharaja. Don’t you know how fortunate you are to have Krsna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, as your son? ”

When Nanda Maharaja heard Uddhava’s words, he said, “How can you say such things to me? I thought you were intelligent, but you are obviously just a young boy. How can you call us fortunate? We are the most unfortunate! And you confirm this misfortune by telling me about Krsna’s uniqueness. Even if Krsna were the Supreme Personality of Godhead, no one has a smile like His. No one does the wonderful things our Krsna does. Krsna killed so many demons who attacked Vrndavana. Krsna did so many wonderful things! How can you say that we are fortunate when He is not here? He is the most wonderful son and He is not here! We are therefore *most* unfortunate! ”

And looking at Nanda Maharaja, Uddhava had to wonder, “What is this deep love? What am I saying to him? ”

Nanda Maharaja added, “Uddhava, are you trying to tell me that my son is Narayana? I have not read any of the scriptures you have read—I don’t know them—but I associate with *mahatmas* and these *mahatmas* have told me who Narayana is. They have told me that Narayana is the shelter of the entire universe and that everyone depends on Him. Are you saying my Krsna is Narayana? ”

“What are you saying? You have no knowledge. The *mahatmas* have called Narayana the most worshipable object in the world. But do you know what my son Krsna, does? He carries my shoes on His head! He is Narayana? O Uddhava! I have heard from these *mahatmas* that Narayana is a*pta-kama,* fully self-satisfied. Do you know what my son Krsna does all the time? He steals butter! *This* is self-satisfied? Not only does He steal our butter, but He goes to everyone else’s house and steals their butter too! ”

“They tell me that Narayana is pure and sinless, but my son is a thief! And not only that, He lies! He says, ‘O mother and father, I don’t know what they are talking about. They are telling so many lies about Me. Please believe Me. ’ My son tells lies! And you are telling me He is Narayana? O Uddhava, what do you know? ”

“Narayana is the cause of all causes, but my son is only a small child. You may say that Narayana is pure, peaceful, and sinless, but my son is fickle-minded. Sometimes He becomes angry. He is also greedy. He is always stealing butter. How can you say my son is Narayana? It makes no sense. My son *cannot* be Narayana. Narayana is the shelter of the entire world; that is what “Narayana” means. Everything rests on Him. But Krsna depends on *me.* He depends on His mother. When He has difficulty, He comes running to His mother in tears, and when He is hungry He says, ‘I want something to eat! ’ Is that self-satisfied? He wants something and when He doesn’t get it, He becomes angry.

“So I know some things about this Narayana. You say He is worshipable. Now tell me, how is the most worshipable object carrying my wooden slippers on His head? I will tell you one thing, Uddhava. I know that Krsna is my boy. I also know that we are burning in the fire of separation from Him. ”

This is *kevala-bhakti.* This love contains no *jnana*—no mixture of the knowledge of God’s greatness with the parental affection. Nanda Maharaja’s love for Krsna is so condensed that nothing can penetrate or dilute it.

Arjuna also has deep love for Krsna, but in the *sakhya-rasa,* the friendly relationship with Krsna. But when Arjuna saw the universal form, he asked Krsna to forgive him for their intimacies. Arjuna had sat on the same couch with Krsna and had eaten from the same plate. Sometimes he had vaunted himself and denigrated Krsna’s prowess. Seeing the universal form made him fearful. This means that unlike Nanda Maharaja, Arjuna’s attachment to Krsna was penetrated; it was diluted by his knowledge of Krsna’s greatness.

When Vasudeva and Devaki saw that Krsna had killed Kamsa and that He had then come over to offer His obeisances to them, they stepped back and asked, “How can we accept Your obeisances? ” They understood His greatness, and Their love for Him as their son was shrunken by that understanding. In Vrndavana the devotees have a different conception, the conception of *mamata-buddhi,* the sense that “Krsna belongs to me. ” Nanda Maharaja thought Krsna was his son. No *jnana* could make him think otherwise. Although Uddhava tried to present *jnana* in order to somehow alleviate Nanda Maharaja’s separation, ” Nanda Maharaja’s *prema* could not be penetrated. Therefore Nanda Maharaja’s *kevala-bhakti* purchased Krsna completely.

Uddhava was astonished by the intense love of Nanda Maharaja, Mother Yasoda, the *gopas,* and especially the *gopis,* and his appreciation of that love purified him.

Uddhava stayed for some time in Vrndavana, and the more steeped he became in the Vrajavasis’ love for Krsna, the more he prayed that in his next life he could somehow take birth even as a blade of grass in Vrndavana so that the *gopis* would walk over him every day and he would receive the dust from their lotus feet on his head. He felt his own love for Krsna, but realized he did not possess the kind of love experienced by the Vrajavasis. He realized that the Vrajavasis had love untinged by karma or *jnana;* they were engaged in unalloyed, *kevala-bhakti.*

Srila Prabhupada explains:

Because the Lord is the property of the devotees and the devotees are the property of the Lord reciprocally, the devotees are certainly transcendental to the modes of material nature. That is the natural conclusion. *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 63)

Such is the power of pure love. Prabhupada adds that we should not think we are incapable of attaining the same destination.

Each of us has an eternal and confidential relationship with Krsna. Prabhupada writes that we should not think we cannot have the kind of loving relationship Mother Yasoda has with Krsna. Every one can share such intimacy with Him. We simply have to make Him the object of our service and try to satisfy Him in all respects. This does not mean that we can *become* Mother Yasoda, Nanda Maharaja, or any of the other Vrajavasis; Nanda Maharaja himself is already filling the post of Krsna’s father. But we can serve Krsna in a similar fatherly way if that is our desire. We can serve Krsna as a friend and frolic in the forest with Him. We can run up behind Krsna, cover His eyes with our hands, and say, “He Krsna, guess who this is! ” The cowherd boys try to touch Krsna first, competing with one another, and they play so many games while pasturing the cows.

And when we are hungry, we can tell Krsna we are hungry. The cowherd boys sometimes tell Krsna they are hungry, and Krsna makes all arrangements to satisfy them. But these boys are not submitting their request to Krsna so that He will serve them; rather, they know that Krsna loves to feed His devotees, and to give Him pleasure, they ask Him to satisfy them. There is no other goal in the spiritual world other than to give Krsna pleasure.

Similarly, The *gopis too* are always absorbed in love for Krsna. They experience *purva-raga,* or the intense anticipation before a first meeting. Every time they see Krsna, they experience it as if it is the first time they have seen Him. Such is the love in the spiritual world. It never becomes dry or old.

The young cowherd girls hear about Krsna and His wonderful activities from everyone else—how He protects His friends in the pasturing grounds, how He plays His flute and attracts everyone by the sound of His flute. Krsna’s features are most attractive to the *gopis* during His *kaisora-lila,* most attractive to His parents during His *kaumara-lila,* and most attractive to His friends during His *pauganda-lila.*

Krsna’s *kaumara-lila* takes place when Krsna is between the ages of birth and five years old. He has particular physical features during this time, and performs certain activities that are most attractive at that age, all of which stimulate *vatsalya-priti.* Sometimes when He is very young, He speaks to Mother Yasoda in broken words. His childish activities of stealing butter, etc., stimulate her love.

For example, one time Krsna was in the house stealing butter. He looked at the pillar next to Him, which was embedded with valuable jewels. When He put his hand into the butter and then turned to the pillar, He saw His reflection. He was frightened and began talking to the reflection: “O brother, please don’t tell My mother what I am doing. If You don’t tell her, I will give You butter and You too can enjoy it. ”

Krsna did not know it, but Mother Yasoda was watching Him from a distance. She smiled to see Him talking to His own reflection, and so approached Him from behind. Krsna saw her reflected in the pillar and immediately said, “Mother, this boy came and tried to steal butter, but I told Him, ‘You shouldn’t steal butter! ’ He became angry with Me and I became angry with Him. I am not greedy for the butter, Mother. ” Such childish behavior is so captivating.

Another time Mother Yasoda was outside the house and Krsna had His hand in the butter pot. After some time, Mother Yasoda decided to come look for Krsna. She called out, “Krsna, where are you? Krsna? Krsna? ”

When Krsna heard His mother’s voice, He pulled His hand out of the butter. When Mother Yasoda came closer, Krsna said, “O mother, there is a bright effulgence coming from the ruby bangles on My arms. This effulgence began to burn My hand, so I put My hand into the butter to relieve the pain. ”

At that, Mother Yasoda took Krsna onto her lap and said, “Let me see Your hand. ” She said, “Yes, it’s true. Your hand is burned. Let me take off these ruby bangles and allow Your hand to cool down and so that these bangles will not burn You again. ” Such are Krsna’s childish activities.

Another time, all the elderly *gopis* were sitting and talking among themselves. One *gopi* said, “Wherever Krsna goes, it immediately brings fortune, and simply by His presence our lives become fully glorious. Who would not be enchanted by the mischievous activities of this young child? ”

The *gopis* spoke like this because they were not at all disturbed by Krsna stealing butter from their homes. Rather, they were captivated by His activities. Still, one day, pretending to be angry, and with a mixture of love and laughter, they came to mother Yasoda and reported, “O Vrajesvari Yasoda, you are going to suffer in the future because of your mischievous son’s activities. You don’t understand it now, because your son is still young. He is like a fresh sprout with soft leaves. But soon He will grow into a big tree with branches, twigs, and a trunk, and judging by all the mischievous activities He performs now, in the future He may destroy this entire village. Then you will really suffer from His activities. What will He do next? Don’t you know that sometimes He comes to our houses early in the morning before the cows have been milked and He lets the calves loose, and that they drink all the milk? And when we rebuke Him He smiles sweetly. Anyone who sees that sweet smile loses their power to rebuke Him. They are so captivated by Him that they no longer feel anger. ”

“And He has a natural effulgence. We try to hide our butter in the dark so He will not find it, but because of His natural effulgence He seems to find it anyway. He reaches up and takes the butter, and if we see Him He immediately drops it and escapes. And if by chance one of us captures Him, He takes her arm, twists it, gets free, and runs away. As He is running, He turns around and says, ‘Stop! Stay right where you are! Don’t come near Me! You think I’m causing trouble now, but just wait and see what I can do. I will tease your children and make them cry. ’”

“And if we say, ‘Stop, thief, ’ Krsna turns and says, ‘Thief? Are You calling Me a thief? You are the thieves! Don’t you know that these houses and everything they contain belong to Me? And you are calling Me a thief? ”’

“In the morning, we take fresh clay and put a layer of it on the house. After putting on this fresh clay, we take white limestone powder and decorate the sides of the house. Then your son comes and throws dirt all over our walls. Look at Him right now. He looks so innocent as He stands before you. You look at Him and think you have an innocent boy! But when He is at *our* house, He speaks sharp words, steals our food, and is very greedy. ”

When Krsna heard these accusations, He feigned fear and looked innocent and began to cry. “I am completely innocent, Mother. These women are lying. Actually, these women do not have any affection in their hearts. They are lying about Me. I’m telling the truth. The reason why I go to their homes is because I have such natural affection for them. Simply by seeing them and their sons, I feel happiness. By My natural affection I go to their homes every morning at sunrise. Therefore, Mother, now you know My motives.

You should know that I am telling the truth. Do not believe them, Mother. But you are also My worshipable superior, and therefore, if they are complaining like this, I will not go to their houses any more. ”

Mother Yasoda took her son on her lap, and smiling shyly, looked at the *gopis.* She then spoke in a way simply to please her son: “All of you *gopis* are liars. How could my son have done any of these things? My son is telling the truth. I think you have reprimanded Him enough. ” She smiled and looked at Krsna, and Krsna smiled back.

Hearing Mother Yasoda’s tone of voice, the elderly *gopis* realized that she was speaking for Krsna’s pleasure. Rohini then took *tilaka* and marked their foreheads. Then the *gopis* left.

Mother Yasoda took Krsna on her lap and said, “Please sit down. Krsna, because You are greedy, You are disturbing all these *gopis.* This is not proper; this is not acceptable behavior. You may be naughty in our house because everything here belongs to You, but it is not acceptable for You to steal from other houses. I think You should stay home. Don’t go anywhere. Just stay here and play in Your own house. ”

Just as she finished speaking, Nanda Maharaja came into the room and Krsna ran over to him and sat on his lap. Wrapping His arms around Nanda Maharaja’s neck, Krsna said, “O father, why is mother reprimanding Me for no reason? ”

Nanda Maharaja asked, “What are You talking about? What is this all this about? ”

Mother Yasoda then explained the *gopis’* complaints and all about Krsna’s mischievous activities. Nanda Maharaja said, “What?

Krsna? I have never seen my son do any of these things. How can you take sides with these women against your own son? They are simply envious of my son. He could never perform such activities. If you take sides with these *gopis, you* should be punished. ” Like Yasoda-ma, Nanda Maharaja was speaking for Krsna’s pleasure, concealing his real thoughts. Then he added, “Krsna, You should simply sit on my lap and don’t go to anyone else’s lap. Just sit here. ”

Then, Krsna, being an unpredictable child, immediately left His father’s lap and went over to His mother. Then both parents laughed and laughed, enjoying the activities of their young, mischievous son.

By these activities, Krsna captivates His parents’ hearts. His stealing captivates the hearts of the *gopis;* they are never really angry but think themselves fortunate to have wonderful Krsna stealing butter from their houses. Actually, they make the butter only so He will come and steal it. In this way Krsna steals away His devotees’ minds, and they become so captivated by His activities that they are absorbed only in thoughts of Him.

Another time, Krsna began pointing to the moon in the sky and said, “I want the condensed butter. I want to have that. ” But the *gopis* told Him, “Krsna, what do You want? Why are You crying? Maybe we can give You some nice, thick yogurt? Maybe we can give You some cream? Maybe we can give You butter? ”

But Krsna continued to cry. “No, I want the condensed butter up there. Please! I want that nice condensed butter. Please give it to Me! Give it to Me! ”

Krsna then went to Mother Yasoda and sat on her lap, trying to get her attention. He pulled her *sari* off of her head, and when that didn’t work, He took her hair and began to unbraid it. When she still didn’t give Him what he wanted, He began to pat her on the back with His small lotus hands and cry, “Mother! Mother! Mother! Please! ”

At that moment Mother Yasoda was filled with such intense *vatsalya-priti* that she could not respond to Krsna’s attempts to get her attention. So one *gopi* told Krsna, “That is not condensed butter. Actually it is *rajahamsa,* the king of swans, gliding across the lake of the sky. ”

This did not pacify Krsna. He said, “Then I want it. I want that *rajahamsa.* Quick! Very soon it will fall into the lake and we won’t be able to get it out! Give it to Me! Give it to Me! ”

Another *gopi* said, “No, Krsna, it’s not actually *rajahamsa* but candra, the moon. ”

Again Krsna was not pacified: “Then I want the moon. Give Me *Candra. "*He was crying and crying: “I want to play with c*andra*! ”

Finally, Mother Yasoda pulled Krsna onto her lap and said, “Krsna, it really is condensed butter. You see all those black spots? They are poison. By the arrangement of providence, this condensed butter is poisonous and no one can eat it. ”

Krsna then asked, “How did the poison get there? Why is there poison? I don’t know anything about poison. ”

Mother Yasoda replied, “There is a big ocean of milk someplace and the condensed butter came from there. ”

“Mother, how many cows did it take to make such an ocean of milk? ”

“No, this milk does not come from cows. ”

“Mother, you must be lying! How can milk not come from cows? ” “Actually, just as someone else made the arrangement for milk to come from cows, so that same person made an arrangement for milk *not* to come from cows. ”

“Really? Are you telling Me the truth? Who is He? ”

“He is Bhagavan, and Bhagavan is everywhere. Bhagavan is all-prevading. But although He is everywhere, unfortunately I cannot show Him to You. ”

“Are you telling Me the truth, Mother? ”

“Yes, my son. Once by the Lord’s arrangement, the demons and the demigods churned the ocean of milk. Mandara Mountain was the churning rod and Vasuki was the rope for churning. The demigods and demons fought each other as they churned the milk. While churning this ocean of milk, poison came out of it. ”

“Poison? Coming from an ocean of milk? I thought poison came from serpents. ”

“Yes, it’s true. Lord Mahadeva took this poison into His mouth and began to drink it. The drops that fell from His mouth were drunk by the serpents. Now serpents keep poison inside themselves. ” “But still, it is true that the ocean of milk is the source of the condensed butter You now see in the sky. And all those spots are poison. Therefore no one can eat that condensed butter. ”

As Mother Yasoda was telling this long story to Krsna, He became tired. He then closed His eyes and fell asleep. Mother Yasoda carried her son to His beautiful bed.

The following morning when Mother Yasoda went to wake Him, she brought a golden plate filled with cream, yogurt, butter, and all kinds of delightful sweets. She woke her son, helped Him rinse His mouth with scented water, and then said, “Krsna, You must be so hungry. You fell asleep last night. You were tired because You had not eaten enough. Here, I have brought You a big plate of butter, cream, and sweets. Just take whatever You want. ”

Krsna said, “Mother, I cannot take anything, because last night You were lying to Me and You put Me to sleep even though I was so distressed by hunger. ”

Mother Yasoda said, “Krsna, if You were sleeping, then who stole the butter? ”

“Mother, you are telling lies again! When can I have stolen the butter? I was asleep! ”

But Krsna had actually stolen the butter. In this way, Mother Yasoda again enjoyed wonderful pastimes with Krsna. These pastimes are very sweet.

The *acaryas* describe that such sweet pastimes only take place in Bhauma Vrndavana, in this world. In Goloka Vrndavana Krsna has no babyhood pastimes. Therefore, there is an extra sweetness available in Bhauma Vrndavana, and because of this, the eternal devotees from Goloka descend into this world simply to taste it.

When a devotee becomes captivated by hearing these descriptions of the Lord—and hears how captivated the devotees are and how deeply attached they are to the Supreme Lord—their hearts too become attracted to Krsna. We too can give our hearts if we awaken faith by hearing the Supreme Lord’s pastimes.

## Krsna, the Greatest Thief

*isavasyam idam sarvam*  
*yat kinca jagatyam jagat*  
*tena tyaktena bhunjitha*  
*ma grdhah kasya svid dhanam*

Everything animate or inanimate that is within the universe is controlled and owned by the Lord. One should therefore accept only those things necessary for himself, which are set aside as his quota, and one should not accept other things, knowing well to whom they belong. ” (*Sri Isopanisad* 1)

Since everything animate and inanimate belongs to the Lord, those who take what does not belong to them are thieves. Srila Prabhupada explains that we have a propensity to steal because Krsna also steals. But when Krsna steals, His stealing is wonderful and never immoral. When we steal, however, it is abominable and something no moral person should do. Whether we steal from someone else or from the Lord, theft is theft.

Prabhupada explains in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* that when we offer everything to the Lord, then take only what we need, then whatever we receive is *prasadam.* We do not claim possession of anything for ourselves but simply take whatever we need—and is given by the Lord’s grace—to serve Him. As long as we inhabit a material body, we must take something to keep body and soul together, so the Supreme Personality of Godhead provides for us and all other species of life.

The *Bhagavad-gita* describes that all our necessities can be obtained through the performance of sacrifice. Krsna says (Bg. 3. 9), “Work done as a sacrifice for Visnu has to be performed; otherwise work causes bondage in this material world. Therefore, O son of Kunti, perform your prescribed duties for His satisfaction, and in that way you will always remain free from bondage. ” The supreme sacrifice is to act for the the Lord’s satisfaction.
