One who acts to satisfy the Lord can accept whatever is necessary to keep body and soul together and will not be considered a thief. But those who are materially attached and who accept more than required are considered thieves who deserve to be punished by nature’s laws. The devotees should therefore feel materially impoverished rather than materially attached to opulence.

Queen Kunti prays *(Bhag* 1. 8. 26): “My Lord, Your Lordship can easily be approached, but only by those who are materially exhausted. One who is on the path of \[material\] progress, trying to improve himself with respectable parentage, great opulence, high education and bodily beauty, cannot approach You with sincere feeling. ”

Only those who are materially exhausted can chant Krsna’s names with feeling. Those who are absorbed in trying to improve their material situation cannot chant feelingly because they take shelter of Krsna’s external energy. The Bhagavatam (10. 88. 8) states:

*sri-bhagavan uvaca*  
*yasyaham anugrhnami*  
*harisye tad-dhanam sanaih*  
*tato ’dhanam tyajanty asya*  
*svajana duhkha-duhkhitam*

“The Personality of Godhead said: ‘If I especially favor someone, I gradually deprive him of his wealth. Then the relatives and friends of such a poverty-stricken man abandon him. In this way he suffers one distress after another. ’”

Krsna is also known as Hari, “one who steals or takes away. ” When Krsna is mercifully disposed toward His devotees, He takes everything from them and leaves them with nothing. But the devotees understand that Krsna has put them into a situation by which all their material attachments are taken away.

Bali Maharaja is one such example of a devotee from whom Krsna took everything. Yet even though all his worldly possessions were lost, Bali was so devoted that he offered one more thing: himself.

The Lord is present in everyone’s heart and therefore knows everything about them. When He sees that we still want material enjoyment, He provides us with another material body. The Supersoul works cooperatively with Yamaraja, who makes arrangements to give the living entities their just rewards and punishments and then fashions their next body. To help us understand that material gain can never make us happy, Krsna allows us to try out one body after another and to satisfy all our unfulfilled desires. But even with this arrangement we lose everything at the time of death. The only thing Krsna allows us to keep are our pious and sinful reactions. The living entities therefore go forward, carrying one conception of themselves or another, from one body to the next, just as air carries aromas on the wind. Our particular definitions of enjoyment remain in the mind even after death, and we travel throughout the universe trying to taste this enjoyment until we become sufficiently frustrated and turn to Krsna.

Of course, sometimes Krsna very kindly takes everything away for us. Lord Caitanya, quoting Krsna, states (Cc. *Madhya* 22. 38-39), “If one engages in My transcendental loving service but at the same time wants the opulence of material enjoyment, he is very, very foolish. Indeed, he is just like a person who gives up ambrosia to drink poison. Since I am very intelligent, why should I give this fool material prosperity? Instead I shall induce him to take the nectar of the shelter of My lotus feet and make him forget illusory material enjoyment. ”

But more often something else happens. When we engage in devotional service and simultaneously want to fulfill our material desires, the Supreme Lord satisfies our desires in such a way that we will not ask for them again. That too is the Lord’s mercy. Not only does He remove our material desires, but He removes our attachment to the types of enjoyment they represent. This is why He is called Hari; He is the thief who steals our minds and hearts away from matter and places them firmly at His lotus feet.

Is this always true? Krsna may not actually steal us away if we do not try to give ourselves to Him first. Krsna does not have to drive us to material frustration; the material nature is quite capable of doing that itself. When people lose things in this world, they lose it through the action of the material nature; material loss and gain are simply the results of past karma. People should understand this and not blame Krsna for taking anything away. Krsna steals only from His devotees; He personally takes nothing from the nondevotees.

For those who are devotees and want to take shelter of Krsna’s lotus feet, Krsna sometimes takes things away out of His great mercy to that devotee. Krsna knows that the devotees want Him more than anything else, so He makes arrangements to help them remember Him. If we want such mercy in our lives, we should simply try to give ourselves, life and heart, to Krsna.

And in the Eighth Canto, Srila Prabhupada adds another dimension: Krsna is sometimes merciful toward His devotees and takes everything away. But sometimes Krsna gives extra mercy to His devotees and allows them to keep everything, but He protects them from becoming proud.

Krsna describes that those who become falsely proud cannot enjoy in the material world without paying the price of bad karma. Better we offer what we have to be used in the Lord’s service. Otherwise, we will simply be punished for stealing in our next life.

We may think it is not so detrimental to our progress to accumulate more karma. There is a sastric story that tells of a sage’s blessings on four persons: a prince, a *brahmacari,* a *sadhu,* and a butcher. The sage tells the prince, “May you live a long life. ” To the brahmacari he says, “May you die now. ” To the *sadhu:* “O saintly person, whether you live or die makes no difference. ” And to the butcher he says, “Don’t live and don’t die. ”

Srila Prabhupada explains the purports of these blessings: the prince may live a long life because he is enjoying the results of his past pious activities. If he dies, he will then suffer his past sinful activities along with any sinful reactions he has accumulated in this life.

The *brahmacari* may die now because his life is simple and free of material anxiety. Later, however, he will find that things will become difficult. Before he gets entangled in life’s complexities, better he die.

To the saint it makes no difference whether he lives or dies. He is detached from matter. Therefore, while living he will not accumulate any more sinful reactions, and in dying he will have no suffering waiting for him in his next life. Thus he will go back to Godhead.

The butcher shouldn’t live or die because he is both causing suffering to other living entities and suffering himself, and when he dies he will continue to suffer horrible reactions to his murderous deeds.

There are examples of exalted Vaisnavas living in opulence without accumulating more karma or acting on selfish desires. Maharaja Prataparudra, for example, was both a king and an exalted Vaisnava. Pundarika Vidyanidhi also lived in opulence. But although externally he looked like a materialist, attached to worldly things, he had given his heart to the Supreme Lord and was dear to Lord Caitanya. Lord Caitanya used to call his name lovingly: “O my father, Pundarika. ”

Thus sometimes the Lord allowed devotees to live in opulence if they could remain *akincana.* But for those of us who do have the tendency to become materialists, Krsna helps us by gradually removing our possessions, leaving us nothing but Himself.

Prabhupada told us in a lecture he gave on his guru’s disappearance day that Krsna had dealt with him like this. He said that his Guru Maharaja had been so merciful to him that he instructed him to preach. Prabhupada said:

So he desired me. I thought, ‘Now I am a family man. Let me adjust things.... My responsibility to family is there. Let me wait. ’ But still, Guru Maharaja was so kind to me that when I was *grhastha,* I was seeing him in dreaming and... he asked me, ‘You come with me. ’ I was going, and after that, I was thinking, ‘Oh,

I will have to take *sannyasa* and go with him? ’ So it appeared to me very horrible. I was not very much inclined to take *sannyasa,* but Guru Maharaja is so kind that he ultimately forced me to take *sannyasa* and do this work. So it is all his kindness. (Lecture, Hyderabad, December 10, 1976)

As the years went on in his *grhastha* life, Srila Prabhupada explained that he could see Krsna gradually taking everything away. He had his business, but it suddenly began to fail. Krsna simply took everything away so he could answer his Guru Maharaja’s call. In an earlier lecture, Prabhupada said that Krsna had even taken away his children, but now he had “thousands of children. ”

Prabhupada told us that when Krsna takes away our material possessions, we should never think we have lost anything. And if we offer everything to Krsna, we are never the loser: “\[We\] are gainer, the best gainer. Because after all, everything belongs to Krsna. Why should we foolishly say, ‘It is mine, it is mine? ”’ (Lecture, Los Angeles, September 24, 1972) We are so fortunate when Krsna takes away our material possessions and attachments. Therefore we glorify Krsna as a thief. Just as the sun can extract water from both a clean place and a sewer and purify the place without itself becoming contaminated, so Krsna can steal away our dirty material desires without ever becoming contaminated by the stealing. If He steals, He must have a purpose, which is always for our benefit; it is to show us His love.

Even when Krsna steals butter from the *gopis,* they never think of Him as being contaminated by the act; they are never disturbed by His thievery, but rather feel intense happiness to see it. Krsna attracts their minds by His mischievous stealing.

In the *Damodarastakam,* Satyavrata Muni prays, “Although You are able to give all kinds of liberation, I do not pray for the liberation of the monists (impersonal liberation) or even for the highest form of liberation, eternal life in Vaikuntha. I simply pray that this form as Bala-gopala will ever be manifest in my heart. What is the use of any other benediction besides this for me? ” Such is the wealth desired and obtained by the Lord’s devotees.

Queen Kunti understands this point and prays, “My obeisances are unto You, who are the property of the materially impoverished. You have nothing to do with the actions and reactions of the material modes of nature. You are self satisfied, and therefore You are the most gentle and are master of the monists. ” *(Bhag.* 1. 8. 27) Here is a nice poem about the Supreme thief, Krsna:

I offer my obeisances to that supreme thief, Lord Hari, who is the best of all thieves. He steals the butter from the houses of the damsels of Vrajabhumi. He steals all the garments of the damsels of Vrajabhumi. He is famous for His acts of stealing. He steals all the sinful reactions of His devotees. Such a great thief! I offer my obeisances to that supreme thief.

He steals the heart of Radharani. He steals the blackish hue of the dark cloud. He steals everything. For those who take shelter at His lotus feet, He steals everything they have. He takes all of their possessions, their material assets, money, and men. He even steals their mind. I offer my obeisances to that great thief, supreme thief.

He takes away everything from those who take shelter at His lotus feet. He takes away their material wealth, their wife, their children, and their kith and kin. He takes everything. Then those persons become paupers, like beggars on the street with no house, no hut, nothing. Such a great thief, who steals away everything. I have not seen or heard of such a great thief, supreme thief, in all the three worlds. I offer my obeisances to that supreme thief.

Such a great thief! If one only hears His name He takes away all their sinful reactions. I have never seen or heard of such a wonderful thief! I pay my obeisances to that supreme thief.

O great thief! Stealing all of our assets, bank balance, money, land, property, prestige, fame, senses, mind, heart, and everything, You are running away! Where are You running? I will catch hold of You! You are such a great thief, but now You are caught! I will bind You with this rope of love! Where will you go now, great thief, running away and taking everything? You are bound up with this very strong rope of love! You cannot run away now!

You may cut off the rope of Yamaraja; You may cut off this material bondage. But You cannot cut this bondage of love. Now You are in bondage. I have bound You up!

O great thief Lord Hari! I am putting You in the very dark prison house of my heart! This is the right prison for You! I am put-ting You here forever! This is the proper punishment for Your act of stealing! Stay forever in this prison house of my heart! I’ll never release You!

Now I have bound You with the rope of love and put You in the prison house of my heart! Stay there, stay there, stay there! You cannot get Yourself free from this very tight bondage! O Krishna! You have stolen everything from me—my material assets, my name, my fame, my beauty, my reputation, my kith and kin, my family members, my heart, and mind! This is the proper punishment for You, to remain in this prison house of my heart, bound up very tightly with the rope of love, forever and forever! If crores of *pralayas* come, still I won’t release You! This is suitable punishment for such a great thief as You! ” (Sri *Cauragraganya-purusastakam,* cited with an expanded translation in *Mathura Meets Vrindavan,* Gour Govinda Swami, p. 83-84)

## Krsna’s Contradictions

Krsna consciousness appears to be filled with contradictions that can only be reconciled by Krsna consciousness itself. For example, Krsna is worshiped by hundreds of thousands of goddesses of fortune, but still He is a thief who steals butter as if He were in need. All the goddesses of fortune will give anything and everything, yet Krsna steals their butter. Krsna says in *Bhagavad-gita* (4-6):

*ajo ’pi sann avyayatma*  
*bhutanam isvaro ’pi san*  
*prakrtim svam adhisthaya*  
*sambhavamy atma-mayaya*

“Although I am unborn and My transcendental body never deteriorates, and although I am the Lord of all living entities, I still appear in every millennium in My original transcendental form. ”

Krsna, the Supreme Truth, whose every word is absolute truth, says, “I am unborn. ” Yet He appears as Mother Yasoda’s son. Is this a contradiction? So many things appear to be contradictory for those who see things separately from Krsna. But for those who see everything in relationship to Him, all contradictions are reconciled.

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura explains that all contradictions are reconciled when we understand Krsna’s most powerful quality. That quality is an all-conquering king. It is the quality of His being *bhakta-vatsala,* the lover of His devotees. Krsna reciprocates the love of His devotees. This quality conquers all other qualities and reconciles all contradictions. Prabhupada says that it is difficult to know Krsna in full, but we need to know just enough about Him to love Him. Understanding His quality as *bhakta-vatsala* will inspire our love.

Sometimes Krsna bewilders His devotees and this appears to create contradictions in the minds of less advanced devotees. For example, Uddhava was bewildered by his own *aisvarya-jnana.* He knew Krsna’s position and saw His majestic feature as the Supreme Lord, but Krsna once approached Uddhava and asked for his opinion. Uddhava said, “This is bewildering to me. Why have You approached me? You are all-knowing. Why do you need my opinion? ” While Uddhava experienced this bewilderment in his great love for the Lord, we may truly not understand it. But the contradiction of Krsna being both the all-knowing Supreme Lord and asking Uddhava for his opinion is reconciled when we understand that Krsna loves to exchange with His devotees in His *nara-lila.*

In another pastime, Krsna was sitting on Mother Yasoda’s lap drinking her breast milk. At that moment, a pot of milk on the stove began to overflow. Mother Yasoda put Krsna down to stir the milk. Krsna became angry at His mother for having abandoned Him to tend to the milk, and while His mother was in the kitchen, Krsna took a stone and broke a pot of butter. Then fearfully but playfully He spread the butter all over the floor. Then He walked through the butter, getting it on the bottoms of His feet. As He walked through the rooms in the house, He left greasy footprints everywhere. Finally, He went into the storage room where the rest of the butter and milk were kept and took a little butter from each pot and ate it. After He had eaten so much butter, His anger subsided, but not His playfulness.

Then He picked up one pot of butter and ran out of the house, anxiously looked around, and said, “Is My mother here yet? ” Still He did not see her. So He went and stood on top of a grinding mortar and invited the monkeys and some of the young boys, distributing the butter to them from the pot. Just then, Mother Yasoda finished settling the pot on the stove and came out to console Krsna, who had begun to cry when she had left. But where was Krsna? She felt her anxiety mounting. She looked for Him everywhere, and then saw the broken pots and the butter all over the floor. The floor was slippery with butter. Then Mother Yasoda saw the small rock Krsna had thrown into a pot. Although she had not at first suspected Him for breaking the pot, when she saw the rock she said, “Ah, this is the work of my little son. ”

She wondered, “How did my son become so impudent? I have tried to train Him properly, but He behaves in such a naughty way. ” She couldn’t understand it. Then displaying a unique type of motherly pride, she thought, “Actually, my son displays such wonderful pastimes. Somehow all His activities simply increase His pleasure and become a festival for all of us. ”

Whenever Mother Yasoda displays her anger before Krsna, it is always false anger. She can never be angry with her son. But when she saw the mess Krsna had created, her false anger manifested.

Searching, she found Krsna feeding butter to the monkeys and boys. When He saw His mother coming, Krsna jumped up and ran away. Mother Yasoda, who is expert in loving her son, and understanding His moods and activities, called out, “Krsna, Stop right where You are! You are the foremost cheater. Don’t You know that? ”

But Krsna continued to run away, looking over His shoulder to see if she was pursuing Him. He saw that she was and that she was carrying a stick. As she chased her son, Mother Yasoda became fatigued and also disturbed by Krsna’s naughty behavior. These combined to make her the perfect picture of beauty. Every limb was beautiful while she ran after Krsna. She called out, “Stop, thief and liar! Where do You think You are going? Why do You behave like this? Stop immediately. Don’t go anywhere! ”

Then suddenly Krsna stopped running, feeling Himself at a safe distance from His mother. He had enough time to say something to her, but there was not enough time for her to catch Him. He said, “I will stop if you put down that stick. Please put down the stick, mother. Don’t punish Me! ” Krsna had become afraid.

Mother Yasoda said, “If You are so afraid of being punished, why did You break the butter pot? ”

“Mother, I promise I will never do it again. Please just put down that stick. Please don’t punish Me! ” Because He could see that his mother was still angry, Krsna began to run again. Then He stopped again and said, “Mother, if you just put that stick down, I will come to you. ”

Understanding Krsna’s appeals, Mother Yasoda put down the stick and Krsna stopped. Mother Yasoda was then able to approach Him. And she wondered, “What can I do with this mischievous boy? ”

As this pastime unfolded, the most powerful denizens of heaven, including Brahma and Indra, were watching from a distance. They were completely bewildered. “How is it possible for the Supreme Lord to be running from His mother in fear? ” The demigods have *aisvarya-jnana;* they are quite aware that Krsna is the Supreme Lord. This pastime astonished and bewildered them. Similarly, Queen Kunti prays *(Bhag.* 1. 8. 31):

*gopy adade tvayi krtagasi dama tavad*  
*ya te dasasru-kalilanjana-sambhramaksam*  
*vaktram niniya bhaya-bhavanaya sthitasya*  
*sa mam vimohayati bhir api yad bibheti*

“My dear Krsna, Yasoda took up a rope to bind You when You committed an offense, and Your perturbed eyes overflooded with tears, which washed the mascara from Your eyes. And You were afraid, though fear personified is afraid of You. This sight is bewildering to me. ”

Although Queen Kunti has pure love for Krsna, her pure love is mixed with *aisvarya-jnana,* which means that when she sees something wonderful, she sees it as a manifestation of the Supreme Lord’s majestic feature. Srila Prabhupada writes *(Teachings of Queen Kunti,* p. 99) of Kuntidevi and her understanding:

Thinking of Krsna’s opulence, Kuntidevi did not dare take the part of Yasoda, for although Kuntidevi was Krsna’s aunt, she did not have the privilege to approach Krsna the way He was approached by Yasodamayi, who was such an advanced devotee that she had the right to chastise the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Yasoda’s *prema,* free from *aisvarya,* is *kevala.* Even if she sees something wonderful she never stops thinking of Krsna as her son. The mood of *aisvarya* never intrudes into the *prema* of the Vrajavasis even though Krsna performs many extraordinary activities in Vrndavana, such as killing Sakatasura when He is only three months old. No one in Vrndavana questions how a three-month-old child, simply by kicking His legs, can kill a demon or even knock over a cart. It is just as mystical that Krsna can steal butter from the elderly *gopis* without ever being seen. Yet it is sweet to see how He behaves like an ordinary, mischievous child. These wonderfully sweet pastimes in Goloka Vrndavana are all revealed through the pure chanting of the holy name.

When Queen Kunti thinks of Krsna crying in fear of His mother, she questions the apparent contradiction. She could have understood it more if Krsna had only been pretending to cry. However, Krsna was not pretending but was absorbed in His own *lila.* This was bewildering.

Another example of Krsna’s bewildering pastimes is given in the *Ananda Vrndavana Campu.* This story demonstrates the *aisvarya-bhava of* the demigods:

Playing in the dirt, Krsna built houses, a toy wall, or a small town. Other times He broke the dirt houses of His friends and they broke His. When Krsna rebuilt His house, they would break it again. While curiously observing these antics from the sky, the

demigods thought, Instead of bothering with that work, He now plays in the dirt making roads, homes, and villages. Although He is becoming exhausted from doing this, He does not give it up. ”

(Ananda *Vrndavana Campu,* p. 59)

All this play with His friends is exhausting for Krsna. When He comes home from His play of building little houses and roads in the sand, He is very tired and sometimes falls asleep in His mother’s lap. The demigods are bewildered; simply by His glance Krsna has created thousands of universes. They have come into existence and are later dissolved, but He is never tired by His work. Neither does He personally involve Himself in their construction but allows His energies to do the work. Universes come and go by Krsna’s will, but here He is, building little houses and roads in the sand and play-fighting with His friends.

The Svetasvatara Upanisad (6. 8) states:

*na tasya karyam karanam ca vidyate*  
*na tat-samas cabhyadhikas ca drsyate*

“He is the origin of material creation, and it is due to Him only that everything changes. He is the protector of religion and annihilator of all sinful activities. He is the master of all opulences. ”

There is nothing Krsna must do. No one is equal to or greater than Him. Everything is carried out naturally and systematically by His energies. Even creation, maintenance, and annihilation happen without His personal involvement. Therefore, there is nothing Krsna must do.

From Ananda Vrndavana Campu:

Krsna’s perplexing pastimes captivated the minds of the demigods. While Krsna delighted in the dirt, He looked like the sun shining in the sky.

The housewives in Vrndavana, full of motherly affection, addressed Krsna with sweet words, “O darling boy, please come to

our beautiful courtyards, play with our children, and take some food. ” Hearing this, Krsna smiled and replied softly, “I cannot come because I do not have any spare time. ” Thus the all-attractive beloved of every mother’s heart cleverly responded to the elderly *gopis.* Anxious to express their motherly affection towards Krsna, these impatient *gopis* held His hand and hurried to their homes.

In their eyes, fortune had blessed Krsna alone as the sole recipient of matchless beauty. Brimming with love, the elderly *gopis* served Krsna by rubbing His body and bathing Him. With great devotion they fed Him butter, *rabri,* and *ksira* and then sent Him home. (Ananda *Vrndavana Campu,* p. 59)

Krsna has two qualities that manifest simultaneously: He is *mugdhah* (bewildered) and *sarvajna* (all-knowing). It is inconceivable how He can be both bewildered and all-knowing at the same time. But Krsna is certainly all-knowing. How else could He reciprocate with devotees who pray to Him from all over the creation? At the same time He is bewildered by the love of His devotees, some of whom rebuke Him or behave in many ways that captivate His complete attention. Krsna is bewildered by His devotees’ love and at that time He forgets that He is God.

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura uses an analogy to describe this phenomenon: when a bee enters a lotus flower at night, it becomes covered by the lotus’s petals as they close over it. But there is so much nectar in the heart of the lotus flower that the bee does not mind; it simply remains inside the lotus flower throughout the night and relishes the sweetness. Similarly, Krsna enters the hearts of His devotees and becomes covered by their love. There is so much nectar there that He does not even notice that He has become covered. This is what it means when we say that Krsna is *mugdhah:* He is so bewildered by His devotees’ love that He forgets His godhood.

Krsna’s pastimes and potencies are difficult for the mundane mind to understand. We should know that if we actually want to understand the inconceivable, we must take shelter of the Supreme

Lord’s inconceivable potency. There are many ways to gather knowledge. Knowledge can then be divided into various stages, such as *pratyaksa, paroksa, aparoksa, adhoksaja,* and *aprakrta. Pratyaksa* means knowledge attained through direct sense perception. *Paroksa* means to hear from authority, even mundane authority. *Aparoksa* is knowledge of the impersonal Brahman. But *adhoksaja* is knowledge that has descended from the spiritual realm by divine grace and which reveals the transcendental realm. We cannot gather *adhoksaja* knowledge by our own endeavors or on demand. It is revealed only by the Lord’s grace.

But even beyond the *adhoksaja* stage of knowledge is *aprakrta*— which is actually *jnana-sunya-bhakti,* or devotion devoid of knowledge. This is the highest platform of knowledge and it too is bewildering—another contradiction. How can being devoid of knowledge be the highest knowledge? Yet this is the *kevala-bhakti* rendered by the residents of Vrndavana. The Vrajavasis cannot be convinced that Krsna is God. To know of Krsna’s majestic features would cripple their intense and very intimate love for Him, because love shrinks when it is mixed with knowledge of a person’s greatness; love’s spontaneity is lost.

Yet it is not that the Vraja-bhaktas do not possess knowledge of Krsna’s position as God. Sometimes in their ecstasies—when they are immersed in all the *sattvika-bhavas*—they may slightly allude to Krsna’s divine position. The *acaryas* compare this to a blade of grass in a pot of boiling milk. The rapidly boiling milk is compared to the Vraja-bhaktas’ ecstatic love and emotions, their experiences of *sambhoga* and *vipralambha* where all the *sattvika-bhavas* manifest. As their ecstasies boil, they feel stunned, their voices falter, tears come from their eyes, and their bodily color sometimes changes.

The grass in the milk is compared to their knowledge of Krsna’s supreme position. If you place a blade of grass in a pot of rapidly boiling milk, the grass will sometimes rise to the surface and at other times sink to the bottom with the action of the milk. Similarly, knowledge of Krsna’s position as God may sometimes boil up in the residents of Vrndavana, and then just as quickly be lost to view with the action of their ecstasies. They do not dwell or meditate on that knowledge, and often when they speak of it, they are repeating something they have heard from a *bhakta* with *aisvarya-jnana.*

For example, when Gargamuni visits Nanda Maharaja to perform Krsna’s name-giving ceremony, he tells Nanda that Krsna is divine. Nanda Maharaja repeated that statement at times, but it did not hinder his ecstatic love and he did not dwell on it. The knowledge occasionally surfaced and then became covered again. The Vraja-bhaktas are *jnana-sunya-bhaktas.* Their knowledge is *aprakrta*—so high that they do not even know they are the topmost living entities in the creation. All they know is Krsna, and all they know to do is to constantly try to satisfy and serve Him in spontaneous love.

This highest knowledge is the spontaneous ecstatic love Lord Caitanya came to deliver. Caitanya Mahaprabhu states (Cc. *Adi* 3. 19):

*yuga-dharma pravartaimu nama-sankirtana*  
*cari bhava-bhakti diya nacamu bhuvana*

“I shall make the world dance in ecstasy, realizing the four mellows of loving devotional service. ”

He mentions *cari bhava-bhakti,* four ecstasies, not *eka bhava-bhakti,* one ecstasy. In his purport to this verse Srila Prabhupada explains that the Lord does not mention five ecstasies because He did not consider santa-rasa in this case. *Cari bhava-bhakti* refers to *dasya-, sakhya-, vatsalya-,* and *madhurya-ratis.*

As Krsna, the Lord instructs the theory of man-mana *bhava mad-bhakto,* but as Caitanya Mahaprabhu, He teaches complete surrender by His own example. He explains in *Caitanya-caritamrta,* “My plenary portions can establish the principles of religion for each age. No one but Me, however, can bestow the kind of loving service performed by the residents of Vraja. ” (Cc. *Adi* 3. 26) He also says, “Unless one practices devotional service himself, he cannot teach it to others. ” He cites *Bhagavad-gita* (3. 21):

*yad yad acarati sresthas*  
*tat tad evetaro janah*  
*sa yat pramanam kurute*  
*lokas tad anuvartate*

“Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues. ” Caitanya Mahaprabhu said that if He did not teach devotional service by His own example, “all these worlds would fall into ruin. ” In *Teachings of Queen Kunti* (p. 164), Srila Prabhupada describes some of the pastimes that display the mood of spontaneous loving devotional service:

When we see everything as nothing, but have only Krsna consciousness, we shall have attained the highest perfection. Therefore the *gopis* are so exalted. Having attained this perfection, they could not forget Krsna even for a single moment. When Krsna went to the forest with His cows and calves, the minds of the *gopis* at home were disturbed. “Oh, Krsna is walking barefoot, ” they thought. “There are so many stones and nails on the path, and they must be pricking Krsna’s lotus feet, which are so soft that we think our breasts hard when Krsna puts His lotus feet upon them. ” Thus they would cry, absorbed in these thoughts. The *gopis* were so anxious to see Krsna back home in the evening that they would stand on the path, looking to see Krsna returning with His calves and cows. This is Krsna consciousness.

Srila Prabhupada also provides an analogy to help us understand how Krsna is controlled by His devotees’ love. Once, the Prime Minister of England had an important meeting with an ambassador. The ambassador arrived at the appointed time and announced himself to the Prime Minister’s secretary. The secretary went into the Prime Minister’s office and told him that the ambassador was waiting. When he came out, he told the ambassador that the Prime Minister was busy and that he should wait.

The ambassador, a patient man, sat down and waited. Five minutes passed, and then ten, then fifteen, and still the Prime Minister did not come out to receive him. After twenty minutes the ambassador thought, “I am an important man. What could the Prime Minister possibly be doing that he cannot receive me? ” Finally, as the secretary left the Prime Minister’s office, he left the door slightly ajar. The ambassador decided, “I will peek in and see for myself what he is doing. ”

What the ambassador saw was the Prime Minister crawling on his hands and knees with his grandson riding him like a horse. Every time the grandson kicked him in the side, the Prime Minister jumped and quickly crawled forward. The grandson had the Prime Minister completely under his control. Prabhupada said, “In the same way, this is Krsna. ”

Krsna is an important person, but He is controlled by His devotees’ love just as the Prime Minister was controlled by the love of his grandson. Although he was the Prime Minister, he was crawling on his hands and knees and allowing himself to be kicked by his grandson—and he was relishing it. Not only that, but the exchange he was having with his grandson was more important than all the other business waiting for him in the other room.

## Spiritual Emotions

Every living being has an eternal relationship with Krsna; this means that each of us is meant to love Krsna. If we have not established our eternal relationship with Krsna, we are like a hand separated from the body. A hand separated from the body has no function. Similarly, every living entity is meant to be connected to Krsna. Without that connection, we serve no function.

We are all seeking an object of love, but we cannot find that perfect object in which to repose our affection. We use the word *love* loosely; real love cannot be applied to material objects. Rather, it is meant for Krsna. Real love means complete affection, and complete affection cannot be reposed in a temporary object. If we are experiencing real love, we are reposing all our affection on one object without diverting it for a moment. Love in this world is a perverted reflection of our original love for Krsna because the only loveable objects in this world are both unworthy of our full affection and temporary. Our love here is also selfishly motivated. The love the devotees in the spiritual world experience is free of these defects; it is unmotivated and uninterrupted and directed toward an eternal and most worthy object.

The pure devotees’ love flows toward Krsna continuously as the Ganges flows forever toward the sea, as Queen Kunti describes in her prayers. That love takes the form of speaking about Krsna *(kirtana).* The elderly cowherd men and women speak about Krsna’s mischievous activities, the *gopis* speak about His beauty, and the cowherd boys speak about their friendly relationship with Him. As their affection for Him flows, Krsna fully satisfies each of them.

Prabhupada has assured us that we too can come to love Krsna and be loved by Him: “If you take one step toward Krsna, Krsna will take ten steps toward you. ” If we endeavor to remember Krsna, He will reciprocate and allow us to do so.

The devotees in the spiritual world do not do anything unless it gives Krsna pleasure. They make no endeavor to satisfy themselves at all. Yet they are most satisfied. They never experience distress, pain, or anxiety as we experience it. There *is* anxiety in the spiritual world, but it is of a quite different nature than the anxiety we find here. The anxiety devotees experience in the spiritual world simply heightens their attachment to Krsna.

Neither the activities nor the emotions felt in the spiritual world are stagnant. The impersonalists are afraid of the soul’s eternality because they wonder what they will do “forever. ” But devotees in the spiritual world feel an ever-increasing ecstatic bliss and have no loss of pastimes. Everything in the spiritual world from the landscape to the other devotees acts to stimulate ecstatic love. And of course, Krsna, the greatest stimulant for ecstatic love, is present in the spiritual world with His wonderful qualities, pastimes, names, and form. His beauty awakens love, as does His attire, ornaments, and the sheer softness of His body. In Goloka Vrndavana, many of Krsna’s ornaments are natural—He wears nicely applied *tilaka,* sandalwood paste, and minerals from the earth to paint His body and face, and He wears beautiful forest flower garlands made with five types of flowers. These garlands extend to His knees or His feet. Aside from such natural ornaments, Krsna wears necklaces, bracelets, and a crown or turban. When a devotee even sees these ornaments, his or her ecstatic love is stimulated.

When a mother who has great love for her child sees her child coming to her with open arms, this stimulates her affection for the child. Because she deeply loves her child, she feels compelled by that love to act—in this case, to embrace her child. In spiritual science this is called *anubhava.*

Sometimes even anxiety will stimulate love. For example, when Mother Yasoda sees Krsna in Kaliya’s coils, she thinks of her son’s welfare and wants to protect Him. These feelings come from her love, but they also increase her love. Pure love by its nature tends to grow under stimulus. In the spiritual world, almost everything stimulates the devotees’ ecstatic love.

In the spiritual world, child Krsna and His friends build sand-castles, go out to the pasturing grounds with the calves or cows, and play together in various ways. There are a variety of devotees in *sakhya-rati,* all of whom love Krsna intensely but according to their age in relation to His age. Some of Krsna’s friends are younger, some the same age, and some older. Some of His youngest friends serve Him by telling Him where His calves have wandered to so that He does not have to find them Himself. Some of the older friends protect Him, standing in front, on both sides, and in back so He won’t face danger. Although there are no demons in the spiritual world, His friends have heard about them and are always on the lookout, wanting only to protect Krsna in case a demon comes.

Krsna’s cowherd girlfriends feel such separation when He goes out to the pasturing grounds that they spend their days meditating on Him. They stand on their roofs watching Him disappear into the forest—and later returning—seeing the cloud of dust raised by the cows’ hooves gradually obscuring their beloved. In Krsna’s absence they also speak about Him. Some of them meet Him in the forest or at Govardhana during the day, escaping their families on some pretext or other. Some of Krsna’s cowherd boyfriends facilitate these meetings. Such pastimes go on eternally in the spiritual world. Krsna is the center of all attention, and He simply enjoys the love exchanges He shares with His devotees.

When Krsna comes to this universe, He invites us home. But we are not attracted, so He engages in pastimes to awaken our attraction. He displays His pastimes here so that we can hear and chant about them and thus become purified of our selfish interests and remember our beloved Lord. These pastimes never end; Srila Prabhupada says the news from the spiritual world is always “going on. ” And the Lord’s devotees are like transcendental newscasters, always broadcasting this news. Such news is *real* news, because it is ever-fresh *(nitya-nava-navayamana).*

Pariksit Maharaja says in the Tenth Canto that devotees who have accepted Krsna in the core of their hearts are always attracted to speaking about Him. The pastimes and other details about Krsna that they discuss remain ever-fresh in their hearts. The devotees are spontaneously attracted to speaking about Krsna just as materialists are spontaneously attracted to speaking about sex. In the material world news quickly becomes old. Why else would we throw away the newspapers after we have read them? But spiritual news is never old and never dry. We can hear about Krsna again and again and the news only becomes fresher.

Krsna satisfies the devotees who develop the urge to hear His messages. The Bhagavatam (1. 2. 17) states:

*srnvatam sva-kathah krsnah*  
*punya-sravana-kirtanah*  
*hrdy antah stho hy abhadrani*  
*vidhunoti suhrt satam*

“Sri Krsna, the Personality of Godhead, who is the Paramatma \[Supersoul\] in everyone’s heart and the benefactor of the truthful devotee, cleanses desire for material enjoyment from the heart of the devotee who has developed the urge to hear His messages, which are in themselves virtuous when properly heard and chanted. ”

We are meant to focus on the *sadhya* by practicing *sadhana. Sadhya* refers to the goal of life, love for Krsna, and *sadhana* refers to hearing and chanting about Krsna. We must practice *sadhana* every day with the idea that we are trying to reach the *sadhya.* If we practice daily, we will understand what this verse means.

What does it mean to focus on *sadhya!* It means striving to render the type of spontaneous devotional service offered by the residents in Vrndavana. But until we come to the platform of serving with their pure love, we must strictly and obediently follow the rules and regulations and execute *sadhana-bhakti* by regularly hearing about the Lord’s pastimes. Such *sadhana* will gradually purify our heart and awaken our attraction to Krsna. Don’t try to jump to the spontaneous platform without first qualifying yourself. Strive to become attracted to Krsna’s qualities, form, and pastimes.

If we go on hearing about Krsna daily with the desire to become attracted to Him in a particular mood, *lobha* (greed) will awaken and we will find ourselves wanting to hear and chant about Him more and more. Do not let a day go by without remembering Krsna.

When we attain *bhava-bhakti,* our devotional service will be governed by spiritual emotions. Don’t try to imitate these emotions now or project an image of ourselves as having a direct relationship with Krsna such as the *gopis* have with Him. Such projection makes one a *sahajiya.* Sometimes the *gopis* appear angry with Krsna, but we should not think we can imitate that apparent anger. Krsna will never accept service offered in anger except from those who love Him spontaneously.

There is a nice story about Mother Yasoda’s spiritual emotions and how absorbed she is in thoughts of Krsna. Take these examples; see how Krsna’s highest devotees are always absorbed in thoughts of Him. This story is from Sanatana Gosvami’s *Brhad-bhagavatamrta:*

Early one morning, the cowherd boys came to Krsna’s house as He was getting dressed to go to the pastures with His friends and cows. All His friends carry flutes and buffalo horns, and they blow them and make noise as they assemble. Krsna heard the commotion from inside His house, and He wanted to join them immediately.

The early mornings are hard for the *gopas* and *gopis,* because it is at that time of day that Krsna leaves for the forest and they are

plunged into feelings of great separation from Him. The devotees cannot tolerate such separation. Mother Yasoda spends her days afraid of what might happen to her beloved son in the forest. What harm will come to Him? Therefore she offers prayers to Lord Narayana to protect her son.

As Krsna readies Himself to leave, Mother Yasoda anticipates His departure with dread. Her anxiety is a spiritual emotion born of her deep attachment to Krsna. The younger *gopis* also experience such anxiety. In one prayer the *gopis* say, “When Krsna leaves in the morning to go to the pasturing ground, we are simply standing, feeling the pain of separation. And when He returns from the pasturing ground, we look at His beautiful face and cannot remove our eyes from His features even for a moment. But then at that moment, our eyelids blink and we curse Lord Brahma for being so stupid as to have created eyes that blink. ” Such are the feelings the *gopis* have when Krsna is departing and returning.

As Krsna heard the sounds of the boys’ flutes and bugles, He rushed outside with His mother. But Mother Yasoda did not want Krsna to leave. She followed Krsna and the cowherd boys out of the yard, and then toward the outskirts of the village. Krsna and His friends were jubilant to be going out and were playing as they moved toward the forest. Mother Yasoda stood on the village outskirts just looking at her son and beholding His beauty. Then just before He entered the forest, she called Him to her. She adjusted His clothes—Krsna should be dressed properly (Krsna wears different types of clothes. Sometimes He wears four pieces of clothing and sometimes two. Usually He wears *cadar,* a cape, a *dhoti,* and a turban. These are described as *uddipana*—objects that when seen stimulate love for Krsna).

After adjusting his clothes, Mother Yasoda turned away as if to return home, but then she turned around again and looked at Krsna, wondering if Krsna were hungry. Again she called Him to her and this time gave Him fruit, sweets, and nectar. Krsna, standing with His friends and calves, accepted it all. Then again Mother Yasoda walked away as if to return home.

But she only walked a few steps before she again turned around. This time she gave Krsna *pan.* Seeming satisfied, she turned, then walked back toward Krsna. She told Him, “Krsna, be very careful. Don’t go too deep into the forest with the calves. There are dangerous thorns there. ” Krsna used to come home with scratches all over His body, and Mother Yasoda thought that Krsna played too roughly with His friends in the forest.

Again she turned and again came back after only a few steps. This time she spoke to Balarama and some of the older boys: “Now Rama, protect Your younger brother. We have seen that the forest is dangerous. You stand in front of Him, and you, Sridama, stand behind Him and protect Him. Amsu, and you, Subala, stand on either side of Krsna. Watch your friend Krsna and make sure nothing happens to Him. ”

The next time she turned back to the village and then back toward Krsna, Krsna fell at her lotus feet and embraced them. Then He stood up and embraced her. He said, “Mother, please.

You have many duties to perform. They are waiting for you back at the village. Please go home. My friends are waiting for Me. ” But still He could not leave until Mother Yasoda allowed Him to go. Finally He pleaded, “Please, just go home! ”

After being embraced by Krsna in this way, Mother Yasoda finally allowed Him to go, although she remained standing in the road, motionless. She appeared like a cow who had just lost her newborn calf. As Krsna entered the forest she became stunned. This ecstasy is called *stambha*; her body and senses were inert. Tears flowed without hindrance from her eyes. Mother Yasoda remained there until the last particle of dust raised by Krsna’s leaving had settled. She looked just like a painted picture, watching Krsna entering into the forest. This is Krsna consciousness. This is absorption.

There is a verse in the Tenth Canto that describes Mother Yasoda dressed in a saffron-yellow sari, with a belt around her wide hips. She is pulling on a churning rope with her hands and laboring considerably. And while she is working, her bracelets and earrings are tinkling and swinging and her whole body is trembling because she is absorbed in her motherly affection for Krsna. This motherly affection causes milk to flow from her breasts and her beautiful face to be suffused with perspiration. The *malati* flowers fall from her hair. Srila Prabhupada calls this section the description of Mother Yasoda’s *bhakti* and says that those who want to attain a similar motherly relationship with Krsna should meditate on and remember this description. Such meditation will allow them to understand Mother Yasoda’s devotion for Krsna and develop a similar devotion themselves. Meditating on a verse like this is called *mantra-upasana,* and it is compared to seeing a still-frame in a movie. As we meditate on this one frame in the ever-changing *lila,* gradually this frame will become manifest in our heart.

After perfecting *mantra-updsana,* we can come to the even more focused meditation called *svarasiki. Svarasiki* is like having the entire movie of Krsna’s *lila* playing on the screen of our heart.

Of course, before we can practice either *mantra-updsana* or *svarasiki,* we have to first focus on serving Krsna favorably, which begins with *anartha-nivrtti.* Without clearing *anarthas,* it is not possible to meditate deeply on Krsna’s pastimes.

For the residents of the spiritual world, Krsna is the transcendental Cupid firing arrows into their hearts. Krsna’s transcendental arrows are His beautiful form, His eyes, the sound of His flute, and the aroma of His body. He uses these transcendental arrows to capture His devotees’ hearts. Because their hearts have been captured, the devotees are filled with constant thought of Krsna. Even a moment’s separation from Krsna feels like twelve years or more.

If we wish to one day approach the transcendental realm, it is useful to understand the constituents of ecstatic love. That which stimulates love for Krsna is known as *vibhava. Vibhava* has two categories, *alambana* and *uddipana. Alambana* are the basic excitants of love of Godhead, and it has two two features, the a*sraya* and the *visaya.* Krsna is the *visaya* or object of love, and the devotees are the *asraya* or reservoir (shelter) of ecstatic love.

*Uddipana* means “that which lights the lamp of devotion, ” and as such it refers to a variety of stimuli. Krsna’s qualities, for example, attract the devotees’ love, as does His body, beauty, attire, and *cesta* (activities). There are many more forms of *uddipana,* including Krsna’s flute-song and even His devotees. Each type of love has specific stimuli. Krsna’s qualities, form, and pastimes during His *kaumara-lila* stimulate *vatsalya-prema,* for example. Throughout His childhood, the love of the devotees in *vatsalya-rasa* is stimulated by Krsna’s age, His childish movements, childish talk, smile, enchanting eye movements, and all His childish ways. When the devotees in *vatsalya-priti* see these things, their desire to love Krsna as a parent loves a child is stimulated.

The *pauganda-lila* contains special stimuli for the devotees situated in *sakhya-rati.* When Krsna goes with His friends to the pasturing grounds, they carry buffalo horns and flutes, and Krsna holds a *keli-padma* (play lotus). Simply seeing such things stimulates love in His cowherd friends.

Certain activities of Krsna stimulate love in one devotee or another. For example, one time, Mother Yasoda said, “Just look at Krsna looking stealthily toward all sides and slowly stepping forward from the bushes. It appears that He is coming just to steal the butter. Don’t expose yourself or He may understand that we are looking toward Him. I want to enjoy the sight of His eyebrows moving in this cunning way, and I want to see His fearful eyes and beautiful face. ” *(The Nectar of Devotion,* p. 346) Mother Yasoda enjoys seeing Krsna afraid and therefore dependent. Krsna is afraid of her reprimands.

Once when Mother Yasoda was churning yogurt into butter and in her heart thinking of Krsna, she sang about Krsna’s wonderful activities. She remembered how Krsna had sometimes told her, “Mother, I am expert in this art of stealing butter. ” While Mother Yasoda was churning, Krsna suddenly came up to her and threatened, “Mother, either you feed Me now or I am going to break that pot! ” Krsna put His hand on the churning rod to stop His mother’s work. He who churns the hearts of all living entities in creation put His hands on His mother’s churning rod to get her to feed Him her breastmilk.

Mother Yasoda was captivated when Krsna did that, and she immediately stopped what she was doing and pulled Krsna onto her lap. She looked at His beautiful face and hair. His hair was scattered over His head, and had fallen to the top of His eyebrows. His scattered hair is one of the stimulants for ecstatic love during Krsna’s *madhya-kaumara-lila.* Mother Yasoda looked at these beautiful features on her son’s body and appreciated how wonderfully gentle He was—and her love for Krsna increased.

Devotees who take shelter in hearing such transcendental pastimes in the association of devotees can become free from the influence of material nature. Narada Muni describes that just like continuous waves of a river, these pastimes of the Lord are flowing. If we become caught up in these waves, we will forget about the necessities of life—hunger and thirst—and become free from illusion, lamentation, and fear. Narada Muni invites the devotees to take shelter in these pastimes.

**Question: How does Krsna maintain the eternally liberated souls in the spiritual world?**

Living beings in the spiritual world are maintained by love. Krsna gives each of them His heart and becomes so controlled by their love that He even becomes covered by that love and forgets He is God.

Everyone is maintained by Krsna’s *svarupa-sakti,* His eternal spiritual potency. The *svarupa-sakti* is by nature *sat, cit,* and *ananda* (eternal and full of knowledge and bliss). The devotees’ full knowledge is that Krsna is their son or friend or lover or master. This is called *mamata-buddhi.* Thus the devotees are maintained by the loving relationship they have with Krsna and by His reciprocation with them.

**Question: How can we experience the emotions of the spiritual world?**

Prabhupada warns that we cannot imitate transcendental emotions. Never make a show of such emotions for the public, as if to prove you are a great devotee. Rather, execute devotional service in such a way that the advanced stage comes. *Bhava* is something to be aspired for. The actual experience of love of God is deeply emotional and not something born of cheap words or external feelings.

Sometimes people say they love Krsna. But what does it actually mean to love Krsna? Love of Krsna is a very, very, overpowering spiritual emotion, tasted only when we are genuinely situated on the platform of *bhava.*

It is good to understand the nature of real ecstasy. When a mother sees her child’s face, her affection is aroused and she wants to do something to give her child pleasure. This service increases her affection. When she sees her child’s face and embraces that child, her embrace actually stimulates her love. This is called *anubhava. Anu* means “to follow, ” or “that which comes after. ” *Anubhava* is the activity that follows the feeling of love. Pure devotees may exhibit *anubhava* by embracing Krsna, singing, or dancing.

There are two types of *anubhava, sita,* where there is not much bodily movement, and *ksepana,* where the devotees roll on the ground, dance wildly in ecstasy, or roar like madmen. There are quite a few examples of *ksepana anubhava* in the *Caitanya-bhagavata.* Sometimes Lord Caitanya or Lord Nityananda roared so loudly that Their roaring could be heard all over the universe.

*Anubhavas* are conscious responses to ecstatic love. But there are also other kinds of ecstasy—*sattvika-bhavas,* for example, which are involuntary and which overtake the *suddha-sattva-e*nriched soul. *Sattva* means goodness, but *suddha-sattva* means pure, unalloyed goodness or devotion. When the living entities are saturated with *suddha-sattva,* they experience the transcendental emotions called *sattvika-bhavas.*

There are eight *sattvika-bhavas:* being stunned *(stambha),* perspiring *(sveda),* having one’s hair stand on end *(romanca),* intense shivering (*kampa),* faltering of the voice *(svara-bheda),* crying tears *(asru),* change of the bodily color *(vaivarnya),* and becoming totally devastated *(pralaya).* Each of these ecstatic symptoms is uncontrollable and involuntary. When all eight *sattvika-bhavas* manifest simultaneously, the ecstasy is called *asta-sattvika-bhava.*

Srila Rupa Gosvami explains that when a strong Krsna conscious emotion manifests, it first appears in the heart and agitates it. Then the mind becomes agitated, and then the life airs. When the life airs are disturbed, they become dislodged and come into contact with the body’s physical elements, disturbing them. When ecstasy contacts earth, one becomes stunned; when it contacts fire, one perspires; when it contacts air, one’s voice falters; when it contacts ether, one becomes devastated, paralyzed. With *stambha* the mind is stunned, but when one experiences *pralaya,* the mind is paralyzed.

And there are degrees of intensity to these ecstasies. The weakest ecstasy is called *dhumayita,* smoldering. Then there is *jvalita,* blazing. Then *dipta,* burning, and *uddipta,* burning brightly. Finally comes *suddipta,* an intensity only Srimati Radharani and Lord Caitanya experience.

When ecstasy is at the level of *dhumayita,* it can still be controlled or hidden from others, because only one or two symptoms are manifest and only to a limited degree. At *jvalita,* two or three symptoms manifest simultaneously and the ecstasy is more difficult to control. At *dipta,* four or five symptoms manifest, and at *uddipta,* all the symptoms manifest simultaneously. Caitanya Mahaprabhu would sometimes manifest *uddipta sattvika-bhava,* displaying all eight symptoms simultaneously.

Additional *sattvika-bhavas to* those previously described are *Snigdha, digdha,* and *ruksa. Snigdha-sattvika-bhava* is an emotion arising from one of the *rasas* (either primary or secondary) that one has with Krsna. For example, once Srimati Radharani was stringing garlands of *kunda* flowers. She heard the sound of Krsna’s flute and immediately stopped Her work. She could not move; She had become stunned by ecstasy that the sound of His flute had evoked. Krsna’s flute is *uddipana* because it stimulates ecstatic love. A miscellaneous item (*prakirna*) that stimulates ecstatic love is Krsna’s flute. Love is always present in the heart of every living being, but it needs to be stimulated to bring it out. To bring out the love of a mother, she needs to see those things that are connected with her child. When Srimati Radharani became stunned when Her love was stimulated by Krsna’s flute-song, it was *mukhya-snigdha,* an ecstasy arising from Her primary *rasa.*

*Gauna-snigdha* arises from one of the secondary *rasas.* For example, Mother Yasoda became angry at Nanda Maharaja for taking Krsna to Mathura. She was so angry that her voice faltered and she could not speak. This faltering of voice is known as *svara-bheda-sattvika-bhava,* an emotion arising from an indirect relationship with Krsna through her relationship with Krsna’s devotee, Nanda Maharaja.

After *Snigdha* comes *digdha,* which refers to *sattvika-bhavas* not connected to either the primary or secondary *rasas.* For example, once Mother Yasoda dreamed that she saw the fearsome form of Putana lying on the ground. Her whole body began to tremble. When she awoke, she became fearful and ran around looking for Krsna. This emotion arose from the dream rather than directly from her relationship with Krsna.

After *digdha-sattvika-bhava* is *ruksa. Snigdha* means *moist, digdha burnt,* and *ruksa dried up. Ruksa-sattvika-bhava* manifests in a person who has no attachment to Krsna. For example, an impersonalist may participate in a *kirtana* with elevated devotees because he has heard that by so doing he can attain liberation. While taking part and thinking, “I will get liberation, ” he may manifest so-called ecstatic symptoms, although he has no *krsna-rati.*

Similarly, Prakasananda Sarasvati’s *sannyasi* disciples saw Lord Caitanya and the hairs on their body stood up, although they were impersonalists. Still, they experienced ecstasy upon seeing Lord Caitanya and the effulgence He was manifesting that shone greater than millions of suns. Later, they became Vaisnavas by the Lord’s mercy.

However, there are four types of *sattvika-abhasa* or semblances of ecstasy. Srila Rupa Gosvami calls the first *rati-abhasa. Rati* is synonymous with *bhava.* At *bhava-bhakti,* since devotional service is completely governed by the devotees’ emotions, devotees may manifest ecstatic symptoms in the form of tears or physical trembling. These symptoms can then be reflected in the hearts of those around them. Therefore, associating with elevated persons may allow others to experience a semblance of ecstasy, but their symptoms and emotions are not real love of God.

Srila Rupa Gosvami also describes *sattvika-abhasa.* Sometimes persons have strong emotional dispositions and the slightest provocation brings tears. For example, a spiritual master who was speaking to his disciples about Sri Radha’s intimate pastimes saw a disciple crying. The spiritual master thought, “Look at this disciple who cries simply upon hearing Sri Radha’s name! ” So he asked his disciple why he was crying. The disciple said, “Whenever I look at you as you speak, I see your beard, and it reminds me of my goat, whose name was Radha and who just died. This makes me cry. ” Thus someone with a strong emotional disposition may hear something about Krsna and cry, but the emotions are not truly focused on love of God and are therefore known as *sattvika-abhasa* rather than *sattvika-bhava.*

There is also *nihsattva. Nih* means *without,* and thus *nihsattva* means without *sattva.* Sometimes persons manifest symptoms as a show. They think, “These people will recognize me as greatly advanced if I roll on the ground and cry. ” Although they may then proceed to roll on the ground, they are feeling no genuine spiritual emotion. The *Caitanya-bhagavata* tells the story of a *brahmana* who was envious of Haridasa Thakura. He saw Haridasa Thakura manifesting genuine ecstatic symptoms and thought, “This Haridasa has taken a low birth and I am a *brahmana.* He was born a *yavana* and yet everyone gives him so much respect. ”

People stood by respectfully with folded hands whenever Haridasa Thakura manifested ecstatic symptoms, and whenever he returned to external consciousness, Haridasa would be embarrassed and slip away quietly. After one such incident, the *brahmana* thought, “I will take advantage of this audience and do the same. ” Crying, he fell to the ground and rolled in the dirt. But the snakebite physician took a stick and began to beat him. Then the envious *brahmana* was really crying, and not out of love of God. He leapt up and ran away in fear. Such displays are called *nihsattva,* showbottle.

The last form of *sattvika-abhasa* is *pratipa,* referring to apparently ecstatic symptoms that manifest in someone who is envious of Krsna. Srila Rupa Gosvami uses Kamsa as an example. Kamsa once saw Krsna, and out of envy his upper lip trembled and his face glowed red like the sun. That is, he was trembling and his body was changing color. But these symptoms were not due to any attachment he had for Krsna whatsoever.

Srila Rupa Gosvami gave us these examples of *sattvika-abhasa* so that we could learn to discriminate genuine *sattvika-bhava* from imitation. Still, the *acaryas* have also encouraged us to pray for the real thing. When Narottama dasa Thakura prays, “When will my body tremble when I chant Gauranga’s name? When will tears flow from my eyes by chanting the name of Krsna? ” he is actually showing us by his own example what we should pray for. We need to pray to develop deep attachment for Krsna. Simply by hearing or chanting Krsna’s name should stimulate such love in our hearts. Eventually it will.

Srila Prabhupada explains that any system of religion that teaches its followers to love God is bona fide. He said, “Krsna came to teach, but still so many fools and rascals did not accept. ”

In order to reclaim those in the lowest classes of humankind, Krsna mercifully appeared as Lord Caitanya.

After Krsna appeared and then disappeared from this world, He thought, “Knowing My opulences, the whole world looks upon Me with awe and veneration. But devotion made feeble by such reverence does not attract Me.... I shall accept the role of a devotee, and I shall teach devotional service by practicing it Myself. ” (Cc. *Adi* 3. 16, 20)

The Lord appears whenever there is necessity. He states in *Bhagavad-gita* (4. 7-8): “Whenever and wherever there is a decline in religious practice, O descendant of Bharata, and a predominant rise of irreligion—at that time I descend Myself. To deliver the pious and to annihilate the miscreants, as well as to reestablish the principles of religion, I Myself appear, millennium after millennium. ” And in *Bhagavad-gita* (3. 21):

*yad yad acarati sresthas*  
*tat tad evetaro janah*  
*sa yat pramanam kurute*  
*lokas tad anuvartate*

“Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues. ” The Lord’s various expansions can come during any age and teach religious principles, but only Lord Caitanya’s gives the devotion practiced by the residents of Vrndavana. He chanted His own names and accepted the mood of the topmost devotee, experiencing the highest ecstasy.

Srila Rupa Gosvami says:

*namo maha-vadanyaya*  
*krsna-prema-pradaya te*  
*krsnaya krsna-caitanya-*  
*namne gaura-tvise namah*

“O most munificent incarnation! You are Krsna Himself appearing as Sri Krsna Caitanya Mahaprabhu. You have assumed the golden color of Srimati Radharani, and You are widely distributing pure love of Krsna. We offer our respectful obeisances unto You. ” (Cc. *Madhya 19. 53)*

Krsna says:

*yuga-dharma pravartaimu nama-sankirtana*  
*cari bhava-bhakti diya nacamu bhuvana*

“I shall personally inaugurate the religion of the age—*nama-sankirtana,* the congregational chanting of the holy name. I shall make the world dance in ecstasy, realizing the four mellows of loving devotional service. ” (Cc. Adi 3. 19)

Srila Rupa Gosvami assures us that if we take one drop of that ecstatic love and try to analyze it, we will see that that one drop is deeper than the deepest ocean. And analyzing ecstatic love of God would allow us to go deeper and deeper. There is no end to the depths of ecstatic love because a devotee experiences ever-increasing bliss. In trying to analyze the bliss, we will realize we are standing on the shore of a vast ocean. We can never conceive of the depth of this ocean. One drop of this ocean can inundate the entire creation with love of God.

There are three types of happiness: happiness derived from sense enjoyment, happiness derived from liberation, and happiness derived from *bhakti.* The topmost happiness arises from devotion. When Lord Caitanya bestows His merciful glance on His devotees, that glance bestows such happiness that it makes everything else pale in comparison. We cannot rise to the transcendental plane of direct association with Radha and Krsna unless we begin to practice Krsna consciousness with Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mercy. To the degree that we serve Lord Caitanya, to that degree we will be able to understand Radha-Krsna’s Vrndavana pastimes. Try to serve Caitanya Mahaprabhu by accepting the holy name He came to distribute and making your own life perfect, then giving His mercy to others.

## The Spiritual Welfare of Others

*svasty astu visvasya khalah prasidatam*  
*dhyayantu bhutani sivam mitho dhiya*  
*manas ca bhadram bhajatad adhoksaje*  
*avesyatam no matir apy ahaituki*

May there be good fortune throughout the universe, and may all envious persons be pacified. May all living entities become calm by practicing *bhakti-yoga,* for by accepting devotional service they will think of each other’s welfare. Therefore let us all engage in the service of the supreme transcendence, Lord Sri Krsna, and always remain absorbed in thought of Him. ” *(Bhag.* 5. 18. 9)

Srila Prabhupada writes in his purport:

The following verse describes a Vaisnava:

*vancha-kalpa-tarubhyas ca*  
*krpa-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitanam pavanebhyo*  
*vaisnavebhyo namo namah*

Just like a desire tree, a Vaisnava can fulfill all the desires of anyone who takes shelter of his lotus feet. Prahlada Maharaja is a typical Vaisnava. He prays not for himself, but for all living entities— the gentle, the envious and the mischievous. He always thought of the welfare of mischievous persons like his father, Hiranyakasipu. Prahlada Maharaja did not ask for anything for himself; rather, he prayed for the Lord to excuse his demoniac father. This is the attitude of a Vaisnava, who always thinks of the welfare of the entire universe.

Anyone who approaches the Lord in prayer will ask for a benediction. Although devotees are *niskama* and have no material desires, still, we see in Prahlada Maharaja’s verse that they still want something. But the *niskama* devotees do not want something for themselves. Rather, they are peaceful, their minds fixed on Krsna. In such a state they can think of others’ welfare. As long as we harbor envy, lust, greed, or anger, it is difficult to think of the welfare of others. Therefore Prahlada Maharaja prays to Lord Nrsimhadeva for good fortune throughout the universe and for all envious persons to become pacified.

It is to the ultimate welfare of all living beings to engage in the Lord’s service. Those who serve the Lord can then engage others in such service. The heart is pacified by practicing *bhakti-yoga.* This verse and purport speak a lot about envy. An envious person cannot think of others’ welfare.

Narottama dasa Thakura describes that five of the six whips *(kama, krodha, lobha, moha, mada,* and *matsarya)* can be dovetailed in the Lord’s service. Only envy, *matsarya,* cannot be dovetailed. He sings, “Let me engage lust by offering the fruits of my activities for the pleasure of the Lord. Let me dovetail anger by becoming angry at those who become envious of the Lord’s devotees. Let me dovetail madness by becoming mad after Krsna. ” A devotee is bewildered when trying to find Krsna. But *matsarya,* envy, cannot be dovetailed. The envious think only of themselves.

Still, Srila Prabhupada wrote a letter stating that faults and envy exist even in the spiritual world. Regarding faults, the example is given that Krsna once became so bewildered by Srimati Radharani’s beauty that He milked a bull instead of a cow. Similarly, when the *gopis* heard Krsna’s flute, they became so bewildered that in their attempt to run quickly to the forest they put the upper portion of their clothes on the bottom and the bottom portion on the top. These are the kinds of faults that exist in the spiritual world.

Regarding envy, the *Padyavali* gives the example of a *gopi* speaking enviously of another *gopi* who had received Krsna’s favor. She said, “You should not think that Krsna favors you exclusively just because Krsna has painted some lines on your forehead. Krsna has painted the lines very perfectly on your forehead, and therefore, we can understand that Krsna was not the least bit agitated when He painted them. ” This is a manifestation of envy, but it contains no malice whatsoever. It is a kind of spiritual envy and should not be compared to the envy we experience in material consciousness.

Those who are malicious cannot consider the welfare of the object of their malice. Malice does not exist in the spiritual world. This means that in the spiritual world, everyone can think of one another’s welfare. The devotees always think about how they and others can serve Krsna better. Whatever spiritual envy they may express will only be to enhance Krsna’s enjoyment and will never be to the detriment of another person’s service to Him.

In the same letter in which he mentioned faults and envy, Srila Prabhupada writes about how some people think that Krsna consciousness is utopian—completely perfect. Then if they see faults, they want to reject everything, as if everything in Krsna consciousness is faulty. But we should know that there are faults even in the spiritual world. Do not be like flies going to the stool; rather, be like bees and go for the nectar.

## Preaching and Leading for the Welfare of Others

Those who have responsibilities in the Krsna consciousness movement sometimes find that we have to become strict when dealing with others. We cannot have anarchy in this movement, allowing everyone do whatever they want. Instead, we want to organize things in a way that is favorable for spreading Krsna consciousness. But it seems that in our attempts to organize things, sometimes we appear to neglect someone’s welfare.

Part of the problem is that we tend to be strict with others and liberal with ourselves. We are strict with others because we want them to follow our plans. When we see discrepancies in their behavior, we are also quick to point them out. But when devotees think of others’ welfare, they are actually thinking about how to inspire them to serve Krsna lovingly. Without offering inspiration, it is difficult to get others to follow our direction.

We sometimes assume a position of authority and bring things under our control, but we should acknowledge that we are not the *supreme* controller. Always remain conscious of that. Prabhupada writes, “Fortunate is he who is so entrusted \[with responsible work\], and if he is always fixed in the sense of being subordinate to the will of the Supreme, he is sure to come out successful in the discharge of his work. ” *(Bhag.* 3. 9. 29, purport) Those who do not work, being aware of their dependence on Krsna, who act in false ego, not hearing Krsna’s instructions, tend to become angry at others. When we forget that we are subordinate to Krsna, then even when we appear to get a good result, the internal landscape may not look so nice.

Sometimes in our efforts to organize our endeavors in Krsna’s service, we look only at the external ways to organize the preaching.

But we should always be careful not to neglect the internal ways of preaching. Lord Caitanya had both internal and external reasons for His descent to this world. Externally He came at Advaita Acarya’s request to distribute the congregational chanting of Krsna’s holy names. Internally, He wanted to relish Srimati Radharani’s position and to understand the transcendental mellows of His own position. He wanted to know, “What is it in Me that Srimati Radharani sees and loves? ” He wanted to taste Srimati Radharani’s bliss, and the only way He could do that was to adopt Her mood.

As Lord Caitanya was relishing Srimati Radharani’s mood and tasting Her bliss in the role of His own devotee, He taught us by His own example how to be the Lord’s devotees. We can apply His example: the internal way of spreading Krsna consciousness is to first experience it ourselves and then to give it to others by our own example. Externally, we may make so many arrangements to spread Krsna consciousness, but internally, we have to know that the most effective way to spread Krsna consciousness is to be Krsna conscious ourselves.

This is how Srila Prabhupada established the Krsna consciousness movement: the substance (his Krsna consciousness) first, and then the form or organization. When he gave Krsna consciousness to others and they became attracted to it, their own desire to do something for Krsna was awakened in their hearts.

And Srila Prabhupada gave us a practical way to offer our service when he encouraged us to preach around the world. He had a vision of how Krsna consciousness should be spread, and he was expert in organizing the preaching, but all of this expertise was based on his own devotional substance.

Prabhupada often said, “When you are preaching, management follows like a shadow. ” If we want to manage others, it is best if those others *want* to follow. The manager’s goal is to see the spark of devotion in everyone and then to fan that spark until their potential is fulfilled.

A reporter once asked Srila Prabhupada if he had mystic power. Prabhupada waved his hand in front of his disciples and said, “This is my mystic power. Previously all these young girls and boys were addicted to all kinds of sinful activities, and now they are free of them. This is my magic. ” Prabhupada was a master magician and a confidential servant of Lord Caitanya, who relished the highest ecstasies of love for Krsna internally, and had the most profound preaching vision externally. Prabhupada is unmatched in the disciplic succession. So many people came forward, and Prabhupada engaged them all with his very deep vision about how to expand Krsna consciousness all over the world.

Prabhupada was also expert at knowing, in his preaching, when to be as hard as a thunderbolt and when to be as soft as a rose. The devotees relished both moods; even when he was as hard as a thunderbolt the devotees accepted it as nectar because Prabhupada had already captured their hearts. It is natural to want to reciprocate with those who give us Krsna consciousness. Our managers can learn this lesson from Srila Prabhupada: When we capture the devotees’ hearts by compassionate preaching and caring for their welfare, then we can be as hard as a thunderbolt and the devotee will accept it as nectar.

Lord Caitanya was also sometimes strict in reprimanding His devotees. The devotees relished the Lord’s attention. Advaita Acarya *wanted to* be reprimanded by the Lord; He didn’t like it that Lord Caitanya offered Him so much respect. He felt ecstasy when the Lord reprimanded Him, and tears flowed from His eyes. He told Lord Caitanya, “Because You have reprimanded Me, You have accepted Me as Your servant! ” Being accepted as the Lord’s servant was Advaita Acarya’s greatest happiness.

In one incident, Advaita Acarya preached Mayavada philosophy to encourage Lord Caitanya to punish Him. When Lord Caitanya heard about Advaita Acarya’s new preaching, He became so angry that he beat the Acarya. He said, “You brought Me to this world to preach devotional service, and now You are preaching Mayavada philosophy? ” Lord Caitanya grabbed Advaita Acarya and dragged him to the ground. The Acarya’s wife, Srimati Sitadevi, who was standing nearby, begged the Lord, “Please! He’s an old man. Don’t hurt Him. What are you doing?! ” But Advaita Acarya was relishing the Lord’s chastisement as if it were the sweetest nectar.

Thus when devotees give their heart to someone, they relish the punishments that that person may mete out. But we should not use this knowledge as if it were a management tactic, because it will not work. We have to actually preach, actually think of others’ welfare, and actually be free of envy. And we should be careful that if we do become angry for some reason that we control it.

*Virodha-yukta-krodha* is anger arising from attachment to the results of our activities. This means that if we see others not participating to bring about our goals, we become angry at them. Such anger should be controlled. But better if we are Krsna conscious and can teach others by our example, inspiring them to serve Krsna voluntarily.
