By the time they enter adulthood, they should know clearly the difference between right and wrong. Srila Prabhupada wanted our children to have high moral values.

Those of you who are now entering adulthood should look back at your childhood and responsibly take note of everything you have learned. And if you did not learn everything, at least take note of what you were taught. Because now you are entering the world of self-responsibility. Children can make excuses that they do not know better; adults cannot.

If you drive a car on the wrong side of the road and you are stopped by the police, you cannot say that no one told you how to drive. If you have accepted the responsibility of operating a car, you should have known the rules of the road. You will have to take your fine.

Some of you may become professionals, going on to university or learning a technical skill, and some may become preachers. Whatever you choose to do, becoming an adult means that your future is now and that you have to think beyond your childhood. The *Bhagavad-gita* (2. 13) states:

*dehino ’smin yatha dehe*  
*kaumaram yauvanam jara*  
*tatha dehantara-praptir*  
*dhiras tatra na muhyati*

“As the embodied soul continuously passes, in this body, from boyhood to youth to old age, the soul similarly passes into another body at death. A sober person is not bewildered by such a change.”

Our life after this one should be spent with Krsna in the world beyond death; that world is filled with love, knowledge, eternality, and bliss. That is our our real future. Working toward attaining that life is part of our adult responsibility. *Bhagavad-gita* (2. 20) states:

*na jayate mriyate va kadacin*  
*nayam bhutva bhavita va na bhuyah*  
*ajo nityah sasvato ’yam purano*  
*na hanyate hanyamane sarire*

“For the soul there is neither birth nor death at any time. He has not come into being, does not come into being, and will not come into being. He is unborn, eternal, ever-existing and primeval. He is not slain when the body is slain. ”

Life with Krsna is who we really are. Remember your real future. Then all of you—children, new adults, old adults, everyone—will find that your future is bright. Take advantage of your education. Make your lives perfect. Make Srila Prabhupada smile. If you can make Srila Prabhupada smile, your life will be perfect and you will go back to Godhead.

## Brahmana Initiates

When we develop greed for Krsna, our life can very easily become successful. This greed is a necessary qualification for anyone who wants to advance in Krsna consciousness, whether that person is uninitiated, first initiated, or second initiated.

Srila Prabhupada once told some devotees who were taking second initiation that if a mother holds her baby on her lap and that child passes stool, she will immediately overlook it. A mother is tolerant with a young child. But if the mother is holding a nine-year-old on her lap and the child passes stool, the mother is not so tolerant. Prabhupada gave this example to show the responsibility of growing up in Krsna consciousness and taking brahminical initiation.

The *Bhagavatam* states that the master receives the blame for the wrongs committed by the servants. It is also said that a spot of white leprosy spoils one’s entire skin. Lord Krsna Himself gave this example while apologizing to the four Kumaras for the behavior of His servants, Jaya and Vijaya. Of course, all disciples are servants, and so the master is held responsible for all of them, but *brahmana* initiates are becoming older disciples and are held that much more responsible. Older children in the family are meant to set a good example for the younger children to follow.

According to the *Mahabharata* (as quoted in Srila Prabhupada’s purport to *Bhag.* 7. 9. 10):

*dharmas ca satyam ca damas tapas ca*  
*amatsaryam hris titiksanasuya*  
*yajnas ca danam ca dhrtih srutam ca*  
*vratani vai dvadasa brahmanasya*

“A *brahmana* must be perfectly religious. He must be truthful, and he must be able to control his senses. He must execute severe austerities, and he must be detached, humble and tolerant. He must not envy anyone, and he must be expert in performing sacrifices and giving whatever he has in charity. He must be fixed in devotional service and expert in the knowledge of the *Vedas.* These are the twelve qualifications for a *brahmana. ”*

*Brahmanas* must be able to think of others’ welfare. If they do not preach, they may lose whatever knowledge they have gathered.

There is a verse that says that all the demigods’ good qualities automatically manifest in those who have developed unalloyed devotion to the Supreme Lord, Vasudeva. These qualities may not appear the moment we take brahminical initiation, but still, we have to become greedy for Krsna and the devotional qualities will gradually be ours.

Prabhupada often explained that in giving brahminical initiation he was giving the devotees the chance to become *brahmanas.* This means that we are being given the chance to develop devotional qualities. While cultivating greed in Krsna consciousness, we should also remember what qualities we need to develop in our brahminical life. These qualities will only help us in our efforts to want Krsna exclusively and will prevent us from becoming an embarrassment to the spiritual master by correcting faults in our character, After all, wrong acts committed by the servants leads people to blame the master.

Although we may claim to be someone’s servant, if we do not demonstrate proper behavior toward others, we actually embarrass the master. Cultivate brahminical life and become qualified *brahmanas.* Then take up the duties Srila Prabhupada gave us and become preachers. Become nonenvious and think of others’ welfare. Make your life successful.
